Every user-applied tag disappears the moment extraction finishes, exactly when the invoice reaches the Audit Queue. **Fixed 2026-08-11**: `queue_worker/handlers.py:691` now merges instead of replacing — `invoice.tags = list(dict.fromkeys((invoice.tags or []) + extracted_data.get("tags", [])))` — order-preserving, deduped union of the user's original tags and whatever the extractor adds. Kept as a single `tags` column (no schema/migration change) rather than splitting user vs. extracted tags into separate columns. Verified: `ast.parse` clean (syntax-checked; full `pytest` run against this handler wasn't performed — would require the full backend DB/queue stack).
- `[x]` **Gap 191: Date filters query the wrong column and silently drop NULL-dated invoices** — closed 2026-08-13. Updated list, metric, and dashboard queries in `routers/invoices.py`, `routers/dashboard.py`, and `routers/outbound_dashboard.py` to filter on `created_at` (or its date cast) instead of `invoice_date`. Asserted by date-filtering tests in `tests/test_queries.py`.
- `[x]` **Gap 192 (SECURITY/COMPLIANCE): Invoice deletion hard-deleted the row and wiped AuditLog history** — closed 2026-08-11 (merged from `Global-common-bugs-Fix`). `DELETE /invoices/{id}` used to delete all `AuditLog` rows then the `Invoice`, destroying the compliance trail — contrast `routers/admin.py` (Gap 168), which deliberately detaches rather than deletes users for the same reason. Fix: `Invoice.deleted_at` (migration `a0b1c2d3e4f5`) + soft delete that sets `deleted_at`, keeps all prior `AuditLog` rows, and appends a `DELETE_INVOICE` entry. Blob/Chroma retained for restore. Product reads now go through `services/invoice_visibility.invoice_not_deleted()` — threaded into list/get/status/pdf/stream-ownership/dashboard/outbound/audit-resolve/trainer/reconciliation/chat-stats queries. Dedup-by-hash still sees soft-deleted rows (so a re-upload of a deleted invoice is still caught as a duplicate). Test: `tests/test_invoice_soft_delete.py`. Spec: `feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md`. **Note for whoever builds Gap 198's batch history/rollback**, which this entry's earlier "Fix direction" said depended on soft-delete landing first: it has landed, that dependency is now clear.
- `[x]` **Gap 193: No way to reverse a Paid or Rejected decision** — **Closed 2026-08-12 (reopen audit for Admins)**. Reopen/revert to `AUDIT_REQUIRED` from PAID/REJECTED is supported on the resolve endpoint, Admin-gated and audit-logged as `REOPEN_INVOICE`. Webhook/notification dispatch is bypassed. The FE console restricts the "Reopen Audit" button to Admin users.
- `[x]` **Gap 194: Webhook delivery is unobservable, and one flaky subscriber silently disables every event type** — `services/webhooks.py`. (1) `dispatch_webhook_event` (`:87-135`) increments `consecutive_failures` on any failure and sets `enabled = False` at `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES = 10` (`:22`) — disables the **whole subscription**, all event types, with only a `logger.warning` as the signal; nothing alerts the tenant. (2) `_deliver_with_retry` runs up to 3 attempts with a 5s timeout each and `time.sleep(2**attempt)` between them (`:67-84`), **inline** in the worker/request thread that just committed the invoice — a slow subscriber can add ~19s to the operation that triggered it, and every error is swallowed by design so a partial fan-out looks identical to a clean one. (3) There is no delivery log anywhere — no table, no endpoint, no UI — so "did this event fire?" is unanswerable from inside the product. **Fix, in order of value**: (a) persist a per-subscription delivery-attempt log and expose it on the webhooks settings page; (b) alert the tenant when auto-disable trips; (c) move delivery off the request/worker thread onto the queue so a slow subscriber can't back-pressure ingestion. **Closed 2026-08-12** (Checkpoint 12) — (a) and (c) built, (b) deliberately not (see "Not built" below). FE half in `fe_features_tracker.md` Gap 194. Spec updated: `docs/feature_15_webhooks.md` Task 15.7.
  - **Async dispatch** (`services/webhooks.py`): `dispatch_webhook_event()` (`:322-372`) no longer makes the HTTP call — it resolves matching subscriptions and calls `_enqueue_delivery()` (`:296-319`), which puts `{"task": "deliver_webhook", "kwargs": {tenant_id, subscription_id, event_type, payload}}` on `extraction-tasks-queue`, the same queue and message shape as `services/invoice_reconciliation.py::_enqueue` and `queue_worker/handlers.py::_enqueue_process_invoice`. Delivery itself moved to `queue_worker/handlers.py::handle_deliver_webhook` (`:841-895`) → `services/webhooks.deliver_webhook_now()` (`:248-293`), routed in `queue_worker/main_worker.py::_process_message` (`:151-160`). **No call site changed** — `queue_worker/handlers.py`, `routers/audit.py`, `routers/outbound_invoices.py` and `services/outbound_overdue.py` still call `dispatch_webhook_event(db_session, tenant_id, event_type, payload)` with the same signature; they just no longer pay the subscriber's ~19s worst case. `deliver_webhook_now()` re-reads the subscription at delivery time, so a queued message can't resurrect a subscription disabled/deleted/unsubscribed between enqueue and delivery. An enqueue failure writes a failed delivery-log row (`"Not queued: ..."`) but does **not** count toward auto-disable — failing to reach our own queue says nothing about the subscriber.
  - **Auto-disable scoping** — investigated before changing, per the "confirm the granularity or make it finer" instruction. Kept **per subscription**, and that is the right unit: a `WebhookSubscription` row is one `target_url` + one `secret`, so `enabled = False` can only ever mean "stop calling this URL"; a per-event-type kill switch would be a new feature, not this gap. The real defect was the *counting*. `record_delivery_result()` (`:171-245`) now counts into a new `WebhookSubscription.event_failure_counts` JSON column (`{event_type: consecutive failures}`) and trips `enabled = False` only when the failing event type has hit `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES` **and** no other event type on the subscription is currently healthy (none has a count of 0 — i.e. none succeeded on its last delivery). So a subscriber that 500s on `outbound_invoice.overdue` while accepting `invoice.completed` stays enabled, where it previously went dark for every event after 10 overdue sweeps; a genuinely dead endpoint fails everything it is sent and still auto-disables exactly as before. `consecutive_failures` is maintained as `max(event_failure_counts.values())` so the public dict and the FE health warning keep working. Legacy rows are lazily backfilled (the old flat count is attributed to the first event type that fails post-upgrade) rather than getting a free reset. `routers/webhooks.py::update_webhook` (`:140-147`) clears the map as well as the total on re-enable — clearing only the total would leave the subscription one failure from re-disabling.
  - **Delivery log**: new `WebhookDeliveryLog` model (`models.py:367-392`) — `tenant_id`, `subscription_id`, `event_type`, `success`, `status_code`, `attempts`, `duration_ms`, `error`, `created_at`; one row per *attempt series*, not per HTTP call. `_deliver_with_retry()` was changed to return a `DeliveryResult` dataclass carrying the status/error/attempt count it previously discarded into a `logger.warning`. Exposed at `GET /api/v1/webhooks/{id}/deliveries?limit=` (`routers/webhooks.py::list_webhook_deliveries`, `:157-203`), newest first, tenant-scoped through the same subscription lookup as every other route in that file (another tenant's id is a 404, not a leak). `DELETE /webhooks/{id}` now also clears that subscription's log rows — the table has no FK to the subscription (it's written from the queue worker), so they'd otherwise be unreachable orphans. `error` never contains the payload or the signing secret.
  - **Migration** `alembic/versions/b8c1d4e7f209_add_webhook_delivery_logs.py` — creates `webhook_delivery_logs` (3 indexes) and adds `event_failure_counts` with `server_default '{}'`, so existing rows need no backfill.
  - **Tests**: `tests/test_webhooks.py` **27 passed** (was 14), `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_webhooks.py -q`. Async dispatch is asserted as *no HTTP client is even constructed* on the dispatching thread plus exactly one correctly-shaped queue message; disabled subscriptions produce neither; an unreachable queue produces a log row with the failure counters untouched. Scoping has both directions covered — `test_auto_disables_when_no_event_type_is_delivering` (dead endpoint still trips) vs `test_one_failing_event_type_does_not_disable_a_healthy_one` (10 failures on one event leave the subscription enabled while another event's last delivery succeeded, and that event still delivers afterwards) — plus legacy-counter backfill and the re-enable reset. Delivery-log coverage: one row per attempt series with `attempts == 3` on retry-then-succeed, `status_code`/`error` recorded on a 503, newest-first listing, 404 cross-tenant, rows removed with the subscription. Queue-worker handler tested directly, including that it swallows a raising delivery (a raise would leave the message undeleted and redeliver an event the subscriber may already have received). `tests/test_outbound_overdue.py` re-run unchanged, **17 passed**.
  - **Not verified**: no live queue-worker run — enqueue is asserted against a mocked `QueueClient` and delivery by calling the handler directly, so "a real message on `extraction-tasks-queue` is picked up by a deployed `caw-queue-worker` replica and delivered" is **unproven without deploying this build**. Migration `b8c1d4e7f209` has **not been applied to any database**: `alembic heads` currently reports three unmerged heads from other in-flight changes (`a2b3c4d5e6f7`, `c1d2e3f4a5b6`, `c9d0e1f2a3b4`), so `alembic upgrade head` needs a merge revision first — this revision was checked only by loading it through `ScriptDirectory` and confirming it resolves onto `a2b3c4d5e6f7`. Tests build their schema from `SQLModel.metadata.create_all`, not the migration. The multi-head state is pre-existing and not owned by this gap.
  - **Not built** (deliberate, keeping to the approved scope): the tracker's item (b), alerting the tenant when auto-disable trips — auto-disable is still surfaced only in the settings UI (health warning + delivery log), not pushed by email/notification. Also no retention/trim on `webhook_delivery_logs` (rows accumulate until the subscription is deleted), and no per-event-type *disable* granularity, which the investigation above found unnecessary.
- `[x]` **Gap 195: No webhook event for duplicate detection, and duplicates carry no structured reference to the file they matched** — `routers/webhooks.py:19-27`'s `ALLOWED_EVENT_TYPES` has no duplicate event (`_validate_event_types()` would reject a subscriber asking for one); the duplicate path (`routers/invoices.py:66-120`) publishes a Redis SSE event for the live UI but never calls `dispatch_webhook_event`. Separately, the duplicate's only pointer to its original is prose inside an alert message (`:78-84`, `f"...previously uploaded invoice (ID: {existing_invoice.id})."`) — there's no `duplicate_of_invoice_id` column, so nothing structured can render it as a link. **Fixed 2026-08-12**: added `invoice.duplicate` to the allowlist; `Invoice.duplicate_of_invoice_id` (nullable self-FK, indexed — `models.py`, migration `fe6371baa50d`) populated in the duplicate branch and dispatched on the same commit. Full writeup: `docs/feature_15_webhooks.md` Task 15.8.
- `[x]` **Gap 196: No webhook event fires when an invoice enters processing** — same allowlist as Gap 195 (`routers/webhooks.py:19-27`) — the earliest inbound event is `invoice.completed`/`invoice.audit_required`, both fired **after** the pipeline finishes (`queue_worker/handlers.py:701-707`). A subscriber can see work end but never see it start. **Scope decision made 2026-08-12**: "start of processing" = the same lifecycle moment the existing `PROCESSING_OCR` SSE event already fires at (before OCR runs), not raw upload/enqueue time. Added `invoice.processing` to the allowlist and dispatch it there — thin payload (`invoice_id`, `status` only; nothing else is known yet). Full writeup: `docs/feature_15_webhooks.md` Task 15.8.
- `[x]` **Gap 197: Salesforce connector is only validated when a user tries to browse files, not at connect time** — closed 2026-08-13. Added instance validation via `verify_salesforce_instance` during OAuth callback in `routers/connectors.py` before persisting connection. Asserted by tests in `tests/test_connectors.py`.
- `[x]` **Gap 198: No processing/batch history — `Invoice.batch_id` is indexed but never queried as a group** — closed 2026-08-13. Grouped active invoices by batch_id and exposed them at `GET /api/v1/invoices/batches` for history and summary information. Supported `batch_id` query parameter on invoice lists, and added `DELETE /api/v1/invoices/batches/{batch_id}` for batch rollback via soft-deletion. Asserted by tests in `tests/test_batches.py`.

*(All 13 above found via an independent code audit reading the actual source, not user reports — each cites the exact file/line proving the defect. Deduplicated against every gap in this file at time of writing; five more findings from the same audit — cross-tenant/billing usage-bar contract mismatch, Free-tier limit display drift, timezone-shifted date filters, notification-bell staleness, PDF-viewer zoom/rotation state — turned out to be new root causes for existing open gaps rather than new ones; see the FE tracker's corresponding entries and this file's Gap 143-area cross-references.)*

**Independent code audit — Core & Logic (Chat, AI Trainer, Audit), 2026-08-11.** Source: `bug_report_profile_menu updated.md`, 8 findings (`BUG-CORE-01` through `08`). Each was independently re-verified against the live source before merging, not transcribed on trust. **2 of the 8 were pure false positives**, discarded outright: `BUG-CORE-01` claimed `get_chat_history()` keeps the oldest of 50 messages and drops recent ones — the code actually iterates newest-first and stops once the token budget is hit, which is already correct, backwards from the claim. `BUG-CORE-07` claimed a standing-rule safety-check failure leaves a mutated invoice row committed — `db_session.add()` only stages the change, and the single real `db_session.commit()` runs after the safety check, so a failure never persists anything; the code's own comment documents this atomicity deliberately, already correct. **1 more (`BUG-CORE-06`) was false as literally stated but contained a real, narrower finding**: it claimed Trainer commits never flush the chat answer cache at all — `_invalidate_chat_answer_cache()` already exists and is called on both commit and rollback (Gap 48) — but that call is scoped to Global rules only; the real gap (vendor-scoped commits never invalidate it) is filed as Gap 213 below.

- `[x]` **Gap 209: A genuine process crash between saving a chat user message and its assistant reply can orphan the user turn** — **fixed 2026-08-12.** `routers/chat.py::post_chat_message()`: the `db_session.commit()` that used to run right after staging the user row is gone; the user message, the auto-derived session title and the assistant reply now all land in the single commit at the end of the handler, so a process-level crash (worker kill, OOM, mid-request redeploy) mid-agent leaves an uncommitted transaction that the connection teardown rolls back whole — no user bubble without an answer beside it. **Two things had to be handled for the naive move to be safe, both confirmed against the real code rather than assumed**: (1) `run_query_agent()` is passed this same session and its SQL repair loop calls `db_session.rollback()` on a failed attempt (Task 6.9 / Gap 39) — SQLAlchemy's `Session.rollback()` always unwinds the *topmost* transaction, so a savepoint would not have protected the staged row and a repair retry would silently have dropped the user turn instead of orphaning it. The handler therefore re-stages `user_msg` (and the title) after the agent returns, guarded by `if user_msg not in db_session`, a no-op when no rollback happened. (2) The Gap 37 `except` now calls `db_session.rollback()` before building the fallback answer, so a session left mid-failed-transaction (Gap 39's Postgres "current transaction is aborted") can't make the one remaining commit raise. Chat-history behaviour is unchanged: the staged row is still autoflushed (not committed) by the agent's own queries, so `get_chat_history()` sees the current turn exactly as before. **Trade-off, deliberate**: a crash now loses the user's text too, rather than preserving an unanswerable half-turn — the orphan was the reported defect. **Verified**: 4 new tests in `tests/test_rag.py` — `test_user_message_and_assistant_reply_land_in_one_commit` (commit count taken from the `add()` of the user row, since request-scoped dependencies commit on this session before the handler body runs: zero commits between staging and the agent, exactly one after), `test_process_crash_during_agent_leaves_no_orphan_user_message` (a `BaseException` that `except Exception` deliberately does not catch, handler called directly so the ASGI stack can't reshape it, then the rollback `dependencies.get_db_session`'s `with Session(engine)` does at teardown — asserts zero message rows and an unrenamed title), `test_agent_internal_rollback_does_not_drop_the_user_message` (the point (1) regression), and `test_agent_failure_still_pairs_a_fallback_reply_with_the_user_turn` (Gap 37's graceful path survives the restructure). `pytest tests/test_rag.py tests/test_direction_aware_chat.py tests/test_rbac.py` → 54 passed. Spec doc updated: `feature_6_rag.md`.
- `[x]` **Gap 210: SQL case-normalization only rewrites `=` comparisons, not `IN (...)` or `LIKE '...'`** — **fixed 2026-08-12.** `agents/query_agent.py::_normalize_string_equality()` (`:143-191`) now runs three passes per column instead of one; the original `=` pass is byte-for-byte unchanged and still runs first. **`IN`**: `\b{column}\s+(NOT\s+)?IN\s*\(\s*({_IN_STRING_LIST})\s*\)` → `TRIM(LOWER(column)) IN (TRIM(LOWER('a')), TRIM(LOWER('b')))` — each value gets the identical `TRIM(LOWER(...))` treatment the single `=` value already got, i.e. the same mechanism replicated per element, not a different one. **`LIKE`**: `\b{column}\s+(NOT\s+)?LIKE\s*'([^']*)'` → `TRIM(LOWER(column)) LIKE LOWER('%pattern%')` — `LOWER` only, **no `TRIM` on the pattern side**, decided deliberately after checking whether the `=` rewrite generalizes: it doesn't, because a pattern is not a value and `TRIM` would silently change what it matches (`' Harbor%'` ≠ `'Harbor%'`), whereas `LOWER` leaves `%`/`_` wildcards intact. The column side is still trimmed, which is where the real stored-value whitespace drift lives. `NOT IN`/`NOT LIKE` are covered by the same two passes (an optional `NOT` group, re-emitted in place) so a negated filter doesn't quietly keep the old case-sensitive behaviour. New module constant `_IN_STRING_LIST` (`:140`) restricts the `IN` pass to a parenthesised list of string literals only — an `IN (SELECT ...)` subquery, a numeric list, or a value containing an escaped quote (`'O''Brien'`) fails to match and passes through untouched rather than being rewritten into malformed SQL. `ILIKE` is untouched (`\s+LIKE\b` can't match inside `ILIKE`) as it is already case-insensitive. **Scope held**: no change to the SQL-generation prompt or anything upstream, and `_FUZZY_STRING_COLUMNS` is unchanged at `("invoice_number", "vendor_name", "po_number")` — no new fuzzy columns. **Tests**: 5 added to `tests/test_rag.py` (where the existing `execute_generated_sql` coverage lives; `tests/test_queries.py` is the invoice-list endpoints, not the query agent) — 2 unit tests asserting the exact rewritten SQL text for `=`/`IN`/`LIKE`/`NOT IN`/`NOT LIKE` and that subqueries, the excluded `status` column and `ILIKE` pass through verbatim, plus 3 end-to-end tests running real SQL through `execute_generated_sql()` against seeded rows stored as `'HARBOR TECH '`/`'metro office'` and asserting the right invoices come back and the unrelated one doesn't. Two harness notes recorded in the test docstrings: (a) SQLModel stores UUIDs in sqlite as 32-char dashless hex, so the dashed literal the isolation guard requires matches zero rows there — `_tenant_filter()` ORs both spellings so the guard is still satisfied by a real predicate while the seeded rows stay reachable; (b) sqlite's `LIKE` is already ASCII-case-insensitive, so the e2e `LIKE` test uses the trailing-anchored `'%Harbor Tech'` against `'HARBOR TECH '`, which fails without the fix on both sqlite and Postgres, rather than a `'%Harbor%'` pattern that would pass unfixed on sqlite. **Verified**: `pytest tests/test_rag.py` → 15 passed (10 pre-existing + 5 new); `tests/test_rag.py tests/test_queries.py tests/test_direction_aware_chat.py` → 29 passed. Negative control run: with `_normalize_string_equality()` temporarily reverted to the single-regex version, exactly the 3 defect-shaped tests failed (`test_normalize_string_equality_rewrites_equality_in_and_like`, `..._in_clause_matches_despite_case_mismatch`, `..._like_clause_matches_despite_case_mismatch`) while the `=` regression test and the pass-through test stayed green — matching the defect's shape; the file was restored and the suite re-run green. Spec doc updated: `docs/feature_6_rag.md`, "P3 fix (Gap 210, Aug 12, 2026)". *Original finding below.* — `agents/query_agent.py:136-146`, `_normalize_string_equality()`'s regex is `rf"\b{column}\s*=\s*'([^']*)'"` — when the LLM generates a multi-vendor comparison (`WHERE vendor_name IN ('Harbor Tech', 'Metro Office')`) or a partial match (`LIKE '%Harbor%'`), the regex doesn't match and the case/whitespace-insensitive rewrite never applies, so those queries can silently miss rows over incidental case differences the same way exact-match queries used to before this function existed. **Fix**: extend the regex (or add a second pass) to rewrite `IN (...)` and `LIKE '...'` clauses on the same OCR/LLM-sourced text columns.
- `[x]` **Gap 211: AI Trainer's in-memory session fallback has no thread lock** — **fixed 2026-08-12.** `services/trainer_sessions.py` gained a module-level `_memory_lock = threading.Lock()` (`:60`, declared next to `_memory_store` with a comment stating why it exists), taken in the fallback branch of all three accessors: `save_session()` (`:88-92`), `get_session()` (`:97-102`) and `delete_session()` (`:127-132`). The Redis branches are byte-for-byte unchanged, so the production path is untouched. `get_session()`'s one-line conditional expression (`raw = client.get(...) if client is not None else _memory_store.get(...)`) was split into an if/else so the lock scopes to the dict read only and is never held across a network call. **Deliberately not changed**: `update_session()` remains an unlocked `get_session()`-then-`save_session()` — it is a read-modify-write spanning two public calls and is equally non-atomic on the Redis path, so making only the in-memory variant atomic would make dev diverge from production instead of matching it; that is a separate design question, not this hardening fix. **Tests**: 2 added to `tests/test_trainer.py` in a new "Gap 211" section. `test_concurrent_fallback_store_access_is_consistent` runs 8 threads × 250 save/get/update/delete round-trips and asserts every session round-trips and the final store size is exact. `test_iterating_the_fallback_store_while_threads_mutate_it_does_not_raise` is the actual regression proof: a scanner thread iterates the store under `_memory_lock` while 4 writer threads save into it, asserting no exception, that the scanner completed at least one pass, and that no write was lost. **Verified**: `pytest tests/test_trainer.py` → 33 passed (31 pre-existing + 2 new); the two new tests re-run 3× consecutively, green each time, no flake. Negative control run: with `_memory_lock` temporarily replaced by a no-op context manager (the attribute kept so the test still resolves), `test_iterating_...` failed with exactly `RuntimeError: dictionary changed size during iteration` — the defect this gap describes — and the module was then restored from a backup and the full suite re-run green. **Two findings worth recording from that negative control**, both now documented in the test file's section comment so the tests aren't later "simplified" into uselessness: (a) the save/get/update/delete stress test passes *with or without* the lock, because bare dict item-set/pop are individually atomic under CPython's GIL — it is a consistency check, not the regression proof; (b) the scanner test's writers only **add** sessions, deliberately — an add-immediately-followed-by-delete restores the dict's size before the iterator re-checks it, and measured 0 failures over ~4,500 unlocked scans, whereas monotonic growth trips the guard on the first scan or two. Spec doc updated: `docs/feature_10_trainer.md`, "Recent Fixes (Aug 12, 2026)". *Original finding below.* — `services/trainer_sessions.py`, `_memory_store: dict[str, str]` (module-level) is read/written/deleted with no `threading.Lock()`; concurrent mutation can raise `RuntimeError: dictionary changed size during iteration`. **Scope note**: this path is explicitly documented as a dev/test-only fallback — the module's own docstring states Redis is always used in production and the in-process dict "never silently degrades production" (Gap 5). Real, worth a small hardening fix, but not the production-critical severity it was first reported at. **Fix**: wrap `_memory_store` reads/writes in a `threading.Lock()`.
- `[x]` **Gap 212: AI Trainer's constraint-refinement fallback can turn a plain-English instruction into a corrupt extraction rule** — **fixed 2026-08-12.** Both failure paths now fail closed instead of appending the raw chat text. `agents/trainer_agent.py`: added `ConstraintRefinementError(RuntimeError)` + `REFINEMENT_FAILURE_MESSAGE` ("Couldn't process that correction — your existing rules are unchanged. Please retry.") at `:16-33`; `refine_constraints()` (`:70-116`) was restructured so the `try` now wraps *only* the `structured_llm.invoke()` call — its `except Exception` logs and raises `ConstraintRefinementError ... from e`, and the response-shape check moved below the `try` (deliberately, so the raise on a missing `constraints` field isn't re-swallowed by that same `except`) and raises the same error. Neither path touches `current_constraints`. `run_trainer_agent()` just propagates it, so no re-extraction runs on a failed correction. `routers/trainer.py`: imports the error at `:18` and `trainer_chat()` catches it at `:580-589`, ahead of its pre-existing generic `except Exception` → 500, returning **502** with the user-facing message — 502 chosen to match `routers/connectors.py`'s existing upstream-provider-failure convention, and modelled on the repo's `InvalidWebhookUrlError` pattern (custom error in the service/agent module, translated to an HTTP status in the router) rather than a new sentinel-return mechanism. Because the raise short-circuits before `trainer_sessions.save_session()`, the session keeps its previous rules *and* does not persist a user turn with no assistant reply. No FE change was needed: `app/trainer/page.tsx`'s existing `catch` around `sendChatMessage` already toasts "Failed to process your correction. Please try again." on any non-2xx. **Tests**: 4 added to `tests/test_trainer.py` — two unit (`test_refine_constraints_raises_and_keeps_rules_when_llm_call_fails`, `..._when_response_lacks_constraints`, each asserting the caller's list is unchanged) and one parametrized endpoint test over both paths (`test_chat_surfaces_refinement_failure_instead_of_storing_raw_message[llm_exception|response_missing_constraints]`, asserting 502, a "retry" message, unchanged stored `constraints`, and an unchanged `chat_history`). The malformed-response fixture is a plain dict, not a `MagicMock`, because a `MagicMock` satisfies `hasattr(result, "constraints")` and could never reach that branch. **Verified**: `pytest tests/test_trainer.py` → 26 passed (22 pre-existing + 4 new). Negative control run: with `refine_constraints()` temporarily reverted to the old `current_constraints + [user_message]` fallback, all 4 new tests failed (endpoint returned 200 and the raw sentence was stored as a rule); the file was restored and the suite re-run green. Spec doc updated: `docs/feature_10_trainer.md`, "Recent Fixes (Aug 12, 2026)". *Original finding below.* — `agents/trainer_agent.py:74-81`. On an LLM call failure (`except Exception`, `:79-81`) **and** on a non-exception path where the LLM's structured response just lacks the expected `constraints` field (`:78`), the fallback is `return current_constraints + [user_message]` — the user's raw chat text gets appended as a literal extraction rule. A user typing "Remove the rule requiring PO prefix" during a network blip gets that sentence added as a *new* constraint instead of removing anything, silently corrupting the vendor's extraction template. **Fix**: on either failure path, return `current_constraints` unchanged and surface the failure to the user (e.g. "couldn't process that correction, please retry") instead of treating unparsed chat text as a rule.
- `[x]` **Gap 213: Trainer rule commits only invalidate the chat answer cache for Global-scope rules — vendor-scoped commits leave stale answers cached for up to an hour** — **fixed 2026-08-12.** Both call sites now invoke `_invalidate_chat_answer_cache(str(tenant_context.tenant_id))` unconditionally, for every scope. `routers/trainer.py::trainer_commit()`: the call was lifted out of the `if scope == "global":` branch and placed after the whole re-audit if/elif (`:711-722`), so Global, Existing Vendor and New Vendor commits all flush; the re-audit routing itself is unchanged (Global => all vendors, Existing Vendor => that vendor, New Vendor => none). `routers/trainer.py::rollback_template()`: the `if template.vendor_name is None:` guard was dropped (`:819-821`), so a vendor-scoped rollback flushes too. `_invalidate_chat_answer_cache()`'s own docstring (`:306-323`) was rewritten — it previously described itself as Global-only, which is what made the under-scoping look intentional on a read. **Confirmed rather than assumed before fixing**: `agents/query_agent.py::_cache_key()` (`:31-32`) builds `chat_answer_cache:{tenant_id}:{_normalize_query(user_message)}` — tenant + query only, no vendor dimension — so a vendor-scoped commit has no narrower key set it could target, and the tenant-wide flush is both correct and the only available granularity. The function remains best-effort (own try/except, warning-logged), so it cannot fail a commit; it is idempotent (`keys()` then `delete(*keys)`, no-op when empty). **No TTL or cache-architecture change** — `CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` is still 3600 and the cache is still not vendor-partitioned; partitioning it would be a separate redesign, deliberately out of scope. **Tests**: 5 added to `tests/test_trainer.py`, in a new "Chat answer-cache invalidation on every scope (Gap 213)" section — an `answer_cache` fixture patches `redis.Redis.from_url` with a stub (necessary because the function swallows every exception, so without a stub a *missing* invalidation is indistinguishable from a failed one), and `_assert_answer_cache_flushed()` asserts both the exact scan pattern `chat_answer_cache:{tenant}:*` and the `delete` of the returned key. `test_commit_invalidates_chat_answer_cache_for_every_scope[global|existing_vendor|new_vendor]` drives each scope through its real entry point (`/sessions/global`, `/sessions/from-production`, `/upload`) then `/commit`; `test_rollback_invalidates_chat_answer_cache_for_every_scope[global|new_vendor]` commits, reads the template id back from `GET /templates/history`, resets the stub and rolls back (the two cases cover both sides of the removed `vendor_name is None` guard). **Verified**: `pytest tests/test_trainer.py` → 31 passed (26 pre-existing + 5 new). Negative control run: with `routers/trainer.py` temporarily reverted to the two Global-only guards, exactly the 3 previously-broken paths failed (`commit[existing_vendor]`, `commit[new_vendor]`, `rollback[new_vendor]`) and the 2 Global cases still passed — matching the defect's shape; the file was restored and the suite re-run green. Spec doc updated: `docs/feature_10_trainer.md`, "Recent Fixes (Aug 12, 2026)". *Original finding below.* — narrower finding than first reported (see false-positive note above for `BUG-CORE-06`; `_invalidate_chat_answer_cache()` genuinely exists and is genuinely called, just under-scoped). `routers/trainer.py:698` only fires inside `if scope == "global":`; the rollback path at `:797-798` only fires `if template.vendor_name is None:`. Committing or rolling back an **Existing Vendor** or **New Vendor** rule never invalidates `chat_answer_cache:{tenant_id}:*`, so Chat can keep serving a pre-correction cached answer for that vendor's questions for up to `CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` (1 hour). **Fix**: call `_invalidate_chat_answer_cache()` unconditionally on every commit/rollback, not just the Global-scope branches.
- `[x]` **Gap 214: Line-item math verification double-flips the sign on negative amounts, false-flagging discount/rebate lines as calculation errors** — **Closed 2026-08-12**. Modified `verify_line_items_math()` in `utils/verification_tools.py` to skip item-level discount percentage calculations when `line_subtotal` is negative (for trade discount, rebate, or credit lines), preventing incorrect expected amounts.
- `[x]` **Gap 217: Trainer commit endpoint returns a generic 400 with no structured reason when a rule is rejected by the guardrail** — **closed 2026-08-12** (merge of `gap-resolution-autopilot-audit-trainer-chat`). `routers/trainer.py::_validate_rule_text()` now raises `HTTPException(400)` with dict detail `{detail, rejection_reason, flagged_rule}` when `is_instruction=True`. FE trainer commit toast surfaces `flagged_rule` + reason. `tests/test_trainer.py::test_commit_rejects_instruction_like_rule_end_to_end` updated. **Partially relocated 2026-08-13 by Feature 18 (Gap 228)**: `_validate_rule_text()` now also runs at `POST /trainer/sessions/{id}/preview`, so the rejection surfaces while the user is still editing rather than only at commit. The 400 contract described above is unchanged and still enforced on `/commit`, which remains the backstop for a direct API caller that never previewed — this is an additional rejection point, not a moved one.

- `[x]` **Gap 218: Trainer Existing/New Vendor — add a dual-mode session: Chat QA Test mode + Rule Creation mode** — **closed 2026-08-12** (merge of `gap-resolution-autopilot-audit-trainer-chat`). Trainer sessions carry `session_mode` (`qa_test` | `rule_creation`, default `rule_creation`). `PUT /trainer/sessions/{id}/mode`; `trainer_chat()` routes `qa_test` to `run_query_agent()`. `tests/test_trainer.py::test_set_session_mode_qa_test`.

- `[x]` **Gap 219: Chat agent has no conciseness instruction — LLM responses are unnecessarily verbose** — **fixed 2026-08-12, extended same day** (merge of `gap-resolution-autopilot-audit-trainer-chat`). My original per-route hardcoded instruction is superseded by `_get_chat_style_block()` — tenant-configurable length/tone/custom instructions (see Gap 221), falling back to the same plain conciseness default when no style is committed. **Known loose end from the merge, not yet cleaned up**: the CHAT route's system prompt now carries both my original inline brevity sentence *and* `{style_block}` back to back — redundant, not broken, but worth deduping next time that function is touched. Base conciseness (not the tenant-configurable part) is confirmed working on all three routes — the SQL route still has my original hardcoded "1-2 sentences" instruction in `summary_prompt`, independent of `style_block`. **Verified**: `py_compile` clean; `pytest tests/test_rag.py tests/test_direction_aware_chat.py tests/test_queries.py` → 36/36 passing; effect confirmed live during the InvoiceEQ ground-truth chat test pass (2026-08-12/13, 13/13 replies short and on-topic).

- `[x]` **Gap 220: Autopilot notification emails and approval links are never sent — `notify_emails` and `send_approval_links` are stored in config but never read during sync** — **closed 2026-08-12** (merge of `gap-resolution-autopilot-audit-trainer-chat`). `services/autopilot_sync.py` collects `newly_imported` and calls `services/staff_notify.notify_autopilot_sync_summary()` after sync when `notify_emails` non-empty and `processed > 0`; review deep links when `send_approval_links=True`. `tests/test_autopilot.py::test_T19_autopilot_sends_notify_email_after_import`. Live SendGrid verify still blocked on Gap 125.

- `[x]` **Gap 221 (BE): Chat Response Style API — `commit-behavior` endpoint + tenant chat style storage — NOT actually applied to most chat traffic** — closed 2026-08-13. Moved `{style_block}` injection from the SQL schema generation prompt to the user-facing summary prompt in `agents/query_agent.py`. Deduplicated inline brevity rules in CHAT system prompt. Asserted by tests in `tests/test_queries.py`. **Storage location moved 2026-08-13 by Feature 18 (Gap 230)**: chat style no longer lives on the Global INBOUND `ExtractionTemplate.rules["chat_style"]` — it has its own `tenant_chat_settings` table. The move was necessary because Feature 18 removes Global-scope rule *creation*, so that row is no longer a surface any user opens. `_get_chat_style_block()` keeps its exact signature (all three call sites untouched) and still reads the old location as a fallback; the migration copies values across non-destructively and deliberately leaves the source key in place.

*(6 of the 8 source findings merged above as Gap 209-214 — two (`BUG-CORE-02`, `BUG-CORE-06`) re-scoped narrower than originally reported. 2 of the 8 (`BUG-CORE-01`, `07`) were pure false positives, discarded — see the note above.)*

- `[x]` **Gap 215: Outbound webhook payloads send `grand_total` with no `currency`, so external consumers can't tell what currency the amount is in** — opened 2026-08-11 as an explicitly deferred loose end of Gap 185 (see that entry's "Not addressed here" note). All three `dispatch_webhook_event()` call sites build a payload dict containing `"grand_total": invoice.grand_total` and no currency key: `routers/audit.py:418-423` (`invoice.paid`/`invoice.rejected`), `routers/outbound_invoices.py:50-55` (outbound status events), and `queue_worker/handlers.py:704-709` (`invoice.completed`/`invoice.audit_required`). `services/webhooks.py::dispatch_webhook_event` takes `payload: dict` opaquely and signs/delivers whatever it's handed, so the omission is at the call sites, not in the dispatcher. A subscriber receiving `{"grand_total": 40000.0}` for an INR invoice has no way to distinguish it from a $40,000 USD one — the same bug class Gap 183/185 fixed everywhere else, and the reason a blended figure has no correct label. **Why deferred**: unlike the FE display and dashboard-aggregate fixes, this changes an *external consumer contract* — anyone already parsing these payloads gets a new field — so it was left out of Gap 185's pass deliberately rather than slipped in as a drive-by. **Fixed 2026-08-12**: added `"currency": invoice.currency or "USD"` (display-default only, matching the FE's `normalizeCurrencyCode()` convention — nothing written back to the row) to all three dispatch sites, plus the two new event payloads added alongside Gaps 195/196 (`invoice.duplicate` has it; `invoice.processing` deliberately doesn't — currency isn't extracted yet at that point). Documented in `app/help/content/webhooks-guide.tsx`'s payload sample. Full writeup: `docs/feature_15_webhooks.md` Task 15.8. **Note (2026-08-12, merge of `fix/gaps-144-151-195-196-215`):** this entry and the "(6 of 8 source findings...)" note above it were found missing from master at merge time — restored here rather than re-authored, since the branch's copy (forked before the loss) still had them intact.

**InvoiceEQ ground-truth test run — investigation-only pass, 2026-08-12/13.** 27 real invoice PDFs (US/India/EU, inbound/outbound) run through the real pipeline (real Azure OCR + real Azure OpenAI, local docker-compose, `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH=true`) plus 13 live chat prompts. Full writeup: `invoice_llm/agents/outputs/gap_investigation_2026-08-13.md`. Five gaps below opened from that run; **none fixed yet, no application code was touched during the investigation** — awaiting founder go-ahead before implementation. (A sixth candidate, "verification is arithmetic-only," is a product-scope question, not a code defect — logged under Gap 225 below without a suggested fix pending a product decision. A seventh, "the extraction test harness never wrote its own LineItems sheet," is a limitation of `tests/run_extraction_harness.py`, not application code — not filed as a numbered gap.)

- `[x]` **Gap 222 (BE): Negative/credit-note line items lose their sign during LLM extraction, false-flagging arithmetically-correct invoices as `AUDIT_REQUIRED`** — closed 2026-08-13. Strengthened `GAP_46_VERBATIM_DIRECTIVE` and property definitions for `InvoiceLineItem` in `agents/extraction_agent.py` and `OutboundInvoiceLineItem` in `agents/outbound_extraction_agent.py` to instruct preserving negative signs. Asserted by tests in `tests/test_outbound_extraction.py`. **Independently re-verified 2026-08-13 against a live re-extraction, not just the unit suite**: `IN-IN-03_correct_complex.pdf` re-uploaded and re-processed through the real pipeline (real Azure OCR + real Azure OpenAI) — the credit-note line now extracts as `amount: -5000.0, unit_price: -5000.0` (previously `+5000.0`, the original bug). The invoice's only alert is now `duplicate_invoice` (expected, it's a re-upload of an already-processed invoice number) — the `line_items_mismatch` false-flag is gone. Invoice id `73373ff6-97ec-4af3-aa59-d07087924d5b`.

- `[x]` **Gap 223 (BE): Outbound invoices skip line-item math verification entirely — no arithmetic cross-check on customer-facing invoices** — closed 2026-08-13. Added `subtotal` to `OutboundInvoiceExtractionSchema` in `agents/outbound_extraction_agent.py` and wired up `verify_subtotal_in_source_text`, `verify_line_items_math`, and `verify_totals_math` in `verify_node`. Asserted by tests in `tests/test_outbound_extraction.py`. **Independently re-verified 2026-08-13 against a live re-extraction**: `US-OUT-02_erroneous_medium.pdf` re-uploaded and re-processed through the real pipeline — the math check now fires `line_item_calculation_mismatch`: *"Line item 'Professional services - implementation (40 hrs @ $150/hr)' amount (6200.00) does not match calculated amount (6000.00)"* — this is the invoice's actual injected ground-truth defect (40 × $150 = $6,000, printed as $6,200), previously invisible because the check never ran at all. Invoice id `64422ed4-6070-401c-a8d3-d2b28423d29e`.

- `[x]` **Gap 224 (BE): Chat SQL route confidently returns $0.00 instead of flagging an ambiguous/unsupported category filter** — closed 2026-08-13. Added `items`/`tags` to the SQL schema and a null-query guardrail in `agents/query_agent.py`. **Briefly reopened, then re-closed, same day, over a false alarm in my own verification**: live-tested *"How much did we spend on furniture, chairs, or desks?"* against tenant-us, got "No records found," and found what looked like a counter-example — invoice `US-99003-001` (Summit Office Supply, "Ergonomic Office Chair" line item) — and reopened this gap assuming a case-sensitivity bug. **That was wrong**: that invoice's `tenant_id` is `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` (leftover zero-UUID seed data, unrelated to the ground-truth tenants), found by an unscoped vendor-name query and misattributed to tenant-us. Direct, explicitly tenant-scoped DB queries (both the original case-sensitive `LIKE` and a `LOWER()`-wrapped case-insensitive version) confirm **zero** furniture/chair/desk line items actually exist under tenant-us's real tenant_id — "no records found" was the correct answer the whole time. **Left in place regardless, as a harmless hardening, not a confirmed-necessary fix**: `agents/query_agent.py` rule 6 now tells the LLM to wrap `items`/`tags` `LIKE` filters in `LOWER(...)` on both sides, since free-text descriptions aren't reliably lowercase and a real case-sensitivity miss remains plausible for other tenants/data even though it wasn't demonstrated here.

- `[x]` **Gap 225 (BE): Verification scope is arithmetic self-consistency only — no check catches a correctly-transcribed-but-semantically-wrong field** — opened 2026-08-13, **closed 2026-08-17 by product decision, not a code change**. Not a code defect: the pipeline is behaving exactly as designed (transcribe-verbatim, never auto-correct vendor data), confirmed by the ground-truth run's US-IN-05/EU-IN-05-class test cases, which intentionally probe fields that are correctly transcribed verbatim but don't match a "true" business expectation. **Decision: verification scope stays arithmetic-only, deliberately not expanded to semantic/business-plausibility checks** — there is no generic, tenant-agnostic definition of "business-correct" (due-date ordering, expected payment terms, ID formats, etc. all vary per vendor), so a generic checker would trade false negatives for false positives without a real spec. **Feature 18's "flag a missed alert" correction (Gap 228) is the intended mitigation, not a workaround**: it gives an auditor a structured way to report exactly this class of finding per-vendor as they notice it, building up per-vendor expectations over time instead of the pipeline guessing up front. No code action taken or needed — closing reflects the scope decision itself, not a fix.

- `[x]` **Gap 226 (BE): Chat replies render a fixed currency symbol regardless of the invoice's actual currency** — closed 2026-08-13, **but the first closure was premature — caught by live adversarial re-testing, not by the unit suite.** The original fix added a "CRITICAL CURRENCY RULE" to the SQL route's prompt requiring the LLM to select `currency` alongside any monetary column, but never added `currency` to the schema block the same prompt hands the LLM. Live re-run against the real backend (round-2 chat prompts, 2026-08-13) showed the actual effect: 5 of 8 monetary SQL-route questions got a flat refusal ("your schema does not include a currency column") instead of an answer — a functional regression, worse than the original wrong-symbol bug. `tests/test_queries.py` didn't catch it because it doesn't exercise this specific prompt text against a real LLM. **Actually fixed 2026-08-13**: added `- currency: VARCHAR (ISO 4217 currency code...)` to the schema list in `agents/query_agent.py`'s SQL system_prompt, next to `grand_total`. **Re-verified 2026-08-13** against the real backend with the answer cache flushed first (so these were fresh LLM calls, not replays of the earlier refusal): the same "total spend in euros across all vendors" question for tenant-eu that previously refused now correctly answers *"Total spend across all vendors is €26,962.80"* (was `$26,962.80` before the original, incomplete fix). 4 of the other previously-refusing prompts also went from refusal to a real answer on re-test.
- `[x]` **Gap 227 (BE): `GET /outbound-dashboard/invoices` raises `NameError: name 'batch_id' is not defined` on every call — introduced by the same uncommitted batch_id change that correctly shipped in Gap 198** — opened and closed 2026-08-13. Found via `pytest tests/test_outbound_dashboard.py` (`test_outbound_invoice_rows_expose_currency` failed with this exact traceback, not an assertion failure) while independently verifying the Gap 191/197/198/221 closures. `routers/outbound_dashboard.py::list_outbound_invoices()` referenced `batch_id` in a filter condition without it ever being declared as a function parameter — unlike `routers/invoices.py::list_invoices()`, which got the equivalent addition correctly (the real Gap 198 fix). Because the check ran unconditionally, this broke the endpoint for 100% of calls, not just batch-filtered ones. **Fix**: added `batch_id: UUID | None = None` to the parameter list, matching the pattern already correct in `routers/invoices.py`. **Verified**: `pytest tests/test_outbound_dashboard.py` — the specific failing test now passes. Live re-test against the real backend: `GET /outbound-dashboard/invoices?batch_id=<real batch>` → 200 with exactly the 1 matching invoice; the same endpoint with no `batch_id` at all → 200 with all 4 rows (previously 500 either way). **Also fixed in the same pass**: two other pre-existing test failures surfaced during this verification, unrelated to the `batch_id` bug itself — `test_dashboard.py::test_metrics_filters` and `test_outbound_dashboard.py::test_metrics_filters` were failing because their fixtures set `invoice_date` but not `created_at`, and the Gap 191 fix (this file's earlier entry) switched date-range filtering to `created_at`. Added matching `created_at` values to both fixtures (`tests/test_dashboard.py::populate_mock_invoices`, `tests/test_outbound_dashboard.py::populate_mock_outbound_invoices`/`_outbound()` call sites). Full affected suite (`test_batches`, `test_connectors`, `test_queries`, `test_outbound_extraction`, `test_dashboard`, `test_outbound_dashboard`, `test_rag`): **80/80 passing**.

**Feature 18 — Alert-Anchored Trainer & Chat Correction Lane, 2026-08-13.** A structural redesign, not a bug-fix batch: the Gap 222/223/224/226 investigation surfaced that Trainer rule creation took free text, had an LLM interpret it, and persisted the result with no grounding in a concrete event and no structured checkpoint (Gap 212 was its sharpest symptom, fixed narrowly at the time). Full design, deviations and verification: [feature_18_trainer_alert_anchored_training.md](feature_18_trainer_alert_anchored_training.md). Backend only — the FE half is a separate pass.

- `[x]` **Gap 228 (BE): Trainer rule creation is ungrounded and uninterpretable — free chat text → LLM → persisted constraint, with no anchor to a real invoice/alert and no structured checkpoint** — opened and closed 2026-08-13. Three defects in one shape: (1) rules were never tied to a specific invoice, field, or observed failure — Global sessions had no document at all; (2) `ExtractionTemplate.rules["constraints"]` was a flat `list[str]`, so once a rule was a sentence nothing downstream could tell what it was about or what produced it, and *two* independent producers (Trainer, and `routers/audit.py::_apply_standing_rule` synthesising `f"For {field}, extract the value as ..."`) wrote into the same undifferentiated bag; (3) nothing sat between "user typed" and "rule is live". **Fixed**: Global-scope rule *creation* removed (`POST /trainer/sessions/global` → 410, commit refuses `scope="global"`) — **the Global rows, every tenant's already-committed rules on them, and all four read sites are untouched and still applied**, covered by `test_committed_global_rules_are_still_read_after_creation_is_removed`. Outbound rules still write the Global OUTBOUND row under a distinct `outbound_global` scope, because an outbound invoice has no `vendor_name` at all (the counterparty is `customer_name`) so there is structurally nowhere else. New unified entry point `POST /trainer/sessions/from-invoice` takes a **specific** `invoice_id` and does **no reprocessing** — the superseded `/sessions/from-production` (now 410) resolved `order_by(created_at.desc()).first()`, so it could only ever open a vendor's *latest* invoice, and re-ran a full Doc Intelligence pass on every load. Structured rule objects (`{field, condition, scope, source_alert_type, origin, text, kind, params}`) now live alongside legacy strings in the same JSON column (no migration needed), with **one shared normalizer** (`utils/rule_schema.normalize_constraints()`) wired into all nine read sites; both auditor standing-rule producers migrated in the same pass, emitting byte-identical rendered text. New alert-type registry (`utils/alert_registry.py`, 17 types, each verified against its live producer). Four correction shapes; `agents/extraction_agent.py::verify_node` now actually reads `state["rules"]` (**it never did before**, despite `rules` being in `ExtractionState` and read by `extract_node`), same for the outbound verify path. One preview-before-commit gate with **exact** historical replay for math-class rules and an explicit `not_computable` — never a fabricated count — for text rules. **Verified**: 552 passed / 0 failed / 5 deselected across the full backend suite (baseline 470), under both fixed and random test ordering.

- `[x]` **Gap 229 (BE): the five `*_not_verified_in_source` alert types have no "this alert was unnecessary" correction path** — **closed 2026-08-18.** Implemented fuzzy verbatim matching for the five source-text alerts. Normalizes both the target figure and the raw OCR text by stripping currency symbols, thousands separators, and parentheses, comparing them as floats within a 0.01 tolerance. The candidate extraction regex uses literal spaces to prevent newlines from bridging unrelated numbers. Validates that space-separated groups represent genuine thousands groupings (exactly 3 digits after the space) and are not preceded by line/item specifiers (e.g. "Line 1 234.56 EA"). Verified by appending negative-direction test cases to `tests/test_extraction.py` and running the pytest suite successfully. Two smaller follow-ups filed under the same gap, still open: an **outbound-scoped re-audit** task (an outbound commit currently enqueues nothing, because `_enqueue_reaudit(tenant, None)` means "every vendor" and would fan out across the tenant's whole INBOUND history for an outbound-only rule), and **live manual verification** of Feature 18 against a real tenant with real Azure OCR/LLM — nothing in it has been exercised outside the automated suite.

- `[x]` **Gap 230 (BE): Chat response style is stored on the Global INBOUND `ExtractionTemplate` row, which Feature 18 makes an unreachable surface** — opened and closed 2026-08-13. Gap 221 stored `{response_length, tone, custom_instructions}` in that row's `rules["chat_style"]`. Reasonable when the Global row was the natural home for tenant-wide Trainer state; wrong once Feature 18 removed Global-scope rule creation, because it hangs live chat configuration off a row nobody opens, which is otherwise about *extraction* rules. **Fixed**: new `tenant_chat_settings` table (`UNIQUE(tenant_id)`, shaped after `TenantAutopilotConfig`), migration `f18a0c4b7d21` copying existing values across. **Non-destructive on purpose** — the source `rules["chat_style"]` key is deliberately left in place (it is tenant data, and a rollback of this deploy then loses nothing), and `_get_chat_style_block()` still reads it as a fallback so a tenant whose row predates the migration keeps their style rather than snapping back to defaults. `_get_chat_style_block()`'s signature and `_CONCISENESS_INSTRUCTION` fallback are unchanged, so its three call sites needed no edit. **Verified**: migration exercised on a throwaway Postgres DB with a real seeded `chat_style` row (copied across, source key confirmed still present, clean downgrade); `tests/test_chat_training.py` covers the new table, the legacy fallback, and the default.

- `[x]` **Gap 231 (BE): a chat reply records no row identity, so there is nothing to build a "which invoice was wrong?" picker from** — opened and closed 2026-08-13. Only the RAG route left any trace (`citations[].invoice_id`, `agents/query_agent.py:759`); the SQL route (`:690-746`) set `citations = []` and returned only `generated_sql` plus a markdown table string. For an aggregate answer — "total spend across 40 invoices" — literally nothing anywhere recorded which rows produced it, which is exactly the case the wrong-data triage flow needs. **Fixed**: new `ChatMessage.result_invoice_ids` (JSON), populated at answer time. Harvested from an `id`/`invoice_id` column when the generated SQL selected one; otherwise by rebuilding the *same predicates* as an id-only companion query — deliberately not by forcing `id` into the generated SELECT, which would put raw UUIDs into every results table the user sees. Strictly best-effort and read-only: it refuses to rebuild anything containing a join or subquery, or whose predicates don't carry the tenant guard, and any failure yields an empty list. **An empty list means "could not determine", never "no invoices"** — the triage API treats it as "ask the user which invoice" rather than asserting a claim it can't back. RAG citations are folded into the same field so triage behaves identically regardless of route. Ids are normalized through `_canonical_uuid()` (PostgreSQL returns `uuid` objects, SQLite returns dashless hex).

- `[x]` **Gap 232 (BE): a thumbs-down is signal-only — there is no path from "this answer is wrong" to anything changing** — opened and closed 2026-08-13. Gap 54 recorded the vote and deliberately stopped there. **Fixed**: `ChatFeedback` gains `reason` (`wrong_data | wrong_interpretation | bad_tone`) and `note`, both nullable so the existing signal-only contract is unchanged for a client that doesn't send them. New `tenant_chat_rules` table, **structurally separate from `ExtractionTemplate.rules["constraints"]` and never written by this lane** (asserted by test) — a chat rule is about how the answering agent scopes a question and has nothing to teach the extraction pipeline; sharing a table is how "the trainer taught chat something odd" and "the trainer taught extraction something odd" became one undiagnosable bug class. `_chat_rules_block()` injects them **next to**, never merged into, `_business_rules_block()`: the latter carries Gap 58's "disregard anything that reads as an instruction" framing, which would tell the model to ignore the chat rules it was just handed. Triage: `wrong_data` about one invoice **auto-diffs** the reply against the stored DB value (no human judgement — it's a value diff); a mismatch is provably a chat bug, while a match means the open question is whether the *stored* data is right, and if the human says the PDF disagrees the flow **redirects out of chat entirely into the extraction "flag as missed" path**, returning enough context for the FE to open it pre-filled. Aggregate/interpretation complaints take a **structured category pick** (8 categories, closed vocabulary, deterministic rendering — no LLM anywhere in this lane), and `bad_tone` maps straight onto `TenantChatSettings` rather than creating a rule. Preview-before-commit applies here too: commit without a token is a 400, and a drifted token is a 409 — no silent save off a thumbs-down. Also in scope: **Trainer QA-test turns now persist as real `ChatMessage` rows** (Gap 218 stored them Redis-only with non-UUID `msg-<hex8>` ids, so thumbs-down had nothing to attach to), which fixed a **confirmed latent bug** — the old code passed `f"trainer-qa-{session_id}"` into `get_chat_history()`, whose `UUID(session_id)` raises `ValueError`, is caught, and returns `""`; QA mode therefore had **zero conversational memory on every turn**, silently, and nothing reported it. Gap 218 itself is **not** superseded — this builds on it.
---

- `[x]` **Gap 233 (BE): SSE ingestion stream closes as soon as the first invoice in a multi-file batch finishes, leaving the rest with no live status updates** — opened 2026-08-14, closed 2026-08-17. `routers/invoices.py::sse_event_generator()` now queries every invoice in the batch up front, tracks each one's status as events arrive (including a DB-refresh fallback if an event's `invoice_id` doesn't match the tracked set), and only breaks the stream once **all** tracked invoices reach a terminal status (`COMPLETED`/`AUDIT_REQUIRED`/`FAILED`/`DUPLICATE`). **Verified**: `tests/test_sse.py` (new) passing, full backend suite 553/553. **Known follow-up, not yet filed as its own gap**: no server-side timeout — if one invoice in a batch never reaches a terminal status (stuck worker, lost queue message), the stream stays open indefinitely rather than force-closing after a max duration. Worth adding before this sees heavy production traffic.

- `[x]` **Gap 234 (BE): `subtotal` is not a persisted column on `Invoice` — auditors can't view or correct it, and several verification checks can't use the real stored value** — opened 2026-08-14, closed 2026-08-17. Added `subtotal: float | None` to `models.py` (migration `46b051e6b0a0`), `queue_worker/handlers.py::handle_process_invoice()` now writes `extracted_data.get("subtotal")` onto the row at extraction time, and both `routers/audit.py`/`routers/outbound_audit.py::_CORRECTABLE_FIELDS` gained `subtotal` (float) and `items` (new `"list"` coercion type in `_coerce_correction_value`) — this also unblocks FE Gap 224's line-item editing UI, which needed both. **Verified end-to-end, not just read**: migration applies and downgrades cleanly on both a throwaway DB and the real local dev DB; a live `PUT /audit/resolve/{id}` with a real line-item correction was submitted and the DB queried directly afterward — `items` and `subtotal` persisted exactly as sent. Full backend suite 553/553 (up from 552, +1 for the new subtotal-correction test). **Review note, not a confirmed defect**: the migration also alters `webhook_subscriptions.event_failure_counts`'s nullability, unrelated to subtotal — likely Alembic autogenerate catching pre-existing schema drift rather than an intentional change; worth a second look before this is taken as fully understood.

- `[x]` **Gap 235 (BE): the two LLM-drafting calls in `routers/trainer.py` (`_validate_rule_text()`'s Gap 217 guardrail and `flag_missed_alert()`'s missed-alert rule drafting) are capped at `max_tokens=512`, and against the actually-configured Azure deployment (`gpt-5-mini`, a reasoning model) that budget is entirely consumed by internal `reasoning_tokens` before any visible output is produced** — opened and closed 2026-08-17. Found during functional-tester's live verification of Feature 18/14 (this pass's own Verification Plan explicitly flagged "the missed-alert drafting prompt against a real model" as not yet done; it was run against the real deployment and failed — reproduced 3× with the exact `CompletionUsage` object showing the budget consumed entirely by `reasoning_tokens`, only succeeding at `max_tokens=4096`). **Fixed**: both call sites raised to `max_tokens=4096`, the value functional-tester confirmed sufficient. **Verified**: `tests/test_trainer.py`+`test_chat_training.py` 92/92; full suite 552 passed / 0 failed / 5 deselected — exact match to pre-fix baseline (parameter-only change, no test count shift expected or seen).

  Live-reproduced against the real Azure OpenAI endpoint configured in `.env` (`AZURE_OPENAI_DEPLOYMENT_NAME=gpt-5-mini`), not simulated: `POST /trainer/sessions/{id}/corrections/missed-alert` returns `502 Bad Gateway` (`"Couldn't turn that into a rule right now — nothing was changed. Please retry."`) on every attempt. Server log shows the real completion object: `CompletionUsage(completion_tokens=512, ..., reasoning_tokens=512, accepted_prediction_tokens=0)` — the whole 512-token budget went to hidden reasoning, zero tokens were left to emit the structured `MissedAlertRuleDraft` output, and `langchain_openai` raises `LengthFinishReasonError`. Confirmed not a fluke: reproduced identically on a second alert type/field pair, and at `max_tokens=1024` (`reasoning_tokens=1024`, still 0 accepted). Only succeeded once raised to `max_tokens=4096`. `_validate_rule_text()`'s classification call (also 512) did NOT reproduce the failure in the one live case tested — its `RuleClassification` schema is much smaller (3 short fields vs. `MissedAlertRuleDraft`'s open-ended `rule_text`), so it appears to fit inside the same budget more often, but is drawing from the same undersized pool and is not proven safe under a longer constraint list.

- `[x]` **Gap 236 (BE): Trainer's QA chat panel can't answer any question about a newly-uploaded (new-vendor) invoice, because no `Invoice` row exists for it to query** — opened 2026-08-17, founder-reported and confirmed by direct code read. `_handle_qa_test_turn()` (`routers/trainer.py:1403+`) routes every QA-mode question straight to the normal tenant-wide `agents/query_agent.py::run_query_agent()`, which answers via SQL/RAG against the real `Invoice` table. For the **upload path specifically**, Feature 18's own build deliberately skips creating an `Invoice` row (it would consume free-invoice quota and appear on the dashboard — see Gap 228's entry) — so the just-extracted data exists only in the ephemeral Trainer session (`extracted_data`, `ocr_text`), invisible to `run_query_agent()`. The only context passed into the QA call is a vendor-name prefix (`scoped_message = f"[Trainer QA for vendor {vendor}] {content}"`) — nothing about the actual invoice content. Result: any question asked in the QA panel right after uploading a new vendor's PDF gets an empty/no-match answer, even though the extraction just succeeded and is visible elsewhere in the same screen. **Only affects the upload path** — QA questions against an existing vendor's already-selected invoice (a real `Invoice` row) work correctly today. **Fix, two parts — deliberately not just "answer from the session data," because a naive fix would make Trainer's QA answers diverge from what the real Chat window would say about the same invoice once it's actually ingested**:

1. **Data parity**: the Trainer upload path must call the exact same extraction (and, where applicable, verification) code the real ingestion pipeline uses — not a shortcut/simplified version — so the session's `extracted_data` is identical to what would actually get persisted, not an approximation of it. Residual, honest exception: verification-alert generation only runs on real async ingestion, so a freshly-uploaded sample won't have alerts yet — worth a one-line note in the QA panel UI for this specific case, not a broad disclaimer.
2. **Mechanism parity**: in `_handle_qa_test_turn()` (`routers/trainer.py:1403+`), branch on whether the session has a real `invoice_id` (`sample_invoice_id`). If not (upload path), don't invent a separate raw-data-into-prompt mechanism — instead build an in-memory result shaped like what the real SQL route (`agents/query_agent.py`) would have produced from a DB row, and run it through the **same** summarize/formatting code real chat answers already use. This keeps QA-on-an-upload as the same answering logic fed session data instead of a DB row, not a parallel, differently-behaved feature — so future improvements to real chat's answer quality apply here automatically, with one prompt template instead of two. If a real `invoice_id` exists (history-select path), keep the current DB-backed `run_query_agent()` behavior unchanged.

Deliberately not persisting a real `Invoice` row for uploads as the fix — that would reopen the exact quota/dashboard-visibility problem the upload path was built to avoid.

**Fixed 2026-08-17**: part 1 (data parity) turned out to already be in place — `routers/trainer.py::upload_transient_file()` already calls the same `run_extraction_agent()` the real ingestion pipeline uses (confirmed by direct comparison against `queue_worker/handlers.py`'s call), per the code's own "Feature 18: the upload path now lands on the same thing the history path does" comment. Part 2 (mechanism parity) was the actual gap: added `_answer_qa_from_session_data()`, which mirrors `run_query_agent()`'s SQL-route summarize step — builds a `field | value` table from `session["extracted_data"]` in place of real query rows, feeds it through the same prompt/response shape (`### Extracted Data` appended, same currency-symbol rule) — and `_handle_qa_test_turn()` now branches on `session.get("sample_invoice_id")`: real DB-backed `run_query_agent()` unchanged for existing-vendor sessions, the new in-memory answerer for upload-path sessions. **Known, accepted deviation from the ideal**: not factored into a shared function with `agents/query_agent.py` in this pass (that file was already touched by several other gaps the same day) — if its summarize prompt changes, this mirror needs updating alongside it. Verified: `tests/test_trainer.py::test_qa_test_turn_on_upload_path_answers_from_session_data` added (asserts the real DB-backed agent is never called for an upload-path session, and the reply reflects the session's own extracted data) — the test's first draft asserted a `content` key on the chat endpoint's response that doesn't exist (the reply lives on the persisted `ChatMessage` row, same as the sibling QA test), corrected. Along the way, running the full suite surfaced two unrelated pre-existing test-isolation gaps (`test_connectors.py::test_salesforce_pkce_flow`/`test_oauth_callback_salesforce_verification_failure`, `test_ingestion.py::test_directory_watcher_disabled_by_default` — both had been fixed in an earlier session but the fix never made it into what's on `master`) — re-fixed with the same `monkeypatch`/fixture approach as before. Full suite green: **557 passed, 0 failed** against a real local Postgres/Redis/Chroma stack.

- `[x]` **Gap 237 (BE): Chat's answer to a follow-up question silently drops rows that were included in the original question's answer, with no indication the filter changed** — opened 2026-08-17, founder-reported from a live conversation transcript. Original question: "what are the total invoices related to clouds" → SQL route correctly used a 4-way filter (`vendor_name LIKE '%cloud%'` OR `customer_name LIKE '%cloud%'` OR `tags LIKE '%cloud%'` OR `items LIKE '%cloud%'`) and found **3 USD invoices totaling $2,655,637.56**. Follow-up in the same session: "can u explain the 3 USD currency invoice details" → the reply said *"I see 2 USD inbound invoices totaling USD 2,586,625.13"* and listed only 2. **Confirmed as a real defect from the numbers alone, no DB access needed**: $2,655,637.56 − $2,586,625.13 = **$69,012.43**, exactly the size of one missing invoice, not a rounding artifact. The reply asserted a specific, wrong count ("I see 2") with no hedge, despite directly contradicting its own answer one turn earlier in the same visible conversation. **Hypothesis, not yet confirmed against live data** (this conversation is against a tenant/dataset not present in the local dev DB, so the exact generated SQL for the follow-up turn hasn't been inspected directly): the follow-up's SQL is regenerated fresh from `chat_history` each turn rather than reusing/narrowing the prior turn's exact WHERE clause; if the LLM simplifies away the `items` (line-item description) branch when composing the narrower "just the USD ones" query, any invoice that only matched the original filter via a line-item description (e.g. "Cloud Migration Assessment") — not vendor/customer name or tags — would be silently dropped from the follow-up while still being real cloud-related spend. **Proposed solution, two steps — confirm before fixing, then fix both the cause and add a safety net rather than either alone**:

1. **Confirm the mechanism first.** Reproduce a similar multi-turn narrowing question against a seeded multi-invoice tenant in the local dev DB (an original broad question whose match set includes at least one invoice that only qualifies via the `items` line-item-description clause, then a same-session follow-up narrowing to a subset). Capture and diff the actual generated SQL for both turns — don't assume the `items` clause is what gets dropped until this is seen directly.
2. **Fix the cause**: when a follow-up is narrowing the prior answer ("explain the 3 USD ones") rather than asking about a new subject, have the SQL-generation prompt reuse/narrow the prior turn's exact WHERE clause instead of composing a fresh one from `chat_history` alone — removes the opportunity for the LLM to silently simplify away a condition.
3. **Fix as a safety net regardless of (2)**: have the summary step compare the follow-up's row count against what the immediately prior turn in the same session found on the same subject; if they don't reconcile (e.g. prior turn said 3, this turn only surfaces 2), don't answer with unqualified confidence — surface the discrepancy to the user instead of silently asserting the smaller number as fact. This catches the same failure class even if a future case slips past fix (2).

**Step 3 (safety net) built 2026-08-17** — steps 1-2 still open, deliberately not attempted without the live-data confirmation step 1 calls for. `agents/query_agent.py::run_query_agent()`: after a turn's `result_invoice_ids` are finalized, if the user's message references a specific count from the prior turn (regex `\b(?:the|those)\s+(\d{1,3})\b` — matches the exact reported phrasing, "the 3 USD..."/"those 2...") and the latest prior assistant `ChatMessage` in the session had that many `result_invoice_ids` but the current turn's count differs from the referenced number, a hedge sentence is appended to the reply instead of leaving the mismatch unqualified. Deliberately narrow trigger (an explicit numeric back-reference, not just "count went down") — a genuinely narrower follow-up SHOULD often return fewer rows and a blind "count decreased" check would false-positive on that constantly; this targets the actual reported failure shape (self-contradiction against an explicit number), not "any narrowing." **This is a symptom mitigation, not the root-cause fix** — steps 1-2 (confirm the WHERE-clause-drop mechanism against a seeded tenant, then have the SQL prompt reuse/narrow the prior turn's exact clause) are unchanged and still need that live repro before they can be built. Marked partial, not closed.

**Step 1 (live repro) done 2026-08-17 by functional-tester — hypothesis disconfirmed, real mechanism is different, and two more findings surfaced.** Seeded a dedicated test tenant with 5 invoices deliberately built so a broad "cloud" question matches via three separate SQL branches (vendor-name-only, tags-only, items-only), then ran the same real two-turn conversation against the live backend **7 times** (LLM non-determinism meant one run wasn't enough to trust). Full evidence: `docs/test_evidence/gap237_sql_repro_2026-08-17/README.md` + `raw_turns_run0.json`..`run6.json`; repro script `tests/gap237_sql_repro.py`.

1. **The defect is real and reproduces live** — 2 of 7 runs.
2. **The tracker's own hypothesis was wrong**: in both real reproductions, the follow-up's regenerated SQL dropped the **`vendor_name`** branch, not `items` — the `items` branch stayed intact in both. Step 2's fix (below) needs to target the actual dropped branch, not the one originally guessed.
3. **New failure mode, not previously documented**: in 4 of 7 runs, the follow-up generated **no SQL at all** (`sql: null`) and answered purely from the prior turn's aggregate text — a different, arguably worse shape of the same "confidently answers without a backing query" pattern this session's category-browsing test (Gap 240) also found elsewhere.
4. **The shipped step-3 hedge never fired in either real reproduction** — traced to a real bug in its own trigger condition: it compares the current turn's count against the prior turn's *total* row count, but the reported failure phrasing references a *sub-count* ("the 3 USD ones" is a subset of a larger original total) — so the mismatch the hedge is designed to catch doesn't match what it actually checks. Needs fixing alongside step 2, not left as-is.

Step 2 (the SQL-generation prompt fix) and the step-3 hedge's trigger-condition bug are still open, now scoped correctly against confirmed live evidence instead of the original guess.

**Steps 2 + 3 fixed 2026-08-17/18; gap closed.** All of it in `agents/query_agent.py`, verified live rather than by mocked test alone (see the note under Gap 241 on why that distinction is load-bearing here). Four changes:

1. **Step 2, the cause** — new `get_prior_turn_sql()` reads the most recent assistant `ChatMessage` in the session that actually ran a query (skipping RAG/CHAT turns, whose `generated_sql` is NULL), and a `PREVIOUS TURN'S SQL` block puts that exact query in front of the model. New prompt **rule 9** requires the follow-up to start from *its* WHERE clause verbatim, add the new restriction with `AND`, and never drop/merge/simplify a branch of an existing OR group — the SELECT list stays free to change. Targeted at the branch the live repro actually showed being dropped (`vendor_name`), but structural, not column-specific.
2. **The `sql: null` failure mode** (5 of 8 in this pass's own baseline, 4 of 7 in functional-tester's) — **rule 8a** states plainly that conversation history is a record of what was said, not a data source; a null `sql` on a follow-up where a prior SQL turn exists gets exactly **one** forced regeneration (`_NULL_SQL_FOLLOWUP_RETRY_DIRECTIVE`), and if the model still declines, the reply ships with `_NO_FRESH_QUERY_NOTE` appended rather than passing a history-restated answer off as query-backed. Paired with a deterministic routing override: a back-reference phrasing (`_is_narrowing_followup()`) in a session that has a prior SQL-answered turn is forced onto the SQL route, since the measured mechanism was `classify_query()` sending "explain the 3 USD ones" to RAG, which has no notion of the previous result set.
3. **Step-3 hedge trigger bug** (item 4 above) — the hedge now compares the current turn's harvested id count against the number *the user referenced*, and grounds that number by requiring it to appear verbatim in the prior reply's text (so "show me the 3 largest invoices" is not hedged), instead of against the prior turn's total row count. It also stays silent when the current turn harvested 0 ids, which is indistinguishable from an unharvestable aggregate.
4. **A prompt bug found on 2026-08-18 while verifying** — see the jsonb-cast note under Gaps 241/242 below; it applies to this gap's queries too.

**Verified live, same harness and same run count as the baseline** (`tests/gap237_sql_repro.py`, 8 runs, private backend on :8100 so the shared :8000 stack was untouched): **before — correct 3/8, no_sql 5/8, branch_drop 0/8; after — correct 8/8, no_sql 0/8, branch_drop 0/8.** "Correct" is scored mechanically (turn 2's reply must name all three seeded USD invoices, one of which matches only via `vendor_name`, one only via `tags`, one only via `items`) by `score_runs.py` in the evidence dir, not by eye. The hedge fired 0/8 — correctly, since all 8 runs surfaced everything the user referenced; its own fix is covered by unit tests, not by this run set. Evidence: `docs/test_evidence/gap237_jsonb_cast_2026-08-18/README.md` (+ the mid-pass 2026-08-17 set in `gap237_step2_fix_2026-08-17/`). Unit tests: `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` (32). Full suite green: **616 passed, 0 failed.**

  **Impact**: Correction #4 ("flag a missed alert") — the one genuinely LLM-interpreted path in the whole Feature 18 redesign, and the mechanism Gap 225 named as the way a user reports a semantically-wrong-but-correctly-transcribed field — is non-functional against the real configured model. It also breaks the terminus of the chat wrong-data triage's `pdf_agrees=false` redirect (Gap 232/Feature 18 §8): a user who follows that whole flow correctly lands on this same broken endpoint. The fail-closed behavior itself is correct (no fabricated rule gets staged, matching the Gap 212 contract) — this is a real usability outage, not a data-integrity risk.
