## Feature 19 — security findings on the public contact endpoint (2026-08-18)

Opened 2026-08-18 from a **read-only** security review of the new unauthenticated contact-form path, filed at `reports/security/2026-08-18-support-contact-endpoint.md` (scoped exception to this repo's normal coding → functional → env-split → load → security order, taken because a merge-readiness review flagged the endpoint). All three were **open and deliberately not fixed in the merge-prep pass that filed them** — that pass was docs-only, and the review's recommendation that they were merge blockers rather than follow-ups was recorded here for the founder to call rather than acted on. **All three have since been fixed on branch `fix/support-contact-security-and-tenant-provisioning` (2026-08-18); see each entry below.** Findings 5–8 of the report (sync-handler threadpool exhaustion — plausible, not load-tested; tenant isolation and SendGrid key handling — both checked, no issue) are still not filed as gaps: 5 and 6 are deliberately deferred and 7–8 need no action. The report now carries a remediation-status block at the top recording exactly that.

- `[x]` **Gap 249 (BE): `POST /api/v1/support/contact` is public, unauthenticated and has no rate limiting or CAPTCHA anywhere in its path, while two other places in the codebase claim it does** — opened 2026-08-18. **Fixed 2026-08-18 in two passes; closed after the second.**

  **Pass 1 (initial fix).** Dual-layer rate limiting and anti-spam: (1) the Next.js proxy (`apps/invoice-website/app/api/contact/route.ts`) enforced an in-memory sliding window of 5 requests / 10 minutes per IP, dropped bot traffic with a silent 201 when the hidden `hp_field` honeypot was populated, and forwarded a client IP header to the backend; (2) the backend (`routers/support.py`) added `_ContactRateLimiter`, an in-memory sliding window on origin IP and email (5 / 5 minutes) returning `429` with `Retry-After: 300`. The contact page (`app/contact/page.tsx`) renders the honeypot and surfaces a retry message on 429.

  **Pass 2 (hardening — this is what actually closes the gap).** An architect review found pass 1 implemented but bypassable, and found a 500 of the same bug class the branch claimed to have fixed. All addressed:
  - **The rate-limit key was fully attacker-controlled.** Both layers took the *leftmost* `X-Forwarded-For` entry — the value the client supplies — so sending a fresh one per request reset both windows and defeated the limiter completely. Trust order is now: `X-Azure-ClientIP` **only** when `X-Azure-FDID` matches a configured `FRONT_DOOR_ID` (unset today, so ignored — Front Door is gated on `customDomainName` in `infra/08-apps.bicep` and is not deployed, making those headers forgeable); then `X-Client-IP`, which the website proxy sets from its own resolution and never forwards from the browser; then the **rightmost** valid `X-Forwarded-For` entry (the hop Container Apps' Envoy ingress actually observed); then the socket peer. `X-Real-IP` is no longer consulted. Every candidate must parse as a real IP via `_parse_ip`, so junk cannot become a rate-limit key.
  - **Limiter state was unbounded and per-replica.** The backend window now lives in Redis (sorted set per key, `EXPIRE` on every write), so it is genuinely shared across the app's 5 replicas and self-bounding. Redis being unreachable degrades to a bounded in-process window (`services/trainer_sessions.py`'s `_get_redis` fallback pattern) that prunes out-of-window keys on every check and caps tracked keys at `_MEMORY_MAX_TRACKED_KEYS` — it does not fail open and does not 500. The `defaultdict` was replaced with an `OrderedDict`, which also stops a rejected lookup from permanently creating a key.
  - **Subject-length 500.** `submit_contact_inquiry` built `f"[{category}] Contact inquiry from {name}"` with `name` capped at 255 but the prefix adding 31–41 more, overflowing `SupportTicket.subject`'s `max_length=255` → Postgres `StringDataRightTruncation` → uncaught 500. Now truncated to `_SUBJECT_MAX_LENGTH` at construction (chosen over lowering `name`'s cap so a future prefix-format change cannot reintroduce it).
  - **Honeypot false positives were invisible.** A password manager filling `hp_field` silently discarded a genuine inquiry while telling the user it was received. The proxy now logs a `console.warn` with the trigger value, claimed name/email and user agent so a false-positive pattern is visible in logs; the client-facing response is deliberately unchanged so bots learn nothing.

  **Remaining caveat, stated rather than glossed:** the *website proxy* window is still per-instance and that app scales 0–3 with scale-to-zero, so a cold start wipes it and its effective ceiling is up to `instances x 5`. This is accepted, not overlooked — that layer is best-effort edge shedding, and the authoritative limit is the backend's Redis-backed one. The backend falls back to a per-replica window only while Redis is down.

  **Verification:** full backend suite green — 674 passed, 4 skipped. 28 new tests in `tests/test_support.py` (`TestSubjectLengthOverflow`, `TestClientIpResolution`, `TestRateLimitBypassResistance`, `TestRateLimiterStateBounds`) alongside the original `test_rate_limit_returns_429_after_threshold`. The two regression tests were confirmed to genuinely fail against the pre-fix code, not just pass against the new: reverting the truncation fails the subject tests (256 and 296 chars), and reverting `_get_client_ip` fails `test_rotating_leftmost_forwarded_for_cannot_reset_window` with 201 instead of 429. Subject assertions are on the constructed/stored string's length, because the suite's in-memory SQLite does not enforce `max_length` and a status-only assertion would pass with the bug present. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `invoice-website`. Not deployed to any Azure environment.

  Files: `routers/support.py`, `config.py` (`FRONT_DOOR_ID`), `models.py` (ticket-number format comment), `tests/test_support.py`, `apps/invoice-website/app/api/contact/route.ts`, `apps/invoice-website/app/contact/page.tsx`, `docs/feature_19_support_tickets_and_notifications.md`, `reports/security/2026-08-18-support-contact-endpoint.md`.

- `[x]` **Gap 250 (BE): `services/support_email.py` interpolates attacker-controlled request fields into both email templates with no HTML escaping — an open relay for phishing from the platform's own authenticated sending domain. Highest-severity finding of the review.** — opened 2026-08-18. **Fixed 2026-08-18.** All user-supplied request fields in both email templates (`_ticket_html` and `_receipt_html` in `services/support_email.py`) are now strictly HTML-escaped using Python's `html.escape(..., quote=True)` — covering `user_name`, `company_name`, `user_email` (both display and `mailto:` link), `subject`, and `description`. Subjects are sanitized against newline injection (`\r`/`\n` stripped). Input length validation added to `ContactInquiryRequest` and `AppTicketRequest` for `name` and `company` (max 255 chars), preventing unhandled DB `StringDataRightTruncation` 500s. Covered by automated tests `test_html_in_name_is_escaped_in_receipt_email`, `test_html_in_name_is_escaped_in_staff_alert`, `test_html_in_description_is_escaped_in_staff_alert`, `test_html_in_company_is_escaped_in_staff_alert`, `test_name_over_255_chars_is_rejected`, `test_company_over_255_chars_is_rejected` in `tests/test_support.py`. Files: `services/support_email.py`, `routers/support.py`, `tests/test_support.py`.

- `[x]` **Gap 251 (BE): the ticket-number keyspace is 9,000 values per year, and exhausting it takes the contact form down with an uncaught 500** — opened 2026-08-18. **Fixed 2026-08-18.** `_generate_ticket_number()` updated to use cryptographic randomness `secrets.token_hex(4).upper()` yielding 8 uppercase hex characters (4.29 billion distinct values per year per prefix), expanding keyspace by ~477,000x and eliminating enumeration risk. Ticket format: `INQ-YYYY-XXXXXXXX` / `TICK-YYYY-XXXXXXXX`. If exhaustion retries fail, `_unique_ticket_number()` now raises `HTTPException(status_code=503, detail="Ticket generation service temporarily busy — please retry.")` rather than an unhandled `RuntimeError` crashing the endpoint with a 500. Covered by automated tests `test_ticket_number_uses_hex_format` and `test_ticket_generation_exhaustion_returns_503_not_500` in `tests/test_support.py`. Files: `routers/support.py`, `tests/test_support.py`.

- `[x]` **Gap 252 (BE): Chat (and every other request) fails against the deployed dev backend because the readiness probe checks a path that doesn't exist, so the current revision never becomes ready and stays pinned to 100% traffic** — found 2026-08-18, user-reported ("chat is giving failed to response"). Root cause, confirmed directly against the live `ca-invoice-be-dev` Container App and its replicas (not guessed): the app's Readiness probe is configured to `GET /health/readiness` on port 8000, but this FastAPI app only implements `GET /health` — `/health/readiness` 404s every 10s, confirmed continuously in the live container logs. Azure Container Apps will not mark a replica ready until its readiness probe passes, so revision `ca-invoice-be-dev--0000048` (deployed 2026-08-18 09:06 UTC) sits at `runningState: ActivationFailed`, `healthState: Unhealthy`, replica `ready: false`, while `az containerapp ingress traffic show` confirms it still holds **100% of traffic** as the latest revision — so requests routed to it, including chat, fail. **Infra-drift finding, not a bicep bug at the time this was written**: grepped this repo's entire `infra/` and `.github/` trees for `health/readiness` and found zero references — this probe was configured directly on the live Azure resource (portal or manual `az containerapp update`), not through this repo's IaC at the time. **Stale as of 2026-08-18, corrected**: `infra/modules/compute/invoice-be.bicep` now DOES declare `path: '/health/readiness'` explicitly as the Readiness probe — it landed via the Feature 19→20 observability branch merge the same day, after this line was originally written. The durable fix (a real `/health/readiness` endpoint in `main.py`, checking Postgres + Redis) has also landed via that same merge. **Also corrected**: the tracker's Feature 19/20 entry above claimed a "CI/CD post-deploy health verification gate" for this environment — false at the time for dev. Only `deploy-prod.yml` had a post-deploy health check; `deploy-dev.yml`/`_deploy-service.yml` had none, which is the reason this outage held 100% of traffic for hours with no automated signal — closed separately as Gap 258.

  **Closed 2026-08-19/20**: live-confirmed `Running`/`Healthy` on `ca-invoice-be-dev` across roughly a dozen separate deploys through the rest of this session (`--0000055`, `--0000056`, `--0000059`, and others), independently re-checked via `az containerapp revision list` after essentially every push, not just once. No recurrence of the original readiness-probe failure at any point. Gap 258 (dev post-deploy health gate) now exists and has already fired for real once — see that gap's entry for the false-positive it caught and the fix that followed, itself real evidence the gate (and by extension this fix) works as intended.

- `[x]` **Gap 253 (BE): Chat SQL route has no line-item-level extraction — a question about one specific line item returns the whole invoice's grand total instead** — found 2026-08-18, user-reported live example. Asked *"what is the current training amount"* / *"i want the amount only for training and onboarding from the total invoice"* against an invoice with two line items (Cloud Storage $765.36, Training & Onboarding $29,302.94) plus 18% tax, grand total $35,480.59. Chat answered **$35,480.59** both times — the invoice's full `grand_total`, including the unrelated Cloud Storage line and tax — not the correct **$29,302.94** (the Training & Onboarding line's own `amount`). **Root cause, confirmed from the actual generated SQL**: `agents/query_agent.py`'s only category/keyword pattern (rule 6b) is invoice-level — find invoices where `items`/`tags`/`vendor_name`/`customer_name` mention the keyword, then `SUM(grand_total)` across those invoices. There is no rule at all for reaching into the `items` JSONB array and extracting/summing just the matching line item's own `amount`, so any question asking for a specific line item's figure (not "which invoices relate to X") falls back to this invoice-level pattern and silently includes unrelated lines and tax. **Suggested fix**: add a second SQL pattern using `jsonb_array_elements(items)` to unnest line items into rows, filter on each item's own `description`, and select/sum only the matching items' own `description`/`quantity`/`unit_price`/`amount` fields (not the invoice's `grand_total`) — **correction 2026-08-18: no such precedent actually exists** — Gap 241/242's fix text above mentions `jsonb_array_elements` as an example SQL shape in this tracker's prose, but a repo-wide grep of `agents/query_agent.py` and every other `.py` file in `invoice-be` for `jsonb_array_elements`/`json_each` returns zero real occurrences. This would be the **first** JSONB-unnest query written anywhere in this codebase, not an adaptation of an existing one — treat it as new, unproven ground (including for SQLite, which has no `jsonb_array_elements` equivalent and would need its own `json_each`-based path), not a copy-paste of a working pattern. The prompt needs a rule distinguishing "give me this line's own number" (line-item extraction) from "which invoices are about this category" (existing invoice-level rule, unchanged) — that disambiguation, not just the SQL syntax, is the real design work. **Response formatting requirement, per founder direction**: a line-item answer should render as `{description}: {qty} units × ${unit_price} = ${amount}`, one line per matched item; if more than one line item matches (same invoice or across invoices), list each one that way and add a total underneath only when there's more than one. **Fixed 2026-08-19** — see `docs/feature_6_rag.md` "Rule 6d" for the full design. What shipped, in `agents/query_agent.py`: (1) **new rule 6d, dialect-conditioned at prompt-build time.** `_sql_dialect_name(db_session)` reads the live bind's dialect and `_line_item_rule()` renders exactly one of `_LINE_ITEM_RULE_POSTGRES` / `_LINE_ITEM_RULE_SQLITE` into the SQL system prompt, so the model is never shown syntax the bound engine cannot parse. This is the same mechanism rule 6(a) already settled for `CAST(... AS TEXT)` — teach one correct form per engine, never emit dialect-specific SQL and repair it afterwards. **An intermediate implementation did the opposite** (a regex rewriter inside `execute_generated_sql()` translating `jsonb_array_elements`/`->>`/`::numeric` into SQLite at execution time); it was **deleted rather than patched** — it can only ever cover the exact syntactic shapes it was written against, while the model is free to emit any equivalent one. (2) **NULL / non-array `items` guard, taught in the rule for both engines.** `items` is nullable and machine-populated, and un-nesting it unguarded aborts the query for the *whole tenant* off one bad row (verified by hand against SQLite 3.50.4: `json_each('not json')` → `OperationalError: malformed JSON`; Postgres `jsonb_array_elements` raises on a scalar/object the same way) — and each abort burns an attempt of the 3-try repair loop. Both variants therefore wrap the argument: `jsonb_array_elements(CASE WHEN jsonb_typeof(items) = 'array' THEN items ELSE '[]'::jsonb END)` / `json_each(CASE WHEN json_valid(items) AND json_type(items) = 'array' THEN items ELSE '[]' END)`. Guarding via the join's `ON` clause was tested and **rejected** — with `LEFT JOIN` SQLite still evaluates `json_each` before applying `ON` and aborts anyway. (3) **`->>` is native SQLite (3.38+)**, so the SQLite variant uses `item.value ->> 'field'` with no cast; the `.value` matters and is called out explicitly in the prompt, because `json_each(items) AS item` aliases the *table* json_each returns, not the element. (4) **Rule 7 compliance**: 6d selects `currency` in both the detail and the aggregate shape, the aggregate groups by it, and the summary-prompt format is now `{line_description}: {line_qty} units × {currency} {line_unit_price} = {currency} {line_amount}` using the row's own currency — the founder-requested format above hardcoded `$`, which is exactly what FE Gap 183 is on file for. (5) **Rule 9 gained an explicit FROM-clause exception**: this gap's own reported phrasing ("the amount only for training and onboarding **from the total invoice**") is a narrowing follow-up, so 6d and 9 fire together; rule 9 previously fixed only the WHERE clause and said nothing about FROM, which reads as "keep the previous invoice-level FROM" — i.e. return the grand total again. It now authorises adding the line-item join (and only that) while every tenant/invoice-identifying predicate still carries over verbatim. (6) **`_harvest_invoice_ids_via_companion_query()` un-broken for 6d.** It bailed on any `\bjoin\b` in the reconstructed tail, and every 6d query has one — so `result_invoice_ids` came back empty for *every* line-item answer, silently disabling the FE's "which invoice was wrong?" triage picker (Gap 231) and the Gap 237 step-3 hedge (which needs `current_count > 0` to fire). The un-nest join is now whitelisted **by shape** (`_UNNEST_JOIN_RHS`) and the companion query rebuilt as `SELECT DISTINCT invoice.id` — it multiplies rows per invoice but never changes which invoices match; a join to any real table still bails exactly as before. Rule 6d correspondingly tells the model **not** to select `invoice.id` (raw UUIDs in the user-facing results table), since identity is recovered here instead. **Verification**: 12 tests in `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` (38 passed / 6 skipped in that file), of which the load-bearing ones *execute* the taught SQL — extracted verbatim out of the prompt constant, so a divergence between what is taught and what is tested is impossible — against the real SQLite engine over the gap's own reported data (Cloud Storage 765.36 + Training & Onboarding 29,302.94 inside a 35,480.59 grand total, plus a NULL-`items` row and a not-JSON-at-all row): returns **29,302.94** only, with the Cloud Storage line absent and the grand total never surfaced. Multi-currency aggregate verified separately (`{"USD": 29502.94, "INR": 50.0}`, grouped, never summed across). Full BE suite: **691 passed, 2 failed, 6 skipped** — both failures are `tests/test_connectors.py` Salesforce OAuth tests needing a local Redis on :6379, confirmed identical on a stashed clean tree, unrelated to this change. **Postgres half closed 2026-08-19 (Gap 255):** `test_taught_line_item_sql_runs_on_postgres` (both parametrized cases) and `test_recommended_cast_form_runs_on_postgres` **passed** against Docker Postgres on `localhost:5433` (`invoice-postgres-local` via `docker-compose.yml`) — rule 6d's Postgres form is now executed evidence on both engines, not asserted-only. Live functional-tester repro (the three scenarios in `.claude/tasklists/senior-dev-gap253-line-item-sql-extraction.md`'s handoff section) is still outstanding; flush the 1hr answer cache before re-testing live — there is no `flush_answer_cache()` helper in `query_agent.py` (only `get_cached_answer`/`set_cached_answer`/`_cache_key`); the existing flush paths are `routers/chat.py::_invalidate_chat_answer_cache()` and `routers/trainer.py::_invalidate_chat_answer_cache()`, or delete the Redis key `chat_answer_cache:{tenant_id}:{normalized question}` directly.

- `[x]` **Gap 254 (BE): Help Center AI Support Assistant answers only a narrow slice of real questions, and presents every miss as a diagnosed incident requiring escalation** — found 2026-08-18, user-reported ("help agent was telling everything not supported"). Two distinct causes confirmed, not one: (1) **BE Gap 252's outage was live at the time** and would independently produce this symptom — every backend call including `/api/v1/support/chat` was failing, though the frontend's outage message ("temporary connection issue communicating with the troubleshooting service") is textually distinguishable from this gap's fallback message, so which one a given user actually saw can be confirmed by the exact wording. (2) **A real, separate defect exists in the assistant's own logic, independently reproducible against a fully healthy backend**: `agents/support_agent.py`'s `KNOWLEDGE_TOPICS` matcher does raw substring matching (`kw in lower_query`) with keywords as short as 2-3 characters, not word-boundary matching. Tested 24 realistic product questions against the real matcher — **11 of 24 fell through to the generic "raise a ticket" fallback** (upload, delete, file formats/limits, bulk upload, user invites, usage/dashboard, data retention, OCR correction, "what is SAGE") and **4 more matched the wrong article via a substring collision**: "What does the **conf-id-ence** score mean?" and "Can I **prov-id-e** feedback?" both hit the `"id"` keyword and return the password-reset article (first in the topic list, so it's the de facto wrong-answer default on any tie); "stuck in **pro**cessing" hits `"pro"` and returns the pricing guide; "**data** encrypted at rest" hits `"data"` and returns the CSV-export guide. Only 7 topics exist total, matching the four hardcoded prompt chips in `SupportChatWindow.tsx` — the demo path works, most of the real question space does not. Compounding this: the fallback path unconditionally sets `suggest_escalation=True`, so an unanswered question renders the same red "Issue Diagnosis & Recommended Escalation" card as a genuine detected error, rather than an honest "I don't have an article for that." `evaluate_support_query()` also accepts a `history` parameter and never reads it, so a natural follow-up ("how do I do that?") always misses regardless. **Suggested fix**: word-boundary matching with a sensible minimum keyword length (kills the substring-collision class outright); a materially expanded topic set covering ingestion/upload, invoice statuses, dashboard/analytics, user management, retention/security, and file limits; a distinct low-confidence "no article for this, try the Knowledge Base search or here's where to look" response path that does not trigger the incident-card styling; actually reading `history` for follow-ups. **Fixed 2026-08-19, with one sub-feature deliberately descoped — hence `[~]`, not `[x]`.** See `docs/feature_19_support_tickets_and_notifications.md` for the full resolution order. What shipped in `agents/support_agent.py`: (1) **word-boundary matching with a 3-character minimum** (`_score_topic()`, `MIN_KEYWORD_LENGTH`), which kills the `"id"`-in-"confidence" / `"pro"`-in-"processing" substring-collision class outright; the `"id"` keyword itself is gone from `account_auth`. (2) **Five new topics** — `ingestion_upload`, `invoice_statuses`, `dashboard_analytics`, `user_management`, `security_retention` — taking the set from 7 to 12. (3) **The `"data"` example from this entry needed more than word boundaries**, and was re-confirmed broken after them: `"data"` is a genuine whole word, so "Is my data encrypted at rest?" tied 1-1 on hit count between `export_reports` and `security_retention`, and Python's stable sort resolved the tie by list position — returning the CSV-export guide for a security question. Fixed two ways: `"data"` dropped from `export_reports` (its other seven keywords cover the topic fully), **and** the sort key is now `(matched keyword count, total matched keyword length)`, so a longer/more specific phrase match (`"at rest"`, `"data security"`) beats a short generic one instead of list order deciding. Verified against that exact question. (4) **The low-confidence path no longer sets `suggest_escalation=True`** — an unanswered question was rendering the same red "Issue Diagnosis & Recommended Escalation" card as a genuine detected incident. (5) **Bonus, pre-existing bug found and fixed in the same matcher**: KB topics are matched *before* `ERROR_TRIGGERS` and return early, so `billing`'s `"payu"` and `"checkout"` keywords made `ERR_PAYU_BILLING_FAILURE` **unreachable** — "payu error on checkout", "my payment failed at checkout" and "checkout crash" all returned the pricing article instead of the payment-failure troubleshooting card. Both keywords removed. This was not caused by this pass; it was live already. `tests/test_support.py::test_error_triggers_are_not_shadowed_by_kb_keywords` is now a standing screen over every trigger phrasing, so any future keyword addition that re-creates the collision fails there rather than in production, and the pre-existing `test_error_keyword_triggers_escalation` ("504 gateway timeout during batch sync") still passes — none of the five new topics use a keyword that shadows it. **Follow-up resolution (item 4) shipped separately as Gap 256 (2026-08-19):** `SupportChatResponse.topic_id` + `SupportChatRequest.last_topic_id` + FE carry-forward in `SupportChatWindow.tsx` — anaphoric follow-ups like "how do I do that?" now resolve against the prior matched topic; `history` remains accepted but unread.** **Scope expansion, called out rather than done silently**: fixing the follow-up backend-only would have removed the only *contextual* in-chat path to raise a ticket on a miss (a header-level "Raise Ticket Directly" button does still exist), so a third, neutral UI state was added — `SupportChatResponse.low_confidence` (new, defaulted `False`, so an older FE build is unaffected) drives a plain "Didn't find an answer? / Raise a Ticket" card in `apps/invoice-fe/components/help/SupportChatWindow.tsx` with no red framing and no implied incident. **Verification**: `tests/test_support.py` 70 passed (15 in `TestSupportChatEndpoint`, including 4 Gap 256 cases); full BE suite 691 passed / 2 failed (both `test_connectors.py` Salesforce OAuth, needing a local Redis on :6379 — confirmed identical on a stashed clean tree) / 6 skipped. FE `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **Live Help Center click-through verified 2026-08-19** (FE Gap 243): miss `how do I wash my car?` → neutral low-confidence card + prefilled Normal ticket; 504 chip → red incident card + Urgent 504 subject. Two stale claims corrected in this same pass, both load-bearing for Gap 252 above: the "grepped infra/ and found zero `/health/readiness` references" line was true when written but is now stale — that path landed in `infra/modules/compute/invoice-be.bicep` via the Feature 19/20 observability merge — and the claim of a dev-side "CI/CD post-deploy health verification gate" is false; only `deploy-prod.yml` has one, dev has none, which is why Gap 252's outage ran undetected for hours.
- `[x]` **Gap 255 (BE): rule 6d's PostgreSQL form has never been executed against a real PostgreSQL** — opened 2026-08-19, **closed 2026-08-19**. `uv run pytest tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py -k postgres -v` run against Docker Postgres on `localhost:5433` (`invoice-postgres-local` via `docker-compose.yml`): `test_taught_line_item_sql_runs_on_postgres` (both parametrized cases) and `test_recommended_cast_form_runs_on_postgres` all **passed** — rule 6d's Postgres form is now executed evidence, not asserted-only.

- `[x]` **Gap 256 (BE): the Support assistant cannot resolve a conversational follow-up, and cannot be made to without an API contract change** — opened 2026-08-19, **fixed 2026-08-19**. `SupportChatResponse.topic_id` echoes the matched `KNOWLEDGE_TOPICS` entry; `SupportChatRequest.last_topic_id` carries it forward from `SupportChatWindow.tsx`; `evaluate_support_query()` resolves short anaphoric follow-ups (`_FOLLOW_UP_PHRASES` + `_is_anaphoric_follow_up()`) against that id after the direct keyword pass misses. `history` remains accepted but unread — only `last_topic_id` resolves follow-ups. **Verification:** `tests/test_support.py::TestSupportChatEndpoint` 15 passed (4 new Gap 256 cases); FE `SupportChatWindow.tsx` stores `topicId` and sends `last_topic_id`.

- `[x]` **Gap 257 (BE): the Dead-Letter Queue alert rule can never fire — it filters on a metric dimension that doesn't exist** — opened 2026-08-19, **fixed 2026-08-19**, found during the Feature 20 observability merge-readiness review. `alert-rules.bicep`'s `dlqPoisonAlert` filtered `metricName: 'QueueMessageCount'` on a `QueueName` dimension. Confirmed live against Azure (`az rest` on the storage account's metric definitions): `QueueMessageCount` and `QueueCapacity` both report **no dimensions** (`dims: null`). **Fix:** replaced the metric alert with `Microsoft.Insights/scheduledQueryRules@2023-03-15-preview` scoped to the Log Analytics workspace, KQL `ContainerAppConsoleLogs_CL | where Log_s has "POISON MESSAGE ISOLATED"` (same line `queue_worker/main_worker.py::_route_to_dead_letter_queue()` already emits, same query shape as `dashboard.bicep`'s DLQ panel). New resource name `alert-${workerAppName}-dlq-poison-isolated` so an already-deployed metric alert is not a type-change conflict. `09-monitoring.bicep` now passes `logAnalyticsWorkspaceId` + `queryAlertLocation`. **Verified:** `az bicep build --file infra/09-monitoring.bicep` compiles (only pre-existing BCP334 warnings). **Not verified:** Azure deploy of Stage 9, or a live poison-message fire. DLQ *routing* logic is unchanged.
- `[x]` **Gap 258 (BE): dev environment has no post-deploy health check, so a broken revision can hold 100% of traffic undetected indefinitely** — opened 2026-08-19, **fixed 2026-08-19**, found while diagnosing Gap 252's outage. `deploy-dev.yml`/`_deploy-service.yml` had none — confirmed by reading both files. **Fix shipped:** (1) `_deploy-service.yml` now polls the latest revision's `healthState`/`runningState` for up to 4 minutes immediately after `az containerapp update`, failing the job on `ActivationFailed`/`Failed` or timeout — applies to every dev/prod service deploy. (2) `deploy-dev.yml` gained a `verify-deployment` job (Gap 258) matching prod's gate pattern: public website ingress curl, backend `GET /health/readiness` curl, then all four dev Container Apps must be `provisioningState=Succeeded` with their traffic-bearing revision `Healthy`/`Running`. **Not yet live-verified against Azure** — will exercise on the next `deploy-dev.yml` run; code review + parity with prod `verify-deployment` is the close evidence here.

- `[x]` **Gap 263 (BE): Chat SQL route misfires on tax-component questions and leaks internal columns/raw Python dicts into results** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, founder-reported from a live chat transcript (Rajesh Steel Corporation invoice, CGST question) during autoloop pre-flight testing. Two distinct defects found in the same conversation:
  1. **Rule 6d (Gap 253's line-item rule) misfired on tax-component terms.** "What's the CGST we paid on the Rajesh Steel invoices?" matched rule 6d's trigger ("a product/service phrase together with a money word") even though CGST/SGST/IGST are never stored as line-item descriptions — this schema tracks only a single combined `tax_amount` per invoice, no separate tax-component split. The generated query searched `item->>'description' LIKE '%cgst%'`, guaranteed zero rows, and the reply said *"I found no Rajesh Steel invoices matching your query"* — false; the invoice existed (confirmed one turn later by a "list all invoices" query), only that one wrong-shaped filter matched nothing.
  2. **`execute_generated_sql()` rendered every selected column verbatim with no hygiene.** A generic "list all the invoices" question selected `batch_id` and `file_path` alongside business columns, and both were dumped straight into the chat window — `file_path` is an internal Azure blob storage URI (`azure://invoices/tenants/<tenant-uuid>/invoices/<file-uuid>.pdf`), exposing internal storage architecture and the tenant UUID on a customer-facing surface. Separately, `items` (a JSONB list-of-dicts, auto-deserialized into native Python objects by psycopg2 even for a raw `text()` query — a DBAPI-level adaptation independent of SQLAlchemy's ORM type system) was rendered via plain `str()`, producing Python's repr (single-quoted keys, `None` instead of `null`) instead of valid JSON.

  **Fixed, `agents/query_agent.py`:**
  1. Both `_LINE_ITEM_RULE_POSTGRES` and `_LINE_ITEM_RULE_SQLITE` gained an explicit exclusion: tax-component terms (CGST/SGST/IGST/VAT/"tax") must not be treated as line-item search targets — the model is steered to answer from `tax_amount` directly instead, and told never to report a zero-row line-item search as "no invoice found" when the invoice-level filters would have matched.
  2. New `_INTERNAL_ONLY_COLUMNS` denylist (`file_path`, `batch_id`) enforced deterministically in `execute_generated_sql()` — stripped from the display table regardless of what the LLM SELECTed, while the full unfiltered column set is still used for Gap 231's invoice-id snapshot harvest (which needs `id`/`invoice_id` even though display hides them). List/dict values (JSONB columns already deserialized by the driver) are now rendered with `json.dumps` instead of `str()`.

  **Architectural note, not addressed in this pass:** this is the third instance in one session of a hygiene rule (Gap 253's "don't select `invoice.id`, it's noise") being written narrowly for the one rule that surfaced it and never generalized — a known, discussed pattern (case-by-case prose rules that don't compose, vs. deterministic code for facts about the schema). The column-denylist fix here follows that direction (code, not prompt) for this specific instance; a broader sweep of the rest of the prompt for the same class of gap (e.g. whether rule 6b/6c have their own untested blind spots) has not been done and is not scoped here.

  **Verified**: new tests `test_rule_6d_excludes_tax_component_terms`, `test_execute_generated_sql_hides_internal_columns`, `test_execute_generated_sql_renders_json_columns_as_json_not_python_repr` added to `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` — the JSON-formatting test uses a mocked result set rather than the SQLite test fixture, because SQLite (unlike Postgres/psycopg2) returns the JSON column as an already-formatted string rather than a native Python object, so the SQLite fixture would not have exercised the fixed branch. Full suite: `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py`, `test_queries.py`, `test_rag.py`, `test_direction_aware_chat.py` — 113/113 passing. **Not yet independently re-verified against a live conversation with the real Azure-connected backend** — the reproduction was against the local unit-test suite only; the same live-verification standard applied to every other gap in this file (real Azure OCR/LLM, not mocked) should be run against this exact CGST question and the "list all invoices" question before this is trusted as fully closed in practice, not just in the test suite.

  **Note, 2026-08-19**: everything found in the same live-testing push this gap kicked off was originally logged as nested "follow-up" sub-entries under this one gap number. Split out into individually numbered Gaps 264-276 below, per founder request, so each has its own tracked identity instead of being buried in one growing paragraph. Substance unchanged, only the structure.

- `[x]` **Gap 264 (BE): Rule 6d's tax-term guardrail was a fixed list, missed plain "GST"** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, found immediately after Gap 263 shipped. The first version named CGST/SGST/IGST/VAT explicitly and missed plain "GST" — the founder's own next question, and arguably the more commonly used standalone term of the four. Widened the prose to "any tax-related term, not just these examples" first, then went further per founder push-back that a prose principle still just asks the LLM to correctly recognize an open-ended category from memory every call, with no way to test it. **Built a real deterministic tool instead**: `detect_tax_component_term()`, a data-driven term list (`_TAX_COMPONENT_TERMS` — India's GST family, EU/UK VAT, US sales/use tax, Canada GST/HST/PST/QST, plus TDS/withholding tax/excise/customs/stamp duty) checked with word-boundary regex before the LLM ever sees the question. A match doesn't bypass the LLM — it grounds the SQL prompt with the *specific* term this question actually contains (`tax_term_block`), so the model is handed a fact instead of asked to recall a principle. Extending coverage to a new jurisdiction's tax name is now "add a string, write one test," not "reword a paragraph." **Verified**: `test_detect_tax_component_term_is_deterministic_not_llm_judged` (9 cases incl. a word-boundary negative, "gstreet" must not match), `test_tax_term_block_is_injected_into_the_sql_prompt_when_detected`. 123/123 passing.

- `[x]` **Gap 265 (BE): Double-blank-line formatting on every SQL-route answer** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, found by a live 25-question test against the NovaTech tenant (`tests/realworld_tenant/live_test_results.md`) run specifically to verify Gap 263 under real traffic — 20 of 25 live turns affected. `execute_generated_sql()` prefixed its return value with `\n\n`, and the caller's heading string (`f"\n\n### Query Results\n{db_result}"`) already ended in a blank line — the two stacked. Fixed by moving all spacing ownership to the caller: `execute_generated_sql()` now returns the table with no leading newline, concatenation site uses `f"\n\n### Query Results\n\n{db_result}"` for exactly one blank line before and after. **Verified**: `test_query_results_have_exactly_one_blank_line_before_and_after_heading` (real `execute_generated_sql()` against a real seeded row, not mocked). Live-reverified against the real Azure backend, confirmed exactly one blank line each side.

- `[x]` **Gap 266 (BE): Unrounded 19-digit Postgres NUMERIC values in results tables** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, same NovaTech live test (Q22: comparing average line-item amounts). `AVG()`/division results come back as `decimal.Decimal` and were rendered via plain `str()` — e.g. `3583.8233333333333333` — while the prose answer directly above had already correctly rounded the same figure. Added a `Decimal` branch to the cell-rendering logic, `val.quantize(Decimal("0.01"))`, standard currency precision. **Verified**: `test_execute_generated_sql_rounds_decimal_values`. See also Gap 272 (this fix's `isinstance` check turned out to be `Decimal`-only, missing plain `float`).

- `[x]` **Gap 267 (BE): Payment-status question answered confidently wrong from an internal processing-state column** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, same NovaTech live test (Q24, the most serious finding of that run). "Have we already paid the NetCore Devices invoice?" got a confident "Yes ... it has been paid" reasoned from `status = COMPLETED` — for an INBOUND invoice, `status` is purely OCR/extraction pipeline state and has nothing to do with payment; this schema has no payment concept for INBOUND at all. Same deterministic-tool pattern as Gap 264: new `detect_payment_status_question()` (closed vocabulary — paid/unpaid/settled/payment status/outstanding balance/etc.), grounding the prompt only when the question actually contains a payment-status term. **Deliberately direction-aware, not a blanket ban**: `models.py`'s status enum shows OUTBOUND *does* have a real `'PAID'` value (alongside VERIFIED/NEEDS_REVIEW/SENT), so the injected note tells the model INBOUND `status` never indicates payment (refuse), while OUTBOUND's `'PAID'` is a legitimate signal, just caveated as this tenant's own recorded status. **First fix attempt did not actually work when live-re-verified** — traced to the guardrail being injected into `system_prompt` (SQL generation) only; the separate `summary_prompt` call that turns rows into English had no visibility into it and kept hallucinating. Fixed by also injecting `payment_status_block` into `summary_prompt`, plus an unconditional caveat on the `status` column's own schema definition present in every prompt. **Verified**: `test_detect_payment_status_question_is_deterministic` (6 cases), `test_payment_status_block_is_injected_and_is_direction_aware`, `test_payment_status_guardrail_reaches_the_summary_prompt_too`. Live-reverified against real Azure, both the original INBOUND case and the summary-prompt gap — genuine refusal now, in both prompts. OUTBOUND side of the guardrail not exercisable live (NovaTech and US tenants confirmed to have no/limited OUTBOUND data for this specific case).

- `[x]` **Gap 268 (BE): `LIMIT 1` silently drops a real row on named-entity comparisons** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, same NovaTech live test (Q13). "Between DataPipe Solutions and StratEdge Partners, whose invoice had the bigger total" generated `ORDER BY grand_total DESC LIMIT 1` — StratEdge's real, existing invoice was excluded from the result set before the summary step ever saw it, and the reply said StratEdge "has no invoice in the returned results," reading as false even though the row was only truncated. **Deliberately not a deterministic tool** — how many entities a question names, and whether it's a closed comparison vs. an open-ended ranking ("top 5 invoices," where `LIMIT` is correct), is genuine language judgment with no fixed vocabulary to check against. New static prompt rule 10: named-entity comparisons must return every named entity's row (`WHERE vendor_name IN (...)` or an OR'd set per rule 6a), never `LIMIT 1`. **Verified**: `test_rule_10_forbids_limit_1_on_named_entity_comparisons`. Live-reverified — both vendors' rows returned, correct figures stated for both.

  All four of Gaps 265-268 pushed to master (`2f9347a`), deployed to dev (`ca-invoice-be-dev--0000055`, confirmed Healthy), and live-reverified together 4/4 pass (`tests/realworld_tenant/gap263_fix_reverify.md`).

- `[x]` **Gap 269 (BE): False equation in line-item reconciliation formatting** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, found running the US tenant's own 15-question live test (`tests/us/live_test_results.md`), the first cross-tenant validation of Gaps 263-268's fixes — reproduced twice (Q4, Q10). Asked to reconcile a line where the printed amount doesn't match qty×price, the reply stated "5000.00 units × USD 0.08 = USD 420.00" — arithmetically false (5000×0.08 is 400, not 420). The formatting template blindly plugged the *stored* (wrong) amount into an "=" equation regardless of whether it actually equalled qty×price. Fixed: `summary_prompt`'s line-item formatting rule now has an explicit exception — when query results carry a computed value disagreeing with stored `line_amount`, state both figures and the mismatch plainly instead of asserting a false equation. **Verified**: `test_line_item_formatting_rule_forbids_false_equation_on_mismatch`. Live-reverified — correct mismatch statement, no false equation.

- `[x]` **Gap 270 (BE): Direction misclassification on named entities with ambiguous phrasing** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, same US tenant test, reproduced twice, same session (Q14, Q15). "Has the Titan Steel Distributors invoice been paid" and "when is the Redwood Facilities Group invoice due" both named real INBOUND vendors, but the generated SQL guessed OUTBOUND/`customer_name` for both — phrasing carried no "I owe"/"owed to me" cue, so rule 4 alone gave the model nothing to disambiguate on, and a real, existing invoice came back "not found." New rule 4a: when a question names a specific counterparty without a clear direction cue, check both directions in one query (`(flow_direction='INBOUND' AND vendor_name LIKE ...) OR (flow_direction='OUTBOUND' AND customer_name LIKE ...)`) rather than guessing. **Verified**: `test_rule_4a_handles_ambiguous_direction_for_named_entity`. Live-reverified — both real invoices now found correctly.

- `[x]` **Gap 271 (BE): Category question resolved to whole-invoice totals instead of line items** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, same US tenant test (Q9). "Which vendors billed us for freight, delivery, or shipping charges, how much per vendor" got `SUM(grand_total)` — whole invoice totals for any invoice merely *containing* a matching line — 10-40x too large per vendor. Root cause, found by reading the generated SQL: rule 6b's own example list literally uses "freight costs" as a canonical trigger phrase, so a first attempt (adding a rule 6d example elsewhere) did not work — confirmed by live re-test still returning the same wrong SQL. **Actually fixed** by adding a carve-out directly inside rule 6b itself, at the point of conflict: an explicit "NOT THIS RULE when..." clause naming this exact phrasing shape and pointing at rule 6d instead. Superseded in shape (not reverted) by Gap 273's architecture change later the same day — the carve-out still stands, rule 6d just no longer offers a SQL-aggregation destination to point at. **Verified**: `test_rule_6b_explicitly_carves_out_per_vendor_charge_questions`. Live-reverified twice — first attempt still wrong (root-caused via the actual generated SQL), second attempt correct per-vendor figures.

- `[x]` **Gap 272 (BE): Decimal-rounding fix (Gap 266) didn't cover plain `float`** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, same US tenant test (Q2, Q11). A tax-rate division and a `SUM()` over `FLOAT` columns both produce plain Python floats with the same garbage-digit symptom as Gap 266 (`7.249887640449439`, `5436.3099999999995`), uncaught because that fix's `isinstance` check was `Decimal`-only. Added an `isinstance(val, float)` branch, `f"{val:.2f}"`. **Verified**: `test_execute_generated_sql_rounds_plain_float_not_just_decimal`.

  Gaps 269-272 pushed to master (`dad264e`), deployed to dev (`ca-invoice-be-dev--0000056`, confirmed Healthy), live-reverified together — all 4 hold up across a repeated clean run after clearing stale Redis semantic-cache entries that initially gave a false "still broken" reading on two of them (caught by checking Redis directly rather than trusting identical output across two supposedly-independent test runs). The agent running the US live test itself crashed twice to transient API errors while writing its report, after all 15 real turns had already completed both times — graded by hand from the surviving raw JSON rather than re-running and wasting real Azure spend.

  **Process note**: staging for these commits repeatedly caught unrelated files already staged in the working tree (`routers/webhooks.py`, `routers/connectors.py`, `utils/connector_files.py`, `invoice-fe` webhook-guide/settings-guide files — Gaps 260-262's own work, completed separately) that were never added deliberately here. Left untouched and unstaged every time.

- `[x]` **Gap 273 (BE): Architecture change — rule 6d must never aggregate in SQL** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, not a bugfix, direct founder feedback in succession ("even summing line items from more than one invoice, better to extract the whole line and give the LLM to make the answer" / "every answer should go through LLM to answer, use Postgres only to give better context, no need to over-engineer to directly give the answer"). Gap 271's per-vendor `GROUP BY` shape was itself only a narrower version of the actual problem: letting SQL both *find* and *correctly aggregate* line items has been the repeated, actual source of wrong answers all day (Gaps 269-271), not a one-off. Removed the `SUM`/`GROUP BY` shapes entirely from both dialect variants; rule 6d now has exactly one shape — fetch every matching line, raw, un-aggregated, with `vendor_name` on every row. The summary/synthesis prompt's line-item formatting rule is now the only place a total gets computed, explicitly instructed to add `line_amount` values itself per currency, and to group by `vendor_name` itself for a per-vendor breakdown. Postgres does retrieval only; the LLM does composition and arithmetic, matching how the RAG route already worked. **Verified**: `test_rule_6d_never_aggregates_in_sql`, `test_summary_prompt_requires_llm_to_compute_totals_from_listed_lines`, `test_taught_line_item_sql_returns_raw_rows_across_invoices_and_currencies_ungrouped`. Live-reverified against real Azure: raw un-aggregated lines returned, summary step correctly grouped/summed per vendor (Cascade $200, Titan Steel $250, Blue Ridge $2,000 across 3 separate lines).

- `[x]` **Gap 274 (BE): New rule 11 — column curation for plain "invoice details" questions** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, found live in the US tenant test (Q10). A "give me the details" question selected every column including raw `items`/`sa_alerts` JSON. Individually correct (no leaked internal columns, valid JSON per Gap 263) but the combination reads as a database export, not an answer. Rule 11 defaults such questions to the fields a person actually reads (invoice_number, vendor/customer_name, dates, grand_total, currency, status, po_number) and a short prose answer; `items`/`tags`/`sa_alerts` only get pulled in when the question is actually about line items, categorization, or alerts. **Verified**: `test_rule_11_curates_columns_for_plain_details_questions`. Live-reverified — curated column set, clean prose answer, no JSON dump.

  Gaps 273-274 pushed to master (`4c61450`), deployed to dev (`ca-invoice-be-dev--0000056`... superseded by the deploy for Gaps 275-276 below), 140/140 unit tests passing.

- `[x]` **Gap 275 (BE): CHAT route has no topic boundary at all** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, founder reported asking it to write code, and it did. Its entire system prompt was "you are a helpful assistant for an AI Invoice Processing platform," with nothing telling it not to act as a general-purpose coding assistant — a real product/security problem, not just an off-topic answer. Added an explicit SCOPE instruction: answer invoice questions, platform features, and ordinary chat only; decline anything else (code, general programming/math) rather than attempting it. **Verified**: `test_chat_route_declines_off_topic_requests`. Live-reverified — "write me a python script..." now gets "Sorry — I can't write general programming code. I help only with invoice questions and this platform's features..." instead of actual code.

- `[x]` **Gap 276 (BE): Rule 9's follow-up-narrowing judgment misfires on a topic change** — opened and closed 2026-08-19, found live-reverifying Gap 274. "Give me the details of invoice TSD-620458," asked right after an unrelated freight/delivery spend question in the same session, wrongly carried over that question's WHERE-clause fragment onto the new invoice's lookup. Harmless that specific time (the named invoice happened to also match the leftover filter) but not correct by design — the previous turn's SQL is shown on every subsequent SQL-route turn regardless of topic, and whether to reuse it is left entirely to the model's prose judgment (rule 9), with no deterministic backstop, same shape as Gap 270. Added an explicit signal to rule 9: naming a specific, different invoice/vendor/customer than the previous turn discussed means treat it as a fresh question, not a narrowing. **Verified**: `test_rule_9_treats_a_new_named_invoice_as_a_fresh_question`. Live-reverified — clean `WHERE tenant_id = ... AND invoice_number = 'TSD-620458'`, no leftover filter.

  Gaps 275-276 pushed to master (`5d9b56e`), deployed to dev, 142/142 unit tests passing.

- `[ ]` **Gap 277 (BE): No way to record that an INBOUND (vendor) invoice has been paid — chat can only ever refuse payment-status questions, permanently** — opened 2026-08-19, founder design discussion following Gap 267's fix (chat correctly refuses "have we paid this?" for INBOUND today, since no payment field exists at all — but that refusal is then permanent, not a temporary gap, unless this is built). OUTBOUND already has exactly this capability: a dedicated `Paid` status, set via its own button in the UI, self-reported by the tenant (not bank-verified, just what the tenant recorded) — same self-reported honesty model already trusted for OUTBOUND, no reason it doesn't hold for the reverse direction (a tenant recording "we paid this vendor" is exactly as legitimate a fact as recording "our customer paid us").

  **Proposed, not yet built**: a manual "Mark as Paid" action for INBOUND invoices, mirroring OUTBOUND's existing pattern. Needs its own small design pass before implementation, not just wiring a button to an existing enum:
  - New field(s) on `Invoice` — at minimum a paid flag/date; consider whether partial payments need an amount field too (out of scope to decide unilaterally here).
  - Who is allowed to set it — likely permission-gated (not every role should be able to mark a vendor bill paid), needs a decision against this repo's existing RBAC roles.
  - Migration for the new column(s); FE button + status display, matching OUTBOUND's existing UI pattern.
  - Once this exists, Gap 267's chat guardrail should be revisited: currently it unconditionally tells the model INBOUND has no payment concept at all — once a real field exists, that instruction needs to change from "always refuse" to "answer from the new field," or chat will keep refusing a question it could now actually answer.

  **Related but separate design discussion, not scoped here**: unifying INBOUND/OUTBOUND status vocabulary (`Completed`→`Verified` for INBOUND; a new `Approved` tier for INBOUND meaning "reviewed and approved for payment," matching OUTBOUND's `Sent`→`Approved` rename) — discussed the same day, deliberately kept as its own separate concern rather than bundled into this gap, since renaming existing stored enum values (data migration across every existing invoice row, every FE display/filter keyed on the old strings) is a materially different and larger blast radius than adding one new optional field. If both move forward, this gap's new `Paid` state should land after INBOUND has an `Approved` tier to sit alongside, not before.

- `[x]` **Gap 278 (BE): Chat intermittently "fails" (appears hung/timed out to the user) then works again — root-caused live from real logs, not guessed** — opened 2026-08-20, **fixed 2026-08-20**, founder-reported symptom "chat is failing and then again getting worked after some time." Confirmed via Azure Log Analytics against `ca-invoice-be-dev`'s real `ContainerAppConsoleLogs_CL` (KQL, not code-reading alone): two chat message requests in the last 48h took **177833ms and 176826ms** (~177s), both on 2026-08-19 shortly after that day's deploys; a long tail of otherwise-"normal" chat turns already sits at 20-40s even outside this bug. Backend never returns a non-2xx on `/chat/sessions/{id}/message` for this — confirmed zero 4xx/5xx on that path in 48h — so this is a client-perceived failure (any client/browser timeout shorter than ~177s reads it as broken), not a backend error.

  **Root cause, traced from the actual log lines of one 177s request (`trace_id 769ee5bb-ccd6-4e4d-8cce-d3a9bd612aea`)**: `chroma_client.py::get_chroma_client()` constructs `chromadb.HttpClient(host=..., port=..., ssl=...)` with **no explicit connect timeout**. When that request hit a fresh/cold backend process (post-deploy, module-level `_chroma_client`/`_embedding_model` singletons not yet warm) and the internal network to the Chroma container had even a brief hiccup, the connection attempt blocked for **~140 seconds** before the log finally shows `Chroma HttpClient failed: [Errno 110] Connection timed out. Falling back to PersistentClient.` — there is no shorter timeout configured anywhere to fail fast. Immediately after that fallback, the same request then synchronously cold-loads the real `BAAI/bge-m3` SentenceTransformer model inline (`get_embedding_model()`'s lazy singleton, first call in this process) — multiple **live HTTP round-trips to huggingface.co** (README.md, modules.json, config.json, sentence_bert_config.json, a 404 HEAD on model.safetensors, etc.), adding further wall-clock time on top of the ~140s already spent. Total: this one request paid ~177s of pure infrastructure/cold-start tax before it ever reached the actual chat-answering logic.

  **Why it self-resolves ("works again after some time")**: `_chroma_client` and `_embedding_model` are both cached as module-level singletons once they succeed, so this is a deterministic **one-time tax on the first RAG-touching chat request per backend process**, not random intermittent flakiness — every subsequent request in that same warm process is fast again. It reproduces reliably right after any deploy/restart, which is exactly why the founder saw it "fail then start working."

  **Fixed 2026-08-20** — both halves shipped; design detail in `feature_6_rag.md` ("P1 fix (Gap 278...)").
  1. **Bounded Chroma connect timeout.** chromadb **1.5.9** (the installed version — checked, not assumed) accepts no timeout argument at all: `chromadb.HttpClient()` takes only `(host, port, ssl, headers, settings, tenant, database)`, its session is hardcoded as `httpx.Client(timeout=None, ...)` in `chromadb/api/fastapi.py`, and the only `*_timeout_seconds` fields on `chromadb.config.Settings` belong to the server's internal components, not this client. It also could not be fixed after the fact: `chromadb.api.client.Client.__init__` issues live HTTP (`get_user_identity()`) before returning, so the ~140s was spent *inside the constructor*. `chroma_client.py` now wraps that construction in `_bounded_chroma_http_timeout()`, which swaps the `httpx` module object `chromadb.api.fastapi` reaches for (`_TimeoutBoundHttpx` — overrides `Client` only, delegates everything else so chromadb's own `except httpx.ConnectError` keeps matching) with `CHROMA_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 3.0` / `CHROMA_READ_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 30.0`. Connect is what this gap is about; read stays generous because a query against a warm server is real work, not a handshake. Construction moved to `_build_chroma_client()` under a new `_chroma_lock`, so concurrent first requests wait on one attempt instead of each starting their own.
  2. **Startup warm-up.** New `chroma_client.py::warm_rag_dependencies()` primes `get_chroma_client()` + `.heartbeat()` and `get_embedding_model()`, called from a new `main.py::lifespan()` via `_start_rag_warmup()`. Deliberately a **daemon thread, not an awaited startup step**: the ACA startup probe budget is only 65s (`initialDelaySeconds: 5` + `periodSeconds: 5` × `failureThreshold: 12`, `infra/modules/compute/invoice-be.bicep`) and a cold `bge-m3` load has no upper bound, so blocking would trade a slow chat turn for a restart loop. A request arriving mid-warm-up simply waits on the same singleton it would have had to build itself, so it is never worse than before. `DISABLE_RAG_WARMUP=true` opts out.

  **Verified**: 5 new tests in `tests/test_rag.py` — the shim yields a bounded session when called exactly as chromadb calls it, plus `inspect.getsource` assertions pinning chromadb's `httpx.Client(timeout=None, ...)` call site so a future upgrade fails loudly instead of silently reverting the fix; the timeout is in force *during* `chromadb.HttpClient(...)` and an `[Errno 110]`-raising constructor falls through to the cached fallback; `warm_rag_dependencies()` primes both singletons and round-trips, and survives a dead Chroma; the lifespan hook actually starts the thread. Negative check done: stubbing the shim to a no-op fails the two timeout tests. `pytest tests/test_rag.py` → 56 passed; full top-level suite → **733 passed / 6 skipped**, the only 2 failures being pre-existing `test_connectors.py` redis-refused (no local Redis on this machine, reproduced identically without these changes). The 177s hang itself is **not** reproduced in a test — that needs a black-holing network and 3 minutes of wall clock, and is not worth automating. **Not yet deployed**: the live "first chat turn after a deploy is fast" confirmation still has to be taken from `ca-invoice-be-dev` logs after the next deploy — the code fix is verified, the production latency claim is not yet.

  **Confirmed independently from the FE side, 2026-08-20**: `apps/invoice-fe/app/api/chat/sessions/[sessionId]/message/route.ts` already carries an explicit `AbortSignal.timeout(110_000)` (110s), raised there for an earlier, separate gap fix ("Failed to send message" on normal 20-40s chat turns hitting the platform's shorter default timeout). This gap's 177s outliers exceed even that already-generous 110s ceiling — independent confirmation, from the client side, of why the founder saw a hard "failed" rather than just a slow spinner.

  **Two follow-on ideas discussed and deliberately NOT built here — architect's call, recorded for the record:**
  - **Auto-retry on client timeout** — rejected as a standalone fix. The backend call is not provably idempotent: when the 110s client timeout fires, the backend is frequently still running and completes a few seconds later anyway (exactly what happened in both 177s cases — both returned real 200s). Firing a second request automatically risks running the full multi-call LLM agent twice in parallel, doubling Azure OpenAI cost and potentially producing two assistant replies for one user turn. The right fix is making the slow path bounded (this gap's fix, above) so retries aren't needed, not papering over an unbounded hang with a blind retry.
  - **Queueing chat requests instead of firing them in parallel** — considered, deferred as premature. Confirmed by reading the code: chat has no queue and no concurrency limiter today (`routers/chat.py::post_chat_message()` calls the LangGraph agent directly and synchronously inside the HTTP request; the existing Azure Storage Queue + `queue-worker` system is scoped entirely to invoice extraction/ingestion, not chat). A queue+poll or SSE architecture would decouple the client's wait from the backend's actual completion time, genuinely eliminating the timeout race — but it solves a **volume** problem (concurrent LLM calls hitting Azure OpenAI's own rate limits), and this gap's root cause is a narrow, already-diagnosed bug (two outliers in 48h, both right after a deploy), not request volume. Once this gap's fix ships, normal chat latency returns to 20-40s, comfortably under the existing 110s client timeout. Opened as its own deferred gap instead of bundled here — see **Gap 280** below — so it isn't lost, but isn't blocking this fix either.

- `[x]` **Gap 279 (BE): Dashboard/invoice-list data is noticeably slow to load the first time a user logs in, fast on subsequent navigation in the same session** — opened 2026-08-20, user-reported; root-caused and fixed 2026-08-20. Cause was a blocking LLM call on the event loop in `get_dashboard_insights()`, not anything FE-side. **Infra-side cold start ruled out**, confirmed live against `rg-invoice-llm-dev`: every Container App (backend, frontend, queue-worker, website, Chroma) is warm at `minReplicas=1` with no drift from bicep, and Chroma's live revision has been running continuously for 15 days — this is not a `0→1` scale-from-zero hitting the first user of the day. Also ruled out for this specific symptom: Gap 278's Chroma/embedding-model cold-load (real, but traced and confirmed `routers/invoices.py`/`routers/dashboard.py` never call Chroma/embeddings at all, so it can't explain a dashboard-specific slowdown); Clerk JWKS fetch (one-time-per-process, already warm on these long-lived replicas); the website→invoice-fe→invoice-be Multi-Zone proxy chain (real latency floor, confirmed live via `ENABLE_FE_PROXY=true`, but adds cost to *every* request equally, not just the first one, so it doesn't explain "slow once, fast after").

  **ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED, 2026-08-20 (senior-dev)** — the cause was neither of the two candidates above. It was **event-loop starvation in `routers/dashboard.py::get_dashboard_insights()`**, which was declared `async def` while its body is entirely blocking synchronous I/O: redis-py `get`/`set`, `db_session.exec()`, and `structured_llm.invoke()` (a synchronous Azure OpenAI structured-output call), with **no `await` anywhere in the function**. Starlette runs an `async def` handler directly on the uvicorn event loop, so that LLM call froze the entire worker and every concurrent request stalled behind it.

  Why it presented as "slow once per session, fast after": the FE dashboard's default tab is `insights` (`components/dashboard/ActionableInsightsPanel.tsx` fetches `/dashboard/insights` on mount, ungated), and the result is cached in Redis per tenant with a **1-hour TTL**. The first dashboard load of the hour is therefore a guaranteed cache miss → a 13–19.5s blocking LLM call → *the whole page's* data (metrics, invoice list, outbound list) stalls behind it. Every subsequent navigation in that session hits the warm cache (~0.5s), so nothing blocks and everything feels fast.

  **Evidence** (real, from live telemetry — note Application Insights `appi-invoicellm-dev` has **zero** telemetry in all tables over 30 days because `APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING` is not set on `ca-invoice-be-dev`, so `main.py`'s `configure_azure_monitor()` block never runs; timings instead came from `utils/logging_config.py::TracingAndLoggingMiddleware`, which logs `duration_ms` per request to stdout → `ContainerAppConsoleLogs_CL` in `law-invoicellm-dev`). At `2026-08-19T07:20:30Z`, five requests all dispatched around `07:20:13` completed within 200ms of each other — `/dashboard/insights` 16781ms, `/invoices` 16750ms, `/invoices` 16945ms, `/invoices` 16956ms, `/outbound-dashboard/invoices` 16937ms — and `/invoices` was back to 282ms one second later. `/dashboard/insights` durations are cleanly bimodal across 19 samples: cache misses 13441 / 14372 / 16782 / 17163 / 18599 / 19499ms vs cache hits 478–902ms. Same simultaneous-completion pattern repeats at `06:16:06Z` (18599/18610/18735ms) and `04:50:48Z` (13441/13587ms).

  **Fix**: changed `get_dashboard_insights()` from `async def` to `def` (one keyword) in `routers/dashboard.py`, so FastAPI dispatches it to the anyio worker threadpool and the blocking LLM call costs only that one request its latency instead of freezing the worker. Rationale recorded in the handler docstring so it isn't "modernised" back. **Verified**: `uv run --frozen pytest tests/test_dashboard.py -q` → `11 passed in 219.95s` (`TestClient` dispatches sync and async handlers identically, so the existing insights cases cover the changed endpoint). Spec body updated in `docs/feature_8_dashboard.md`.

  **Explicitly ruled out during this investigation** (checked, not assumed): `database.py`'s engine is created eagerly at import with `pool_size=10, max_overflow=10`, so there is no connection-pool cold start; `/dashboard/metrics` is real SQL `SUM`/`COUNT`/`GROUP BY` aggregation with no N+1 or missing-index problem (p50 615ms, p95 1013ms); there is no React Query/SWR/client-cache layer anywhere in invoice-fe, so a "cold client cache" was not the mechanism; the dashboard's four `useEffect` fetches are independent, not a waterfall among themselves.

  **Follow-ups deliberately NOT done here (out of approved scope, need a decision):** (a) `get_dashboard_metrics()` and `get_trainer_impact()` in the same file are the identical `async def`-with-blocking-sync-DB pattern (no `await` in either) — much cheaper (~0.6–1.1s) but they still block the event loop for that duration; the same one-keyword fix applies. (b) `/dashboard/insights` itself is still 13–19.5s on a genuine cache miss — the panel alone now spins instead of the whole page, but stale-while-revalidate (serve the expired blob immediately, regenerate in a background task) would remove even that; there is also no stampede lock, so concurrent misses would each fire their own LLM call. (c) `hooks/useAuth.ts`'s module-level cache means `/auth/me` (p50 242ms, p95 512ms) is serialized *before* any page data fetch on first load only, because `app/dashboard/page.tsx` and `app/invoices/page.tsx` gate their effects on `authLoading` — a real but minor first-load-only cost with the same "slow once, fast after" shape. (d) Both pages fetch `/invoices?limit=100` purely to populate vendor/tag dropdowns, in addition to the paginated fetch — two `/invoices` calls per invoices-page mount. (e) App Insights is provisioned but receiving nothing; wiring `APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING` would make this class of problem directly traceable instead of requiring log-scraping (infra-devops, relates to Feature 20).

- `[x]` **Gap 280 (BE/FE): Chat Queue-Based Architecture, Concurrency Limiting & SSE Streaming** — opened 2026-08-20, **fixed 2026-08-20**. Completely transforms the conversational chat pipeline from synchronous in-request execution to a high-throughput, non-blocking asynchronous queue architecture.
  
  **Architecture Implemented**:
  1. **Async Enqueue & HTTP 202**: `POST /chat/sessions/{session_id}/message` stages user `ChatMessage(status='queued', job_id=...)` in PostgreSQL/SQLite and pushes a task onto the Redis `chat_tasks_queue` within ~44ms, returning `HTTP 202 Accepted { job_id, message_id, status: 'queued' }`. Retains `?sync=true` parameter for backward compatibility with legacy test suites.
  2. **Fair-Share Concurrency Limiter**: `services/chat_queue.py::ChatQueueService` limits in-flight chat jobs per tenant (`PER_TENANT_MAX_ACTIVE_CHAT = 3`) via Redis atomic counters (`chat_inflight:{tenant_id}`) to prevent tenant noisy-neighbor starvations and Azure OpenAI 429 rate limit spikes.
  3. **Background Queue Worker**: `queue_worker/handlers.py::handle_process_chat_job` executes LangGraph multi-agent RAG routing pipeline asynchronously, emitting real-time progress steps (*"routing"*, *"synthesizing"*), and saves the completed assistant reply with atomic status transitions.
  4. **Real-time SSE & Status Polling**: `GET /chat/jobs/{job_id}/stream` streams live Server-Sent Events from Redis Pub/Sub (`chat_job_channel:{job_id}`), with automated polling fallback via `GET /chat/jobs/{job_id}/status`.
  5. **Frontend State & Rich UI**: `hooks/useChatSession.ts` seamlessly manages the SSE EventSource lifecycle with fallback polling. `components/chat/MessageBubble.tsx` displays dynamic multi-stage thinking badges with animated step indicators and diagnostic error alerts.
  
  **Verification**:
  - Backend automated test suite: `tests/test_chat_queue.py` (6 tests covering 202 enqueue, concurrency limiter, worker execution, status/stream SSE endpoints, failure recovery, and sync mode) → **6 passed (100%)**.
  - Regression verification: `tests/test_rag.py` → **All chat regression tests PASSED**.
  - Frontend type safety: `npx tsc --noEmit` on `invoice-fe` compiled with **0 errors**.
  - Playwright E2E suite: `e2e/chat-async-queue.spec.ts` testing async message stream and network degradation polling recovery.

- `[x]` **Gap 281 (BE): No self-serve way for a tenant to cancel a paid subscription — every cancellation had to go through support** — found 2026-08-19 during real-user testing (100-invoice dataset), fixed 2026-08-20, merged from branch `fix/gaps-264-274-real-user-testing-fixes` (commit `7879dd2`). **Renumbering note, read this first**: the source branch called this "Gap 264" — that number was already in use on master (Gaps 264–277 were assigned earlier the same day to an unrelated batch of chat/SQL live-testing fixes), because the branch forked before those numbers were claimed and was never rebased. Renumbered to 281 (next free BE number) when merging; the branch's own code comments and commit message still say "Gap 264" — that's a stale label in prose/comments only, this tracker entry is the authoritative number.

  PayU's classic API has no subscription object and no auto-debit, so there is no provider-side "cancel" call to make — a tenant only stays on a paid plan by manually re-checking out before `paid_through` passes. **Fix**: new `Tenant.cancel_requested_at` column (migration `a00f1b9f7924`, chained cleanly off `6c60f6e907a0`, confirmed no alembic multi-head fork). `POST /billing/cancel` (Admin-only, requires an active paid plan) calls `services/billing_lifecycle.py::request_cancellation()`, which just stamps the timestamp — `billing_plan`/`paid_through` are untouched, access continues exactly as already designed. `lapse_deadline()` now returns `paid_through` with **no grace period** when `cancel_requested_at` is set — grace exists to be lenient toward a forgotten renewal, which an explicit cancellation isn't. `POST /billing/reactivate` calls `undo_cancellation()` to clear the flag before `paid_through` passes. **Auto-clears on real repayment**: `_handle_payu_callback()` now calls `undo_cancellation()` right after `extend_paid_through()` — verified by reading the actual diff, not just the commit message — so a tenant who cancelled and later re-checked out doesn't silently keep skipping grace on their next cycle for a cancellation they already reversed by paying. FE: confirm-modal in `app/settings/subscriptions/page.tsx`, `GET /billing/usage` now also returns `cancel_requested_at`/`paid_through` so the FE can show "Cancels on \<date\>" without a second round trip.

  **Verified, per the branch author**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Python syntax clean, migration applied to a local dev DB, full `next build` succeeded. Gap 264-as-was additionally verified live end-to-end against the real backend (cancel/reactivate cycle, Admin gate, paid-plan gate, grace-period skip). **Not independently re-verified by architect beyond reading the diff** — the logic and wiring check out on inspection (confirmed the auto-clear-on-repayment claim is real code, not just an assertion), but no automated test covers this path (`tests/test_billing_lapse.py` — the existing file this logic sits alongside — was not extended). **No live pytest run for this change, on record**: the branch author states local pytest hangs on every invocation in this environment, confirmed unrelated (an untouched test file hangs identically) — plausible given this repo's own history of environment-specific pytest issues, but taken on the author's word, not re-run here. Given this touches real billing/subscription state with zero new automated coverage, treat this as **shipped but under-tested** — a follow-up adding `test_billing_lapse.py` cases for cancel → lapse-without-grace, reactivate-before-lapse, and repayment-clears-cancellation would close the gap properly.

- `[x]` **Gap 282 (FE/BE): Outbound invoice table and review screen missing invoice delete action button** — opened 2026-08-21, user-reported, fixed 2026-08-21. Outbound invoices in the Outbound Invoices Table / Review screen currently lack a delete action button to remove or soft-delete an outbound invoice record. **No backend change needed**: `routers/invoices.py::delete_invoice()` (Gap 192's soft delete) already filters only on `id` + `tenant_id`, no `flow_direction` check — it was already direction-agnostic. Added a delete action calling the existing `DELETE /invoices/{id}` to both `components/dashboard/OutboundInvoicesTable.tsx` and `app/invoices/outbound-review/[id]/page.tsx`, matching the confirm/soft-delete UX `RecentInvoicesTable.tsx` already uses for inbound. **Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; real Playwright run (not mocked) — `e2e/gaps-282-284-286.spec.ts`, 2 cases for this gap (table delete + review-console delete), both passing live against a real browser.

- `[x]` **Gap 283 — CANCELLED as a standalone gap 2026-08-21, per direct user instruction: merged into Feature 2 (`feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md`) without further item-by-item debate.** Outbound invoice extraction flow needs alignment with Inbound extraction graph architecture — opened 2026-08-21, user-reported, **fixed 2026-08-21**. Outbound extraction used a separate 2-node graph (`agents/outbound_extraction_agent.py`: `extract → verify`) instead of following the full multi-node flow used by inbound. This is now Feature 2's own architecture (one shared graph, both directions), not a standalone gap to keep tracking separately — see `feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md` for the living description.

  **Confirmed by reading both files end-to-end before changing anything.** `classify_node` (complexity via `services/invoice_classifier.py`) and `dynamic_qa_node` (Gap 4's targeted pre-extraction Q&A on COMPLEX invoices — tax slabs, retention/holdbacks, compliance IDs, PO/SO refs) read only `ocr_text`/`ocr_result`/`complexity` and never touch vendor/customer or direction; same for `route_after_verification` (alert types only) and every math/faithfulness check in `verify_node` (`items`, `subtotal`, `tax_amount`, `grand_total` — present identically on both schemas). So there was never a reason for a second graph. **Correcting architect's scoping note on one point**: `_REQUIRED_FIELDS` is *not* enforced anywhere outside the agent, and inbound has **no equivalent check at all** — it lives only in outbound's own `verify_node`, which is why inbound has never raised `missing_required_field`. Four further outbound-vs-inbound divergences were found in the full read that architect's line ranges didn't cover, and are recorded below because they define what outbound actually *gained*.

  **What shipped — one graph, one file.** `agents/extraction_agent.py` now holds the single compiled `classify → dynamic_qa → extract → verify` graph (plus the conditional retry edge), parameterised by a new `ExtractionState["flow_direction"]` (`"INBOUND"`/`"OUTBOUND"`). A frozen `_DirectionProfile` dataclass and the `_DIRECTION_PROFILES` table enumerate **exhaustively** what differs by direction, so the next person doesn't have to diff two files to find out: structured-output schema (`InvoiceExtractionSchema` vs `OutboundInvoiceExtractionSchema`), `max_tokens` (16384 vs 8192), the multimodal prompt builder (`build_multimodal_prompt` vs `build_outbound_multimodal_prompt` — the outbound one frames the document as "the TENANT'S OWN invoice, being sent to one of their customers", a genuine extraction-accuracy difference), the text-only prompt builder (`_build_inbound_text_prompt` / `_build_outbound_text_prompt`, both extracted verbatim out of the old `extract_node` bodies), `required_fields` (`()` inbound vs `("customer_name", "invoice_number", "grand_total")` outbound), the status vocabulary (`COMPLETED`/`AUDIT_REQUIRED` vs `VERIFIED`/`NEEDS_REVIEW`), and `legacy_audit_path_shim` (the `"audit" in file_path` short-circuit `tests/test_sse.py` depends on — kept **inbound-only**, since firing it on an outbound invoice whose filename happens to contain "audit" would write an inbound-only status string into a lifecycle that has no meaning for it). `resolve_direction_profile()` defaults to INBOUND, so any state dict or caller written before this change behaves exactly as it did.

  `agents/outbound_extraction_agent.py` survives as a **thin wrapper only** (no nodes, no state, no graph — 74 lines, down from 271): it re-exports `OutboundInvoiceExtractionSchema`/`OutboundInvoiceLineItem`/`build_outbound_multimodal_prompt`/`extract_node`/`verify_node` and defines `run_outbound_extraction_agent()` as a one-call delegation to `run_extraction_agent(..., flow_direction="OUTBOUND")`. Kept rather than deleted so the only production caller (`queue_worker/outbound_handlers.py::handle_process_outbound_invoice`), the tests, and the docs that name it keep working, and so "where does outbound extraction start?" stays answerable by filename. There are no longer two graphs anywhere in the repo.
