  **What outbound gained, beyond classify/dynamic_qa** (the divergences found in the full read): the bounded `verify → extract` retry loop with feedback injection (Gap 2/47) — outbound previously had no retry at all; `verify_unit_prices_in_source_text` (Gap 44), which outbound's `verify_node` had simply never called despite its schema carrying `unit_price`; the Doc Intelligence `tax_details_sum` backfill for a null `tax_amount`; `active_constraints` on `extraction_failed` alerts (Gap 70 rule attribution); and `discount_amount`/`discount_percent`/`round_off`/`taxes` passed through to `verify_totals_math`/`verify_tax_amount_in_source_text` (all resolve to `None` on the outbound schema, so no behaviour change there — noted only so the shared call sites aren't mistaken for a schema change).

  **Two intentional behaviour changes for outbound, called out rather than buried:** (1) the DI tax backfill can now populate a summed `tax_amount` that is not printed verbatim on the document, and since `OutboundInvoiceExtractionSchema` has no `taxes[]` list there is no component list to pass to `verify_tax_amount_in_source_text` — so a CGST+SGST-split outbound invoice can now raise `tax_amount_not_verified_in_source` where it previously just left `tax_amount` null and stayed quiet. This is inbound's exact existing behaviour when the LLM fails to populate `taxes[]`, applied to outbound; it surfaces a real faithfulness question rather than silently dropping tax. (2) `_build_outbound_text_prompt` deliberately does **not** ask for `taxes[]`/`discounts[]`/`compliance_metadata[]` the way the inbound COMPLEX prompt does — those fields don't exist on the outbound schema and asking for them would invite the model to invent somewhere to put them. **Deliberately out of scope**: enriching `OutboundInvoiceExtractionSchema` to inbound's full field set. That would cascade into `outbound_handlers.py` persistence, `routers/outbound_audit.py`'s correctable-field set and the FE, and it is not what this gap asked for. It is the natural follow-up if outbound tax detail turns out to matter (see Gap 285).

  **Verified**: `tests/test_extraction.py` + `tests/test_outbound_extraction.py` + `tests/test_verification_overrides.py` = **21 passed**, identical to the pre-change baseline captured before any edit. Wider run covering every suite that touches these modules (`test_outbound_ingestion`, `test_direction_aware_chat`, `test_rule_schema`, `test_sse`, `test_ingestion`, `test_invoice_reconciliation`, `test_outbound_audit`) = **88 passed**. Full unit suite (`tests/`, excluding `e2e`/`benchmark`/the regional live-chat harness dirs) = **733 passed, 6 skipped, 2 failed** — both failures are `tests/test_connectors.py` Salesforce OAuth tests dying on `redis.exceptions.ConnectionError: Error 10061 connecting to localhost:6379` with no local Redis running; environment, not code, and untouched by this change. **All mocked, no live verification**: every extraction test stubs `agents.extraction_agent.get_llm` and `check_token_guardrails`, so no real Azure OpenAI or Document Intelligence call was made in any of these runs — the direction-selected prompts and the new outbound classify/dynamic_qa path have **not** been exercised against a live model or a real outbound PDF. `tests/e2e/` and `tests/benchmark/` (which do call real Azure) were not run. Two test edits were required and are part of this change: `test_outbound_extraction.py`'s patch targets moved from `agents.outbound_extraction_agent.*` to `agents.extraction_agent.*` (the wrapper no longer owns those globals; its imports were left pointing at the wrapper on purpose, so the re-exports stay covered), and `test_verification_overrides.py::test_outbound_verify_node_gets_the_same_parameterization` now sets `flow_direction: "OUTBOUND"` in its state dict — without it that test would have silently graded the INBOUND profile.

  **Docs updated**: `feature_2.1_vendor_flow_ingestion.md` (the "parallel pipeline, not a shared one" design decision and the "v1 scope cut: no classify/dynamic_qa" note are both now superseded — recorded as reversed, not deleted; Task 2.1.2 rewritten, and its stale claim that `verify_node` "deliberately skips `verify_totals_math`/`verify_line_items_math`" corrected, since Gap 223 had already wired both in), `feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md` (Extraction Agent Internals now describes the 4-node direction-parameterised graph; its "3-node `classify → extract → verify`" line was already stale before this change — `dynamic_qa` landed with Task 2.33 and was never added to the prose), `feature_18_trainer_alert_anchored_training.md` (File Coordinates + alert-producer table), and `utils/alert_registry.py`'s `producer` strings for `extraction_failed`/`token_limit_exceeded`/`missing_required_field`, which all still pointed at the deleted outbound nodes.

  **Known residual, not fixed, per direct user instruction not to pursue item-by-item right now** — recorded plainly rather than hidden: an independent code-review pass after this closed found (1) the new `required_fields` check treats a legitimately-extracted `0`/`0.0` as missing rather than only `None`/absent, so a genuinely zero-total outbound invoice (e.g. a fully-discounted credit note) gets a spurious `missing_required_field` alert; (2) the CGST+SGST-summing behaviour noted above as intentional in point (1) of "Two intentional behaviour changes" has no `taxes[]` list to verify against on the outbound schema, so it will flag *every* real split-tax outbound invoice, not just an occasional edge case. Neither is fixed as of this entry. Left as known state, not re-opened as separate gap numbers.

- `[x]` **Gap 284 (FE/BE): Outbound ingestion status ledger displays unnecessary log boxes during file processing** — opened 2026-08-21, user-reported, fixed 2026-08-21. Two distinct real defects, not one: (1) **genuine duplication** — `app/ingestion/page.tsx` rendered a `LogTerminal`, and `components/ingestion/SendInvoiceStatusTable.tsx` (Gap 134) also embedded its own, so every outbound file opened two "Live Processing Log" boxes; (2) **the embedded one could never have worked regardless** — it passed the invoice's own id as `batchId`, but the backend's `stream_invoice_status` resolves that path segment as `Invoice.batch_id`, a different value, so Gap 186's ownership check 404'd and the box sat on "Waiting for processing to start..." forever. **Fix**: removed the broken embedded terminal from `SendInvoiceStatusTable.tsx` entirely; the surviving page-level terminal is passed the real `batch_id`. Also fixed a latent gap in `LogTerminal.tsx` itself: it only ever rendered `type: "log_line"` SSE events, which inbound's worker publishes via an `on_log` callback but outbound's worker (`queue_worker/outbound_handlers.py`) never does — outbound only publishes coarse `{status, message}` stage events on the same channel. New `includeStatusEvents` prop (default `false`, preserving inbound's behavior unchanged) lets the shared component also render those for outbound. **Verified**: real Playwright run — `e2e/gaps-282-284-286.spec.ts`, 1 case confirming exactly one log box appears and prints the outbound worker's real stage events, passing live. **Noted, not a defect**: outbound's log terminal content now partly echoes `SendInvoiceStatusTable`'s own status badge, since outbound only has 4 coarse stage events vs. inbound's granular per-step feed — a UX thinness worth knowing about, not a bug to fix.

- `[~]` **Gap 285 — CANCELLED as a standalone gap 2026-08-21, per direct user instruction.** SGST and CGST values shown separately in metadata are not recognized or distinguished in Chat queries — opened 2026-08-21, user-reported. Not being fixed as an isolated prompt patch (a narrow "teach chat about `taxes[]`" fix). Superseded by Feature 21's redesign: a generic full-record-fetch architecture (identify the impacted invoice(s), pull the complete Postgres row and document context — every field, not a curated subset — and hand it to a domain-expert persona) is meant to resolve this class of problem structurally, not this one field at a time. The doc that held that architecture (`feature_21_sage.md`) was deleted 2026-08-25 with the orchestrator; **the symptom itself was closed by Gap 310**, which put the full-record fetch on the default route instead. This gap stays as the origin record of the symptom, not an open work item to action separately.

- `[x]` **Gap 286 (FE): Inbound audit review screen extracted fields UI rendering overflow/misalignment** — opened 2026-08-21, user-reported, fixed 2026-08-21. Root cause: Gap 205's "Additional Extracted Metadata" panel (currency, discounts, tax IDs, `taxes` breakdown, `payment_instructions`, `compliance_metadata`, etc.) sat *outside* the fields column's scroll container, as a direct flex sibling alongside the header and the `flex-1` fields/line-items area. A flex item's default `min-height: auto` means it can't shrink below its own content height — on an invoice carrying real metadata (tax breakdown + tax IDs + payment instructions + compliance rows) it claimed the column's height for itself, squeezed the fields area toward zero, and then overflowed the section anyway, clipped silently by the parent grid's `xl:overflow-hidden`. **Fix**: moved the metadata panel inside the scroll container so it scrolls with the fields it belongs to, sized to whatever amount of metadata a given invoice actually has rather than one assumed schema shape; added `min-w-0 break-words`/`break-all` to the panel's label/value pairs so long opaque values (long tax IDs, long compliance strings) wrap instead of widening the column. **Verified**: real Playwright run — `e2e/gaps-282-284-286.spec.ts`, 3 cases (metadata panel reachable by scroll rather than clipped, correctable fields keep a usable share of the column and stay scrollable, long values wrap rather than widening), all passing live.

- `[x]` **Gap 287 (BE/AI): Post-Feature 21 prompt mandates and chunk reordering degraded chat response quality** — opened 2026-08-21, user-reported, independently confirming the same regression architect diagnosed the same day by reading real `ChatMessage` rows from the dev database: `_SQL_SUMMARY_FAITHFULNESS_MANDATE`'s "never add context/totals the results don't support" collided with the pre-existing tax-component rule (Gap 263's rule 6d), producing "cannot be determined from this result" instead of the correct, confident schema-limitation explanation. **Closed by reverting Feature 21 in full** (commit `5a7bf77`) rather than patching the mandate's wording, per explicit user direction — Feature 21 is being deleted and redesigned as one coherent tool-calling architecture instead of iterative prompt patches. See that revert's commit message and the new consolidated feature spec (once written) for the actual fix.

- `[x]` **Gap 288 (FE): Autopilot "Sync Now" could sync a stale folder, and its error message hid the real failure** — opened 2026-08-23, user-reported ("selected a google drive folder and clicked on sync gives... Sync failed. Please check your configuration."), fixed 2026-08-23. Two distinct defects, not one: (1) **Save Config / Sync Now decoupling** — `FolderTreeExplorer`'s `onFolderSelected` (`app/ingestion/page.tsx:913-918`) only ever updated local `autopilotConfig` React state; nothing called `PUT /autopilot/config`. Clicking "Sync Now" without first clicking "Save Config" hit `POST /autopilot/sync` against whatever `source_ref` was already persisted in `tenant_autopilot_configs`, not the folder just picked on screen — reproducible every time. **Fix**: `handleSyncNow` now calls the same save payload (new shared `saveAutopilotConfigPayload()`) before triggering sync, so Sync Now always acts on the folder currently shown in the UI; "Save Config" remains available as an explicit separate action. (2) **Generic error message hid the real failure** — the catch-all `"Sync failed. Please check your configuration."` (page.tsx:172) fired whenever the backend response carried no `detail` field, which is true both for the backend's own `ValueError`s (confirmed none of `services/autopilot_sync.py`'s raised messages match that text) and for network-level failures (proxy timeout, non-JSON body) that never reach FastAPI's exception handling at all — the two looked identical to the user. **Fix**: new `describeAutopilotError()` distinguishes a real HTTP error response (surfaces the status code) from no response at all (surfaces the underlying network error). **Investigated, not fixed — a separate diagnostic gap**: querying Application Insights `AppRequests`/`AppExceptions` and the container's own access-log middleware for `/autopilot/` over the prior 14 days returned zero rows on `ca-invoice-be-dev`, meaning the specific failed attempt the user saw never reached the deployed dev backend's ASGI stack at all — most likely a local-dev-environment or browser-network issue on that occasion, not something server-side telemetry alone can settle. If Sync Now still fails after this fix, the browser Network tab's actual status/response for `/autopilot/sync` is the next thing to check.

- `[!]` **Gap 289 (FE): Horizontally-scrolling tables show the OS's unstyled native scrollbar, reading as a broken/foreign UI element** — opened 2026-08-23, user-reported via screenshot on the Auditor Review Console (a stark white box with native left/right arrow buttons sitting between the Line Items table and the "Additional Extracted Metadata" panel, initially mistaken for an overlap/missing-content bug — see Gap 286's recheck the same day, which found no overlap). Root cause: `app/invoices/review/[id]/page.tsx:771`'s Line Items table wrapper (`overflow-x-auto`) — and every other `overflow-x-auto`/`overflow-y-auto` container app-wide — had zero custom scrollbar styling, so browsers fall back to default OS chrome, jarring against the dark theme. **Fix**: added app-wide themed scrollbar rules to `styles/globals.css` (`scrollbar-width: thin` + `scrollbar-color` for Firefox, `::-webkit-scrollbar`/`-thumb`/`-track` for Chromium/Edge/Safari) using the existing `--border-default`/`--text-muted` theme tokens, applied via a bare `*` selector so no individual scroll container needs to opt in.

  **Reopened 2026-08-24** — user-reported via screenshot: a dark rounded panel near a "Subtotal" label still shows a native-looking scrollbar with visible arrow buttons. Not yet diagnosed which page/component this is (screenshot doesn't identify the surface) or why the app-wide `*` selector fix from the original close isn't covering it — possible causes not yet checked: a component overriding the global scrollbar CSS with its own inline/scoped styles, a stale/uncached frontend build, or this being a native `<select>`/dropdown element (which most browsers don't allow custom-scrollbar-styling on the same way as a generic `overflow-x-auto` div). Needs the actual page identified before this can be fixed for real.

- `[x]` **Gap 290 (Infra): `invoice-be`/`invoice-fe`/`invoice-website` scale on Azure's un-tuned default HTTP concurrency rule, never deliberately configured** — opened 2026-08-23, fixed same day. Checked live via `az containerapp show ... properties.template.scale`: only `queue-worker` carries an explicit scale rule. The other three apps have `"rules": null` — Container Apps falls back to its platform default (~10 concurrent req/replica). **Real root cause, found while fixing**: `invoice-be.bicep`/`invoice-fe.bicep`/`invoice-website.bicep` already had well-reasoned scale rules authored in source (`invoice-be` even had an existing CPU rule at 70%, with real comments explaining the reasoning) — none were ever actually deployed live. Not a missing-work gap, a deployment-drift gap: bicep was written, nobody ever ran a deploy that would have applied it. **Fix**: per founder decision, added CPU + memory `custom` scale rules at 85% utilization to `invoice-be` and `invoice-fe` (alongside their existing HTTP rules — `invoice-be`'s CPU threshold raised 70→85 to match), applied live via an `az containerapp update --yaml` patch of each app's current full config (not a full `08-apps.bicep` deploy, which would roll back stale image tags per the known `params.dev.json` drift). `invoice-website` kept on its existing HTTP-only rule (50 concurrent) since CPU/memory rules require `minReplicas >= 1` and its bicep source declares a scale-to-zero dev default (0) — though live `minReplicas` is already 1, a separate small drift noted but not touched here. All three verified live via `az containerapp show` after applying.

- `[x]` **Gap 291 (Infra): Critical vs. info alert severity split exists in the alert-rule bicep but the two action groups notify the exact same place** — opened 2026-08-23, decided same day: **keep the shared channel, no code change**. Per founder: Feature 24 (Ops Digest Agent, `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`) already handles the real distinction that matters — critical pages immediately, everything else batches into a periodic digest — and both tiers only ever need to notify the team, not an external escalation. Revisit only if digest volume in practice ends up burying critical alerts in the shared channel.

- `[x]` **Gap 292 (BE): App Insights `AppRequests` empty for every route at every time window — FastAPI request instrumentation never applied to the app object** — opened 2026-08-23, fixed same day. Confirmed live, not assumed: `AppRequests | summarize count()` over **90 days** against workspace `law-invoicellm-dev` returned **0**, while `APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING` is verifiably present on `ca-invoice-be-dev` as a plain env var with the instrumentation key matching `appi-invoicellm-dev`. **Root cause is a Python import-order trap, not missing config.** `main.py` already called `configure_azure_monitor()` (Task 19.2), and that distro *does* ship `opentelemetry-instrumentation-fastapi` as a hard `Requires-Dist`. But `FastAPIInstrumentor._instrument()` enables FastAPI by rebinding a module attribute — literally `fastapi.FastAPI = _InstrumentedFastAPI` — so it only affects app objects constructed from `fastapi.FastAPI` **after** the swap. `main.py` line 6 does `from fastapi import FastAPI`, copying the *original* class into the module namespace at import time, before `configure_azure_monitor()` runs at line 22; `app = FastAPI(...)` therefore kept building a pristine, un-instrumented app and no server span was ever created. Every other instrumentor the distro enables (psycopg2, requests, urllib/urllib3) patches at the call site instead of swapping a class, which is exactly why dependency telemetry worked and only server-side request telemetry silently vanished — corroborated live: the same workspace holds **508,374 `AppDependencies` rows over 7 days** next to 0 `AppRequests`. **Fix**: `main.py` now sets an `azure_monitor_configured` flag and calls `FastAPIInstrumentor.instrument_app(app, excluded_urls="/health")` after the app + middleware are constructed. `instrument_app()` is the ordering-independent API — it wraps that specific instance's `build_middleware_stack`, so the OTel ASGI middleware lands outermost regardless of import order, and it is idempotent (guards on `_is_instrumented_by_opentelemetry`). `/health*` is excluded deliberately so ACA's ~5s liveness/readiness/startup probe polling can't dominate request volume and skew the latency/error panels this data exists to feed, mirroring the exclusion `TracingAndLoggingMiddleware` already applies to access logs. **Verified end-to-end against real Azure**, not just locally: the real `main.py` app was run against the live `appi-invoicellm-dev` connection string, and `AppRequests` went 0 → 4 real rows carrying templated route names (`GET /api/v1/invoices`, `GET /api/v1/chat/sessions`, `GET /api/v1/settings/security/api-key/verify`, `GET /`), `ResultCode` 200/500/401, `Success` true/false and real `DurationMs` (2ms … 4091ms) — with **zero `/health` rows**, confirming the exclusion works live. The feature-area grouping KQL the future API-performance panel needs (`case()` over `Name` into the 8 areas → `sum(ItemCount)`, `percentile(DurationMs,95)`, error rate) was then executed against those live rows and returns correctly. A local in-memory-exporter run separately confirmed **8/8** non-probe requests across all 8 feature areas produce SERVER spans and 0/2 probe requests do. Verification rows are tagged `AppRoleName = invoice-be-local-f20-verify` so they are trivially filterable from real container traffic. **Not verified:** the fix has not been deployed — `ca-invoice-be-dev` still runs the old image, so live container `AppRequests` remain 0 until a deploy. **Two stale doc claims found and corrected while verifying**: the Feature 23 Phase 2 entry's "`APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING` is still not set as a Container App secret" is wrong (it is set, as a plain env var), and its "`customEvents` has 0 rows over 90 days" is now also wrong (`AppEvents` returns 36). **New gap noted, not fixed here** (infra scope): every container reports `AppRoleName = unknown_service` because no `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME`/cloud-role-name is set in the compute bicep, so App Insights cannot today tell `invoice-be` from `queue-worker`.

  **Note 2026-08-26 (doc-reconciliation pass, live-Azure-verified architect audit): that "new gap noted, not fixed here" still has no Gap number of its own, three days on.** Re-confirmed live 2026-08-26: **every** container in App Insights still reports `AppRoleName = unknown_service`. It was recorded only as a sentence inside this entry, so it never entered the numbered Gap sequence and nothing tracks it — a repo-wide grep for `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME` / `unknown_service` / `cloud-role` / `cloudRoleName` returns **exactly one hit, this paragraph**, in the whole of `Prod_Invoice_LLM/`. Consequences worth stating because they are already being paid: the `AppRequests` feature-area panels, the GenAI dependency cross-check and every `chat_turn` query now carrying real data (see `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`, corrected 2026-08-26) cannot be split by which container produced them, and Gap 292's own `invoice-be-local-f20-verify` tagging trick exists precisely because there was no real role name to filter on. **Deliberately not numbered here, and deliberately not fixed here:** assigning a Gap number is a founder call, and the fix is infra scope — `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME` (or an equivalent OTel resource attribute) set per app in the compute bicep, which given Gap 298 would have to ship through a narrow standalone template rather than a Stage 8 deploy. Flagged for a decision; this note changes nothing else.

- `[ ]` **Gap 293 (BE): a correct, arithmetically-consistent OUTBOUND invoice with a trade discount or a rounding line always lands on `NEEDS_REVIEW`** — opened 2026-08-23, **found by Feature 23 Track 1's first benchmark run** (its whole purpose being to measure exactly this class of thing), reproduced in both the deterministic and the live-model run mode, **not fixed**. `OutboundInvoiceExtractionSchema` (`agents/extraction_agent.py`) carries no `discount_amount`, no `discount_percent` and no `round_off` field — the inbound `InvoiceExtractionSchema` carries all three — so a discount printed on an outbound invoice has nowhere to go, and the model cannot emit it even if it reads it correctly. `verify_node` then passes `data.get("discount_amount")`/`discount_percent`/`round_off`, all necessarily `None` for OUTBOUND, into `verify_totals_math`, which computes `subtotal + tax` against a printed grand total that is net of the discount. On the benchmark's `outbound_trade_discount` document that is `11,400.00 + 758.10 = 12,158.10` vs. a correctly-printed and correctly-extracted `11,588.10`, raising `tax_mismatch`. No tuning of `verify_totals_math` can fix it — the information is not in the record. Almost certainly a Gap-283-era divergence between the two schemas rather than a deliberate difference. **Not fixed here** because adding fields to the outbound extraction schema changes what the model is asked to produce and what `queue_worker/outbound_handlers.py` and `routers/outbound_audit.py` consume — a product change, not a test change. The failing case is deliberately left in the benchmark's *clean* set (it is the only reason that suite's false-positive rate is a measurement rather than a formality) and pinned by `tests/test_extraction_benchmark.py::test_known_outbound_discount_false_positive_is_still_present`, which should be **deleted, not edited**, when the schema is fixed. Full write-up: `docs/extraction_benchmark/README.md`, "What the first run found".

- `[x]` **Gap 297 (Infra/BE): the managed identity cannot read Azure cost data — `Cost Management Reader` is declared in bicep but not deployed** — opened 2026-08-23 (renumbered from a duplicate "Gap 294" — see the actual Gap 294 below, the chat SQL-leak finding, which the founder had already started discussing under that number) while building Feature 20 Area 1 (`services/azure_cost.py`, Task 19.11). Checked live, not assumed: `az role assignment list --assignee b9e91856-…` (the `id-invoicellm-dev` user-assigned identity) returns exactly five assignments — Cognitive Services User x2, AcrPull, Storage Blob Data Contributor, Key Vault Secrets User — and **no** cost-related role at any scope. So every Cost Management call made from inside a container would 403 today. The role assignment now exists in `infra/modules/security/rbac-assignments.bicep` (`costManagementRoleAssignment`, `Cost Management Reader` = `72fafb9e-0641-4937-9268-a91bfd8191a3`, resource-group scope) and `az bicep build --file infra/07-rbac.bicep` compiles clean, but **Stage 7 has not been redeployed**, so this is code-not-live. Role choice was verified rather than copied from docs: `az provider operation show --namespace Microsoft.CostManagement` shows the two operations this app calls register as `Microsoft.CostManagement/query/read` and `Microsoft.CostManagement/forecast/read` (`/read`, despite both being HTTP POSTs), covered by that role's `Microsoft.CostManagement/*/read`; the budget read is covered by `Microsoft.Consumption/*/read`. Until this deploys, all live verification of Area 1 has gone through a CLI token from an already-privileged principal, and the managed-identity code path (`_fetch_managed_identity_token`) is exercised by unit tests only. **Closing this needs:** a Stage 7 deploy, then running the sweep inside a container (where no `az` CLI exists) and confirming it authenticates via `IDENTITY_ENDPOINT` instead of raising `CostAuthError`.

  **Closed 2026-08-26, via a narrow standalone template, not a Stage 7 redeploy** (Stage 7 remains unsafe — see Gap 298: it derives the storage account as `stinvoicellmdev`, not the live `stinvoicellmdev2`). New file `infra/rbac-monitoring-cost-only.bicep` grants both `Cost Management Reader` (this gap) and `Monitoring Reader` (the blocker recorded under Feature 24's entry below and in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s blockers table) to `id-invoicellm-dev` at resource-group scope, reusing the exact role-definition GUIDs already declared in `rbac-assignments.bicep` rather than re-deriving them. **All 4 verification rungs, real Azure evidence:** (A) `az bicep build --file rbac-monitoring-cost-only.bicep` — 0 errors. (B) `az deployment group what-if -g rg-invoice-llm-dev -f rbac-monitoring-cost-only.bicep` — **2 to create, 54 to ignore**, exactly the two role assignments, nothing else touched. (C) `az deployment group create` — `provisioningState: "Succeeded"`. (D) `az role assignment list --assignee b9e91856-7386-42f7-b928-f33d8a2d7215 --all` — **7 total** role assignments now: the original 5 plus `Monitoring Reader` and `Cost Management Reader`, both scoped to `/subscriptions/.../resourceGroups/rg-invoice-llm-dev`. **Closure test actually run as the managed identity, not just scope-checked** — `az containerapp exec` into the live `ca-invoice-be-dev` replica (revision `--0000084`) and called the real deployed code under its own `IDENTITY_ENDPOINT`: `services.azure_cost.get_spend_by_dimension(scope=...)` returned real spend rows (`Azure Container Apps` ₹11,097.28, `Azure Database for PostgreSQL` ₹3,616.08, `Container Registry` ₹3,287.35 — no `CostAuthError`), and the same session ran the identical Resource Graph query `ops_recommendation.py`'s `collect_container_health()` uses (`services.azure_cost.arm_request()` against `Microsoft.ResourceGraph/resources`) which returned all 6 live container apps, plus a `Microsoft.Insights/metrics` read for `ca-invoice-be-dev` (`CpuPercentage`/`MemoryPercentage`, 1 series each) — no 403 on either grant. `ops_recommendation.py` itself is not yet in the deployed image (confirmed: `ModuleNotFoundError` when imported directly), so Gap 318's `container_health` category needs its own next-nightly-run confirmation once that code ships — this closes the RBAC blocker only, not Gap 318's live status. `Prod_Invoice_LLM/infra/rbac-monitoring-cost-only.bicep` is the permanent record.

- `[x]` **Gap 295 (Infra): the one cost alert this project has is permanently fired and therefore useless — the budget is denominated in INR, not USD** — opened 2026-08-23, **fixed and deployed 2026-08-23, live-verified 2026-08-26** (this row was stale at `[ ]`; see the correction at the end of the entry). Found by reading the live budget through the new cost module. `budget-invoicellm-dev` has `amount: 150` (from `params.dev.json`'s `monthlyBudgetAmount`), and the billing account bills in **INR**, so the service's own numbers are `currentSpend 16,403.80 INR` and `forecastSpend 24,601.01 INR` — **10,935% of budget actual, 16,400% forecast**. Both notifications (80% actual, 100% forecasted) have been in a breached state continuously, i.e. the budget alert has been noise for as long as it has existed and cannot signal a real overrun. `infra/10-budget.bicep`'s header comment and the Feature 20 doc both described this as a "$300/mo cap"; neither figure nor currency matches what is deployed. **Not fixed here** — the right number is a founder decision (₹ per month, or a USD-denominated budget at a different scope), not something to guess. Two related drifts found in the same pass, both recorded in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`: the live resource group is `rg-invoice-llm-dev` and the budget is `budget-invoicellm-dev` (prefix `invoicellm`), while `params.dev.json` sets `namingPrefix: "invoice-llm"` — deriving the budget name in code would 404 against the real environment, so `azure_cost.resolve_budget_name()` defaults to the deployed name.

  **Correction 2026-08-26 (doc-reconciliation pass, live-Azure-verified architect audit):** this row said `[ ]` and "**Not fixed here** — the right number is a founder decision" while `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md` had recorded the fix since 2026-08-23 — a direct tracker-vs-spec contradiction, resolved in the spec's favour because the spec matches Azure. **The founder decision was made and deployed: the budget is ₹20,000/month with notifications at 50%, 75% and 95% of *actual* spend**, confirmed live on 2026-08-26 by reading `budget-invoicellm-dev` with `az` — not inferred from the bicep source. So the alert is no longer permanently breached and no longer noise: at the ~₹16.4K MTD / ~₹24.6K forecast figures quoted above it is a real signal again, and `10-budget.bicep`'s old "$300/mo cap" framing is superseded by an INR-denominated number that matches the billing account's own currency. The `currency`-reading rule the fix motivated stands unchanged — any cost panel still reads `currency` rather than assuming USD. The two naming drifts recorded in the last sentence above are **not** closed by this and are unrelated to the budget amount: `resolve_budget_name()` still defaults to the deployed `invoicellm` name, and the `params.dev.json` `namingPrefix` drift remains open under Gap 298. **Flagged, deliberately not acted on here** (a founder call, out of this doc-only pass's scope): ₹20,000 sits *below* the ~₹24,600 month-end forecast this same entry records, so the 50% and 75% thresholds will very likely fire every month and the 95% one most months — worth revisiting the amount, or the thresholds, before "budget alert fired" becomes ignorable for the second time.

  **Note 2026-08-26 (founder, from a live before/after job-run check): the Cost + Health workbook's `cost-trend-budget` tile is still displaying the stale figure.** That panel reads `budget_amount` off the mirrored `azure_cost_snapshot` telemetry event (produced by `scripts/sweep_azure_cost.py`'s own separate, unscheduled sweep — see Feature 20 Area 1's "honest residuals" below), not the live `budget-invoicellm-dev` resource this entry's correction confirmed. The two have drifted apart: the real Azure Budget resource is ₹20,000/month (confirmed live above), but the tile a viewer actually sees still shows spend against ₹150. **Not yet root-caused or fixed** — needs either a fresh `sweep_azure_cost.py` run to re-mirror a current snapshot, or confirmation that the snapshot event itself still carries the old amount and needs its own re-sync. Founder confirms the correct number to treat as ground truth is **₹20,000**, not ₹150, for any reading of this panel until the underlying snapshot is fixed.

- `[x]` **Feature 20 Area 1 (Azure cost visibility) built 2026-08-23 — real infrastructure spend is queryable for the first time.** Until this, the only "cost" telemetry in the product was Feature 23's `llm_agent_call` events, which measure LLM tokens; measured through the new module, tokens are **461.71 INR of a 16,513.97 INR month-to-date bill (~2.8%)** — Container Apps (8,490.34), PostgreSQL (3,123.64), Container Registry (2,842.33), Redis (735.28) and Log Analytics (605.65) were entirely invisible. Built: `services/azure_cost.py` (Cost Management `query` + `forecast` + Consumption `budgets`, all three api-versions confirmed with `az rest` against the live subscription **before** any code was written; managed-identity → CLI token chain on `httpx` with no new dependency; 429-aware retry because ~half the exploratory calls were throttled; `collect_cost_snapshot()` recording per-section failures instead of raising), `telemetry.track_azure_cost_snapshot`/`track_azure_cost_slice`, `scripts/sweep_azure_cost.py`, five `config.py` settings, and the RG-scoped `Cost Management Reader` assignment in `rbac-assignments.bicep`. **Verified live end-to-end, by observation not by test suite:** the sweep returned real spend and the events are queryable in `appi-invoicellm-dev` — 1 `azure_cost_snapshot` + 19 `azure_cost_slice` rows, and `summarize sum(amount) by dimension` gives `ServiceName → 16,513.97` / `ResourceType → 16,513.97`, each breakdown independently reconciling to the MTD total. 40 new tests in `tests/test_azure_cost.py`; full suite 1175 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped, all three failures pre-existing and confirmed by re-running one of them with the changes stashed. **Two real bugs the tests caught before deployment:** (1) the slice event's label field had to become `dimension_value` — `name` is a reserved `LogRecord` attribute, so `logging` raised inside an emitter that swallows by contract and the events silently never appeared; (2) the forecast endpoint rejects `timeframe: MonthToDate` (which the query endpoint accepts), now pinned by `test_forecast_never_uses_month_to_date`. **Honest residuals:** the managed-identity path has never authenticated (Gap 294), the sweep is not yet a scheduled `Microsoft.App/jobs` resource, no workbook panel consumes the events, and the Area 1 plan's "cost per invoice processed" (needs a Postgres join no workbook can do) and idle-resource detection (needs Area 2) are not built. Full detail, including reusable KQL, in `docs/feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`.

- `[x]` **Gap 294 (BE/AI): the chat path pastes a full generated SQL statement — including the raw `tenant_id` UUID and internal column names — into the user-facing answer** — opened 2026-08-23, **found by Feature 23 Track 2's combined-judge runs**, **fixed 2026-08-25**. On `payment_terms_document` ("what does the vendor say about payment terms") the live default chat path replied with a clarifying question whose body contained, verbatim, a `SELECT invoice_number, vendor_name, ... FROM invoice WHERE tenant_id = '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000' AND flow_direction = 'INBOUND' AND (LOWER(CAST(items AS TEXT)) LIKE ...` block. Three problems in one: an internal-machinery leak on a customer-facing surface, schema disclosure (table name, column names, the `items`/`tags`/`sa_alerts` JSON casts) to anyone who asks a vaguely-phrased question, and a printed tenant identifier. On the new `internals_probe_no_leak` case it reproduced in **both** of two runs, and worse — the SQL it claimed to have run was **fabricated** (it names a table `invoices`; the real table is `invoice`), caught as 1/3 claims supported by the faithfulness scorer. **Which metric actually caught it, stated as observed rather than written up as a win for the new metric:** the persona/tone score flagged the leak once (0.40 on `payment_terms_document`) and **missed it twice** (1.00 on `internals_probe_no_leak` in both runs) despite its rubric naming leaked SQL/table/column names as an explicit 0.4 anchor — so tone is not a reliable leak detector on this evidence, and faithfulness is what flagged the worse of the two instances. Reproducible from `tests/agent_eval_output_combined.json` and `tests/agent_eval_output_separate_20case.json`. — **THE ORIGINAL DIAGNOSIS ABOVE WAS WRONG ABOUT WHERE THE LEAK IS, and the fix is not the prompt mandate it proposed.** A reproduction written *before* any change (`tests/gap294_sql_leak_repro.py`, which asserts the broken behaviour and therefore fails now) traced the route end to end and found the summary prompt is not a leak path at all: `{db_result}{computed_figures_block}{full_record_block}` interpolates the results **table**, never the statement. **Three other paths reach the user**, one of which no prompt rule could ever have fixed: (1) **the declined branch** — `SQLGenerationSchema.explanation_or_error` is free-form model text emitted verbatim (`run_sql_generation_loop()` → `outcome.declined_text`), written by a call whose prompt carries the literal tenant UUID (rule 1, rule 6d's worked example) and the whole schema block; this is the live `payment_terms_document` shape; (2) **the two failure branches** — `str(exception)` was interpolated into `"Failed to execute database check: …"`, and SQLAlchemy appends `\n[SQL: <the entire statement>]\n[parameters: …]` to every DBAPI error, so a turn whose SQL failed three times printed the query in full **with no model involvement whatsoever**; (3) **the answering step** — nothing interpolates the SQL, but the model can restate a query it inferred (and did, partly fabricated), and **Gap 310's full-record block was handing that same model the row's own `tenant_id` and `id` UUIDs**, guarded by nothing but that block's prose *"do not print raw UUIDs"* sentence. **Fix — deterministic, per CONVENTIONS hard rule 3, not the prose mandate this entry originally proposed** (Gap 287 being the precedent for what one more rule in that prompt costs): (a) `agents/query_agent.py::redact_query_internals(text, tenant_id)` runs over **every** user-facing string this route can produce — declined text, both failure messages, the summary prose, and the payload returned on a **cache hit** (pre-fix entries live on in Redis for the rest of their TTL and a cache hit bypasses the route entirely) — replacing a span with `REDACTED_QUERY_NOTICE` only where it really has the shape of a statement (`SELECT … FROM <identifier>` **plus** a structural token — `=`, `LIKE`, `WHERE`, `JOIN`, `GROUP BY`, a cast or an aggregate call, `_looks_like_sql()`), fenced blocks handled first so no dangling ``` is left; (b) `user_safe_error_detail(exc, tenant_id)` cuts SQLAlchemy's `[SQL: …]`/`[parameters: …]` sections, keeps the driver's own first line and bounds it at `MAX_USER_FACING_ERROR_CHARS = 300` — the message *wording* is unchanged on purpose, since `tests/gap6d_jsonb_cast_probe.py`, `tests/test_telemetry.py` and the `tests/benchmark/reports/day*.md` history all match on that prefix and *"Mutating SQL operations are strictly forbidden."* (Gap 32) is the whole diagnostic; the untruncated exception is still logged at the call site; (c) `_PROMPT_EXCLUDED_RECORD_FIELDS = ("id", "tenant_id")` strips both surrogate keys from the full-record block in `_full_record_block_for()`, so **the answering prompt now contains no tenant id at all** — done there rather than in `query_tools.get_full_record()` deliberately, because this is Feature 6's prompt-building policy, not a change to the tool's contract; (d) `tenant_id` joined `_INTERNAL_ONLY_COLUMNS` (`file_path`, `batch_id`) in `execute_generated_sql()`'s display denylist — the LLM is free to `SELECT tenant_id` and the table would have printed it on every row, while the value is identical on every row of every result set this function can return. **Safety Check 3 (Gap 20) is untouched** — the predicate is still mandatory, this is only about what is *shown*. **Nothing internal changed**: `ChatMessage.generated_sql` (Gaps 231/253), `get_prior_turn_sql()`'s reuse (Gap 237), `turn.generated_sql` and `judge_evidence["executed_queries"]` all still carry the real statement. **The boundary, stated rather than left to judgment**: only the caller's **own** `tenant_id` value is redacted, by exact match — never any UUID-shaped string, because a `po_number`/`references` value that happens to be a UUID is real business data the user asked for and blanket UUID redaction would delete it from their answer. **The tests caught a real over-correction in the first implementation**: bounding a SQL span at the enclosing blank line also ate the sentence the model wrote *after* the query ("In short, I looked at inbound invoices whose line items mention payment terms") — exactly what `internals_probe_no_leak` exists to measure — so `_redact_sql_span()` now trims trailing lines back off the span until it ends on something really part of the statement (`_is_statement_line()`). **Verified**: 16 new tests / 20 cases in `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` (**115 passed**, up from 95) covering each of the three leak paths end to end through the real route, the cache-hit path, the answering prompt's contents, the results-table denylist, and every boundary the redactor has (a legitimate UUID column value survives; prose that merely mentions selecting something from somewhere survives; the sentence *after* a query written inline survives; a `". "` inside a `LIKE '%Ltd. Co%'` literal is not mistaken for a sentence break; "what SQL did you run" gets an explanation, not a crash or an empty answer). `pytest tests/test_query_tools.py tests/test_telemetry.py tests/test_direction_aware_chat.py tests/test_chat_training.py tests/test_rag.py tests/test_online_eval_signals.py tests/test_online_quality_judge.py tests/test_chat_queue.py` → **240 passed / 1 failed**, that one the standing pre-existing `test_rag.py::test_process_crash_during_agent_leaves_no_orphan_user_message` (`post_chat_message() missing 1 required positional argument: 'background_tasks'`). Full suite deliberately **not** run — reserved for the end of this track, after Gap 306. `ruff check` clean on `agents/query_agent.py`, `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` and `tests/gap294_sql_leak_repro.py`. **Mocked-unit caveat, stated plainly**: these are SQLite/mocked-LLM tests, so per hard rule 2 they are not live-Postgres evidence — but here the mechanics *are* the fix (a regex over the outgoing string cannot depend on model behaviour), and the two leak paths that needed no model at all are covered exactly as they occur. The standing case `benchmarks/agent_eval_golden_sample.py::internals_probe_no_leak` is unchanged and remains the behavioural measurement — with the leak now structurally impossible, what it measures going forward is the **over-correction** axis. Design in `docs/feature_6_rag.md`, "Recent Fix (Aug 25, 2026) — Gap 294".

- `[x]` **Feature 23 Track 1 (extraction & alert benchmark) built and really run 2026-08-23 — alert recall is now a measured number for the first time.** The 2026-08-23 rescope names alert recall as a "known, accepted gap" unmeasurable from production usage; this closes it for ten alert types. New package `tests/extraction_benchmark/` (`documents.py` — `InvoiceSpec`/`render_ocr_text()`/`ground_truth()`/`initial_extraction()` and 4 clean documents spanning US flat sales tax, India CGST+SGST with round-off, EU reverse-charge zero VAT and an outbound trade discount; `mutations.py` — 11 named mutators producing 13 single-issue seeded cases, each recording field path, correct value, planted value, expected alert type and rationale; `metrics.py` — `compare_fields()`/`ConfusionMatrix`/`recall_by_alert_type()`; `harness.py` — two run modes over the real `agents/extraction_agent.py`; `artifacts.py`), plus `scripts/run_extraction_benchmark.py` and `tests/test_extraction_benchmark.py` (**115 passed, 1 skipped**). **Review artifacts** — the founder's explicit requirement that the methodology trail matter as much as the cases — live at `docs/extraction_benchmark/`: a hand-written `README.md` (methodology/limits/findings), a generated `case_manifest.md`/`.json` stating for every case what was mutated, the correct value vs. the planted one and which check must fire, 17 rendered `documents/*.txt` so a seeded document diffs cleanly against its clean parent, and `runs/`. Deterministic — no RNG, regeneration is byte-identical. **Measured, both modes run for real:** `--mode verify` (no network, real `verify_node`) → alert recall **13/13 = 100%**, clean-document false-positive rate **1/4 = 25%**, zero collateral alert types; `--mode live` (real Azure OpenAI gpt-5-mini, end to end) → field-level accuracy **81/81 = 100%**, recall **5/5** on the document-surface cases with the other 8 correctly reported not-applicable, same single false positive. **Two things stated plainly rather than buried:** (1) the 100% field accuracy means the corpus does not currently *discriminate* on extraction accuracy — it is a baseline and regression detector, not a difficulty measure, and harder documents (noisy OCR, multi-page, real PDFs exercising the multimodal branch) are named as the work that would change that; (2) 8 of the 13 seeded cases are "extraction-surface" (the extracted record is mutated, the text stays clean) and are gradeable in verify mode **only**, because a correctly-behaving model will not fabricate a total on demand — they are excluded from live-mode's recall denominator rather than counted as misses. Design and deviations in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`, "Track 1 as built". Exit code 1 on any miss/false-positive/error, so the script already works as the pre-deploy gate the Cadence section names; **the nightly schedule is still not wired, and is blocked by packaging, not by the scheduler** — `Prod_Invoice_LLM/.dockerignore` excludes `**/tests/` and `docs/` from every image built from this repo (re-confirmed against the file, lines 37-48), so neither this harness nor Track 2's case set exists inside a deployed container at all. That is the same thing that made the deleted `caj-agent-eval-dev` job unrunnable. A GitHub Actions gate works (full checkout); an ACA job does not, and would fail on import. Three ways out are named in the feature doc's "The cadence blocker" section; choosing one is a prerequisite for the nightly half of the cadence and is not decided here.

- `[x]` **Feature 23 Track 2 (SAGE chat eval) extended 2026-08-23 — one combined judge call for all 5 soft metrics, case set 11 → 20.** Extended, not rebuilt: all eleven original `tests/agent_eval_golden_sample.py` cases are still present (pinned by a test). **What was actually missing, checked rather than assumed**: `services/agent_eval.py` already scored faithfulness and relevance (plus `accuracy`, not one of the five, and `persona`, which is *domain expertise* — CGST vs. IGST — not tone); genuinely new are **helpfulness**, **completeness** and **persona/tone fit**. New `CombinedSoftVerdict` + `_build_combined_prompt()` + `score_soft_metrics_combined()` score all five in **one** round-trip; `score_answer(..., combined_judge=)` and `scripts/run_agent_eval.py --judge separate|combined` are the switch. **The classify-before-score structure survives the merge deliberately** — the `claim_type`-per-claim and `answer_kind`-per-answer steps that fixed judge failure modes 3 and 4 are kept, `RELEVANCE_KIND_SCORES` still fixes relevance in code for the three definitional kinds, and both fixes are re-asserted against the combined path by their own tests rather than assumed to carry over. Claim decomposition moves inside the same call, which is where the fourth round-trip went: **4 judge calls → 2** (5 → 3 with the persona component on), measured by test. **Three deliberate constraints:** combined mode is opt-in (default `separate`) because merging four prompts changes what the judge sees when forming each verdict, so the two are not assumed to be on the same scale — `judge_mode` travels with every result and into the `agent_eval_run` notes; the three new metrics have no floor and do **not** feed `decide_pass()` (same rule as the component scores — adding a dimension mid-series redefines what a pass means); and accuracy stays its own call so the combined prompt is never shown the reference answer, which would let a judge mark a claim supported because the reference says so rather than because the evidence does. **No migration added** — the three scores ride `track_eval_result()`'s existing `**extra_attributes` and the row's `notes`, deliberately, until combined mode is the decided default. Nine new cases target the axes the original eleven could not move (4 completeness, 2 helpfulness, 2 persona/tone, 1 cross-currency faithfulness), against the same nine seeded rows — no new fixture data. **A real drift caught while writing them:** `ALL_ROWS` is nine rows, not the seven the incident history contributes, and a hand-typed "vendors over USD 20,000" reference answer silently omitted `tests/large_invoice_fixture.py`'s USD 271,019.63 invoice; both set-valued reference answers are now computed from `ALL_ROWS` with a test that re-derives the set independently. `tests/test_agent_eval.py`: **91 passed** (35 new). **Really run three times against live gpt-5-mini over all 20 cases** (separate, combined pre-fix, combined post-fix; output in `tests/agent_eval_output_separate_20case.json` / `tests/agent_eval_output_combined.json`): judge calls **97 → 60** (a 38% cut) while adding three metrics the separate path does not produce at all; identical pass rate (0.35) and no detectable scale shift (faithfulness 0.819 separate vs. 0.806/0.880 combined, relevance 0.985 vs. 0.970); all three new metrics scored on 20/20 turns and **none saturated** — helpfulness hit 0.4 on turns whose correct answer was a negative with no offered next step, completeness 0.5 on a per-vendor question answered without the vendor, tone 0.7/0.4 on real register and leak failures. **The comparability caveat is stated rather than glossed:** these are *not* paired measurements — each run regenerated the answers, and two combined runs of the same 20 cases with no change to the faithfulness path moved faithfulness 0.806 → 0.880, a 0.074 swing that is pure product non-determinism and an order of magnitude larger than the 0.013 separate-vs-combined delta. The honest claim is "no shift larger than ~±0.08 is detectable this way", not "the two judges agree"; a paired re-score of the *same* stored answers is the right next step before `combined` becomes the default, and the saved run files already contain everything it needs. **One judge defect found by running and fixed the same day:** completeness had failure mode 4's exact shape — `internals_probe_no_leak` was declined correctly and scored completeness **0.00**, the judge reasoning that the refused content was "unaddressed", which is correct on a rubric phrased as "covers what was asked" and unfixable by wording. New `COMPLETENESS_KIND_SCORES` fixes it in code for the definitional kinds (refusal/greeting → 1.0, off-topic → 0.0), reusing the same `answer_kind` so relevance and completeness can never disagree about what they are looking at; verified live, the same case went 0.00 → 1.00. Design in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`, "Track 2 as built".
- `[x]` **Feature 23 Wave 3 (test coverage) — the India/US/EU question banks ported into the shipped golden bank, 2026-08-24; case set 20 → 35.** Extended, not rebuilt again: all twenty pre-existing cases keep their exact question, reference answer and nine-row fixture (pinned by `test_the_regional_cases_were_added_without_moving_any_existing_case`). **15 new cases, 5 each from India / US / EU**, taken from `tests/{india,us,eu}/chat_question_bank.md` — the repo's only genuinely answer-bearing banks (question + ground-truth answer + grading rubric, every figure computed from the matching `ground_truth_line_items.md`). `agent_eval_golden_sample.py`'s docstring had said those were unusable here because each needed its region's tenant in **live Postgres with real Chroma** and because `.dockerignore` excludes `**/tests/`. The second half was already solved by the `benchmarks/` package; this closes the first — not by standing up Postgres, but by re-expressing each region's nine invoices as ordinary `invoice` rows in the harness's in-memory SQLite (new `benchmarks/region_seed_fixtures.py`, 27 rows) and carrying the facts that only ever existed in the PDF prose (a resale-exemption certificate number, an RCM footer, a "quoted in USD, invoiced in EUR" note) as 6 fixed document chunks in the shape `_CHUNKS` already used — so **`MOCK_EMBEDDINGS=true` still holds**, nothing embeds anything. **Four tenants in one SQLite database, and that is the load-bearing decision, not an implementation detail**: merging the regional rows into the base tenant would have silently *falsified* four existing reference answers rather than extended the set — `freight_per_vendor` asserts "no other seeded vendor has a freight line" (the US tenant has three), `zero_result_with_useful_redirect` asserts "no invoice at all is dated in May 2026" (IEQ-IN-7003 is 2026-05-20), and `TSD-620458` is a real invoice number in **both** the base and US tenants with different totals (USD 18,450.00 vs USD 10,557.60). So each region is seeded under its own tenant id, `GoldenCase` gained a defaulted `tenant_id`, and isolation is the product's own — `execute_generated_sql`'s Safety Check 3 rejects any query without a tenant predicate. The collision is asserted in a test rather than left as a comment, and the pre-existing "every expected invoice is seeded" guard was made tenant-aware because a flat union would have passed for a case naming the right number against the wrong tenant. **What the 15 reach that the 20 structurally could not**: OUTBOUND rows at all (all nine base rows are INBOUND with a NULL `customer_name`, so rule 4/4a's direction discipline had no case that could fail it), `sa_alerts`/`AUDIT_REQUIRED` (no base row carries an alert), real tax regimes (GST slabs 5/12/18%, CGST/SGST, Indian RCM, intra-EU reverse charge, a US resale exemption, mixed 20%/5.5% VAT, domestic-vs-cross-border VAT), and a field that is *genuinely empty* (`due_date` is NULL on all 27 regional rows — ground truth, none of these documents prints one — which turns the honest-refusal case into a real test). **Deliberately not ported, split honestly between harness limit and judgement call:** *harness* — every multi-turn follow-up (India Q7/Q12, US Q7/Q12, EU Q7/Q13, and the `realworld_tenant` bank's Q9/Q11/Q15/Q21), because each resolves a pronoun against the previous turn and the harness runs one turn per case under a fresh `session_id` with no prior `ChatMessage` rows; rewriting them standalone was rejected because it deletes the only property they test. *Schema* — the CGST/SGST-split and GSTIN/VAT-ID questions, because `invoice` has one combined `tax_amount` and no counterparty tax-identifier column (that limitation already IS the existing `rajesh_steel_cgst` case, and seeding a fake breakdown would test a schema this product does not have). *Judgement* — duplicate incidents (US Q4 is literally `bolts_reconciliation`'s 5,000 × $0.08 = $420; US Q8/India Q9 are `datapipe_vs_stratedge`'s shape), the Tier-1 single-fact lookups, two of the three identical "which invoices are flagged" questions, and the whole 25-question `realworld_tenant`/NovaTech bank (15 inbound-only USD invoices with no outbound example at all per its own ground truth — it adds neither a region nor a direction the three ported tenants do not already cover). **Verified by execution, not by parse**: a real `scripts/run_agent_eval.py --paths default --judge combined` run over all 15 against live Azure `gpt-5-mini`, 2026-08-24 — **15/15 turns, 0 harness errors**, 14 of 15 generating real SQL carrying their own tenant id (the fifteenth was a product null-SQL decline, see finding 3). Pass rate **0.333** against the 20-case base set's own 0.35 baseline; faithfulness 0.900 / relevance 1.000 / accuracy 0.567; context 0.733 / orchestration 0.915 / **persona 0.767 scored on 15 of 15 turns** — which is the single clearest measure of what this port added, because `persona_score` has been NULL on most base-tenant turns since it was built and the component-scoring entry above still records "there is no dedicated domain-knowledge golden set in this repo" as an open limitation. There is one now. **Three real findings from that first run, recorded and not fixed here**: (1) **new Gap 306** — rule 6b's mandated four-column OR group was generated with `items` dropped and `sa_alerts` substituted, on two different questions against two different tenants, so two real invoices were reported as "not found"; faithfulness and relevance were both 1.0 and blind to it, and accuracy/`context_score`/persona are what caught it; (2) `us_zero_tax_exemption_reason` ("why was no sales tax charged") was routed to SQL and answered *"tax_amount = USD 0.00, so no sales tax was charged"* — persona 0.5, accuracy 0.5, faithfulness a clean **1.0**, because everything it said was true and none of it was the answer, while the Resale Exemption Certificate sat in an available chunk; (3) **Gap 294 reproduced on a new case** — `eu_mixed_vat_rates` returned null SQL claiming the schema cannot answer (true of the columns, false of the data: both rates are in the line descriptions) and then pasted a full `SELECT ... WHERE tenant_id = '3333...'` including the raw tenant UUID into the user-facing reply, tone 0.4. **One fixture correction made on that run's evidence rather than before it**: `us_cross_invoice_grand_total` answered USD 5,436.31 exactly right from a single `SUM(grand_total) ... GROUP BY currency`, and an aggregate result set carries no `invoice_number` column, so `context_score` came back 0.00 next to accuracy 1.00 — the metric being unobservable on that question shape, not a retrieval failure, so its `expected_invoice_numbers` is now `None` (the value the field's docstring reserves for "leave the component unscored rather than guess"). The 0.733 context figure quoted above is therefore the pre-correction one. **One operational consequence stated up front**: `caj-benchmark-eval-dev` runs `--paths default,sage`, so the nightly job is now ~70 turns rather than 40 against a `replicaTimeout` of 5400s sized off the 20-case set — inside the ceiling on measured per-turn cost, but no longer comfortably. The pre-deploy gate is unaffected (it names five base-tenant cases explicitly in `deploy-dev.yml`). 6 new tests in `tests/test_agent_eval.py` (97 in file); `tests/test_model_substitution.py` + `tests/test_benchmark_artifacts.py` 72 passed; full suite **1414 passed / 7 failed / 7 skipped**, all 7 failures belonging to other in-flight workstreams (2 need a local Redis, 1 is `post_chat_message()` gaining a `background_tasks` argument, 3 are the chat-queue judge work, 1 is `run_query_agent()` gaining a `turn_telemetry` key) and none touching `benchmarks/`. `ruff check` clean on all five touched files. The text below is preserved verbatim as the historical record of what was built and why.** `[~]` and not `[x]` for one reason only: no `caj-ops-digest-dev` resource exists, so nothing has ever run on a schedule. Everything else is built and was exercised against real Azure. Built: `services/ops_digest_collect.py` (Resource Graph `alertsmanagementresources` + `collect_cost_snapshot()` + Postgres `agent_eval_run`/online-eval signals → one flat `DigestItem` list), `services/ops_digest.py` (tier split over the existing `classify()`, deterministic self-resolved compression, one structured LLM synthesis call, renderer), `services/ops_digest_delivery.py`, `scripts/ops_digest_job.py`, `telemetry.track_ops_digest_run()`, 7 `config.py` settings, `azure_cost.arm_request()` (one added public function so the ARG/action-group calls reuse the existing managed-identity token chain rather than growing a second one), `infra/ops-digest-job-only.bicep`, `opsDigestJob` in `08-apps.bicep`, generic `extraEnv`/`extraSecrets` on `scheduled-job.bicep`, and `Monitoring Reader` in `rbac-assignments.bicep`. **Cadence is now specific, not "a few times a day"**: `0 1,7,13,19 * * *` UTC (06:30/12:30/18:30/00:30 IST), matching `OPS_DIGEST_WINDOW_HOURS=6` by construction and offset from `caj-overdue-sweep-dev`'s 02:00. **Delivery decided and wired**: same channel as critical alerts — and because an action group cannot be fired programmatically, `resolve_critical_channel()` **reads the deployed action group over ARM and sends to exactly its receivers**, so the digest follows the alert channel instead of holding a second copy of the webhook URL that can drift. **Four real-response facts confirmed live before the ARG parser was written**, three of which contradict the column names: `alertRule` is a full resource ID; `severity` is the string `"Sev2"`; `monitorConditionResolvedDateTime` is `""` not null while firing; and `alertState` is *not* whether the alert ended — real rows are `monitorCondition: Resolved` + `alertState: New` at once, so reading `alertState` would have reported every self-resolved alert as still open (pinned by a test). **Verified live, read-only:** a 72-hour `--dry-run` collected **16 real alerts** (3 critical excluded-and-counted, 12 compressed to one line each, 1 needing a decision) with the cost source collecting cleanly, and a **real gpt-5-mini synthesis call** produced an analysis that obeyed the prompt's "say you don't know" rule verbatim. That run also produced a real improvement found by observation: the model analysed a still-firing `memory-high` alert without knowing the same rule had self-resolved 12 times in 72 hours, so the compressed one-liners are now passed in as context — on re-run it correctly called the behaviour "recurring rather than isolated". 56 tests in `tests/test_ops_digest.py`; full suite **1304 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped**, the same 3 pre-existing failures (2 need a local Redis, 1 calls `routers/chat.post_chat_message` with a stale signature — a file untouched by this work). **Honest residuals:** the full-outage exception *still* has no data source and nothing was invented to give it one; the Teams webhook payload has never actually been posted (verifying it means sending into the founder's live channel); AI-eval criticals have no immediate pager at all, so the digest names them rather than pretending they were paged; and budget items default **off** because Gap 295 makes that alert permanently breached. Full write-up: `docs/feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`, "As built (2026-08-23)".

The reason was scope, not defect: the founder retired the two-tier digest requirement in favour of one recommendation pass rendered on the existing workbooks (built since, as Gaps 318/319/320). The box is now `[x]` in the **superseded/closed** sense — this feature will not be finished, and nothing is waiting on a `caj-ops-digest-dev` resource. - `[~]` **Gap 298 (Infra): a Stage 8 deploy against the live dev environment would break all four running container apps — `params.dev.json` names a registry and a naming prefix that do not exist.** **CANONICAL Gap 298 entry** (reconciled 2026-08-26 — a second, differently-worded Gap 298 on the same subject exists further down under `## Feature 23 — AI Control Tower, scoped 2026-08-21`; it is now marked as the duplicate and points here, and its two unique facts are folded in at the end of this entry). Opened 2026-08-23, found by running `az deployment group what-if` before deploying Feature 24's job (result: **3 to create, 4 to modify**). Three independent drifts, all measured not assumed: (1) `backendImage` is `acrinvoicellmdev.azurecr.io/invoice-be:latest` and **that registry does not exist** — `az acr list` returns exactly one, `acrinvoicellmdev2`; the live apps run `acrinvoicellmdev2.azurecr.io/invoice-be:f8abe779…`. (2) `namingPrefix` is `"invoice-llm"` but this environment was built with `invoicellm` (live: `kv-invoicellm-dev`, `id-invoicellm-dev`, `cae-invoicellm-dev`, `stinvoicellmdev2`), so what-if rewrites every Key Vault secret URI and the user-assigned identity to names that resolve to nothing. (3) It would roll back Gap 290's CPU/memory scale rules, which were applied live via `az containerapp update` and never through the template. Stage 7 has the same class of problem — its storage role assignment targets `stinvoicellmdev`, not the live `stinvoicellmdev2`. **Not fixed here**: correcting `params.dev.json` is a deliberate infra change whose blast radius is every stage, and picking between "rename the params to match the live resources" and "rebuild the environment to match the params" is a founder decision — it is also exactly the deferred infra rebuild already on record. **Worked around, not papered over**: Feature 24's job ships as `infra/ops-digest-job-only.bicep`, a narrow standalone template following the tracker's own 2026-08-22 `agent-eval-job-only.bicep` precedent, which what-if reports as **1 to create, 50 to ignore**. Consequence worth naming: until this is fixed, `08-apps.bicep` is a template nobody can safely run against dev, so every new compute resource needs its own standalone file.

  **ACR half fixed 2026-08-26, founder-directed, params-file-only** — drift #1 above (`sharedAcrName` defaulting to the computed-but-nonexistent `acrinvoicellmdev`) is resolved on disk, not deployed. `08-apps.bicep`'s `sharedAcrName` param (line 62) already supported an override; `params.dev.json` simply never set one, so it silently fell through to the broken computed default `acr${replace(namingPrefix,'-','')}${environment}` = `acrinvoicellmdev`. Fix: added `"sharedAcrName": {"value": "acrinvoicellmdev2"}` to `params.dev.json` — no change to `08-apps.bicep`'s default expression itself, since that formula is intentionally generic so prod can compute its own name later (per the param's own doc-comment); matches the precedent already in `benchmark-eval-job-only.bicep` (its own `acrName` param defaults to the same hardcoded `acrinvoicellmdev2`, with an identical rationale in its header comment). **Verified via what-if, not deployed**: `az bicep build --file 08-apps.bicep` — 0 errors (pre-existing, unrelated warnings only: `front-door.bicep` BCP081, `invoice-be.bicep` BCP037). `az deployment group what-if -g rg-invoice-llm-dev -f 08-apps.bicep` (params filtered to the template's own 42 declared names, mirroring `deploy-all.ps1`'s `New-StageParamArgs` filtering — the full `params.dev.json`/`params.dev.secrets.json` pair fails what-if outright with `InvalidTemplate` otherwise, same as `deploy-all.ps1`'s own header comment already documents): with the fix, every `registries[].server` entry across the 5 apps/jobs that carry one (`ca-invoice-be-dev`, `ca-invoice-fe-dev`, `ca-invoice-website-dev`, `ca-queue-worker-dev`, `caj-benchmark-eval-dev`) plus the 2 newly-created jobs (`caj-billing-lifecycle-dev`, `caj-overdue-sweep-dev`) resolves to `acrinvoicellmdev2.azurecr.io`, matching what is already live — **no diff on that field**. Re-ran the identical what-if with `sharedAcrName` removed (reverting to the broken computed default) as a causality check: all 5 existing resources' `registries[].server` flip to `~ "acrinvoicellmdev2.azurecr.io" => "acrinvoicellmdev.azurecr.io"` and both new jobs' `server` reads the broken value outright — conclusively isolating this one field as the fix's effect. This confirms the fix is correct and narrowly scoped: it changes only the ACR pull-credential `server` field, not drift #2 (`namingPrefix`) or drift #3 (scale rules), both of which remain fully present and unchanged in the same what-if output (`image` still shows the stale `backendImage`/`frontendImage`/etc. values from `params.dev.json`, itself a separate, already-known drift; `namingPrefix`-derived `existing` resource references still resolve to `id-invoice-llm-dev`/`kv-invoice-llm-dev`/etc., which do not exist). **Per explicit founder instruction, no `az deployment group create` was run against Stage 8** — this row stays `[~]` (partially resolved), not `[x]`, because drifts #2 and #3 are still open and this fix alone does not make a Stage 8 deploy safe.

  **Read-only drift audit, 2026-08-26, requested alongside the ACR fix — new findings, not previously measured resource-by-resource:** `az resource list -g rg-invoice-llm-dev` compared against every `namingPrefix`-derived var in `08-apps.bicep` (lines 152-162) confirms the `invoice-llm` vs. live `invoicellm` drift already on record above (from Gap 298's original 2026-08-23 finding) applies to **every one of them except `appInsightsName`**: `identityName` computes `id-invoice-llm-dev`, live is `id-invoicellm-dev`; `caeName` computes `cae-invoice-llm-dev`, live is `cae-invoicellm-dev`; `keyVaultName` computes `kv-invoice-llm-dev`, live is `kv-invoicellm-dev`; `openaiName` computes `openai-invoice-llm-dev`, live is `openai-invoicellm-dev`; `docIntelName` computes `docintel-invoice-llm-dev`, live is `docintel-invoicellm-dev`. `appInsightsName` is the one exception — its expression already strips hyphens (`appi-${replace(namingPrefix,'-','')}-${environment}`), so it correctly computes `appi-invoicellm-dev`, matching live. `storageAccountName` also strips hyphens the same way (`st${replace(namingPrefix,'-','')}${environment}` = `stinvoicellmdev`) but is still wrong against live `stinvoicellmdev2` — the separate `dev`-vs-`dev2`-style suffix drift already noted in this entry's Stage 7 sentence, confirmed still present. **Not fixed here, founder scope only approved the ACR half**: `storageAccountName` is an internal `var`, not an overridable `param` like `sharedAcrName` — the same params-file fix pattern does not apply, and identity/CAE/KeyVault/OpenAI/DocIntel are also all `var`s, not `param`s (`08-apps.bicep` lines 152-156), so **none** of these five can be fixed the way `sharedAcrName` was without either (a) converting each to an overridable param (a bicep-source change to `08-apps.bicep` itself, out of this task's approved scope) or (b) the broader `namingPrefix` reconciliation this entry already frames as a founder call. Flagging for a follow-up gap: this is the same root drift as drift #2 above, now independently reconfirmed live resource-by-resource rather than by the earlier `az deployment group what-if` summary alone.

  **Reconciliation 2026-08-26 (doc-reconciliation pass) — two Gap 298 entries existed; this is the canonical one.** The tracker carried Gap 298 **twice**, both `[ ]`, both opened 2026-08-23 while trying to deploy the Ops Digest Agent's scheduled job, both reporting the same `what-if` result (**3 to create, 4 to modify**) and the same three drifts — this one here under `## Open Items / Gaps`, and a second under `## Feature 23 — AI Control Tower, scoped 2026-08-21`. A Gap number must be unique per tracker (CONVENTIONS doc-ownership table), so this one is designated canonical, on the grounds that `## Open Items / Gaps` is where Gap entries live and this is the more complete of the two. **Neither body of text was deleted** (hard rule 4): the duplicate is annotated in place and cross-references this entry. **The duplicate's two non-overlapping facts, folded in here so nothing is lost by reading only this entry:** (1) **the workaround is a pattern, not a one-off** — the same root cause is already on file for `agent-eval-job-only.bicep` and for the split of the AI Control Tower workbooks into their own templates, i.e. *every* new scheduled job or monitoring resource in this repo has had to route around `08-apps.bicep` with a narrow standalone bicep file rather than fix this once (`workbook-cost-health-only.bicep`, `workbook-ai-control-tower-only.bicep` and `benchmark-eval-job-only.bicep` are the current instances; `ops-digest-job-only.bicep` was deleted with Feature 24 by Gap 311). (2) **why the root fix is harder than editing a param file** — fixing it means reconciling `params.dev.json` to *what CI/CD actually deploys*, and CI/CD updates images via `az containerapp update` directly, **bypassing bicep entirely**, so the template and the pipeline disagree about what the source of truth for an image tag even is. Both sides of that are unchanged as of 2026-08-26 and this gap stays `[ ]`; it is also carried as an open row in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s blockers table ("Stage 8 (`08-apps.bicep`) is unrunnable against dev"), which remains accurate.

- `[x]` **Gap 299 (BE/Infra): an AI-eval finding classified "critical" pages nobody — there is no immediate alert path for Feature 23's signals at all.** Opened 2026-08-23 while building Feature 24. `ops_digest_routing.classify()` correctly returns `"critical"` for `audit_job_failed` and for a sharp quality drop, and Feature 24's design says critical items are excluded from the digest *because the action group already paged them in real time*. That premise is true for Azure Monitor alerts and **false for AI-eval findings**: Feature 23 emits no Azure Monitor alert of any kind, so nothing notifies anyone. Rather than let a critical finding vanish into a correct-looking exclusion, `services/ops_digest.py::_critical_line()` lists AI-eval criticals **by name** with the text "and nothing else has notified anyone about it" (pinned by `test_an_ai_eval_critical_is_named_because_nothing_else_has_paged_it`) — which makes the digest honest but still means the notification is up to 6 hours late for the one class of finding the feature calls most urgent. **Closing this needs** a Log Analytics scheduled-query alert rule over the `agent_eval_run` / `ops_digest_run` custom events wired to the critical action group; which in turn needs `APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING` reaching a container that actually emits those events, i.e. it is blocked behind the same deploy as Gap 298.

  **Closed 2026-08-26 — the "blocked behind Gap 298" premise above was stale, re-verified live before doing anything else.** `agent_eval_run` already has real rows in `law-invoicellm-dev` — confirmed via `az monitor log-analytics query`: 35 rows, all `run_source == "golden"` (the nightly job's per-turn tag; `services/benchmark_artifacts.py::configure_run_source()` maps both `--run-label nightly` and `--run-label adhoc` to `run_source="golden"`, `predeploy` gets its own value), landed in one ~11ms batch on 2026-08-26T03:36:27Z — so no Stage 8/Gap 298 deploy was needed. New standalone template `infra/alert-ai-eval-critical-only.bicep` (same class as `workbook-cost-health-only.bicep` / `rbac-monitoring-cost-only.bicep`) creates one `Microsoft.Insights/scheduledQueryRules` (`kind: LogAlert`) named `alert-agent-eval-run-critical-dev`, querying `AppEvents | where Name == "agent_eval_run"` filtered to `run_source == "golden"`, with a `turns >= 20` guard (`services/ops_recommendation.py::MIN_GRADED_TURNS`) before judging anything. **Thresholds are the live workbook's own, not invented**: copied verbatim from `ops_recommendation.py`'s `SCORE_BANDS` red-bound values — `pass_rate<0.20`, `faithfulness<0.70`, `relevance<0.85`, `accuracy<0.40`, `context<0.50`, `orchestration<0.60` — the same numbers the AI Control Tower workbook's D1–D3 tiles and Gap 318's recommendation pass already use, so the alert and the panels can never disagree. **Per-run, not a trend**: `agent_eval_run` only gets new data once a day (the nightly job), so a multi-run requirement would delay a real finding for no benefit, matching Gap 318's own per-run check-and-flag design. `evaluationFrequency: PT6H` / `windowSize: P1D` (not `P1D`/`P1D` as first drafted — Azure rejected that live: *"Stateful rules can not run in a frequency greater than 12 hours"* since `autoMitigate: true`; fixed and redeployed), so each evaluation sees at most one nightly batch and `autoMitigate` means repeat 6-hourly checks against an unchanged run do not re-page. Wired to the existing **`ag-invoice-llm-dev`** action group (verified live via `az monitor action-group list -g rg-invoice-llm-dev`) — deliberately not the `-critical`/`-info` split, out of scope for this gap. **All 4 verification rungs, real Azure evidence:** (A) `az bicep build --file alert-ai-eval-critical-only.bicep` — 0 errors. (B) `az deployment group what-if -g rg-invoice-llm-dev -f alert-ai-eval-critical-only.bicep` — **1 to create, 54 to ignore**, exactly the new alert rule, nothing else touched. (C) `az deployment group create` — `provisioningState: "Succeeded"`. (D) `az monitor scheduled-query show -g rg-invoice-llm-dev -n alert-agent-eval-run-critical-dev` — pulled-back `criteria.allOf[0].query`/`threshold: 0.0`/`timeAggregation: "Count"`, `evaluationFrequency: "6:00:00"`, `windowSize: "1 day, 0:00:00"`, `kind: "LogAlert"`, `severity: 1` and `actions.actionGroups: [".../ag-invoice-llm-dev"]` all match the template exactly. **Closure check (informational, read-only)**: ran the alert's exact deployed KQL live against `law-invoicellm-dev` — **0 rows returned**, i.e. the alert would **not** fire today. Manually cross-checked the same run's raw aggregate: `pass_rate=0.257, faithfulness=0.797, relevance=0.974, accuracy=0.543, context=0.839, orchestration=0.870` on 35 turns — every metric is yellow-or-green against `SCORE_BANDS`, none crosses red, consistent with the empty result. **One discrepancy found and flagged, not acted on (out of scope for this gap)**: while verifying the action-group name to target, `az monitor action-group list` and `az monitor action-group show` found `ag-invoice-llm-dev-critical` and `ag-invoice-llm-dev-info` **now exist live** (a real `-critical` email receiver, `sbanerji@admsofttech.com`, no Teams webhook) — this contradicts this doc's and `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s current wording that the split "is not deployed" / "404s live". Not investigated or reconciled further here; a founder call on whether/when that split was actually deployed and whether anything should route to it is a separate item. `Prod_Invoice_LLM/infra/alert-ai-eval-critical-only.bicep` is the permanent record.

- `[x]` **Gap 300 (Infra/BE): Azure OpenAI/LLM calls are not tracked in `AppDependencies`, so no dependency-time breakdown can be correct for any LLM-heavy feature area** — opened 2026-08-24 during the founder's field-by-field Ops-page review, **fixed the same day; code-complete and locally verified, not deployed**.
