Before the fix, **re-confirmed live 2026-08-24** by direct KQL against `law-invoicellm-dev` (workspace `a0f26ce7-43d6-457d-9f7b-47e36af39a02`), not carried over from the earlier note: `AppDependencies | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d) | summarize count() by DependencyType` returns exactly five kinds — `InProc` 378,158, `Azure queue` 189,010, `postgresql` 55,705, `HTTP` 507, `Azure blob` 260 — and a filter for `openai` in `DependencyType`/`Target`/`Name` over the same 30 days returns **zero rows**. (A broader filter that also matches `cognitiveservices` returns 207 rows; those are Document Intelligence, not Azure OpenAI.) So every other outbound dependency this app makes is instrumented and the one that is almost certainly the dominant latency source for the Chat and Ingestion & Extraction areas is not. Cause is the same class as Gap 292 — the Azure Monitor distro auto-instruments `psycopg2`/`requests`/`urllib3` and the Azure SDKs, but the `openai`/LangChain client path produces no dependency span, and nothing in the app creates one by hand. Note this is *not* the same thing as the `llm_agent_call` custom event: that event exists and works (Feature 23 Phase 1) and records tokens/latency per call, but it lives in `customEvents`, not `AppDependencies`, so it cannot participate in a dependency-vs-request-duration breakdown. **Consequence, stated because it blocks a named build item**: the proposed "API perf: dependency-time breakdown per feature area, with recommendation text generated from whichever dependency dominates" panel cannot give a correct answer for LLM-heavy areas until this closes — it would either omit LLM time entirely or misattribute it as "app logic" time (the residual between total request duration and the sum of tracked dependencies). **Both fix options this entry originally proposed were checked against the real installed packages before choosing, not picked by preference.** Option (a) — enable an OpenTelemetry OpenAI instrumentation in `main.py`'s `configure_azure_monitor()` setup — **rejected on evidence**: `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` carry only the django/fastapi/flask/logging/psycopg2/requests/urllib/urllib3 instrumentations that `azure-monitor-opentelemetry` 1.8.9 pulls in; there is no `opentelemetry-instrumentation-openai`, `-openai-v2`, `openinference` or `traceloop` in the lock at all, so it would be a **new** dependency pinning its own `opentelemetry-instrumentation` (currently `0.64b0`, owned by the Azure distro), and it patches the `openai` SDK client only — covering neither the `ollama` provider nor the `MockInvoiceLLM` fallback this app really runs on, and sitting outside the "telemetry never raises" contract every emitter in `telemetry.py` follows. **Fix (option b)**: `telemetry.py` now opens one `SpanKind.CLIENT` span per LLM call from inside `tracked_llm_call()` — the wrapper that is *already* at every real call site (`agents/query_agent.py`, `agents/sage_orchestrator.py`, `agents/query_tools.py`, `agents/extraction_agent.py`, `agents/trainer_agent.py`, `routers/trainer.py`, `routers/dashboard.py`, `services/agent_eval.py`, `services/ops_digest.py`), so **no call site changed and no agent behaviour changed**. Four new functions: `resolve_gen_ai_system()` (→ `az.ai.openai` / `ollama` / `mock`, reusing `resolve_model_name()`'s mock detection so a mock-answered call is never labelled as a real Azure OpenAI dependency — the same rule that function already enforces for cost), `resolve_gen_ai_peer()` (endpoint **hostname only**, because the configured endpoint's query string is where `api-version` and in some SDK shapes a key travel), `_start_llm_dependency_span()` and `_end_llm_dependency_span()`. Span name is the semconv `chat {model}`; attributes are `gen_ai.system`/`gen_ai.operation.name`/`gen_ai.request.model`/`peer.service`/`server.address`, plus non-semconv `agent_name`/`tenant_id`/`request_id` (`agent_name` because `DependencyType` collapses every LLM call to one value, so the per-feature-area breakdown this gap blocks needs a grouping key in `customDimensions`), plus `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens`/`output_tokens`/`llm_calls` set at close when they are known. The span is *started*, not entered as the current span, deliberately: making it current would mutate OTel context across the `yield` of a contextmanager used at both sync and async call sites, while a plain start still reads the current context for its **parent** — which is the only part that matters, since that parent is the FastAPI request span. Fails closed like every other emitter here: the start path returns `None` on any exception, and the end path ends the span from its own `finally` even if annotating it threw. **Exporter contract read off the installed `azure-monitor-opentelemetry-exporter` 1.0.0b56, not assumed**: a CLIENT span exports as `RemoteDependencyData` (the `AppDependencies` table, `export/trace/_exporter.py:353-369`), `gen_ai.system` sets `DependencyType` to `GenAI | {value}` (`_GEN_AI_ATTRIBUTE_PREFIX`, `:120`) and is applied *after* the HTTP/DB/messaging branches so it wins over them, and `peer.service` sets `DependencyTarget` (`export/trace/_utils.py:148`). **Verified by execution, locally**: 9 new tests in `tests/test_telemetry.py` install a real OpenTelemetry SDK `TracerProvider` with an in-memory exporter and assert the actual span object — full file **23 passed**, adjacent `test_query_tools.py`/`test_agentic_sage.py`/`test_agent_eval.py`/`test_ops_digest.py` **277 passed**, `ruff check` clean on both changed files. One of those tests runs the recorded span through the exporter's own `_convert_span_to_envelope` — the same function a deployed container runs on export — and asserts `base_type == "RemoteDependencyData"`, `type == "GenAI | az.ai.openai"`, `target == "oai-invoicellm-dev.openai.azure.com"`, `success is True`; another asserts the span's parent is the surrounding request span (same `trace_id`, `parent.span_id` == the request's), which is the property that makes a dependency-vs-request-duration breakdown possible at all. **Honest scope note**: `tests/test_rag.py::test_process_crash_during_agent_leaves_no_orphan_user_message` fails in this working tree, and was confirmed **pre-existing** by re-running it against `git show HEAD:telemetry.py` restored in place — it is a stale test calling `post_chat_message()` without its required `background_tasks` argument, unrelated to this fix. **Not deployed** — same deploy blocker as Gap 292's fix; live `AppDependencies` will keep returning zero `openai`/GenAI rows until a backend image carrying this code ships. First live check after that deploy: `AppDependencies | where TimeGenerated > ago(1h) | summarize count() by DependencyType` should return a sixth kind, `GenAI | az.ai.openai`. Full write-up in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md` ("2026-08-24 — Gap 300" section + File Coordinates) and `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`, "New gaps found during Ops-page field-by-field review".

- `[x]` **Gap 301 (Infra): CPU-high and memory-high alerts fired on an averaged threshold alone, with no check on whether autoscale had already maxed out** — opened 2026-08-24 during the same Ops-page field review, **fixed, deployed and verified live the same day**. Before the fix, `modules/monitoring/alert-rules.bicep`'s `cpuAlerts`/`memoryAlerts` loops each carried a single criterion (CPU > 90%, memory > 85%) over a `PT15M` window. The window was already deliberate — 15 minutes exists so autoscale (Gap 290, which triggers at 85% for both) gets a chance to stabilise first, which filters brief scale-then-resolve blips — but a *sustained* 15-minute elevation still paged even when autoscale had correctly scaled out and was simply catching up rather than stuck. Founder's requirement: fire only when autoscale is genuinely maxed out and the condition still hasn't cleared. **Fix**: a second required criterion (`criteria.allOf`, i.e. AND) per alert — `{ name: 'ReplicasAtMax', metricName: 'Replicas', operator: 'GreaterThanOrEqual', threshold: app.maxReplicas, timeAggregation: 'Maximum' }` — with the loop's per-app record gaining a `maxReplicas` field fed by five new params (`backendMaxReplicas`/`workerMaxReplicas`/`frontendMaxReplicas`/`chromaDbMaxReplicas`/`websiteMaxReplicas`, defaults matching `08-apps.bicep`'s and `modules/data/chromadb.bicep`'s own maxReplicas). Separate params rather than a cross-stage output on purpose: Stage 9 deploys independently of Stage 8, so they are kept in sync by hand. `09-monitoring.bicep` threads the same five params into the `alertRules` module call. **Verified live after deploying, not just what-if'd**: `az monitor metrics alert show -g rg-invoice-llm-dev -n alert-ca-invoice-be-dev-cpu-high` returns two criteria — `CpuPercentage` Average > 90 **and** `ReplicasAtMax` Maximum >= 5 — with the same second criterion confirmed present on `alert-ca-invoice-be-dev-memory-high` (>= 5, alongside `MemoryPercentage` > 85), `alert-ca-invoice-fe-dev-cpu-high` (>= 2) and `alert-ca-queue-worker-dev-cpu-high` (>= 10), each threshold matching that app's real `maxReplicas`, all still on `windowSize: PT15M`. **Honest note on what else that deploy carried**: the same Stage 9 run also applied this session's earlier dual-action-group, 90%-threshold and PT5M→PT15M-window edits, which were pre-existing uncommitted drift in the file, not part of this fix — the live dev alerts simply had not been redeployed since those were written. Full pre-deploy reasoning in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`.

- `[x]` **Gap 302 (BE/AI): Trace-level observability does not exist — a full SAGE turn cannot be reconstructed from telemetry, only counted** — opened 2026-08-24, **never filed as a numbered gap before** despite being described as design intent since 2026-08-21. `telemetry.py::track_agent_call()` (`telemetry.py:206-224`) emits exactly ten flat per-call fields — `agent_name`, `model`, `tokens_in`, `tokens_out`, `tokens_total`, `latency_ms`, `status`, `tenant_id`, `request_id`, `trace_id` — and nothing else structural. `trace_id` is a bare correlation string pulled from `trace_id_ctx`; it groups events, it does not describe a turn. Nothing anywhere records the node sequence (`plan → act → plan → … → synthesize` vs. `→ clarify`), the tool calls with their **real results**, the generated SQL, or `stop_reason`. `stop_reason` is genuinely computed — `agents/sage_orchestrator.py:380` declares it on the state, `:784`/`:866`/`:918` set it (`tool_call_budget_exhausted`, `planner_step_budget_exhausted`, `clarification_requested`), `:1304` returns it — and then it is dropped: no telemetry field carries it and it never reaches `ChatMessage`. Same for the tool-call budget: `MAX_TOOL_CALLS = 4` (`agents/sage_orchestrator.py:105`) is enforced at `:851` but budget exhaustion is invisible outside a debugger. Net effect: several `llm_agent_call` events sharing one `trace_id` tell you a turn cost N tokens and took M ms; they cannot tell you *what the agent did*, which is the only thing that diagnoses a non-deterministic failure. Described as design/plan only in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s "Observability primitives — Run, Trace, Thread" section (lines 431-452, whose Trace row already says these properties are "currently invisible outside a debugger"), and the same doc already states the fix shape — extend the existing `tracked_llm_call()`/`track_agent_call()` primitive rather than build new instrumentation. **Not built.** Consequence worth naming: every eval and workbook surface Feature 23 has is therefore a per-call or per-run aggregate; there is no per-turn record to drill into when one goes wrong. **Closed in code 2026-08-24 — one `chat_turn` custom event per turn, carrying the real SQL and the real tool output. Not yet verified live, same deploy blocker as everything else built today.** `telemetry.py` gained `CHAT_TURN_EVENT_NAME`, a `ChatTurn` accumulator, `chat_turn_scope()`/`current_chat_turn()` and `track_chat_turn()`, all under the same never-raises contract as every other emitter in that file. `agents/query_agent.py::run_query_agent()` is now a thin Trace wrapper around a renamed `_run_query_agent()` body: it opens the scope, resolves the thread position, and attaches the accumulated record as a `turn_telemetry` key — on the line *after* `set_cached_answer()`, the same placement and the same reason as `judge_evidence` the day before, so the Redis payload keeps exactly the size it had. **The two things that made this more than a summary event.** (1) **Full content, per the founder's decision**: `generated_sql` is the real generated statement and `tool_output` is the real results table / retrieved chunk text — a turn whose SQL was subtly wrong but executed fine is not diagnosable from `sql_generated=true`. `tool_output` is deliberately the *same string* `judge_evidence["context"]` carries rather than a second rendering, so a Trace and a quality score for one turn are demonstrably about the same evidence, and `tool_output_chars` carries the **pre**-truncation length so a short result and a cut one are distinguishable without parsing the marker. The caps are `MAX_TURN_SQL_CHARS = 3000` / `MAX_TURN_TOOL_OUTPUT_CHARS = 12000`, asserted by test to be *equal to* `services/agent_eval.py`'s `MAX_QUERY_CHARS`/`MAX_CONTEXT_CHARS` rather than being new numbers. **This event now carries customer-adjacent content with no scrubbing and no retention policy of its own — flagged for security-tester and deliberately not reviewed here**; the specifics (what, where, how long, and the fact that the workspace retention is the only control that applies) are in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s "Trace and Thread as built" section. (2) **Every outcome emits, including the three that previously emitted nothing at all.** All three of `run_query_agent()`'s return paths were audited and fill the turn: the SAGE branch (no orchestrator change needed — it has *always* returned `stop_reason`/`tool_calls_made`/`tools_called` in `agentic{}` and every caller has *always* discarded them, which is exactly why `tool_call_budget_exhausted` was "invisible outside a debugger"), the cache hit (`status=cache_hit`, `llm_call_count=0`, `route="cached"` rather than a guessed original route — guessing from `generated_sql` is wrong for a declined SQL turn, which *is* cached with no SQL, and would file it under RAG), and the normal path with status set at each of its five real outcome points. Plus a fourth that is not a return path: both callers emit an errored turn event from their own `except`, because a failure can come from the commit or the Redis publish as well as from the agent. `routers/chat.py::post_chat_message()` and `queue_worker/handlers.py::handle_process_chat_job()` emit after their commit, at the same hook point as `submit_turn_judgement()`, and unlike judging it is **not flag-gated**. Two smaller pieces: `SqlGenerationOutcome` gained `attempts` (the turn-level round-trip count — `attempt` was already on each per-call event, but a Trace needs the total without a `summarize`), and Gap 237's routing override now sets `turn.route` to the route that really ran plus `stop_reason=route_override_followup`, because an event showing "RAG" for a turn that ran SQL is a worse record than none. **What this does NOT close, stated plainly:** nothing is deployed, so `chat_turn` has never reached `appi-invoicellm-dev`; **no workbook or panel reads it**, and there is no AI Control Tower workbook in the repo at all to add one to (see the Gap 303 entry and the corrected Phase 2 note in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md` — `infra/monitoring/ai_control_tower.workbook.json` was deleted in commit `bd6a255` and the spec doc's claim that it was "built 2026-08-21, not deployed" was stale, now corrected); and **prompt text is not captured** — the Run-level primitive lists the assembled system prompt, and `chat_turn` carries neither prompts nor the question nor the answer, which was not asked for and would be a materially larger data-exposure decision than the one taken. Verified: 13 new tests in `tests/test_telemetry.py`'s "Gap 302 (Trace) + Gap 303 half (a)" section (**39 → 52** measured by running that file alone before and after), five of them driving the real `run_query_agent()` SQL route end to end and three driving the real `handle_process_chat_job()`. Full `tests/test_*.py`: baseline re-established from a clean run *before* any change at **1411 passed / 4 failed / 7 skipped**; after, **1431 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped** (404s), and the final run was repeated after the last two edits with a byte-identical result. **The +20/-1 delta is deliberately not claimed as all mine**: 13 of the new passes are this work, and the rest — plus the disappearance of the 4th baseline failure (`test_agent_eval.py::test_every_expected_invoice_number_exists_in_the_seeded_fixture`) — came from parallel tasks that modified `tests/test_agent_eval.py`, `tests/test_ops_digest.py` and `tests/test_online_eval_signals.py` mid-session, confirmed by file mtimes falling between the baseline run and the final one. The 3 remaining failures are the long-standing pre-existing set (2× `test_connectors.py` needing a local Redis, 1× `test_rag.py` calling `post_chat_message()` without `background_tasks`), read off the run's own output rather than assumed. `ruff check` on all 9 touched files: 3 findings (2× `E402` in `queue_worker/handlers.py`, 1× `F401` in `tests/test_agentic_sage.py`), each confirmed pre-existing by running ruff against that file's `HEAD` version — zero new. One real defect the run caught: `tests/test_agentic_sage.py`'s flag-off parity golden failed on the new return key, and unlike `judge_evidence` it could **not** be kept as a golden record field — `turn_telemetry` carries a fresh `turn_id` UUID and a wall-clock `seconds_since_prev_turn` per run, so a golden containing it could never match twice; `run_case()` now projects three stable properties out of it and the parity test asserts those on all eight turns including the decline and the repair-loop failure.

- `[~]` **Gap 303 (BE/AI): Thread-level observability does not exist at all — nothing links the turns of a chat session or detects context drift** — opened 2026-08-24, **never filed as a numbered gap before**; the underlying need has been described since 2026-08-21 but only as future design work. There is no mechanism that reconstructs a session's turn sequence from telemetry, and no signal of any kind for context drift. `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s own words for this level are "Where this codebase is weakest" (line 445), with lines 451-452 stating plainly that "Thread-level capture needs new work: no mechanism today reconstructs a session's turn sequence with drift detection", and line 707 repeating it in the still-open list. **Important scoping correction, because the doc's phrasing invites the wrong reading**: Gaps 237 (follow-up dropped rows) and 276 (prior SQL reused after a topic change) are cited there as *examples of the failure class* a thread-level detector would have to catch — both are already-closed bugs, not open gaps, and closing them did not build any detector. The detector itself was never given a number until now. What exists today that a detector could consume: `ChatMessage` rows (real session history, populated by production traffic) and the golden-bank seed's ordered multi-turn scripts (10 follow-up links plus Gap 237's 2-turn capture, per `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s "Still open after this pass") — i.e. the bounded input is there, the detector is not. This is also the blocker under the Multi-turn evaluation tier the same doc scopes at line 474 as the "only tier that catches Gaps 237/276-shaped failures". **Not built, deliberately** — the doc's own position is that a general multi-turn detector is the hardest thing here and should be scoped down to fixed 2-3 turn scripts with pinned expectations before anything is promised. **Half closed in code 2026-08-24; the marker is `[~]` and not `[x]` on purpose, and the remaining half has been split out as its own number rather than left making this entry look half-done.** The original entry ran two distinct pieces of work together — *reconstructing* a session's turn sequence, and *detecting drift* in it — and only the first was in scope for this pass. **(a) Reconstruction: built.** `turn_index` (this turn's position within its session) and `seconds_since_prev_turn` ride Gap 302's `chat_turn` event as fields, so no second event type was needed; they come from `agents/query_agent.py::_session_turn_position()`, one query returning both, best-effort and never fatal (a Trace with no thread position is worth strictly more than a turn that fell over collecting one). `turn_index` counts **assistant** messages, not all messages, for a reason that is easy to get wrong: on the async queue path the user's row is committed before the handler runs and on the synchronous path it is not, so counting every row would make the same turn the 2nd on one path and the 1st on the other. Absent values stay absent rather than becoming 0 — a first turn genuinely has no predecessor, and a 0 would read as "the user sent two messages in the same instant". **(b) Session-level analytics at the founder's 30-minute idle cutoff: built as KQL, with no new scheduled component, and the decision not to build one is the substance here.** Feature 24's `scripts/ops_digest_job.py` runs every 6h, so a 30-minute idle definition genuinely could not ride it — a session ending at 09:05 would first be noticed at 12:00. The next option was a new `Microsoft.App/jobs` resource on a ~15-minute cron, and it was **rejected**: `chat_turn` already carries `session_id` and a timestamp on every turn including the declined, errored and cache-hit ones, so a session is fully reconstructible from the event stream and "the latest turn has no successor within 30 minutes" is `row_window_session(timestamp, 1d, 30m, session_id != prev(session_id))`, a built-in KQL operator. All three analytical needs the scoping identified — session length distribution, abandonment rate, turns per session — are a `summarize` over data already being emitted, against a new job's own bicep, image pull, deploy and Gap 298's Stage 8 blocker. The artifact is `infra/monitoring/chat_thread_sessions.kql` (Query A: one row per *sitting* with length/turns/how it ended; B: the three distributions per day; C: abandonment rate split by what it was abandoned on; D: the per-turn drill-down that only Gap 302 makes possible). Abandonment is defined narrowly on purpose — the sitting is over *and* its final turn did not give the user an answer — because counting a session that ended on a successful answer as abandonment is the easiest way to build a metric that goes up when the product gets more useful; `cache_hit` is deliberately not an abandonment condition either, since a cached answer is a real answer. The two thread-position sources are kept side by side rather than one being canonical: the emitted fields are per-`session_id` (a session returned to after two days is turn 9 of one long session) while `row_window_session` splits that same id into idle-separated sittings, which is what the cutoff *means* — Query A reports both so a divergence is visible rather than silent. **Consequence for Gap 305**: its "fold the missing `emit_online_signals()` caller into the same new job" contingency never applied, because no new job was built; a parallel task had in any case already wired that caller into `ops_digest_job.py` the same day, and neither `scripts/ops_digest_job.py` nor `services/online_eval_signals.py` was touched by this work. **What is explicitly NOT closed:** context-**drift detection** — the thing Gaps 237/276 are cited as the failure class for — is not built and is now **Gap 307**; nothing is deployed, so every query in the new .kql file returns zero rows today (unlike the `sage.*` queries this needs no feature flag, so the emptiness is the deploy, not the instrumentation); and the one honest limitation of the query-time approach is that an *alert rule* built on these queries evaluates on its own schedule, so the freshness of "abandoned" is that rule's evaluation frequency, not 30 minutes — irrelevant for a panel, not irrelevant for an alert. Verified together with Gap 302: `test_turn_index_and_the_idle_gap_are_measured_across_a_multi_turn_session` drives two real turns through `run_query_agent()` in one session with a backdated 45-minute gap (past the cutoff, i.e. proving the same `session_id` really can contain two sittings), and `test_a_first_turn_omits_the_thread_fields_rather_than_sending_zero` pins the absent-stays-absent rule at the emitter boundary.

- `[~]` **Gap 304 (BE/AI): every Feature 23 metric is single-sourced — production traffic has no quality signal and the golden bank has no cost/latency baseline** — opened 2026-08-24, **both halves now closed in code on 2026-08-24 (see the two closing paragraphs of this entry); neither half has a live producer yet, so the marker stays `[~]` and not `[x]`** by reading the actual emit paths, not the design doc. The intended design is that each tile field is dual-sourced (real traffic **and** golden-bank runs) so the two can be compared; in code, no field is. **(1) Cost and latency come from real traffic only** *(as opened; this half is now closed in code — see the end of this entry)*. They read `llm_agent_call` (`telemetry.py::track_agent_call`), which every production LLM call emits — and golden-bank runs were *deliberately excluded*: `services/benchmark_artifacts.py::configure_run_telemetry()` (lines 146-189 as of opening) attached the Azure Monitor exporter **late**, immediately before the aggregate mirror rather than at script import, and its own docstring gave the reason — configuring at import "would also start exporting every `llm_agent_call` event the benchmark's own turns produce, which is a real (if small) change in ingestion volume and cost that belongs in its own decision". So a benchmark run's per-call events never reach `customEvents` by design. **(2) Quality and component-quality come from golden-bank runs only.** They read the `agent_eval_run` table, and `AgentEvalRun` is constructed in exactly one non-test place in the entire repo — `scripts/run_agent_eval.py:800` (repo-wide grep: `models.py:581` defines it, `scripts/run_agent_eval.py:800` builds it, everything else is `tests/`). Its telemetry mirror `track_eval_result()` has the same single caller (`scripts/run_agent_eval.py:819`). No production code path — not `routers/chat.py`, not the orchestrator, nothing — ever writes a row. **Net effect, stated plainly**: production chat has *no* quality measurement at all, and the golden bank has *no* cost/latency baseline, so the one comparison the design exists to make ("is live quality drifting from the bank?" / "is the bank representative of live cost?") cannot be made from either side. **Not fixed.** Closing it needs a decision on each half — for cost/latency, whether to accept the benchmark's ingestion cost and export its per-call events under a run label (the `--run-label` plumbing already exists); for quality, an online scoring path that writes `agent_eval_run`-shaped rows (or a distinct table) from real turns, which is a product decision about judging live user traffic, not a wiring change. **Partial progress 2026-08-24 — the blocking prerequisite for half (1) is built; the gap itself is still open and the `[ ]` is deliberate.** Before a golden-bank run's per-call events could ever be allowed into `customEvents`, there had to be a way to tell them apart from real traffic — without one, letting them through would silently corrupt every production cost and latency number in the same dashboards, which is precisely why `configure_run_telemetry()` defers the exporter. That discriminator now exists: `telemetry.py` gained `RUN_SOURCE_PRODUCTION`/`RUN_SOURCE_GOLDEN`/`RUN_SOURCE_PREDEPLOY`, a `run_source_ctx` contextvar (same pattern as `tenant_id_ctx`/`request_id_ctx`/`trace_id_ctx`, defaulting to `production`), `set_run_source()`/`_resolve_run_source()`, and a `run_source` field on every `llm_agent_call`. **No production call site changed** — the default is what makes that correct. Eval/benchmark runs override it through the **existing** `--run-label` switch rather than a second flag: `services/benchmark_artifacts.py::configure_run_source(run_label)` maps `nightly`/`adhoc` → `golden` and `predeploy` → `predeploy`, and both `scripts/run_agent_eval.py` and `scripts/run_extraction_benchmark.py` call it immediately after `parse_args()`, before the first graded turn. An explicit `run_source=` keyword is popped before the `**extra_attributes` loop (that loop drops any key already in the attribute dict, so passing it as an extra would have mis-tagged the call with no error at all — pinned by `test_an_explicit_run_source_keyword_is_not_silently_dropped`). **What was explicitly NOT closed by that prerequisite pass:** `configure_run_telemetry()`'s deferred-export behaviour was **unchanged** — a benchmark run's per-call events still never reached `customEvents`, so the golden bank still had no cost/latency baseline, and half (2) (an online quality signal from real traffic) was untouched entirely. Both remained founder decisions *(the first of the two was taken and built later the same day — see the closing paragraph of this entry)*. Verified: 5 new tests in `tests/test_telemetry.py`'s "Gap 304 (partial)" section, file **33 passed**, full `tests/test_*.py` **1362 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped** with the 3 failures re-confirmed pre-existing by re-running them with the change stashed. One real ordering defect the run caught: `tests/test_run_extraction_benchmark_cli.py` drives the benchmark CLI *in process*, so its `configure_run_source()` leaves `golden` set for every test after it — correct for a script that owns its process, wrong as a test assumption, so the default-value test now runs inside an explicitly empty `contextvars.Context()`. **Half (1) closed in code, later the same day (2026-08-24) — the founder took the deferred decision and eval runs now export their own telemetry.** `configure_run_telemetry()` no longer waits for the mirror: both `scripts/run_agent_eval.py` and `scripts/run_extraction_benchmark.py` attach the exporter on the line *after* `configure_run_source()`, before the first graded turn, so a golden/predeploy run's own `llm_agent_call` events, its per-turn `agent_eval_run` events and its Gap 300 GenAI dependency spans all reach Azure tagged `golden`/`predeploy`. Three code changes made that safe rather than polluting: `track_eval_result()` resolves `run_source` as a first-class field (nothing set it before, so every eval-result event was untagged); `_start_llm_dependency_span()` carries `run_source` on the CLIENT span — **the load-bearing one**, because `DependencyType` collapses every LLM call in this app to `GenAI | az.ai.openai`, so without it an eval turn's `AppDependencies` rows are indistinguishable from a real user's and the event-level tag protects `customEvents` only; and the attach is **idempotent** (cached `_exporter_attached`), because `configure_azure_monitor()` is not — a second call attaches a second handler and exports every event twice, silently doubling the run's own cost figures, and the mirror block still calls it. The attach also passes `instrumentation_options` disabling `azure_sdk`/`psycopg2`/`requests`/`urllib`/`urllib3` plus `enable_live_metrics=False`/`enable_performance_counters=False`: an eval run really executes its generated SQL per graded turn and the nightly job's `replicaTimeout` is 5400s, so full auto-instrumentation would bury the LLM spans this exists to surface under DB/HTTP rows, and live metrics/perf counters are meaningless for a batch job. The five names are pinned by a test against the distro's own `_ALL_SUPPORTED_INSTRUMENTED_LIBRARIES`, because an unknown key there is silently ignored and a typo would leave the instrumentation on with no error. **One consequence that lands on every consumer, not just this code**: `services/agent_eval.py::_invoke_structured()` runs judge calls through the same `tracked_llm_call()` wrapper, so `eval.claim_decomposition`/`eval.faithfulness`/`eval.relevance`/`eval.accuracy`/`eval.persona`/`eval.combined_soft` are exported under the same tag — a "golden cost" rollup mixes grader cost with system-under-test cost unless it filters `agent_name !startswith "eval."`, and on the stored 20-case combined run that is 60 judge calls against 48 system-under-test calls. KQL-side filters (`llm_cost_rollup_nightly.kql`, `llm_cost_by_tool.kql`) were infra-devops's in parallel and are not part of this change. Verified: 14 new tests (`tests/test_telemetry.py` 33 → **39**, `tests/test_benchmark_artifacts.py` 29 → **35**, `tests/test_run_extraction_benchmark_cli.py` 10 → **12**; those three files **86 passed**), full `tests/test_*.py` **1376 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped** — exactly +14 against the previous run's 1362, with the same 3 pre-existing failures (2× `test_connectors.py` needing a local Redis, 1× `test_rag.py` calling `post_chat_message()` without `background_tasks`), read off the run output rather than assumed. `ruff check` clean on all 7 touched files. The dependency-span attribute was mutation-checked: deleting it fails exactly the three span tests and nothing else. Two tests execute rather than assert-by-reading — one runs the recorded span through the installed exporter's real `_convert_span_to_envelope` and asserts `run_source` survives into `customDimensions` (that conversion does drop attributes: `peer.service`/`server.address` are consumed into the target), the other drives the real `run_extraction_benchmark.main()` and asserts the observed order `configure_run_source` → `configure_run_telemetry` → first case. **What this does NOT close, stated plainly:** `caj-benchmark-eval-dev` **has never been deployed to Azure** (confirmed by the architect's scoping pass; `infra/benchmark-eval-job-only.bicep` has only ever been what-if'd at "1 to create"), so there is **no live producer of `golden`/`predeploy` events in Azure today** — this closes the code-level prerequisite, and "golden cost is now visible in the dashboard" would be false. Nothing is verified live, same deploy blocker as every other 2026-08-24 telemetry change. Half (2) — an online quality signal from real traffic — is untouched: `AgentEvalRun` is still built only at `scripts/run_agent_eval.py:800`, so `agent_eval_run` remains golden-bank-only, and that half stays a product decision about judging live user traffic. Full write-up in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`, "Gap 304 half (1) — an eval run now exports its own telemetry (2026-08-24)". **Half (2) closed in code, also 2026-08-24 — every production chat turn is now scored.** The founder took every open decision before any code was written: every turn is judged (no sampling), the mechanism is an **in-process hook after the response** rather than a deferred batch scorer, the row stores scores plus a `message_id` and **no customer question or answer text**, and `persona_score` is included as an accepted extra judge call. New `services/online_quality_judge.py` (`judge_turn()`/`submit_turn_judgement()`) runs the golden bank's own reference-free judge — `services/agent_eval.py::score_soft_metrics_combined()` plus `score_persona()` and the deterministic `score_orchestration()`, no new rubric anywhere — and writes an `agent_eval_run` row tagged `run_source=production` plus the matching `track_eval_result()` event. Hooked into **both** write paths, because both build their own `ChatMessage`: `routers/chat.py::post_chat_message()` after its `db_session.commit()`, and `queue_worker/handlers.py::_execute()` after its own commit and `complete_job()`. Both call the same function; the queue path submits rather than calling inline because `queue_worker/main_worker.py` releases the tenant's concurrency slot in a `finally` after the handler returns, so blocking there would throttle a tenant while scoring a turn they already have. **Why an in-process hook was the only option, read off what the app persists rather than assumed**: a RAG turn's faithfulness evidence is the *chunk text*, and `ChatMessage` stores citations (invoice id, vendor, page) and nothing else — the text never reaches Postgres at all, so a deferred scorer would have to re-run retrieval against a moved index or grade against an empty context, which `score_soft_metrics_combined()` correctly treats as a hard 0.00 faithfulness (a harness defect reported as a model result). So `run_query_agent()` gained a `judge_evidence` return key (`{route, context, executed_queries}`, built from the SQL route's `db_result` and the RAG route's chunk text, rendered in the *same* shape `scripts/run_agent_eval.py::_ToolOutputRecorder` uses, so a live score and a bank score are computed from identically-formatted input). It is attached on the line **after** `set_cached_answer()`: the Redis payload keeps exactly the size it had before, and a cache hit therefore returns no evidence key, which `judge_turn()` treats as skip — correct, because that identical answer was already judged when first produced. The same skip covers the SAGE branch and the router's error-fallback dict, neither of which is a model answer. **The schema decision, which was a real conflict and not a formality**: `question`/`actual_answer` were `NOT NULL` and the founder's rule is that no customer text is ever duplicated into this row. A separate lightweight table was rejected — every consumer (ops digest, online-eval signals, workbook quality tiles) already reads `agent_eval_run`, and comparing live against bank is the entire point of this gap, so a second table means a `UNION` at every call site to ask one question. Nullable-alone was rejected too: it would also permit a golden row with no text, which is a corrupt row. Built: both columns nullable **plus** `ck_agent_eval_run_text_or_message` — `message_id IS NOT NULL OR (question IS NOT NULL AND actual_answer IS NOT NULL)` — so a row either points at the message holding its text or carries the text itself, and the privacy rule is enforced by the database rather than by convention. Migration `a7c3d5e91f04` also adds `run_source` (`NOT NULL DEFAULT 'golden'`, so every existing row and every existing writer keeps its meaning with no data migration), `message_id` (**no FK**, matching this table's own `tenant_id`: a measurement must survive its subject being deleted, and a cascade from `chatmessage` would silently rewrite quality history the first time a user deletes a thread) and `idx_agent_eval_run_source_time`. `notes` gets the same treatment as the text columns for a reason that is easy to miss: `EvalScores.note_text()` embeds unsupported claims *verbatim*, and those are sentences lifted out of the answer — it is dropped for a structural summary, pinned by a test. **What a production row cannot say, by construction, and this is correct rather than a bug**: `accuracy_score` and `context_score` are always NULL (both need a reference answer / known-correct invoice set that live traffic does not have), so `pass` is a **reduced** predicate — the same `decide_pass()`, which only grades dimensions that produced a number, hence faithfulness+relevance where a golden row is faithfulness+relevance+accuracy. The two pass rates are not comparable, which is stated on the column in `models.py`, in the judge module's docstring, and — the part that actually enforces it — in the consumer filters below. `llm_call_count` is 0 meaning "not counted" (counting a turn's round-trips needs the eval harness's `_counting_llm_calls()` patch, which does not belong in a request path, and the row's own notes say so); `latency_ms` is real wall clock, measured at both call sites. **A judge outage writes no row at all** — `decide_pass()` returns False for "nothing could be graded", which is right for a nightly harness and wrong here, since an outage across live traffic would otherwise render as a production quality collapse; writing nothing makes it show up as missing volume, which is what it is. **Two consumer fixes shipped in the same change, both regressions otherwise**: `services/ops_digest_collect.py::_eval_window_stats()` now filters `run_source != production` — without it every mean blends two non-comparable populations, and worse, Feature 24's `audit_job_failed` finding ("rows in the baseline, none in this window", i.e. the nightly job died) **would never fire again**, because production rows are present in every window by definition. `services/online_eval_signals.py::_eval_run_notes()` was **checked before being changed** and the finding there is narrower: both consumers reduce the list to notes matching `stop_reason=` before counting anything, and production notes carry no such marker, so no *rate* would have moved — but `budget_exhaustion_rate`'s `detail["eval_rows_in_window"]` counts the unfiltered list and would have grown with live traffic while the denominator stayed flat, reading as "the harness ran 4000 turns and 12 had a stop reason". Both filters are written as `!= production` rather than `== golden`, so a future predeploy run that persists rows still counts as the bank. **Cost, stated rather than discovered later**: two extra billable model calls per judged turn (`eval.combined_soft`, `eval.persona`), which is why `config.py::ENABLE_PRODUCTION_QUALITY_JUDGE` defaults **False** — the first flag in that file whose argument is per-turn spend rather than behaviour risk; the check sits in `submit_turn_judgement()`, so with it off nothing is imported and no thread is touched. Half (1)'s judge-cost caveat now applies to production too: both judge calls ride `tracked_llm_call()`, so a production cost rollup that does not filter `agent_name !startswith "eval."` attributes grader cost to the product. One trade-off named rather than found later: judging occupies one of `_chat_background_pool`'s 8 workers for two model calls, so with the queue path also on, a burst can leave queued chat jobs waiting behind judge calls — the pool is reused deliberately (a second executor would double the thread budget for the same class of work) and the flag is the mitigation. Verified: **28 new tests** (20 in the new `tests/test_online_quality_judge.py`, 3 in `test_rag.py`, 2 in `test_chat_queue.py`, 2 in `test_ops_digest.py`, 1 in `test_online_eval_signals.py`), full `tests/test_*.py` **1404 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped** — exactly +28 against half (1)'s 1376, with the same 3 pre-existing failures (2x `test_connectors.py` needing a local Redis, 1x `test_rag.py` calling `post_chat_message()` without `background_tasks`, re-confirmed from its `TypeError` in this run's own output rather than assumed). `ruff check` clean on the 12 touched files; the 6 findings that remain inside those files were each confirmed pre-existing by running ruff against that file's `HEAD` version. The migration was **applied, not just written**: a full `alembic upgrade head` on SQLite is impossible in this repo (it fails at the *pre-existing* revision `71d18e2c3349`, unrelated to this change), so the revision was run against a scratch SQLite DB built to the exact pre-migration `agent_eval_run` shape and stamped at `c4a91e77b208` — `alembic heads` reported the single head `a7c3d5e91f04`, the upgrade applied, and the post-state was read back from `sqlite_master`: both new columns present, the two text columns nullable, the CHECK present, **all five original indexes still there** plus the three new ones (batch mode recreates the table on SQLite, so index survival is a real thing to check), and the pre-existing golden row defaulted to `run_source='golden'`; a production-shaped row inserted OK while a row with neither text nor pointer was rejected by the CHECK; `alembic downgrade -1` round-tripped (production row deleted, `NOT NULL` restored, original index set intact); and the Postgres DDL rendered offline is metadata-only (2x `ADD COLUMN`, 3x `CREATE INDEX`, 2x `DROP NOT NULL`, 1x `ADD CONSTRAINT`). One real defect the run caught: `tests/test_agentic_sage.py`'s flag-off parity golden failed on the new return key — **not** fixed by regenerating the golden (that would silently absorb anything else that had drifted since it was recorded); `run_case()` now splits `judge_evidence` into its own record field, so "the pipeline is unchanged" keeps meaning the answer/SQL/citations/prompts, and the parity test now additionally asserts the payload is present on every route. **What this does NOT close, stated plainly**: the flag is **off**, so **nothing is judged live today and no production row exists anywhere** — this is the code and the schema, not a live capability, exactly like half (1)'s undeployed job; there is **no backfill** of historical turns and there cannot be, because their evidence no longer exists (which is why the hook is in-process); the **SAGE path is not covered** (`ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE` is off, and `run_agentic_sage()` returns no evidence payload, so those turns are skipped rather than mis-scored); **no workbook panel reads the new rows** — the quality tiles were built against golden-bank rows and have not been re-pointed or given a `run_source` split, so reading a production number off them is not possible today and would be wrong if they were changed to blend the two; and **security/retention review was deliberately not done here**, flagged for security-tester: the row holds no customer text by design, but it is a new per-turn record keyed to a `chatmessage` id with no FK and no retention policy of its own, and the judge prompts send question/answer/evidence text to the model provider on every turn, which is a different data-flow volume from the bank's fixed 87 cases. Full write-up in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`, "Gap 304 half (2) — every production turn is now scored (2026-08-24)". **Correction to half (2), same day — a live attribution defect in the code above, found and fixed while building Gap 302.** As shipped, every judge call this half makes emitted an `llm_agent_call` event with `trace_id=""`, `tenant_id=""` and `request_id=""`. Cause: `submit_turn_judgement()` hands `judge_turn` to `routers/chat.py::_chat_background_pool`, and `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor.submit()` copies **no contextvars** — so `eval.combined_soft` and `eval.persona` ran on a thread where all three of Feature 19's correlation vars read empty, and a production quality score could not be joined back to the turn that produced it. That join is the one thing this module exists to make possible, so this was not cosmetic. The same defect covered the *entire* queued turn on the `ENABLE_ASYNC_CHAT_QUEUE` path, not just its judging: `routers/chat.py` submits `handle_process_chat_job` to the same pool. **Fixed by explicit propagation**, matching `queue_worker/main_worker.py::_process_message`'s established pattern (bind the correlation vars at the top of the unit of work) rather than by `contextvars.copy_context().run(...)` — copying the whole context would also carry the OpenTelemetry span context and silently re-parent the queued turn's GenAI dependency spans under an HTTP request that has already returned 202, a larger semantic change than the one being asked for. New `utils/logging_config.py::correlation_context()` binds `tenant_id`/`request_id`/`trace_id` for a block; `handle_process_chat_job()` and `judge_turn()` both gained `trace_id=`/`request_id=` parameters, and `judge_turn()` is now a binding wrapper around a renamed `_judge_turn()`. One deliberate difference from `main_worker`, which sets and never resets: `correlation_context()` **releases** on the way out, because that pool is shared between queued chat jobs and the online judge and a leaked value would attribute one tenant's judge call to whichever tenant's chat job ran on that thread previously. **Behaviour change stated rather than shipped silently**: worker-path telemetry that previously reported `trace_id=""` now reports the originating request's real trace id, so any saved query or portal tile that used empty-vs-populated `trace_id` to separate request-path from worker-path events will stop separating them and needs a different discriminator (nothing in the repo does this — checked — but a portal-side saved query is not visible from here). Pinned by two real tests rather than by reading the code: `test_the_queue_path_binds_the_originating_requests_correlation_ids` observes all three contextvars from *inside* a fake agent driven by the real `handle_process_chat_job()` (proving them bound for every `tracked_llm_call` at once, since that is what the wrapper reads) and additionally asserts they are released afterwards; `test_the_online_judge_runs_with_the_turns_correlation_ids_bound` does the same from inside the judge's own scorer. Neither half of Gap 304 changes status from this — it is a correctness fix to half (2)'s telemetry, not new capability, and the flag is still off.

  **2026-08-26 — the flag is now on, live, on `ca-invoice-be-dev`, at the founder's request, so the workbook's Production Scores panel (Section G, `g1`/`g2`) can start showing real production quality-judge results.** Confirmed before touching anything: `ENABLE_PRODUCTION_QUALITY_JUDGE` was declared nowhere in `Prod_Invoice_LLM/infra/` (repo-wide grep, zero hits) — it only existed as the Python default above, so the live container had no override and the flag was genuinely off. `ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE` (checked as a possible precedent for how a boolean flag reaches this bicep) turned out never to have been a real bicep-declared env var either (`git log -S` across all `*.bicep` history — zero diff hits, only ever a code comment); the actual pattern followed instead is the one `payuMode`/`sharedAcrName` already use — a top-level param on `08-apps.bicep` with a safe default, threaded into `invoice-be.bicep`'s module call, overridden per environment in `params.dev.json`/`params.prod.json`. **Mechanism decided by reading, not guessing**: `.github/workflows/deploy-dev.yml`'s `deploy-backend` job calls `_deploy-service.yml`, whose only container-app-mutating step is `az containerapp update --image <tag>` — an image-tag-only bump with no bicep involved, confirmed by reading the workflow file directly, so a bicep-only change would have sat inert on every future push and Azure would keep the flag off regardless of what the source declared. A full Stage 8 (`08-apps.bicep`) deploy was out of scope (this session's own established rule — unaudited drift risk, rolls back live scale rules). The mechanism used: `az containerapp update --name ca-invoice-be-dev --resource-group rg-invoice-llm-dev --set-env-vars ENABLE_PRODUCTION_QUALITY_JUDGE=true` directly against the live app — narrow and safe because it is a single additive env var, not a structural change — **plus** the bicep source updated in the same pass (`invoice-be.bicep` gains an `enableProductionQualityJudge` param, default `false`, rendering `ENABLE_PRODUCTION_QUALITY_JUDGE` into the container's `env` list; `08-apps.bicep` threads it through, also default `false`; `params.dev.json` sets it `true` for dev only) so a future full deploy stays consistent with the live CLI change instead of silently reverting it — this addresses the founder's standing rule that every env var's value must be visible in `params.dev.json`, not live only via an ad-hoc CLI set with no params-file record. `params.prod.json` is untouched, so prod stays off by default at the bicep-parameter level. `az bicep build` on `08-apps.bicep` and on `invoice-be.bicep` standalone both compiled clean (two pre-existing, unrelated warnings only). **Verified live**: the CLI update produced revision `ca-invoice-be-dev--0000087`, confirmed `provisioningState: Succeeded` and the revision `Healthy`/`Running` at 100% traffic; `az containerapp show`'s `properties.template.containers[0].env` lists `ENABLE_PRODUCTION_QUALITY_JUDGE` = `"true"`; container logs show clean startup with no errors, health/readiness probes passing. **Not verified — a genuine gap, not glossed over**: no real production `agent_eval_run` row exists yet. A live Log Analytics query (`AppEvents` in `law-invoicellm-dev`, `Name == "agent_eval_run"`, `run_source == "production"`) at the time of this edit returns rows only with `run_source: golden` (from the concurrent F23 3-way model comparison work), zero with `run_source: production` — expected, since flipping the flag does not itself generate a chat turn, and this agent cannot generate an authenticated turn against `ca-invoice-be-dev` itself (its ingress is internal-only, `external: false`, and reaching it needs a real Clerk-authenticated browser session). **Founder follow-up**: send one real chat message through the app, then re-run that query — a production row should appear within minutes tagged `run_source: production`, with `accuracy_score`/`context_score` both NULL by the design this entry's half-(2) section already documents. Cost/latency tradeoff restated for the record per the task's own instruction, not newly discovered: judging occupies one of `_chat_background_pool`'s 8 workers for two model calls per turn; this is a dev environment with low traffic, so accepted as-is. Also updated: `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s "What is real data today vs. structurally empty" table, Section G row.

- `[~]` **Gap 305 (BE/AI): `emit_online_signals()` has zero callers — all five online-eval signals are permanently unpopulated no matter how much real traffic exists** — opened 2026-08-24, **the zero-callers problem itself is closed in code the same day (see the final paragraph of this entry); `[~]` and not `[x]` for one reason only — the job that now calls it has never been deployed to Azure, so no `online_eval_signal` event exists anywhere yet**. Confirmed by repo-wide grep rather than inferred: `emit_online_signals` appears exactly five times in the codebase — its definition (`services/online_eval_signals.py:715`), its own `__all__` entry (`:776`), and three references inside `tests/test_online_eval_signals.py` (`:34`, `:548`, `:578`/`:595`). No scheduled job, no ACA cron, no request path, no startup hook calls it. The function's own docstring says so in bold (`:729`): "**Not wired to anything yet, stated plainly.**" **What makes this worth a number rather than a footnote**: the source data is *not* missing. `ChatMessage`/`ChatFeedback` are populated continuously by real production chat traffic, and `compute_online_signals()` reads them in SQL correctly — the only thing absent is the step that converts a computed window into `online_eval_signal` events. So all five signals (clarification rate, zero-result rate, thumbs-down clustering, slow-turn rate, budget-exhaustion rate) render empty forever, and that empty state means "nothing has run", not "no problems found" — a distinction that has to be read off panel text rather than the data. **Not fixed.** Closing it needs a caller on a schedule; the cheapest honest option is adding it to an existing scheduled job (Feature 24's `scripts/ops_digest_job.py` already runs every 6h and already reads online-eval signals through `services/ops_digest_collect.py`), which avoids a new ACA job resource and therefore avoids Gap 298's Stage 8 blocker. Related but distinct from Gap 304: that gap is about which *sources* feed a metric; this one is about a computed metric never being emitted at all. **Partial progress 2026-08-24 — exactly one of the five signals now has a live telemetry source; `emit_online_signals()` still has zero callers and the `[ ]` is deliberate.** `agents/query_agent.py::run_sql_generation_loop()` already detected the zero-result condition (`if db_result == NO_RECORDS_FOUND`) and emitted nothing there. It now records `zero_result` and `zero_result_fallback_recovered` on `SqlGenerationOutcome`, and `run_query_agent()`'s SQL route carries both onto its **existing** `chat.sql_summary` `tracked_llm_call` — a flag on an existing event, not a new event type. `chat.sql_summary` is the carrier rather than `chat.sql_generation` because that block deliberately closes at `.invoke()` (so its `latency_ms` stays model time and never absorbs query execution) and the row count is not known yet when it is emitted; the summary event is emitted exactly once per turn whose SQL actually executed — a declined turn or one that failed all three attempts never reaches it — so `countif(zero_result) / count()` over that `agent_name` is a well-formed rate with no separate denominator to build. The second field is free at the same point and is what separates "the generated SQL was wrong but the deterministic invoice-number fallback found the row" from "there is genuinely no such invoice". **What is closed:** `zero_result_rate` is now measurable from **Log Analytics on real production traffic**, with no Postgres access, no scheduled job and no new infrastructure — where before the only route was `services/online_eval_signals.py` scanning `chat_message.content` after the fact. **What is explicitly NOT closed:** this is a *different mechanism* from `emit_online_signals()`, so it does **not** populate the `online_eval_signal` events the `ai_control_tower` workbook's online panel reads — that panel is still empty for all five signals; the other four (clarification rate, thumbs-down clustering, slow-turn rate, budget-exhaustion rate) still have no source at all; and the flag covers the **default chat SQL route only** — `agents/query_tools.py`'s `identify_invoices`/`aggregate` share the same loop and get the flags on their outcome, but neither makes a follow-up LLM call, so the SAGE path (`ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE`, default off) has no event of its own to ride. A scheduled caller for `emit_online_signals()` is still the fix for this gap. Verified: 5 new tests in `tests/test_telemetry.py`'s "Gap 305 (partial)" section, three of them driving the real `run_query_agent()` SQL route end to end. **The scheduled caller was built later the same day (2026-08-24) — `emit_online_signals()` no longer has zero callers, and all five signals now have a live emission path.** The caller is `scripts/ops_digest_job.py::main()` (Feature 24's digest job) rather than a new Container App Job, which is the option this entry itself named as cheapest and honest: that job's cadence was **verified in the bicep rather than taken from this gap's text** — `infra/08-apps.bicep::opsDigestCron` is `0 1,7,13,19 * * *` UTC, four runs a day six hours apart, matching `OPS_DIGEST_WINDOW_HOURS=6` by construction — and it already reads these same five signals through `services/ops_digest_collect.py::_collect_online_signal_items()`, so the DB session, the window arithmetic and the telemetry exporter attach all already existed. A job of its own would have needed its own bicep, its own image pull and its own deployment (and would have inherited Gap 298's Stage 8 blocker) for one `track_online_signal()` call per signal. **Two new helpers in the job, both fail-soft**: `_compute_online_signals()` runs inside the existing `run_ops_digest()` try/finally — while the session is still open — and returns `(None, None)` on any exception; `_emit_online_signals()` swallows anything the mirror raises. Neither can change the job's exit code, its digest, its delivery or its `ops_digest_run` event. **The placement is the load-bearing part and is pinned by an ordering test rather than by reading the source**: emission happens *after* `configure_telemetry()`, because `track_online_signal()` logs through the `invoice_be_telemetry` logger and that logger only carries an Application Insights handler once `configure_azure_monitor()` has attached one — emitting at collection time (the obvious place, since that is where the signals are already computed) would produce stdout lines and **nothing in `customEvents`**, i.e. a state indistinguishable from the gap being unfixed. It is also after `track_ops_digest_run()`, so a broken mirror cannot cost the run event, and it fires on **every** run including `--dry-run` and a clean window that delivers nothing, for the same reason the run event does. The signals are **recomputed** rather than lifted off the collection pass (`_collect_online_signal_items()` keeps only `signals.breaches` and discards the object; threading it out would mean adding a field to `DigestCollection`, `build_digest()` and `OpsDigestResult` that one line reads) — five windowed reads over six hours, cheap next to the LLM synthesis call the job already makes — and the window is taken from `result.window_start`/`window_end`, so the events describe exactly the window the digest reported. **One real defect the wiring exposed and fixed**: `telemetry.track_online_signal()` cast `window_days` with `int()`, and every caller this creates passes a *fraction* of a day (six hours = 0.25), so every event the scheduled emitter produced would have carried `window_days=0` — a zero-length window, worse than omitting the field. Now `round(float(window_days), 6)`; whole numbers still compare equal (`7.0 == 7`) so the pre-existing `window_days=7` assertion was untouched. A run with **no DB session** emits no events at all rather than five zero-denominator ones — "measured, nothing found" and "never measured" must not render identically. Verified: **8 new tests** (7 in `tests/test_ops_digest.py` driving the real `main()` against a real SQLite DB with real `ChatMessage`/`ChatFeedback` rows, 1 in `tests/test_online_eval_signals.py`), the three files `test_ops_digest.py`/`test_online_eval_signals.py`/`test_telemetry.py` **143 passed**, full `tests/test_*.py` **1412 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped** — exactly +8 against Gap 304 half (2)'s 1404, with the same 3 pre-existing failures (2x `test_connectors.py` needing a local Redis, 1x `test_rag.py` calling `post_chat_message()` without `background_tasks`, read off this run's own `TypeError` rather than assumed). Mutation-checked both ways rather than assumed: deleting the `_emit_online_signals(...)` call from `main()` fails exactly 4 of the new tests and nothing else, and restoring `int(window_days)` fails exactly the 2 window tests. `ruff check` clean on all 5 touched files. **What this does NOT close, stated plainly:** `caj-ops-digest-dev` **has never been deployed** — that is the sole reason Feature 24 itself is still `[~]` — so the caller runs on every invocation of a job that never runs, and **no `online_eval_signal` event has reached Azure**; "the online panel now has data" would be false. **No workbook reads these events either**: a repo-wide search of `infra/` for `online_eval_signal` returns nothing, so the panel described in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s "Workbook — the second pass" is not in any deployed workbook JSON. And the module's own two measurement gaps are untouched by wiring an emitter — `stop_reason` still never reaches `ChatMessage` (so `budget_exhaustion_rate` still measures the offline harness only), turn latency is still a row-timestamp delta rather than a timer, and `clarification_rate` will read near-zero because `ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE` is off, which is a configuration fact and not a quality result. Full write-up in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`, "Gap 305 — `emit_online_signals()` has a scheduled caller (2026-08-24)". **One of those two measurement gaps narrowed later the same day, from Gap 302's side rather than this one's**: `stop_reason` still never reaches `ChatMessage`, so nothing above changes — but it is now on the `chat_turn` telemetry event for every turn, including the SAGE path's `tool_call_budget_exhausted`/`planner_step_budget_exhausted`. So a budget-exhaustion rate is computable from Log Analytics (`countif(stop_reason == "tool_call_budget_exhausted") / count()` over `chat_turn`) with no Postgres access, which is a *different mechanism* from `emit_online_signals()` and does **not** populate the `online_eval_signal` events — exactly the same relationship the `zero_result` flag already has to this gap. Turn latency also stops being a row-timestamp delta on that event specifically: `chat_turn.latency_ms` is a real `perf_counter` measurement taken by both callers. Neither changes this entry's status.

  **2026-08-26 — the two signals Gap 316 was said to have killed were investigated before building the `Microsoft.App/jobs` resource, on a founder instruction to fix the dead signals first. Result is a split verdict, and Gap 316's own wording turns out to be half wrong.** Gap 316's entry says `services/online_eval_signals.py`'s `clarification_rate`/`budget_exhaustion_rate` "parse `stop_reason`/clarification state only SAGE ever produced and are now *permanently* degenerate". That is exactly right for the first and an overstatement for the second, and the difference decides whether a number on Section F is worth reading. **Frozen-path check first, because Gap 316 is SAGE work**: `active-work.md` freezes "SAGE Phase 3 — gated on Gap 310's real-world result; 4 product decisions deliberately unresolved (see `feature_21_sage.md`)". **No collision** — Gap 316 deleted `agents/sage_orchestrator.py`, the `ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE` flag and `docs/feature_21_sage.md` on 2026-08-25, the same day `active-work.md` was last written, so there is no SAGE code, flag or doc left for this work to touch; everything below is confined to `services/online_eval_signals.py` and its test file. (Recorded as a doc discrepancy for the founder, not resolved here: that frozen-path bullet points at a file Gap 316 deleted.) **(1) `budget_exhaustion_rate` is dead forever and is NOT fixable — retiring it is a product decision, not a bug fix.** Its numerator *and* denominator come only from `stop_reason=` fragments in `AgentEvalRun.notes`; Gap 316 removed that fragment from `scripts/run_agent_eval.py`'s `notes` join (the surviving comment at that spot says so), so no row written from 2026-08-25 onward can ever match. The measured *concept* is gone too, not merely unwritten — `MAX_TOOL_CALLS` and `tool_call_budget_exhausted` were the deleted planner loop's, and a repo-wide grep finds **zero live code files** carrying either name (only this module, its tests, old `agent_eval_output*.json` fixtures and docs). Every candidate surviving source was checked before concluding, not assumed: `ChatMessage` has no `stop_reason` column (`models.py:186-211` — `status`/`error_message` describe a crash, not a budget), and the one live source that *does* carry a real per-turn `stop_reason` is the `chat_turn` telemetry event, which is Log Analytics and therefore out of this Postgres-only module's reach by design. **(2) `clarification_rate` did NOT die.** Its headline `value`/`numerator`/`denominator` are computed by `looks_like_clarification()` over `chat_message` assistant rows and never had a SAGE dependency; only the three `offline_*` keys in its `detail` read the dead notes. The real damage was in its **caveat**, which is the string that reaches the panel and the thing a reader uses to decide whether to believe the rate: it said a near-zero rate "is expected today and is a configuration fact, not a quality result" *because `ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE` is off* — a flag that no longer exists, so the text would have taught a reader to dismiss a genuine rising clarification rate as a config artefact. The population is real rather than theoretical: Feature 23 Track 2's judge runs caught the **default** route asking a clarifying question of its own accord, one that leaked raw SQL into the answer (Gap 294, recorded at `agents/query_agent.py`'s scrubber comment), and `agents/query_agent.py:1584` states plainly that this route "has no clarifying-question step" — so anything counted here is the model choosing to ask rather than answer. **Change made — deliberately narrow, no behaviour change, no design decision taken.** `services/online_eval_signals.py` only: corrected the module docstring (new "Gap 316 killed one of these outright" section), the `STOP_REASON_*` constant comments, the `_CLARIFICATION_PATTERNS` comment and `_eval_run_notes()`'s docstring (all four named deleted code), and both signals' `caveat` text. Neither signal was deleted, no computed value changed, no field was added to the `online_eval_signal` event and the workbook JSON was not touched — dropping a signal from the five, adding an event field, and editing a deployed panel are the founder's call and infra's respectively, not this pass's. **Verified by execution**: 4 new tests in a "Gap 305 / Gap 316" section of `tests/test_online_eval_signals.py`, driving the real functions against a real session — one pins that a `notes` string reproduced field-for-field from `run_agent_eval.py::persist()`'s *current* join yields `denominator == 0` / `value is None` (never `0.0`) while `eval_rows_in_window == 5`, i.e. the rows are read and the signal is dead rather than the query being broken; one pins that a *historical* `stop_reason=` row still reads correctly, so "clean up the dead constants" cannot silently change every past window's answer; one pins that `clarification_rate`'s headline rate still measures live traffic with zero eval rows present; one pins the corrected caveat. `pytest tests/test_online_eval_signals.py tests/test_emit_online_signals_job.py -p no:randomly` → **51 passed** (47 baseline + 4). **Mutation-checked rather than assumed**: reintroducing `stop_reason=` into that reproduced note string fails **exactly** the 2 budget/offline tests and nothing else. `ruff check` clean on both files. **Honest evidence note (hard rule 2)**: this is a SQLite run and no Postgres run was made — stated rather than glossed. No SQL, filter or computation changed in this pass, so there is no dialect-sensitive behaviour to re-verify; the Postgres-fidelity risk this rule exists for does not apply to caveat text and its assertions. **Two mislabelled *deployed* panels found while tracing the consumer, reported and NOT changed (infra scope, needs a founder/infra call):** (a) `infra/monitoring/ai_control_tower_workbook.json`'s `f1-breached-signals` tile computes `Status = iff(latest_breached == "1", "breached", "ok")` — it keys off `breached` alone and never looks at `value`, so a signal with a permanently empty denominator renders **green "ok"** forever. That is precisely the failure mode `SignalResult`'s own docstring exists to prevent ("a dashboard that conflated them would show a healthy green on the day ingestion stopped"); the module honours the contract on `value`, the panel reads the wrong field. (b) `b6-stop-reasons` already queries live production `chat_turn.stop_reason` — which **is populated today**, with `sql_attempts_exhausted`/`sql_declined`/`sql_summary_failed`/`rag_answer_failed`/`chat_answer_failed`/`route_override_followup` from `agents/query_agent.py` plus `agent_raised`/`queue_handler_raised` — but hard-codes `Detail = "SAGE-only field — structurally empty while ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE=false"`, telling a reader real data is empty. **Founder decision this gap is now waiting on, stated as a recommendation and not decided here**: recommend **(a) drop `budget_exhaustion_rate`, ship the emitter as a four-signal job**, because the successor measurement already exists, is already deployed and needs no backend code — abnormal-stop behaviour is readable from `chat_turn.stop_reason` in the workbook, which is a better source than the one that died (a real instrumented field on every live turn, versus a regex over prose covering only the eval harness). Keeping the signal in the set costs a permanently green tile that means nothing. `clarification_rate` needs no decision — it stays, and its `offline_*` detail keys can be dropped whenever someone is in the file for another reason. Status stays `[~]`: the `Microsoft.App/jobs` resource is still unbuilt and no `online_eval_signal` event has ever reached Azure.
- `[x]` **Gap 306 (BE/AI): rule 6b's mandated four-column OR group is generated with `items` dropped, so a category question whose phrase lives only in a line-item description reports a real invoice as “not found”** — opened 2026-08-24, **found by Feature 23 Wave 3's newly-ported regional cases**, **fixed 2026-08-25**. Rule 6b (`agents/query_agent.py`) is explicit that a category/subject-matter question must check the **same four columns, in one parenthesised OR group, never a subset**: `tags`, `items`, `vendor_name`, `customer_name` — and its own text says checking only a subset “is a bug: it silently misses real matches that qualify through one of the other columns”. Live gpt-5-mini generated that group **with `sa_alerts` substituted for `items`** on two different questions against two different tenants in the same run: `india_reverse_charge_vendor` (“which vendor billed us under a Reverse Charge Mechanism arrangement”) searched `tags`/`sa_alerts`/`vendor_name`/`customer_name` for `%reverse charge mechanism%`, and `eu_reverse_charge_inbound_line` searched `vendor_name`/`tags`/`sa_alerts` across four spelling variants of the phrase. In both fixtures the phrase appears **only** in a line-item description, which is exactly the case rule 6b's four-column shape exists for. Both queries returned zero rows and both answers were a confident negative — *“No vendor billed us under a Reverse Charge Mechanism — the query returned no matching records”* and *“No inbound vendor used intra-EU reverse charge for the queried period”* — for invoices that exist (KE-2026-0089, RIT-2026-0456). **Which metric caught it, stated as observed:** faithfulness **1.0** and relevance **1.0** on both, because a no-results report is genuinely faithful to an empty result set and `RELEVANCE_KIND_SCORES` fixes a `no_results_report` at 1.0 by design — so the two headline soft metrics were blind to it. It was caught by **accuracy 0.0, `context_score` 0.00 (recall 0/1, “context missed: KE-2026-0089”), and persona 0.0**, i.e. the deterministic component score and the reference-answer comparison, not the judge's holistic read. Same family as Gap 224 (a false-confident negative) and Gap 270 (a real invoice reported as not found) but a different root cause — those were direction/filter guesses, this is a mandated column silently missing from an otherwise correctly-shaped query. — **Reproduced before anything was changed**, and the reproduction ships as a test rather than a description: `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py::test_the_dropped_items_column_is_what_misses_the_invoice` seeds an invoice whose only occurrence of the phrase is a line-item description and executes **both** predicates verbatim against a real engine — the emitted group returns `[]`, the mandated group returns `KE-2026-0089`, and nothing else differs. **The fix is structural, per this entry's own instruction and CONVENTIONS hard rule 3, and rule 6b's prompt text is unchanged by it** (deliberately — that rule is already ~600 words insisting on this exact point and it is the instruction that was disobeyed; Gap 287 is the precedent for what one more paragraph there costs). Of the two candidates this entry named, neither was taken as written: repairing the emitted SQL is Gap 253's deleted regex rewriter in another costume, and a fill-in-the-phrase template still depends on the model emitting the template. What shipped instead is a **deterministic zero-result category fallback**, in the same position and the same shape as the invoice-number fallback that has sat in `run_sql_generation_loop()` since Gap 34: **(a) `category_search_phrases(generated_sql)`** decides whether the query was a category search at all, structurally — it returns phrases only if the query LIKE-matched against at least one **JSONB** column, reflected via `sage_prompts.category_match_json_columns()`, because a LIKE against a JSON blob is by construction a subject-matter search while a name lookup (6a/4a), an invoice-number lookup and a status filter never touch one. That trigger holds for **every** subset of the group the model might emit (the observed failure kept two JSON columns of the four) and it is derived from the model, not a typed `(“tags”,“items”,“sa_alerts”)` list that would drift exactly the way rule 6b's four did. Its regex only **reads** the statement — nothing is edited, repaired or re-executed — and its `[^']` gap cannot cross a string literal, which is how rule 6d's `LOWER(item.value ->> 'description') LIKE ...` is told apart from a category match on `items` by construction rather than by hoping the shapes differ. **(b) `category_search_fallback()`** re-runs the search over the **reflected** column set using `sage_prompts.category_match_branches()` — Feature 21's `aggregate`-tool machinery, orphaned by Gap 316 and flagged there as a founder call on whether the reflected-schema idea was worth keeping; **this is that use, and it is what makes the reflection load-bearing rather than tidy**: the reflected set is **18 columns**, including `taxes`, `references`, `payment_instructions` and `compliance_metadata`, which rule 6b's hardcoded four never covered *however perfectly the model wrote them*. A clause that wide is not something any model can be asked to type verbatim and reliably not drop a branch of — which is the argument for building it in code rather than teaching it. `category_match_branches()`/`category_match_expression()` were **added** to `sage_prompts.py` as the executable sibling of the existing text renderer, both reading one shared `_category_match_columns_typed()` pass so the two cannot disagree about scope or casts: bound parameters rather than interpolation (the phrase is lifted out of model-written SQL), `sa.cast(col, sa.Text)` so the dialect emits its own `CAST(... AS TEXT)`, and a typed `Invoice.tenant_id == <UUID>` predicate — the only form that matches on both engines, since SQLite stores those columns dashless and the dashed literal a text query carries matches zero rows there. That is also why this fallback **can** be driven by real rows in a test while the older invoice-number one cannot. **(c) Boundaries, stated rather than left to judgment.** It fires only on a zero-row result, so it can never replace an answer that already found something — what it replaces is always the `NO_RECORDS_FOUND` sentinel. It runs **after** the invoice-number fallback (narrower and more certain). `_direction_in_generated_sql()` reads, never rewrites, and carries a `flow_direction` filter over only when the query committed to exactly one — a fallback that ignored a direction the question did establish would answer “who billed us for X” with the tenant's own outbound invoice, i.e. Gap 224/270 arriving through the fix for this one. Date ranges and status filters are deliberately **not** reconstructed (that means parsing the statement, the mechanism Gap 253 removed); `invoice_date` and both counterparty columns are projected instead, so an out-of-period match is visible in the evidence rather than hidden by it. A `matched_in` column names which reflected column each row qualified through, because the search is wider than the question implied and “matched in `items`” and “matched in `sa_alerts`” mean different things. `CATEGORY_MATCH_EXCLUDED_COLUMNS` still excludes `addresses` (a street name matching a spend category is a false positive) and the five non-business blobs, enforced by the same reflection pass.
