Fail-soft with a rollback, like every other enrichment on this route. `render_result_cell()` was extracted verbatim from `execute_generated_sql()` so the recovered table renders identically to a normal one — both are read back by `parse_results_table()` and totalled by `_computed_figures_block_for()`, and a second copy of those rules that drifted by one branch would put a 19-digit float in front of a user on exactly one of the two paths. **Telemetry unchanged and `telemetry.py` untouched**: a recovered turn reports `zero_result=False` / `zero_result_fallback_recovered=True`, one flag for both fallbacks on purpose — what it measures is “the generated SQL missed something that exists”, which is the same defect either way. **Verified**: 19 new tests / 21 cases in `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` (**136 passed**, up from 115) — the executed reproduction; the end-to-end recovery through the real route (asserting the invoice reaches the user's answer **and** the summary prompt's evidence); `matched_in`; the other three mandated columns each seeded in isolation and still matched; a `references`-only match rule 6b's four could never have found; a patched-`invoice_columns()` test proving a column removed from the model stops being matched and comes straight back when it returns; the `addresses` exclusion; the anti-false-positive set (a genuine name-lookup zero result stays one, a rule 6d line-item query never triggers, `NOT LIKE` is not a search phrase, another tenant's row is unreachable, a turn that found rows is untouched); all four spelling variants carried over; direction preserved; the `zero_result_fallback_recovered` distinction; the recovered table totalled by Gap 315's block; fail-soft; the text and executable clauses agreeing on the column set; and every JSON branch compiled against the real **PostgreSQL** dialect to prove the cast is there and the phrase is bound, not interpolated. Both end-to-end tests were confirmed to **fail** with the fallback call disabled and to pass with it. Narrow run `pytest tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py tests/test_telemetry.py tests/test_query_tools.py tests/test_direction_aware_chat.py tests/test_rag.py tests/test_chat_training.py` → **309 passed / 1 failed**, that one the standing `test_rag.py::test_process_crash_during_agent_leaves_no_orphan_user_message` (`post_chat_message() missing 1 required positional argument: 'background_tasks'`). Full suite (end-of-track checkpoint, run twice, identical): **1338 passed / 1 failed / 1 skipped**, against a same-tree pre-change baseline measured the same day of **1316 passed / 2 failed / 1 skipped** — +21 cases, and the second baseline failure (`test_extraction_benchmark.py::test_regenerating_the_corpus_is_byte_identical`) is a suite side effect unrelated to this change: something in that file regenerates the shipped `docs/extraction_benchmark/case_manifest.{json,md}` with a fresh embedded timestamp, and both files were already dirty in the working tree before this work began. `ruff check` clean on `agents/query_agent.py`, `agents/sage_prompts.py`, `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py`. **Mocked-unit caveat, stated plainly**: per hard rule 2 these are SQLite/mocked-LLM tests, not live-Postgres evidence — but the mechanism does not depend on model behaviour at all (the fallback is a code-built query that runs whatever the model wrote), the Postgres-specific risk (rule 6(a)'s `lower(jsonb)`) is covered by dialect compilation, and the two cases that produced the gap remain permanent members of the shipped golden bank, so the live before/after is measurable by `scripts/run_agent_eval.py` on the next graded run. **One consequence for Gap 316's open founder call, reported rather than resolved**: `sage_prompts.py`'s reflection half (`invoice_columns()`, `_category_match_columns_typed()`, `category_match_columns()`, `category_match_json_columns()`, `category_match_branches()`, `category_match_expression()`, `render_category_match_clause()`, `quoted_column()`, `CATEGORY_MATCH_EXCLUDED_COLUMNS`) is now imported by `agents/query_agent.py` and covered by tests — that half of the founder question is answered by use. The rest of the file — `IDENTIFY_*`, `AGGREGATE_*`, `build_identify_system_prompt()`, `build_aggregate_system_prompt()`, `aggregate_schema_block()` — still has **zero callers and zero tests** and remains exactly the founder call Gap 316 recorded. Design in `docs/feature_6_rag.md`, “Recent Fix (Aug 25, 2026) — Gap 306”.

- `[x]` **Gap 307 (BE/AI): context-drift detection does not exist — a thread can now be reconstructed, but nothing judges whether it drifted** — opened 2026-08-24, **split out of Gap 303 rather than left implicit in it**, because that entry was written as one gap covering two genuinely different pieces of work and closing only the first would have made it look half-done for no stated reason. What Gap 303 built is *reconstruction*: `chat_turn` events carry `session_id`, `turn_index` and `seconds_since_prev_turn`, and `infra/monitoring/chat_thread_sessions.kql` derives session length, turns-per-session and abandonment at a 30-minute idle cutoff. What it did not build, and never claimed to, is a **detector** — anything that looks at turn N in light of turns 1..N-1 and says "the filter narrowed unexpectedly" or "the prior turn's SQL was reused after a topic change". Those are Gap 237 and Gap 276's failure shapes; both are **closed bugs** cited as the failure class, and closing them built no detector. What now exists that a detector could consume, which is strictly more than when Gap 303 was opened: the full per-turn Trace including the real generated SQL and the real tool output for every turn in a session (Gap 302), so a drift check no longer has to re-run retrieval or reason from `ChatMessage` text alone — turn N's SQL and turn N-1's SQL are both queryable side by side from one event stream. Also available: the golden bank's ordered multi-turn scripts (10 follow-up links plus Gap 237's 2-turn capture). **Not built, and the doc's own position is still that it should be scoped down before it is promised** — `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s Multi-turn evaluation tier calls a general multi-turn detector the hardest thing in the feature and recommends fixed 2-3 turn scripts with pinned expectations rather than a general detector. Two shapes are worth costing separately when this is picked up: an **offline** one (a multi-turn tier in the golden bank, which needs no new production code at all) and an **online** one (a signal over live `chat_turn` events, which is where the new Trace content earns its keep but which also means judging live traffic — a product decision of the same class as Gap 304 half 2, not a wiring change). **CLOSED 2026-08-26 — the OFFLINE half is built; the ONLINE half is deliberately NOT, and is recommended below as its own number.** The entry above costed the two shapes separately and asked for them to be picked up separately; this closes the one that “needs no new production code at all”. **Nothing in this change touches a request path, emits a new telemetry event type, adds an Azure resource or edits a bicep file** — it is an eval tier plus one deterministic scorer.

  **What was built.** `benchmarks/agent_eval_multiturn.py` (new): **5 scripted conversations, 12 turns, 7 of them drift-scored**, following Wave 3's precedent (its cases are ordinary `GoldenCase`s against the same seeded in-memory SQLite tenant, graded by the same judge, selected by the same `--cases`). The doc's own scoping is honoured literally — `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md` calls a general multi-turn detector the hardest thing in the feature and recommends “fixed 2-3 turn scripts with pinned expectations”: every script is 2 or 3 turns and every expectation is pinned to a seeded row, asserted by test rather than trusted. The five are `drift_subject_switch_vendor` (Gap 276's shape: “now show me DataPipe **instead**” after a Blue Ridge turn), `drift_narrowing_followup_keeps_filter` (Gap 237's shape and its mirror image — “which of those is oldest” must KEEP the over-USD-20,000 filter, because the oldest invoice *overall* is a USD 420.00 one outside it), `drift_referent_moves_with_conversation` (3 turns; “and when is **that one** due” must resolve to turn 2's invoice, not turn 1's — the two carry different due dates on purpose, so a stale referent is a wrong date rather than a matter of opinion), `drift_ambiguous_previous_invoice` (“the previous invoice” said when the prior turn named two), and `drift_scope_stated_once_still_applies` (3 turns; a June-2026 scope stated once, in a turn that asks no question at all, then two questions that never mention it — both with a pinned wrong answer reachable only by dropping it).

  **The scorer is deterministic, not a fourth judge call**, and that is the substantive design decision. `services/agent_eval.py::score_context_drift()` + `DriftExpectation` score *checks passed / checks pinned* over four surfaces kept separate on purpose: `forbidden_sql_terms` (the generated SQL only — Gap 276's predicate survives in the WHERE clause whether or not stale rows reach the prose), `forbidden_terms` (prose *and* SQL), `required_entities` (prose only, one **alias group** per entity) and `forbidden_invoice_numbers` (what the tools actually fetched). Two reasons a judge was rejected: a judge asked “did this drift?” cannot know what the earlier turns established unless it is shown them (more prompt, more variance, more cost per turn), and `services/agent_eval.py` already carries four documented instances of a free-floating judged number systematically under-scoring correct answers. `context_drift_score` is **component-level** like `context`/`orchestration` — recorded and trended, **not** in `decide_pass()`, because folding a new dimension into the pass criterion halfway through a series silently redefines what a pass means. Its stated bias is toward false *passes* (a turn that answers nothing satisfies every forbidden check), the same disclosure `score_orchestration()` makes about itself, and for the same reason: the alternative under-scores correct answers, and a degenerate answer is what faithfulness/relevance/accuracy already catch.

  **The three things that make twelve turns a conversation rather than twelve first turns**, all in `scripts/run_agent_eval.py`: (1) one `session_id` shared across a script (`run_multi_turn_script()`); (2) a `ChatMessage` write-back between turns (`record_turn_messages()`) — necessary because `run_query_agent()` persists nothing, `routers/chat.py::post_chat_message` does, and the two functions drift actually happens in (`get_chat_history()`, and Gap 237's own `get_prior_turn_sql()`, which filters on `generated_sql IS NOT NULL`) read exactly those rows — without it every drift check would pass vacuously; (3) its own summary bucket `default-multiturn` (`services/benchmark_artifacts.py::MULTI_TURN_PATH`), so the 35-case `default` series keeps the exact population every historical figure in the docs was computed over. `--no-multi-turn` skips the tier; otherwise it runs on the nightly path the way every other tier does, with no bicep and no job-argv change. The tier's `agent_eval_summary` event rides the **existing** per-path mechanism (built for `default`/`sage`), and `context_drift_score` rides `agent_eval_run`'s `**extra_attributes` exactly as Track 2's helpfulness/completeness/tone already do — no new event type, and no `AgentEvalRun` column or migration (the value also lands in `notes`, like `judge_mode`).

  **Nightly review wiring.** `evaluate_ai_improvement()` reads the drift bucket by its own key (`_multi_turn_stats()`, never averaged into the single-turn numbers) and raises a `context_drift` finding graded on **`d3-context`'s existing band** rather than a new pair of numbers — drift *is* a retrieval failure, and every threshold in that module is a live tile's, pinned by a test that parses both workbook JSONs. There is no drift tile today and adding one is a workbook deploy, out of scope here; inventing an uncalibrated `CONTEXT_DRIFT_RED` would have been the first band in that file with no tile behind it. It is exempt from the n=20 sample guard, and that is stated in code: the tier is a fixed, exhaustive, deterministic script set run identically every night, so there is no sampling error for the guard to protect against and applying it would disable the tier permanently. `_agent_eval_stats()` now also excludes the drift bucket from its path *choice*, so a `--cases <script>` run cannot get its 12 deliberately harder turns graded against the 35-case bank's bands.

  **Real evidence, not “should work” — two live runs against the configured Azure `gpt-5-mini`, real agent and real judge, `--no-persist --no-mirror`.** **Run A**, all 5 scripts: **12/12 turns ran, 0 errors**, `$0.005`/turn, and the `default-multiturn` bucket reported `pass_rate 0.333`, `faithfulness_mean 0.806`, `accuracy_mean 0.742` and **`context_drift_mean 0.943` over 7 scored turns**. Six of the seven scored 1.00, and the generated SQL proves it was not luck: the June-2026 scope survived **two** later turns (`invoice_date >= DATE('2026-06-01') AND invoice_date <= DATE('2026-06-30')` present in both turn 2's and turn 3's SQL, two and three turns after it was stated), and `currency = 'USD' AND grand_total > 20000` survived the “which of those is oldest” follow-up. **The seventh scored 0.60 — and it was the harness's fault, which is the whole point of having run it.** `drift_ambiguous_previous_invoice` turn 2 answered “Do you mean the invoice dated 2026-06-27 (SEP-4410) or 2026-06-30 (DPS-9981)? SEP-4410 tax = USD 2,236.00; DPS-9981 tax = USD 3,384.00” — the exact clarifying question that case exists to reward, with SQL filtering on both vendors — and was marked drifted because the check demanded the vendor *names* while the answer used the invoice *numbers*. That is precisely the under-scoring-correct-answers trap this repo has four prior instances of, found on the first live run rather than in production: `required_terms` was replaced by `required_entities` (alias groups — one check per entity, satisfied by any of its stable surface forms), and that exact answer text is now a regression test. **Run B**, the same script re-run after the fix, is the check firing on a genuine failure: the model **silently** resolved “the previous invoice” to `DPS-9981` without saying which of the two it had picked — `context_drift 0.80` with the note “the subject under discussion went missing: StratEdge/SEP-4410”, and the independent accuracy judge scored the same turn `0.0`. False positive removed, true positive kept.

  **Tests: 43 new in `tests/test_agent_eval_multiturn.py`**, plus 9 in `test_ops_recommendation.py` and 1 in `test_benchmark_artifacts.py`. They pin the scorer's mechanics and — more usefully — the scripts' well-formedness against the real fixture: every pinned invoice number is seeded, every pinned term is a string the fixture can actually produce, no pinned term is a money figure (formatting varies by route and a missed forbidden match reads as “no drift”), every alias group resolves to exactly one seeded invoice, no term is both required and forbidden on the same turn, every required entity appears in its own reference answer, every forbidden term is explained by that answer, no first turn carries a drift expectation and every later turn does. The three fixture facts the wrong answers depend on are asserted too (oldest-overall is outside the >20k set; largest-overall is outside June; the only freight line is outside June) — a future fixture edit that quietly makes a drift case undiscriminating fails a test instead of going green. **339 tests pass across the six affected files** (`test_agent_eval_multiturn` 43, `test_ops_recommendation` 83, `test_benchmark_artifacts` 40, `test_run_agent_eval_cli` 19, `test_agent_eval` 97, `test_telemetry` 60); the full suite is deliberately **not** claimed here — it is reserved for a track-boundary checkpoint. Two existing tests changed and both are worth stating: one in `test_benchmark_artifacts.py` was re-anchored from “all nine dimensions are on the event” to “every dimension the run actually *scored* is on the event”, because `context_drift` is legitimately absent from a `default`-bucket event and demanding otherwise would demand a 0.0 for a dimension never measured; and `_run_main()` in `test_run_agent_eval_cli.py` now stubs the tier out for the same reason it already stubs `CASES` — without it a CLI unit test makes twelve real model calls, found by running it.

  **NOT closed by this, and it needs its own number: the ONLINE judge — recommended as `Gap 321`.** A live signal over production `chat_turn` events is explicitly out of scope here and is **not** part of this closure. It is the half the entry above describes as “where the new Trace content earns its keep but which also means judging live traffic — a product decision of the same class as Gap 304 half 2, not a wiring change”, and that assessment is unchanged. **321 is the next free number in this tracker** (320 is the current maximum). What it would inherit from this gap and should not rebuild: the definition of drift, the four-surface check shape, and `services/online_eval_signals.py`'s existing signal/threshold/`online_eval_signal` plumbing. What it must decide, and what this gap deliberately does not: whether production turns may be judged at all and at what sampling rate; what plays the part of a pinned expectation when there is no golden script (the hard problem — nothing tells a live detector what turn N *should* have been about); and what it costs per judged turn.

SAGE tool-calling rewrite, the 2026-08-25 live head-to-head, and the deletion decision) moved to
`docs/feature_6_rag.md` § "Feature 21 — the SAGE alternative, tried and closed" on 2026-08-25 — the two
functions that survived (`get_full_record`, `compute`) are ordinary Feature 6 dependencies now, so

## Feature 23 — AI Control Tower, scoped 2026-08-21

- `[x]` **Phase 1 — Telemetry.** Built 2026-08-21. `telemetry.py` emits one `llm_agent_call` Application Insights custom event (`agent_name`, `model`, `tokens_in`, `tokens_out`, `latency_ms`, `status`, `tenant_id`, `request_id`, `trace_id`) per LLM round-trip, wired into 14 invocation points covering every registry row. Reuses Feature 19's existing `configure_azure_monitor` init and `request_id`/`trace_id` contextvars — no second SDK, no second ID scheme. 5 new tests in `tests/test_telemetry.py`; full suite at 818 passed / 6 skipped / the same 6 pre-existing failures (pre-change baseline 813 passed + the 5 new telemetry tests), re-run 2026-08-21 after the last two call sites landed. **One honest deviation from the scoped shape:** SENTINEL's named tax/payment-status detectors are pure regex (`detect_tax_component_term`, `detect_payment_status_question` in `agents/query_agent.py`) — there is no LLM call there to instrument, so that registry row is covered only by the extraction calls SENTINEL's audit runs on top of. The two call sites originally left out (`routers/dashboard.py::get_dashboard_insights` → `dashboard.insights`, and `routers/trainer.py::_answer_qa_from_session_data`, the QA-panel upload summary → `trainer.qa_summary`) were instrumented the same day and added to the registry table, so Phase 2's cost rollup has no known under-reporting. ~~Not deployed: `APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING` must be set as a Container App secret before any event reaches Azure (founder action).~~ **Deployed and live as of 2026-08-22** — that founder action has happened. The first `llm_agent_call` event landed in `appi-invoicellm-dev` at **2026-08-22T03:48:30Z**; a direct query over 90 days now returns real rows (`chat.sql_generation`, `chat.sql_summary`, `chat.classify`, `dashboard.insights`, all `gpt-5-mini`, one tenant), so `verified in Application Insights` is finally claimable for Phase 1 by observation rather than by test suite. The event history is only hours long, which the workbook's Cost and Latency tabs now state on the tab so a mostly-empty 14-day window is not misread as a quiet product.
- `[x]` **Phase 2 — Cost + quality dashboard. SUPERSEDED 2026-08-25 — the tabbed `ai_control_tower.workbook.json` this entry describes was deleted and replaced by the two current flat workbooks; closed as superseded, not as shipped. See the correction at the end of this entry; the seven-round build narrative below is preserved verbatim as the historical record.** Built 2026-08-21, **not deployed**. `infra/monitoring/llm_cost_rollup_nightly.kql` (cost by agent/day/tenant, token counts × published per-model rate, unpriced models report blank not zero) and `infra/monitoring/ai_control_tower.workbook.json` — day-over-day trend for cost, quality and alerts on **one shared time axis**, per the 2026-08-21 "workbook must show trend" decision. All six workbook queries executed live before filing: four Log Analytics against `appi-invoicellm-dev`, two Azure Resource Graph (`alertsmanagementresources`) against subscription `2ae37d8b-…`. `[~]` not `[x]` for two real reasons: (1) **`customEvents` has 0 rows over 90 days** — confirmed by direct query, not assumed — because `APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING` is still not set as a Container App secret, so the cost and quality panels render empty (the alerts panel works today); (2) nothing schedules the "nightly" rollup — no Log Analytics scheduled-query rule exists, so it is a query a human runs. Workbook deliberately **not imported** — that is a founder Portal action. **Extended 2026-08-21 from 3 sections to 8** (cost, cost-by-tool, latency, quality, component quality, online signals, golden-bank coverage, health) on the same shared time axis, with the header's "what's live today" honesty table extended to match. Latency is now its own trended panel (p50 **and** p95 per agent per day, no longer a column folded into the cost query) because Gap 278 was two ~177s turns with zero 4xx/5xx, which no error or cost chart would ever have surfaced. All **10 new Log Analytics queries executed live** against `appi-invoicellm-dev` (app id `d2add3c5-…`) via `az monitor app-insights query` before filing — every one parses and returns the correct column schema with zero rows, and `customEvents | summarize count()` over 90 days was re-confirmed at **0** in this pass rather than assumed from the previous one. **Restructured 2026-08-22 on direct founder feedback** — "the current workbook is not understandable, you can't scroll 10 pages to see everything". The 8 stacked sections are now **8 tabs** behind a row of **6 single-stat overview tiles** that need no scrolling: cost today, latest quality pass rate, alerts fired today by severity, latest clarification rate, latest zero-result rate, and the static golden-bank 8-of-53. Every tile reduces the query already in its section rather than inventing a metric, and each carries the same honesty discipline — a `noDataMessage` naming the specific reason it is empty, and **never a misleading zero**. That last point took a real fix: KQL `sum()` over an empty set returns **0, not null**, so the first cost tile rendered a confident `$0.00` on a day with no telemetry; it now guards on `calls > 0` and returns null (never 0) when no model in the window has a hardcoded rate. Layout only — all 26 pre-existing items are asserted **byte-identical** after the rebuild (carried by object reference, re-read from disk and compared), so no query, table or caveat changed. The Tenant parameter is kept and now resolves to `all` on load: `isRequired: true` over an empty `customEvents` left the dropdown with no selectable options, so its `"value": "all"` default matched nothing and blocked every panel until picked by hand; the parameter query now always returns `all` as its first row. All **23 queries in the rebuilt file re-executed live** (20 Log Analytics + 3 ARG, extracted from the written JSON rather than retyped) — 0 failures, correct column schemas, and the new alerts-today tile returned a real `Sev2: 1`. **The tab mechanism shipped in that restructure was wrong, and the founder caught it the same day** — the workbook still rendered as one continuous scrolling page with no tab row. Root cause: `"style": "tabs"` on a `type: 12` group **is not a supported property** — verified absent from Microsoft's own `schema/workbook.json`, where `definitions.group.content` has no `style` at all and `style: "tabs"` exists only on `definitions.link` (`type: 11`) — so Azure silently ignored it and stacked the child groups. **Fixed 2026-08-22** with the documented mechanism: a `type: 11` links step (`LinkItem/1.0`, `style: tabs`, each link `linkTarget: parameter` / `cellValue: SelectedTab` / `subTarget: <tab value>`) inserted as a sibling of the 8 groups, plus top-level `conditionalVisibility` on each group keyed to the same parameter; `SelectedTab` is deliberately not declared as a pill because the first tab (**Read me first**) fires on load. **Content untouched** — the serialiser was first proved to round-trip the pre-fix file to an identical 68,777 bytes, so the diff is provably only the tab-bar step, the 8 `conditionalVisibility` blocks, and the deleted bogus line. Schema-validated (`jsonschema` Draft 7, 0 errors), and structurally identical key-for-key to a shipped Microsoft tabbed template (`Azure Security Center/Containers Security`). **A real Azure resource was created this time**, closing the verification gap the restructure left open: throwaway workbook `AI Control Tower - TAB FIX TEST` (`0f1e2d3c-4b5a-4c9d-8e7f-a1b2c3d4e5f6`, `rg-invoice-llm-dev`, api-version 2022-04-01), `PUT` 200, and a `GET ...&canFetchContent=true` returned `serializedData` **byte-identical** to what was sent (71,978 chars, non-ASCII intact). Also found while doing it: the live `Invoice LLM AI Tower` workbook resource still holds the **pre-restructure flat 27-item** definition (`timeModified` `2026-08-22T03:02:03Z`, earlier than the restructured file was written) — it was left untouched. **The founder has since confirmed the tab bar renders and switches** on the test resource, with real telemetry showing on the Cost tile — so the tab mechanism is settled. **The tab bar was still below the fold, and that was a second real defect, fixed 2026-08-22**: the six tiles were written `"customWidth": "15%"` (×5) + `"20%"`, and a step's `customWidth` **takes a bare number, never a unit** — units belong to grid *column* widths only. Counted, not assumed: across all **708 workbook templates Microsoft ships**, **5,207 `customWidth` values contain zero `%`/`px`/non-numeric characters**, and the official schema's only example is `"50"`. A malformed width is ignored exactly like the bogus group-level `style` was, so each tile fell back to full width — **six stacked tile rows instead of one**, which is what pushed the tab bar off-screen. Fixed to `15, 15, 20, 15, 15, 15` (= 95, under 100 so margins fit; `15` with a `tiles` visualization is shipped Microsoft practice, and tile step `size: 4` was already the most compact option). The caption above the tiles was also cut from 384 characters (`##` heading + a ~4-line paragraph) to **one 113-character line with no heading** — nothing was lost, because the honesty rule and the per-source live/empty table are stated at length in the **Read me first** tab, which the one-liner points at. **No tab content, query or caveat was touched**, and that is proven rather than asserted: the previous definition was `GET`-ed back from the test resource and diffed against the new file — **37 diff lines, all 7 intended** (1 caption, 6 widths). Schema-validated (Draft 7, 0 errors), re-`PUT` 200, and the round-trip `GET` returned `serializedData` **byte-identical** to what was sent (72,594 chars, 49 em dashes + 7 arrows intact — via `urllib` + an ARM bearer token, because `az rest` piped to a file on Windows silently drops non-ASCII under cp1252). **The founder has since confirmed both the tab mechanism and the one-row tile layout render correctly**, which closes the two open render questions above and leaves only a content complaint: *"the workbook is not understandable"* — every panel carried paragraphs of caveats and data-source explanation interleaved with the numbers, so finding one figure meant reading a wall of text (the Online signals tab alone was **3,499 characters of prose above three panels**, and the six tiles' *empty-state messages* were multi-sentence essays where the founder wanted `— (not run)`). **Content redesigned 2026-08-22** to a shape agreed in chat first, against two example tables the founder confirmed directly: terse tiles, and reference material as **one table row per metric, no prose**. "Read me first" was renamed **Documentation** (`linkLabel`, group title/name, and the `subTarget`/`conditionalVisibility` pair `readme` → `documentation`, kept 1:1) and now holds seven blocks — a 24-row `What it means / What makes it move` table covering all 6 tiles and all 18 panels across all 8 tabs (the tile rows are the founder's own wording verbatim), the pre-existing live/empty honesty table, the hardcoded `$/1M tokens` rates as a 5-row table, the component-scoring mechanism table with the `persona_scored_turns` "not applicable" limitation, the 5-signal confidence table plus the two reading rules, a new consolidated `Number / why it is not a direct measurement / what would make it one` table gathering every proxy disclosure (`synthesis_share_pct`'s inference-from-prompt-size, latency's one-round-trip scope, the turn-latency row-timestamp delta, `budget_exhaustion_rate`'s eval-harness-only source, `clarification_rate`'s wording match, golden-bank staticness, the Gap 278 rationale and the "why 8 and not 53" reasoning with its two caveats and Gap 287), and the import instructions + ARG-vs-built-in-Alerts rationale + a layout-history table. **Each of the other seven tabs is now a `###` heading plus exactly one line of context, then its charts** — text outside the reference tab fell **10,998 → 2,260 characters (−79%)**, and **20 prose paragraphs across 7 tabs became 79 table rows on one tab**. Two caveats were judged load-bearing enough to survive on their own tab, compressed to one line each (`get_full_record` makes no LLM call, and the turn-latency panel's `(PROXY — row timestamps, not a timer)` title). Tile empty-states went from 67-232 chars each to `— (no data)` / `— (not run)` / `— (query failed)`; the Alerts tile deliberately reads `0 (none fired today)` instead, because ARG is genuinely live and an empty result there really is a zero — the single most important reading rule in the workbook, now stated once in Documentation instead of once per tile. **This is relocation and compression, not a cut, and that is proven mechanically rather than by eye**: every backtick identifier and numeric literal in the old text was extracted and checked against the new — **79/79 identifiers and 47/47 numbers present**. The first build *failed* that check on three items (the five instrumented SAGE call sites with `agents/query_tools.py`, the `llm_cost_by_tool.kql` pointer, and the `gap_coverage` block name); they were restored and the whole build re-run from the untouched backup. **All 23 queries byte-identical**, proven three ways including against what Azure had stored before the PUT rather than only locally, and the entire before/after maps of `queryType`/`resourceType`/`crossComponentResources`/`visualization`/`tileSettings`/`timeContext*`/`size`/`customWidth` asserted equal. Schema-validated (Draft 7, **0 errors**), re-`PUT` 200, round-trip `GET` **byte-identical** (79,737 chars / 79,903 UTF-8 bytes, 75 em dashes + 7 arrows + 1 `≥` intact). Items 49 → 53; tabs 8 → 8. **Insight-first ordering, 2026-08-22**, on the founder's *"put the insights at top then explanation then graph"* plus blunt separate feedback that it is still *"not at all user friendly"* in one glance. Every one of the 7 non-Documentation tabs is now **heading → insight → explanation → charts**, charts untouched. Cost, Latency and Health get a **computed** insight — three new query steps, each written and **executed live before** being added to the file, each over the *same* events as the tab's own chart: `Biggest spender: chat.sql_generation — 47% of the window / $0.02 / 9 calls from 4 agents`, `Slowest tail: dashboard.insights — p95 is 1.0x its own median of 14875 ms`, and `1 of 25 alerts still firing right now / 25 / worst severity Sev1`. The four tabs with no data yet state what a reading will *mean* once there is one, rather than saying "no data". The blunter half of the feedback was treated as an instruction to go find what is wrong, and it surfaced **three real defects, one of them created by the previous round**: (1) **Documentation was the first tab**, and the first link in a `type: 11` tab bar fires on load, so every open landed on the workbook's own 22,170-character reference wall before any number — moved to **last**, Cost is now first; (2) **the Data sources table still asserted `customEvents` was empty** ("0 rows over 90 days") while the Cost tile showed a live `$0.02`, a direct self-contradiction — corrected and dated to the real 2026-08-22T03:48:30Z first event, and the `doc-import` "renders empty" row narrowed to the three tabs that genuinely are; (3) **no colour or icon existed anywhere** — checked rather than assumed that thresholds are real (`formatter: 18` + `thresholdsOptions: "icons"` + `thresholdsGrid`, **164 uses inside `tileSettings`** across Microsoft's 708 shipped templates), and applied to **exactly one tile, Alerts today**, mapping each severity to its own icon with a `tooltipFormat`. Only one, deliberately: a threshold is a claim about what "bad" means, alerts have one this repo did not invent, and there is no cost budget, target pass rate or coverage target anywhere in this codebase to base the others on — inventing one to make a tile go red would break the same honesty rule that made the empty states read `— (no data)` instead of `0`. The two online-signal tiles *do* carry a real `threshold` on their own event; their four-branch status logic was written and **proved live on synthetic rows** this pass but deliberately **not shipped**, because both tiles are empty until something calls `emit_online_signals()` on a schedule. Verified: **25/25 query steps re-executed live** (22 Log Analytics + 3 ARG, extracted from the written JSON), 0 failures; diffed against **what Azure had stored**, not a local copy — all 23 pre-existing queries present and unchanged, 3 added, 0 removed, and of 22 pre-existing query steps **exactly one** property changed (the alerts tile's `titleContent`); content-loss audit **88/88 identifiers and 43/43 numbers** surviving (and the audit itself was caught passing *vacuously* on its first run and fixed); schema-validated Draft 7 **0 errors**; serialiser proved to round-trip the pre-change file byte-for-byte first; re-`PUT` 200 (`timeModified 2026-08-22T04:33:06Z`) with the `GET` returning `serializedData` **byte-identical** (94,466 chars / 94,672 UTF-8 bytes, 92 em dashes + 10 arrows + 1 `≥`). Two vocabulary questions are **left to the founder rather than guessed**: the tile titles (`Clarification rate (%)` / `Zero-result rate (%)` are internal signal names; the plain-English versions already exist in Documentation but a longer label costs vertical space above the tab bar) and the tab labels (`Online signals` / `Component quality`, both cross-referenced by name throughout Documentation). Still `[~]`: no pixels were seen this round either — specifically unverified are whether a full-width `size: 4` tile with a sentence as its `titleContent` reads as an insight, and whether the severity icon renders — and deploying the real workbook and scheduling anything remain founder actions. Three rounds in, this is **not** claimed as "now it is user friendly"; what is claimed is that three nameable defects are gone. **Reverted outside this workstream and rebuilt 2026-08-22 (round 5).** The file was found back at its **first 27-item flat state** — 46,472 bytes, no `type: 11` tab bar, no `type: 12` group, no overview tiles, no Documentation tab — written by another IDE, not by this workstream; the disposable Azure test resource held a matching early copy (45,962 chars) so it was no help as a reference either. Recovery was **exact rather than reconstructive**: replaying the surviving round-4 build script over the surviving round-3 backup reproduced the round-4 definition at **94,466 chars**, byte-for-byte the recorded figure, so the 22,170-character Documentation tab, all 23 original queries and every preserved caveat came back verbatim and nothing was re-derived from prose. On top of that, the founder's new requirement — **every non-Documentation tab is now four sections on one screen**: (1) a `tiles` step of **2-3 real numbers** reduced from that tab's own query (same events, same hardcoded rate table, never a new metric), (2) one plain-language line, (3) the same chart at `size: 1` instead of `size: 0`, (4) the same table at `gridSettings.rowLimit: 5` with a filter box. Four secondary detail tables moved into nested `loadType: "explicit"` groups, which render as a single **Show …** button instead of a panel. Layout evidence was re-counted against a shallow clone of Microsoft's 668 shipped templates rather than assumed: `customWidth` has **4,674 values and not one carries a unit** (the 17 non-integers are decimals like `33.3`), there are **422** `type: 11` tab bars with `isEqualTo` used 3,462 times, `gridSettings.rowLimit` is used **748** times, `loadType: "explicit"` **21** times, and timechart `size: 1` is used **50** times against `size: 4`'s 9 — which is why `1` was chosen as the most compact *well-attested* chart height. **Two real defects were found by running the seven new queries rather than reading them**, both in the same family as the earlier `sum()`-returns-`0` finding: **`latest` is a reserved KQL word** (three queries failed to parse, `SYN0002`), and **`avg()` over an empty set returns `NaN`, which passes `isnotnull()`** — so the Quality and Component-quality tiles would have rendered a literal **`NaN`** with a `0 turns` subtitle where the honesty rule requires `— (not run)`; both fixed, and both branches then **proved on synthetic rows** (a seeded 2-turn datatable returns real scores, an empty one of the same schema returns 0 rows, and persona correctly reports a denominator of 1 where context and orchestration report 2, so NULL-is-not-zero survives). **One tab genuinely cannot have four sections and says so**: Golden-bank coverage has three, because its source is a repo file and a workbook has no data source that can read one over time — inventing a trend axis for a static figure would be a fabrication. Verified: **30/30 query steps executed live** (26 Log Analytics + 4 ARG, extracted programmatically from the written JSON), **0 failures**, correct column schema on every one; content-loss audit **95/95 identifiers and 44/44 numbers** preserved; **26/26 pre-existing queries byte-identical** with exactly **3 deliberately superseded** (round 4's single-value insight tiles, each replaced by a 3-number scores query over the same events, pinned to three specific substrings so nothing else can vanish through the allowance); schema-validated Draft 7 **0 errors** whole-document and per-component; **concurrent-edit defence was a real check** — the destination SHA-256 was recorded at start and re-verified twice immediately before writing, with the write refusing to proceed on a mismatch; `PUT` 200 (`timeModified 2026-08-22T05:28:57Z`) with the `GET` returning `serializedData` **byte-identical** (111,428 chars / 111,660 UTF-8 bytes, 104 em dashes + 10 arrows + 1 `≥`), and the four-section shape re-read back out of what Azure stored rather than only asserted locally. Still `[~]`: **no pixels were seen this round either**, and the explicit success bar — whether the four boxes genuinely fit one screen with no scrolling — is a rendering question that is **not claimed**. Stated as arithmetic rather than left to be discovered: a tile row + parameter pills + tab bar + a `size: 1` chart + a 5-row table is roughly 540px of the ~580px a 1080p window leaves, so a laptop-height window will still scroll. Deploying the real workbook remains a founder action. **The six overview tiles were abandoned for a plain table, 2026-08-22 (round 6).** Founder feedback listed one defect per tile, in tile order, and the truncations named themselves — *"quality pass **r**"*, *"**Clarifi**"*, *"Zero-result **rat**"*, plus scrollbars, oversized fonts, "no alignment", and golden-bank showing *"a vague partial comment of '53 gaps - static, 2026-08-21'"*. Root cause is not a mis-set parameter: a `tiles` step at `customWidth: 15` is ~15% of the page, so the title clips mid-word and the `formatter: 12` **big-number renderer** fills what is left. Three prior rounds each fixed a tile parameter, each passed schema validation + live query execution + a byte-identical round-trip, and each still rendered broken in a new way — so the agreed fix was to **stop using `tiles` for the overview row**, not to tune it a fourth time. The overview is now a caption plus **two `visualization: "table"` steps** sharing one `Metric | Value | Detail` column layout: `Cost today (USD) / 0.02`, `Quality pass rate (%) / — (not run)`, `Clarification rate (%) / — (not run)`, `Zero-result rate (%) / — (not run)`, `Golden-bank coverage / 8 of 53`, `Alerts today / 1`. **Two steps and not one is a platform constraint, tested rather than assumed**: five metrics are Log Analytics and *Alerts today* is Azure Resource Graph, a query step carries exactly one `queryType`, and the cross-service `arg()` escape hatch **succeeds** on `api.applicationinsights.io/v1` but **fails on the ARM path a workbook step actually runs on** — `BadArgumentError / "The 'adx' pattern cannot be used with the current authentication scheme"`. A merge step over hidden sources, and a hidden ARG-backed parameter (`isHiddenWhenLocked`, 3,935 uses in Microsoft's 668 templates, so a real pattern) were both rejected as *more* exotic than the thing being removed, and because two independent steps fail independently. The two grids carry identical `customColumnWidthSetting` values on identically-named columns, so they line up as one continuous table — the direct answer to "no alignment". **Empty states are now structural, not a message**: `noDataMessage` only fires on zero rows, so in a union a metric with no data would have had *no row at all*; every leg therefore ends in a `summarize` with **no `by` clause** (guaranteed exactly one row, verified live for both LA and ARG) and `Value` is a **string**, so it can hold `— (no data)` / `— (not run)`. `max()` over empty was confirmed to return **null, not NaN**, and round 5's `latest`-reserved-word and `isnan()` guards are carried forward unchanged. All four reachable empty branches were **executed live** — `Tenant=all`, a real tenant, a tenant with no data, and a forced-empty alerts set (which correctly reads `0` with *"none fired today - this source is live, so 0 here is a real zero"*, still the deliberate exception to the dash rule). Golden-bank's clipped fragment is now `8 of 53` in **Value** plus `closed answer-quality gaps with a re-runnable test - static, hand-entered 2026-08-21` in a `1fr` **Detail** column. On the separate *"for top tiles make the fonts very very small"*: **no font-size control exists for a grid in this schema, stated plainly rather than fabricated** — `font` appears **0 times** in Microsoft's official `schema/workbook.json`, `gridSettings` accepts only `formatters`/`labelSettings`/`filter`/`rowLimit`/`hierarchySettings`/`sortBy`, and `styleSettings` only `margin`/`padding`/`maxWidth`/`showBorder`; the only three font-ish keys across 668 shipped templates are `statSettings.valueFontStyle` (19, *stat* viz), an undocumented `tileSettings.styleSettings.fontSize` (1 use, value `"large"`) and `/layout/options/rowHeight` (2, an Azure *dashboard* key) — none applies to a grid. Removing `formatter: 12` is what shrinks the text; beyond that the next lever is browser zoom. **Deliberately avoided** so this cannot be misconfigured back into the same failure: no big-number formatter, no `tileSettings`, no `customWidth` (both steps full width, so no title can clip), no `rowLimit`, no filter box, and no fixed-unit width on the longest column (`Detail` is `1fr`, a share of *remaining* space, so it cannot cause a horizontal scrollbar). Both steps use `size: 4` — every table with `rowLimit <= 5` in Microsoft's 668 templates uses it (6 of 6, in the same `Containers Security` template this workbook's tab bar was modelled on, at `customWidth: 50`); the numeric meaning of `size` is **not documented anywhere public**, so that is usage evidence, not a specification. **One real loss, recorded rather than dressed up**: the `Sev0`–`Sev4` severity icons (round 4's only colour) are gone, because thresholds on a shared string column that also holds `— (not run)` would be meaningless; their sentence *"Sev0/Sev1 are the ones worth interrupting someone for"* survives as a `tooltipFormat` (858 grid formatters carry one across Microsoft's templates), which is a hover and therefore weaker. One behavioural divergence, stated: the six tiles had three different time contexts and one step can only have one (`TimeRange`) — for all four values the pill offers, every leg returns exactly what its tile did, but a *custom* range shorter than the elapsed part of today would make "Cost today" under-report. Verified: **25/25 query steps executed live**, 0 failures (the first run's 3 "failures" were a **harness** bug — `{TimeRange:seconds}` left unsubstituted — recorded because a harness bug that looks like a product bug is the same class as round 4's vacuously-passing audit); measured against **what Azure had stored**, 24 of 30 queries byte-identical with exactly **6 removed and 2 added**; `shared-parameters` and the entire `sections` subtree (tab bar + all 8 tabs) **byte-identical as JSON**; content-loss audit **17/17 identifiers and 20/20 numbers** plus 14 load-bearing phrases preserved; schema-validated Draft 7 **0 errors** whole-document and per-step; serialiser proved lossless first; concurrent-edit defence re-asserted the destination SHA-256 immediately before writing (round 5's revert made this load-bearing); `PUT` 200 (`timeModified 2026-08-22T05:59:38Z`) with the `GET` returning `serializedData` **byte-identical** (106,862 chars / 107,096 UTF-8 bytes, 105 em dashes + 10 arrows + 1 `≥`), and the new shape re-read out of what Azure stored. Still `[~]`: **no pixels were seen**. That a table avoids the scrollbar and the big fonts is a **reasoned bet, not a measurement** — the big-number renderer is gone and a grid has no font control to get wrong, but whether `size: 4` gives a five-row grid enough height is exactly the kind of rendering question the last three rounds each got wrong in a new way; the argument is only that a grid has far fewer ways to be misconfigured than a tile. Also left untouched and worth knowing: the seven per-tab `*-scores` steps inside the tabs are **still `tiles`** — out of scope this round, same visualization, same failure modes. Deploying the real workbook remains a founder action. **Round 7 (2026-08-22) — seven score tiles, one merge step, every redundant heading deleted.** Six founder asks in one round, the last reversing the first: the overview is now **7 tiles** (Cost, Median latency, Quality, Clarification, Zero-result, Golden-bank, Alerts) styled from the Cost tab's own `cost-scores` step, produced by **one** visible step — a `queryType: 7` merge (`mergeType: "union"`) over the LA and ARG steps, which are kept as **hidden data sources** (`conditionalVisibility` on an undeclared `nevershow` parameter, Microsoft's own `WVD/AtScale/Overview` idiom). Round 6's constraint stands — the two *queries* still cannot be unioned — but merging their *results* is ordinary shipped practice: **163 merge steps across the 668 templates, 49 `union`, 21 pairing `queryType: 0` with `queryType: 1`**. **Why these tiles are not the three that failed:** the broken ones were **six separate steps at `customWidth: 15`**; this is **one full-width step whose query returns 7 rows**, which is exactly what `cost-scores` does — top-level `tileSettings` key set asserted identical to it, same `size: 4`, same `tileSettings.size: "auto"`, same `formatter: 12`. **One deliberate difference:** no `numberFormat`, because `Value` is a string carrying `— (not run)` per row; shipped precedent for `formatter: 12` over a `strcat` column is **3 of 802** tile steps (`SapMonitor2.0/AIOpsInsights`), all without `numberFormat` — thin, so if the three never-run tiles render blank the fix is `formatter: 1`, and the `Detail` subtitle still states the reason either way. Cost of the merge, stated: the whole overview is behind one step, so a merge failure blanks it rather than degrading it. Alerts `Detail` dropped the `worst Sev` framing for a plain breakdown (live: `Sev2: 1` today, `Sev1: 8, Sev2: 18` over 30 days); the `Sev0/Sev1 … worth interrupting someone for` tooltip is **gone** (tiles have no grid formatters). **Latency added** as a 6th LA row, reduced from the Latency tab's own `percentile(latency_ms, 50/95)` / `top 1 by agent_p95 desc` query (`percentile()` over empty returns null, not NaN — checked live). **The overview caption and all 7 non-Documentation tab headings deleted** (group `title` + the first step's `title`, which began with the tab's own name in all 7 cases); six said nothing their explainer line did not, the seventh (`Online signals — live traffic, no ground truth`) carried a caveat found nowhere else in the workbook, so it now opens that tab's explainer. **Scope deviation, flagged:** the Documentation tab *was* edited — to keep the caption's honesty rule (now covering the dash convention), to carry the ground-truth caveat, and to de-stale two round-6 leftovers ("six overview tiles", and an `Alerts today` row still describing severity icons that had already been removed) plus a new `Median latency (ms)` row. **The scrollbar question, answered as far as JSON can:** the official schema has **no height property in any of its 40 definitions**, there is no `rowLimit` anywhere in the overview, `size: 4` is the largest value occurring in any of the 668 templates, and the overview's height settings are now byte-identical to the tile step the founder already accepts. Rendered pixels remain unmeasured — still `[~]`. Verified across four hash-guarded writes: **25/25 executable query steps ran live after each, 0 failures** (the 26th is the merge descriptor — client-side portal logic with **no query API to call**, an honest gap, not a pass); every new query run live in all 4 LA branches and all 3 alerts branches before being written; diffs measured against **what Azure had stored**; 24/24 load-bearing strings kept and all intended removals absent file-wide; Draft 7 schema **0 errors** every time; SHA-256 re-asserted immediately before each write; final `PUT` 200 (`timeModified 2026-08-22T06:30:41Z`) with the `GET` round-trip **byte-identical** (106,348 chars / 106,572 UTF-8 bytes) and the merge descriptor re-read out of what Azure stored. **Also this round, from "can the summary be the 1st tab since its not visible totally":** there is no Summary tab and Cost is already first and default, so the question was turned into a measurement — Cost was the **longest** tab in the workbook (6 rendered blocks vs 4-5 elsewhere), and two of them (`cost-by-tool-table`, `cost-by-tool-turn-composition`) return **0 rows** live because `ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE` is off. The Documentation tab has claimed since the consolidation round that detail tables "sit behind a **Show …** button"; checked — **no such button ever existed**, none of the three `*-detail` groups carried any visibility control. All three now carry `expandable: true` / `expanded: false` / a clickable `title` (**198 shipped uses of `expandable` across the 668 templates, all 198 with a title**; absent from the published schema, which is known-incomplete — it also omits `crossComponentResources` and `timeContextFromParameter`). Cost now opens as 3 blocks, not 6; the duplicated `### Cost by tool` markdown heading was dropped and `doc-intro`'s "Show … button" claim corrected. **No tab was renamed or reordered** — that would have been guessing at what "summary" meant. Re-verified after this write: 25/25 executable steps live, **all 26 queries byte-identical** to what Azure stored, 13 diff lines, schema 0 errors, `PUT` 200 (`timeModified 2026-08-22T06:36:14Z`) with a **byte-identical** round-trip (106,766 chars / 106,990 UTF-8 bytes).

- `[~]` **Phase 3 — Golden-set regression, built and really run 2026-08-21; the *schedule* is not.** `AgentEvalRun` (`models.py`) + alembic `b5d2c8a41f30` (chained onto `f3e8b2a1d6c9`, confirmed the real head by running `alembic heads` first); `services/agent_eval.py` (faithfulness = RAGAS' claim-decomposition definition, relevance, accuracy, three independent pass floors — an LLM judge rather than the `ragas` package, because ragas pulls `datasets`/`pyarrow`/`pandas` into an image built with `uv sync --frozen` and shared with production); `scripts/run_agent_eval.py`; `tests/agent_eval_golden_sample.py` (9 cases). **Really executed**: 2 runs × 9 cases × both chat paths = 36 real Azure `gpt-5-mini` turns, 0 harness errors, 18 rows persisted per run; raw output `tests/agent_eval_output.json`. `tests/test_agent_eval.py` 19 passed (incl. running the migration's own DDL up and down). Three honest amendments: (a) `test_chat_sql_quality.py`/`test_rag.py` are **not** answer-bearing golden sets — they are mocked mechanics tests with a scripted LLM and no expected answer to score against, so their question *phrasings* were reused and reference answers written from the seeded fixture; (b) **no schedule exists** (no cron, no ACA job); (c) the judge demonstrably under-scored two answer shapes (a correct "no records found" scoring 0.0 faithfulness; an identical correct refusal scoring 1.0 on one path and 0.0 on the other), so **absolute pass-rate was not yet a quality verdict** — usable as a trend on a fixed question set, which is what the workbook plots. **Amendment (c) is now half-closed**: both shapes are fixed the same day (see the judge-failure-modes entry below), but the *general* statement still holds — four identified biases are removed and that does not by itself make the absolute level trustworthy, which needs a human-graded calibration set or a stronger judge model. Neither exists.
- `[~]` **Phase 4 — Substitution testing.** The substitution *axis* is built 2026-08-23; **no candidate has been benchmarked**, so there is still no cost-vs-quality delta table. `utils/llm.py::build_llm(provider, *, model, max_tokens, api_version, allow_mock_fallback)` is `get_llm()`'s existing `azure`/`ollama`/`mock` construction logic parameterised on provider/model instead of reading both off `Settings`; `get_llm()` is now a thin wrapper over it and its behaviour (including the fail-safe mock fallback) is unchanged. `scripts/run_agent_eval.py` gains `--provider` / `--model` / `--api-version` / `--persist-candidate`, running the identical case set against a named model **for that run only**: the override patches the `get_llm` binding in the three chat-path modules (`agents.query_agent`, `agents.query_tools`, `agents.sage_orchestrator` — read off the code, with a drift-guard test) inside a context manager, and writes nothing to `.env`, `os.environ` or the settings object, so the live app's default provider is unreachable from it. Four properties, each tested: **the judge stays on the configured default** (built before the override is entered; `services/agent_eval.py` resolves its judge through `utils.llm` directly and is deliberately not a patch target) so candidates stay comparable; **a named provider refuses to become `MockInvoiceLLM`** (`allow_mock_fallback=False` → `LlmConfigurationError`, because a table reporting mock output under a candidate's name is worse than no table); **a candidate's rows do not join the baseline trend** (no `agent_eval_run` write without `--persist-candidate`, and rows written carry `model_under_test=<provider>:<model>` in notes); **a candidate run gets its own output file** rather than overwriting `tests/agent_eval_output.json`. 37 tests in `tests/test_model_substitution.py`, plus two real smoke runs (`--provider mock` produced mock output against an Azure-configured `.env`, proving the bypass; `--provider azure --model gpt-5-mini --api-version 2024-08-01-preview` ran a live turn, proving the Azure branch). **GPT-4o candidate deployed and live-verified 2026-08-24**: `infra/gpt4o-deployment.bicep` provisions a `gpt-4o` (`2024-11-20`, `GlobalStandard`, 10K TPM) deployment under the existing `openai-invoicellm-dev` resource — deployment name `gpt-4o`. Verified live three ways (raw chat completion, strict-mode `json_schema` structured output, and a real `run_agent_eval.py --provider azure --model gpt-4o --api-version 2024-08-01-preview` turn: `pass=True`, 0 errors, not persisted). Real ongoing cost confirmed via the Azure Retail Prices API: $2.50/1M input tokens, $10.00/1M output tokens (`eastus2` `GlobalStandard`) — roughly 17-20x gpt-5-mini's per-token rate. No candidate *benchmark* has been run yet (that needs `--persist-candidate` over the full case set). **Ollama candidate deployed and live-verified 2026-08-24** (Option A, founder-approved same day): `infra/ollama-eval-only.bicep` provisions a CPU-only `ollama/ollama:latest` Container App (`ca-ollama-eval-dev`) in the existing `cae-invoicellm-dev` environment — endpoint
`https://ca-ollama-eval-dev.thankfulmeadow-4281ea23.eastus2.azurecontainerapps.io`, model `llama3.2:latest` pulled and persisted on a mounted Azure Files volume so it survives scale-to-zero cold starts. Sizing corrected live from the approved 4 vCPU/8 GiB to **2.0 vCPU/4.0 GiB** — `cae-invoicellm-dev` turned out to be the classic Consumption-only plan (`workloadProfiles: null`), whose real per-container-app ceiling is 2.0/4.0, not the 4.0/8.0 a workload-profile-enabled Consumption plan allows. External ingress (IP-restricted, since Ollama has no built-in auth) was required rather than internal-only, because `run_agent_eval.py` runs from a local dev machine, not from inside the CAE. Verified live: `/api/tags` shows the model pulled with `capabilities: ["completion","tools"]`; a real `/api/generate` call returned a real completion; and a full run through `run_agent_eval.py --provider ollama --model llama3.2:latest --paths default --cases greeting_no_tool` against the live endpoint produced `llm_calls=1, relevance=1.0 accuracy=1.0 pass=True, errors=0`. Real cost confirmed via Azure Retail Prices API: $0.216/hour while a replica is active, $0 while scaled to zero (`minReplicas: 0`) — revises the research pass's ~$3.50/month estimate to **~$1.75/month** at the same ~8 active-hours/month cadence, reflecting the corrected sizing. See `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s 2026-08-24 "Ollama candidate — Option A built and live-verified" section for full detail. Remaining for `[x]`: no GPT-4o *or* Ollama comparison table has been produced yet (needs `--persist-candidate` over the full case set), extraction is still uncovered (the override patches chat-path modules only — this harness does not exercise `extraction_agent.py`), the llama3.2 invented-routing caveat from the 2026-08-23 smoke test has not been re-tested against this live server, and Phase 3's caveat (c) still applies — the judge's under-scoring fixes have never been re-run against a live model, so there is no post-fix baseline for a candidate to be compared against.

- `[x]` **Gap 296 (BE): a hallucinated chat route was answered conversationally instead of being caught.** `QueryRoutingSchema.route` (`agents/query_agent.py`) was typed `str` with a description merely *asking* for `RAG`/`SQL`/`CHAT`, so the emitted JSON schema carried no `enum` and nothing constrained the model's output. Because `run_query_agent()` dispatches `if SQL / elif RAG / else: # CHAT`, **any** unrecognised value fell through to the conversational branch: the user got a chatty answer, no retrieval ran, and nothing recorded that routing had failed. Found 2026-08-23 by a live smoke test of the corrected `OLLAMA_MODEL` default (`llama3.2:latest`), which returned syntactically valid structured output with an invented `route` — schema-valid and semantically wrong. Fixed by typing the field `Literal["RAG", "SQL", "CHAT"]` (a real `enum` the provider constrains generation against) plus a `mode="before"` validator that strips/upper-cases, so the case-tolerance the old `result.route.upper()` provided is preserved exactly; an out-of-vocabulary value now raises and `classify_query()`'s existing `except` falls back to RAG, which does retrieve. **Azure regression evidence, measured before and after rather than asserted**: 30/30 live gpt-5-mini classifications parsed with 0 validation errors in both states, and a same-process old-schema-vs-new-schema A/B over 96 live calls had 0 errors in either arm and identical routes on every unambiguous question. Qualification stated rather than buried: on *genuinely ambiguous* questions the sampled distribution does shift (reproducibly for one question, and in the opposite direction for another), but the old typing was a coin flip on exactly those questions, so neither arm is a correct baseline there. **The premise is unverified** — that the enum fixes non-Azure models could not be tested, no Ollama server was running.

- `[x]` **The two judge failure modes Phase 3 left open are fixed** — 2026-08-21, later the same day, both as rubric corrections rather than per-question special cases, and both diagnosed from the persisted output of the run that exhibited them (`tests/agent_eval_output.json`) rather than guessed at. **(3) A correct "no records found" scored 0.00 faithfulness because the judge was never shown the query.** The evidence handed to it was the whole of `"DATABASE RESULTS:\nNo records found matching the query criteria."` while the claims under judgement named *Nonexistent Holdings* and *last quarter* — neither of which appears anywhere in that evidence, so the 0/2 verdict was correct on what it was given. The verdict prompt's own rule 5 already anticipated this ("supported when the context shows that query was the one executed") but its precondition could never be met. Fixed by threading `executed_queries` (SQL + every tool call with its arguments) through `score_faithfulness()`/`score_answer()` and `scripts/run_agent_eval.py`, and by replacing the bolted-on empty-result carve-out with a per-claim `claim_type` (`positive_fact`/`absence`/`query_scope`/`non_factual`) the judge must assign *before* deciding, with the evidence standard stated per type; `non_factual` claims now leave the denominator instead of costing score. **(4) The same correct out-of-scope refusal scored 1.0 on SAGE and 0.0 on the default path** because every numeric anchor in the relevance rubric was phrased as *"answers what was asked"* — which a refusal by construction does not — so the three carve-outs appended after them were fighting the rubric they were attached to. Fixed by making the judge classify an `answer_kind` first, with the score fixed **in code** (`RELEVANCE_KIND_SCORES`) for the kinds whose relevance is definitional (`no_results_report`/`out_of_scope_refusal`/`capability_or_greeting` → 1.0, `off_topic` → 0.0) and judged only for `direct_answer`/`clarifying_question`. Tests are pinned to the **real answer texts from the failing run**, and the refusal test feeds in the actual 0.0-vs-1.0 divergence to assert it no longer propagates. `tests/test_agent_eval.py` 19 → 55 passed. **Not re-run against a live model**: the fixes change scoring, so pre-fix and post-fix figures are not comparable and post-fix numbers do not exist yet.
- `[x]` **Component-level scoring** — `context_score` / `orchestration_score` / `persona_score` on `AgentEvalRun` + alembic `c4a91e77b208` (chained onto `b5d2c8a41f30`, confirmed the real single head by running `alembic heads` first, single head re-verified after; three nullable floats, no server default, so every existing row reads as "not scored" and never as 0.0). Three genuinely different mechanisms, not one check run three times: **context** is a deterministic F1 of fetched invoice ids against the golden case's known-correct set (no judge — a case whose correct retrieval is *nothing* scores 1.0 for fetching nothing, which is Gap 224's shape); **orchestration** is mechanical figure traceability (every number in the answer must appear in a tool result or be arithmetic over ones that do — also no judge); **persona** is the one LLM-judged component, with an explicit `applicable=false` escape. Mirrored onto the `agent_eval_run` custom event and given **three separate trend lines** in the workbook, per the feature's own "not one blended average" instruction. **Additive and inert — they do not feed the pass decision** (folding them in would redefine what a pass means halfway through a trend series). **One honest limitation, in the code, the doc and the workbook panel**: there is no dedicated domain-knowledge golden set in this repo, so persona is scored against the general sample with a persona-focused rubric and returns NULL on most turns — read `persona_scored_turns` before reading `persona`. Writing that bank is still outstanding. A second under-scoring path was found by running the orchestration check against the 2026-08-21 round's real output (a correct answer's derived `USD 20.00` discrepancy marked untraceable) and fixed by allowing a second arithmetic hop restricted to already-grounded figures.
- `[x]` **Per-tool cost/token breakdown** — `infra/monitoring/llm_cost_by_tool.kql` + workbook section 2, by the real `sage.*` agent names in use (`sage.planner`, `sage.synthesis`, `sage.identify`, `sage.aggregate`, `sage.search` — read off the code, not assumed). Answers "how much does `get_full_record`'s full-chunk dump cost relative to the rest of the turn" via `synthesis_share_pct` per `request_id`, with the structural reason stated in both places rather than papered over: **`get_full_record` makes no LLM call at all**, so it has no event of its own and its cost sits inside `sage.synthesis`'s `tokens_in` and nowhere else. That makes the answer an inference from prompt size, not a direct measurement; the direct measurement exists only in the offline harness's matched `large_invoice_full_detail` (11 indexed pages) / `small_invoice_full_detail` (1 page) pair, and the file says so.
- `[x]` **Online-signal telemetry mirror** — `services/online_eval_signals.py::emit_online_signals()` + `telemetry.track_online_signal()`, so the workbook's online panel has a data source at all (a workbook cannot query Postgres — the same constraint that already forced the `agent_eval_run` mirror). `confidence` travels **on the event**, and a None value emits no `value` field rather than a 0.0, because None means "the denominator was empty" and a 0.0 would render an ingestion outage as a healthy day. **Nothing calls it on a schedule** — the panel says an empty result there means "nothing has run", not "no problems found". Neither of the two gaps that module reported is closed by this: `stop_reason` still never reaches `ChatMessage` on a live turn, and turn latency is still a row-timestamp delta rather than a timer. Both are reproduced verbatim in the panel's own header table.

Phase 1 is built **and now live in Application Insights** (first event 2026-08-22T03:48:30Z); Phases 2/3 are built but the workbook is still an unimported definition and nothing is scheduled; Phase 4 outstanding.

  **The single combined tabbed workbook was retired and split into 9 standalone workbooks — 2026-08-23.** Reason: Azure lets you pin a whole workbook or an individual visual to a Dashboard, but a pinned tile does not evaluate the source workbook's own `conditionalVisibility`/`SelectedTab` tab logic, so a single tab could never be reliably pinned out of the combined file. `infra/monitoring/ai_control_tower.workbook.json` (the tab bar + 8 `SelectedTab`-gated groups) was deleted; in its place are 9 standalone JSON files in the same folder (`ai_control_tower_summary.workbook.json` plus one per former tab: `_cost`, `_latency`, `_quality`, `_component_quality`, `_online_signals`, `_golden_bank`, `_health`, `_documentation`), each carrying its own copy of the shared `TimeRange`/`Subscription`/`Tenant` parameter block (`type: 9`) so every KQL query's `{Tenant}` substitution and `timeContextFromParameter` still resolve standalone. Each non-Summary file is its former tab's `items` array unwrapped (group wrapper and `conditionalVisibility` dropped, content otherwise untouched); the Summary file preserves the old overview group's hidden-query merge mechanism (`nevershow` `conditionalVisibility` on the two source steps, `queryType: 7` union feeding the `tiles` step) byte-for-byte. Per later founder direction the same session, Summary also **embeds the full Documentation content as a collapsed `type: 12` group** (`loadButtonText: "Show full documentation"`, `expandable: true`, `expanded: false` — the same pattern as the pre-existing "Cost by tool" collapsible section) so pinning Summary's tiles to a Dashboard and clicking through reaches Documentation with no extra navigation, rather than a dead end at just the 7 tiles; the embedded copy and the standalone `_documentation` file are asserted textually identical so they cannot drift apart. New `infra/monitoring/ai_control_tower_workbooks.bicep` deploys all 9 as `Microsoft.Insights/workbooks@2022-04-01` resources (`serializedData` via `loadTextContent()` per file, `appi-invoicellm-dev` referenced as `existing`, not routed through `08-apps.bicep`/`params.dev.json` — same narrow-standalone-deploy rationale as `agent-eval-job-only.bicep`). `az bicep build` passed; `az deployment group what-if` against `rg-invoice-llm-dev` returned a clean **9 to create, 0 to modify, 0 to delete**. **Not yet deployed** — `az deployment group create` was deliberately not run, pending founder go-ahead.

- `[x]` **Golden-bank eval scheduled job deployed** — 2026-08-22. `caj-agent-eval-dev` (Azure Container Apps Job, cron `0 3 * * *` UTC, `python scripts/run_agent_eval.py --paths default,sage`, 90-minute timeout) deployed via a **narrow, standalone** bicep file (`infra/agent-eval-job-only.bicep`), not through `08-apps.bicep`'s normal path. Reason, found via `what-if` before deploying anything: `08-apps.bicep`'s image params (`backendImage`/`frontendImage`/`websiteImage`/`queueWorkerImage`) are stale relative to what CI/CD actually has running — CI/CD updates images via `az containerapp update` directly, bypassing bicep — so a full `08-apps.bicep` deploy against `params.dev.json` would have **rolled back all four container apps to stale image tags** (confirmed live: queue-worker would have reverted from today's real build to a stale `:latest` tag on a *different* ACR name, `acrinvoicellmdev` vs. the real `acrinvoicellmdev2`) as a side effect of adding one job. Also found the same way: `params.dev.json`'s `namingPrefix` (`invoice-llm`) and the derived `sharedAcrName` formula both compute names that do not match what is actually deployed (`invoicellm`, no hyphen; `acrinvoicellmdev2`, a `2` suffix the formula never produces) — real, pre-existing drift, out of scope to fix here. The standalone file hardcodes every resource reference to the real, live name instead of the computed ones, and its own `what-if` confirmed **1 resource to create, 0 to modify** before deploying. Manually triggered once after deploy (`az containerapp job start`) to confirm it actually runs end to end, not just that the resource exists — result recorded in the next entry.

- `[ ]` **Gap: activate the two other scheduled jobs already coded but never deployed.** Found while deploying the item above: `caj-overdue-sweep-dev` (Gap 126, the outbound overdue-webhook sweep) and `caj-billing-lifecycle-dev` (Gaps 119/121, daily paid-lapse + free-quota-refill sweep) are both fully coded in `08-apps.bicep`, both documented elsewhere as shipped, and **neither actually exists in Azure** — confirmed via `az containerapp job show`, both return `ResourceNotFound`. Activating them is a real, separate product decision (a real webhook sweep and real billing automation would start running), not something to bundle into an unrelated deploy — deliberately not done as a side effect of the item above. Needs the same treatment: fix or work around `08-apps.bicep`'s stale-image-param problem first, `what-if` to confirm blast radius, then a deliberate decision to switch each one on.

See the canonical Gap 298 entry under `## Open Items / Gaps` ("a Stage 8 deploy against the live dev environment would break all four running container apps"), which now carries this entry's two unique facts (the standalone-template pattern, and CI/CD's `az containerapp update` bypass of bicep).** Reconciled 2026-08-26: same subject, same open state, same 2026-08-23 origin — one Gap number, two entries. Kept in place rather than deleted, per CONVENTIONS hard rule 4; **do not update this copy — status changes belong on the canonical entry.** Original wording follows.

  **Gap 298 (Infra): `08-apps.bicep` is unrunnable against dev as-is — image and naming-prefix drift, unrelated to any single feature.** Found 2026-08-23 while trying to deploy the Ops Digest Agent's scheduled job. `az deployment group what-if` on the full `08-apps.bicep`/`params.dev.json` path returns **3 to create, 4 to modify** — the 4 modifications are every running container app, for reasons that predate this feature: `params.dev.json`'s `backendImage` names `acrinvoicellmdev.azurecr.io`, a registry that **does not exist** (`az acr list` shows only `acrinvoicellmdev2`); `namingPrefix` is `invoice-llm` but the real environment was built `invoicellm` (no hyphen), so what-if would rewrite every Key Vault URI and managed identity to names that resolve to nothing; and it would roll back Gap 290's scale rules. Same root cause already on file for `agent-eval-job-only.bicep` and the AI Control Tower workbooks split — every new scheduled job in this repo has had to route around `08-apps.bicep` via a narrow standalone bicep file instead of fixing this once. Worked around this time too: `infra/ops-digest-job-only.bicep`, what-ifs clean (1 to create, 50 to ignore). Not fixed at the root — that would mean reconciling `params.dev.json` to what CI/CD actually deploys (which updates images via `az containerapp update` directly, bypassing bicep entirely), a real, deliberate infra task, not a side effect of any one feature's deploy.

  pattern exactly: `telemetry.track_extraction_benchmark_run()` (the 5 raw
  confusion-matrix cells **and** the 3 derived percentages — the counts are what let a

  documents ran" from "0% false positives") and `telemetry.track_agent_eval_summary()`
  (all 9 `EvalScores` dimensions as means, each next to its own `*_scored_turns`
  denominator, because `persona_score` is NULL on most turns by design and a bare
  `avg()` over the per-turn events silently averages a different denominator than the
  other eight); new `services/benchmark_artifacts.py` uploading each run's **full** raw
  JSON to a `benchmark-artifacts` blob container under
