- `[x]` **Gap 308 (BE/Infra): the nightly benchmark job did all of its work correctly and then
  crashed on its own default output path, reporting `Failed` every night** — opened and fixed
  2026-08-24. **Found by deploying `caj-benchmark-eval-dev` and running it, not by reading
  anything**; two full sessions of code review over these exact files had not caught it.
  The job's persisted args (identical in `infra/08-apps.bicep`'s `benchmarkEvalJob` module and
  `infra/benchmark-eval-job-only.bicep`) end with `python scripts/run_agent_eval.py --paths
  default --run-label nightly` — **no `--out`** — and that script's default was
  `tests/agent_eval_output.json`, under a directory `Prod_Invoice_LLM/.dockerignore` strips
  from the image (`**/tests/`, line 47 — the same exclusion that killed the deleted
  `caj-agent-eval-dev` job with `ModuleNotFoundError` and prompted the `benchmarks/` package
  move). Live evidence, execution `caj-benchmark-eval-dev-d0gm1bo`: every piece of real work
  succeeded — Track 1 alert recall **1.0**, Track 2 **20 real `agent_eval_run` rows committed
  to Postgres**, the `agent_eval_summary` mirror emitted — and then the process died on the
  final write with `FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory:
  '/app/tests/agent_eval_output.json'`. With `retryLimit 0`, Container Apps records that as a
  **`Failed` execution**, so the 03:00 UTC run would have gone red every night on a run whose
  results were entirely correct. That is worse than a plain crash: it makes the job's own
  execution status — the signal Gap 299 and the Ops Digest collection layer both intend to
  read — permanently noisy, i.e. either useless or actively trained-to-be-ignored.
  **Fixed in the script, not in bicep.** `default_output_dir()` (new,
  `scripts/run_agent_eval.py`) returns `tests/` when that directory exists — every source
  checkout, so **local dev, the six committed `agent_eval_output*.json` files and every doc
  quoting a `tests/…` path are bit-for-bit unchanged** — and `tempfile.gettempdir()` when it
  does not, which is the image. Chosen over adding `--out /tmp/…` to the two bicep files
  because that would need an infra redeploy to take effect and would leave the script's own
  default still fatal for any future caller, whereas this ships in the image the Gap 309 fix
  below already requires. `tempfile.gettempdir()` rather than a literal `/tmp` because this
  script is also run from Windows. The pre-deploy gate was never affected — `deploy-dev.yml`
  already passes `--out /tmp/agent_eval_gate.json` explicitly. 7 new tests,
  `tests/test_run_agent_eval_cli.py`, driving the real `main()` on the literal nightly argv
  with `CASES` emptied (no LLM call, no turns — the defect is in the write at the end of
  `main()`, reached identically either way); two of them read the two bicep files directly and
  fail if either ever gains an `--out`, so the premise cannot silently rot. Reverting
  `default_output_dir()` to its pre-fix form reproduces the live failure exactly —
  `FileNotFoundError` on the `open(args.out, "w")` at the end of `main()`, naming a
  `…/app/tests/agent_eval_output.json` that does not exist — confirmed by running it.

- `[x]` **Gap 309 (BE/AI): `extraction_benchmark_run` telemetry was silently discarded by
  `logging`'s own level check — the exporter was attached correctly and never received a
  single record** — opened and fixed 2026-08-24, **found live in the same deployed run as Gap
  308**. Both of that execution's tracks printed a success line; only Track 2's
  `agent_eval_summary` was actually findable in `customEvents`. Track 1's stdout said
  `mirror [nightly] -> Application Insights + stdout: 1 telemetry event(s)` and **nothing
  arrived, on that run or any earlier one.** Root cause is one line before the exporter or the
  flush could matter: `telemetry._emit_event()` calls `logger.info(...)` on
  `invoice_be_telemetry`, and `configure_azure_monitor()` **adds a handler without ever
  setting a level** (verified against the installed distro — `azure/monitor/opentelemetry/
  _configure.py` only does `getLogger(logger_name).addHandler(handler)`). The logger therefore
  sits at `NOTSET`, inherits root's `WARNING` in a bare `python scripts/...` process, and
  `Logger.isEnabledFor(INFO)` is **False** — the record is dropped inside `logging` before any
  handler, Azure Monitor's included, is consulted. The reassuring stdout line is
  `MirrorResult.describe()` counting emitter *calls*. Exactly the silent-no-op class Gap 292
  named, one level lower down. **Why Track 2 was unaffected, which is what made this hard to
  see:** `scripts/run_agent_eval.py::_counting_llm_calls()` attaches its per-turn
  `_LlmCallCounter` to both event loggers and calls `lg.setLevel(logging.INFO)` to do it, and
  its `finally` removes the handler but never restores the level — so Track 2 has been carried
  the whole time by a side effect of an unrelated measurement helper firing on its first turn.
  `scripts/sweep_azure_cost.py` and `scripts/ops_digest_job.py` are covered by a different
  accident: both call `utils.logging_config.setup_structured_logging()`, which sets the
  **root** logger to INFO. `scripts/run_extraction_benchmark.py` does neither and was the only
  one of the four with nothing holding the level up. **Consequence beyond the mirror event:**
  every per-call `llm_agent_call` from a Track 1 `--mode live` run was being dropped the same
  way, so Gap 304 half (1)'s "the run's own per-call events now export, tagged `golden`" was
  true of Track 2 only. **Fix:** `_enable_event_logger_level()` (new,
  `services/benchmark_artifacts.py`), called first thing in `configure_run_telemetry()` — the
  one function both benchmark scripts already call to make telemetry work in a standalone
  process. Raises both names in `telemetry._EVENT_LOGGER_NAMES` to INFO, leaves a level the
  caller deliberately set *lower* (DEBUG) alone, and runs on the no-connection-string path too
  so a stdout-only run produces the structured console line the module docstring promises.
  **Why the 1400-test suite never caught this:** every existing assertion on these events
  either uses `caplog.at_level(logging.INFO)` (which raises the level itself) or patches
  `telemetry._emit_event` outright (so the level check never runs). The 5 new tests avoid both
  — 4 in `tests/test_benchmark_artifacts.py` (including one pinning the *premise*: at default
  levels an INFO event reaches no handler at all) and 1 end-to-end in
  `tests/test_run_extraction_benchmark_cli.py` driving the real `main()` with a recording
  handler attached exactly where the distro attaches its own. Both fail without the fix,
  verified by reverting it.

  **Verification for Gaps 308 + 309 together.** Baseline re-measured before touching
  anything (the previously-recorded `1414 passed / 7 failed` was stale — other workstreams
  had landed since): `uv run pytest tests/test_*.py -p no:randomly -q` → **1431 passed, 3
  failed, 7 skipped** in 406s. After: **1443 passed, 3 failed, 7 skipped** in 410s — +12,
  exactly the 12 new tests, with the same 3 pre-existing failures (2 in
  `tests/test_connectors.py` needing a local Redis, 1 in `tests/test_rag.py` from
  `post_chat_message()` gaining a `background_tasks` argument), none of them in a file
  touched here. `ruff check` clean on all 5 touched files. **What is NOT verified:** that
  `extraction_benchmark_run` now lands in `customEvents` — that needs a CI build and the
  next job execution. The test spies on the handler the distro attaches, which is the level
  at which the defect lives; no test in this suite opens a socket to Azure.
  **No redeploy needed.** No bicep changed; the job's template pins
  `acrinvoicellmdev2.azurecr.io/invoice-be:latest` and `deploy-dev.yml` sets
  `also_tag_latest: true` on every backend deploy, so a CI build containing this commit is
  what takes both fixes live and the next execution pulls it.

  approved minimum-sample guard (n=20 turns/calls, n=3 runs) via `cost-trend-budget`'s
  exact `-1 → blue, "n/a — see Detail"` sentinel pattern, with the real number still
  surfaced in Detail text.
  **Schema translation, done by hand, not pasted from the existing `.kql` files**: those
  three files are written against Application Insights' `customEvents`/`customDimensions`/
  `timestamp`; the workbook queries the Log Analytics workspace schema instead
  (`AppEvents`/`Name`/`parse_json(Properties)`/`TimeGenerated`), matching
  `cost_health_workbook.json` exactly. Translating surfaced **two real bugs in the
  untouched source `.kql` files**, never caught before because those files' own header
  states they were never executed live: `chat_thread_sessions.kql`'s
  `last_status = arg_max(timestamp, status)` mis-binds the alias to the arg-max *key*
  column (a `datetime`), not the requested value, and fails with `SEM0025` the moment it's
  compared against a string — fixed here with tuple syntax `(ts, status) = arg_max(...)`;
  and a bare `top 1 by ... desc` used for "latest run" panels returns **zero rows**, not a
  guaranteed one, when the input is empty — replaced everywhere with a `summarize`-based
  `arg_max` (guaranteed exactly one output row even over 0 input rows, matching the
  `cost-trend-budget` sentinel's own precondition). Also found and fixed live: **`latest`
  is a reserved KQL word** (`SYN0002` the first time `d1.kql` ran, renamed to
  `latest_run`) and **`avg()` over an empty set returns `NaN`, not `null`**, which would
  have rendered a literal `NaN` on `chat_turn`'s SQL-repair-loop and answer-shape panels
  before any real turns exist — guarded with `iff(isnan(x), real(null), x)` everywhere an
  `avg()` is used over a possibly-empty set.
  **The boolean-field risk the scoping pass flagged was checked live rather than assumed
  either way.** `chat_turn` emits `sql_generated`/`zero_result`/
  `zero_result_fallback_recovered` as real Python bools, unlike every 0/1-emitting field
  elsewhere in `telemetry.py`. No real `chat_turn` event exists yet (see below), so this
  could not be proven against a live row — but a synthetic-literal live query confirmed
  both `tobool("True"/"False")` **and** `tolower(tostring(x)) == "true"` parse both the
  Python `str()` form (`"True"`/`"False"`) and the JSON-boolean form (`true`/`false`,
  confirmed via `AppDependencies`' own `Properties`, which stringifies everything the same
  way) identically and correctly. Written with the defensive `tolower(tostring(...))`
  form per the scoping instruction anyway, and flagged in the workbook's own Section B
  header text to be re-verified against a real event once one lands.
  **Verification, the same standard as every other query this session**: all 40 query
  steps were extracted **programmatically from the written JSON** (not retyped) and
  executed live via `az monitor log-analytics query` against `law-invoicellm-dev` —
  **40/40 succeeded, 0 failures**, including every query expected to return 0 rows
  (SAGE-off panels, `chat_turn`-based panels, `extraction_benchmark_run`,
  `online_eval_signal`) — proving each one parses/casts/projects correctly rather than

  published Draft-7 `schema/workbook.json`: **0 errors**. `az bicep build`: clean. `az
  deployment group what-if` against `rg-invoice-llm-dev`: **1 to create, 53 to ignore, 0
  to modify** — no existing resource touched. **Not deployed** — `az deployment group
  create` deliberately left for founder go-ahead.
  **What is real data today vs. structurally empty, checked live rather than assumed
  from the task brief**: Section A (production `llm_agent_call`, 46 rows/30d) and Section
  D (one real `agent_eval_summary` predeploy row, 2026-08-24T12:31:23Z — every D tile's
  guard correctly shows the n=3 sentinel while still surfacing that real number in
  Detail, proving the panel binds) have real data. Sections B/C (`chat_turn`), E
  (`extraction_benchmark_run`, Gap 309's fix not yet in a deployed image) and F
  (`online_eval_signal`, nothing calls `emit_online_signals()` on a schedule) and G
  (`agent_eval_run` where `run_source=="production"`) are all correctly 0 rows — the
  backend image at the time of this build (`e19d1e4...`) is one commit behind
  `ed6f8c1` (today's Wave 1-4 telemetry push), so `chat_turn` itself has not reached
  Azure yet. `[~]` not `[x]`: built, schema-valid, what-if clean, but genuinely
  deploy-pending on two fronts — this workbook resource itself, and the backend image
  that would make most of its sections show real rows. Found 2026-08-23 building the Ops Digest Agent's collection layer. The two-tier design (`services/ops_digest_routing.py::classify()`) correctly labels a sharp AI-quality-score drop or a Track-1/2 benchmark-job failure as `"critical"`, but no delivery mechanism for AI-eval criticals exists anywhere yet — Azure Monitor alert rules only fire off Azure Monitor/Log Analytics signals, and nothing evaluates `classify()`'s output outside of a digest run. Until the digest scheduler is actually deployed and running (blocked on Gap 298's prerequisites — see Wave 2 in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`), an AI-eval "critical" is realistically **silent** until the next digest batch reads it, the same as everything else. The Ops Digest Agent code deliberately still **names** these items explicitly in its output as "critical, not yet paged" rather than silently treating them the same as an ordinary digest item once the scheduler is running — but until it is running, this is an open gap, not a documented limitation of a shipped system.

- **Real product defect surfaced by the Phase 3 run, recorded here rather than lost in a JSON file** — 2026-08-21. Asked *"whats the CGST we paid to Rajesh Steel"* against a fixture holding one combined `tax_amount` of INR 18,000.00 and no per-component breakdown, the **current default chat path** answered *"The CGST recorded for Rajesh Steel is INR 18,000.00"* — relabelling the combined tax as CGST. That is Gaps 263/264's failure mode, live, reproduced against the real model. SAGE Phase 2 answered the same question correctly (*"This schema does not store per-tax-type lines…the combined tax total…is ₹18,000"*). Not opened as a new Gap: it is the same class Feature 21 exists to fix structurally, and Gap 285's entry already records the decision not to patch this one field at a time. Evidence: `tests/agent_eval_output.json`, case `rajesh_steel_cgst`. **Closed by Gap 310 (below, 2026-08-25)** — and not in the way this entry expected: rather than shipping SAGE, Gap 310 retrofitted SAGE's core full-record fetch onto the default path, so the fix landed on the cheap route and SAGE's Phase 3 remains gated. The `rajesh_steel_cgst` golden case has been rewritten accordingly — it now expects the real INR 9,000.00 CGST rather than the decline SAGE Phase 2 gave, because the decline was never true of the *data*, only of the prompt.

- `[x]` **Gap 310 (BE/AI): the default chat route answered detail questions from a hand-typed 19-column schema block while the real `Invoice` row had long since outgrown it — the identified invoice's whole record now reaches the answering step** — 2026-08-25. **The failure class, not one field.** `taxes` (the itemized per-component tax rows, each with its own `tax_type`/`rate_percent`/`amount`), `subtotal`, `tax_ids`, `discounts`, `deductions`, `payment_instructions`, `references`, `compliance_metadata` and `field_confidence` are all real columns on `Invoice`, all populated by `queue_worker/handlers.py`, and **none was visible to the SQL route at all** — schema drift between a live model and a prompt maintained by hand. Measured live 2026-08-21 (the "Real product defect surfaced by the Phase 3 run" entry above): *"whats the CGST we paid to Rajesh Steel"* → *"The CGST recorded for Rajesh Steel is INR 18,000.00"*, relabelling the combined `tax_amount` as CGST while the invoice's own `taxes` held CGST 9% INR 9,000.00 + SGST 9% INR 9,000.00. **Fix**: `agents/query_agent.py::_full_record_block_for(invoice_ids, tenant_id, db_session)` calls `query_tools.get_full_record(..., include_document_pages=False)` (new keyword, added for this — SAGE keeps the default, so its behaviour is byte-identical) and interpolates the rendered record into the SQL route's summary prompt as `{db_result}{full_record_block}`. **Four decisions, each of them the point rather than an implementation detail**: (1) **generic, never keyword-gated** — it fires on every turn that identified at least one invoice, whatever was asked. A tax-keyword gate was built and then explicitly rejected on founder correction mid-run (2026-08-24): a keyword gate is the same failure mode this route already has two named instances of (rule 6d's tax-component miss, Gap 264's fixed term list), and "which discounts were applied" / "what's the vendor's GSTIN" / "what's the subtotal before tax" are the same gap wearing different words. (2) **Deterministic, no extra LLM round-trip** — binding a `get_invoice_full_record` tool to the summary model would cost a whole additional generation per turn to decide a `db_session.get()` on a primary key. (3) **Bounded on both axes**, so it cannot inherit SAGE's unmeasured cost profile: `MAX_FULL_RECORD_INVOICES = 3` (a turn that identified 40 rows is an aggregate or a listing, where 40 complete records would be both useless and the largest single thing in the prompt) and `MAX_FULL_RECORD_BLOCK_CHARS = 12_000`; the surplus is **disclosed in the block** with its count, not silently dropped, the same honesty rule `columns_omitted`/`pages_omitted` follow — with one deliberate exception, the first record renders however large, because an empty block plus "everything was held back" is strictly worse than a long one. No Chroma call and no page dump (`include_document_pages=False`; Feature 21's B4 run measured a page dump at up to 16,010 tokens on an 11-page invoice), since this route already has its own document channel in the RAG branch. (4) **Fail-soft and tenant-safe** — any failure logs, rolls back and returns `""`, leaving the turn exactly the results table it had before; cross-tenant ids come back `not_found` from `get_full_record` itself, never a distinguishable error, which is the second of two independent checks since the ids came from a query `execute_generated_sql`'s Safety Check 3 already forced to be tenant-scoped. **Two ordering/consistency changes came with it**: the Gap 231 companion-query id harvest moved **above** the summary-prompt construction (it decides which invoices the turn is about; running it after would have left the block permanently empty for exactly the single-invoice detail questions it exists for, since rules 6d/11 forbid selecting `id`), and the block is appended to `judge_context_parts` (Gap 304 half 2 — otherwise a correct CGST figure read off `taxes` scores unfaithful purely because the judge saw a narrower evidence set than the model did). **The tax-term note was corrected, not removed**: `_tax_term_block_for()` fires on the same detection but no longer ends *"This schema has no breakdown by tax type; select tax_amount directly"* — true when Gap 263 wrote it, false ever since extraction began populating `taxes`, and a prompt asserting a capability gap the product no longer has instructs the model to decline an answerable question. **Benchmark fixtures moved with the product**: `benchmarks/sage_seed_fixtures.py`'s Rajesh Steel row seeds the real CGST/SGST breakdown (9% + 9% summing to the INR 18,000.00 already in `tax_amount`, on an INR 100,000.00 subtotal — the two representations agree, or the golden case would be ungradeable), with `taxes` defaulted to `"[]"` for the other rows; `benchmarks/agent_eval_golden_sample.py`'s `rajesh_steel_cgst` was rewritten from expecting a **decline** to expecting **INR 9,000.00**, its rubric still guarding the original live failure (the figures must be read, never derived by halving the total); `benchmarks/region_seed_fixtures.py`'s "CGST/SGST questions cannot be ported, the schema has no tax-component column" rationale is struck through rather than deleted, because it was wrong in an instructive way — there was never a schema limitation, only a prompt one, and adding a regional CGST case is now a fixture edit plus a `GoldenCase` with no product change behind it. **Verified**: 8 tests in `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` (`-k full_record`) covering all five properties — the real breakdown plus `subtotal`/`tax_ids` reach the summary prompt *and* the judge evidence; a question with no tax word in it (`detect_tax_component_term()` → `None`) gets the identical record, which is what proves the mechanism is not keyword-gated; another tenant's row yields nothing directly, nothing when mixed with a legitimate id, and nothing through the whole route; a raising `get_full_record` leaves the answer and results table intact; **both** bounds hold (count bound, character budget with the held-back count disclosed, and the single-oversized-record exception); and a turn that identified nothing is byte-identical to its pre-Gap-310 behaviour. Full `pytest tests/test_*.py -p no:randomly` before **1381 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped**, after **1383 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped** (+2, the new character-budget tests), the **same 3 pre-existing failures** as Gap 311's run (2x `test_connectors.py` needing a local Redis, 1x `test_rag.py` calling `post_chat_message()` without `background_tasks`). `ruff check` clean on all six touched/related files. **Deliberately still open**: these are mocked unit tests and assert mechanics only — the behavioural evidence is the `rajesh_steel_cgst` golden case under a live-model `scripts/run_agent_eval.py` run, which has **not** been re-run since the rewrite, so the case's new expectation is not yet backed by a measured pass. That live result is also the blocking condition on SAGE Phase 3 (Feature 21 entry above). Design in `docs/feature_6_rag.md`, "Recent Fix (Aug 24–25, 2026) — Gap 310".

- `[x]` **Gap 311 (BE/Infra): Feature 24 (Ops Digest Agent) removed from the codebase — superseded as over-scoped, never deployed** — 2026-08-25. Not a defect; a deliberate deletion of working code, filed as a Gap because every code change in this repo needs one. **Why**: the two-tier critical/digest alerting-and-LLM-synthesis agent built 2026-08-23 answered a requirement the founder retired on 2026-08-25 — what is actually wanted is one periodic pass that writes a recommendation per workbook field back onto the Feature 20/23 workbooks (not yet built, tracked as `(a)/(b)/(c)` in `docs/feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`), and critical alerting already exists in `alert-rules.bicep` + `action-group.bicep`. The agent was **never deployed** — no `caj-ops-digest-dev` resource ever existed — so nothing in Azure changed and no telemetry stream was lost. **Deleted (10 items)**: `services/ops_digest_routing.py`, `services/ops_digest_collect.py`, `services/ops_digest.py`, `services/ops_digest_delivery.py`, `tests/test_ops_digest.py`, `tests/test_ops_digest_routing.py`, `scripts/ops_digest_job.py`, `infra/ops-digest-job-only.bicep`, the `opsDigestJob` module + `opsDigestCron`/`opsDigestDelivery` params in `infra/08-apps.bicep`, and the seven `OPS_DIGEST_*` settings in `config.py` (mirrored out of `.env.example`). **Also deleted in the same pass, unrelated to Feature 24**: `infra/modules/monitoring/dashboard.bicep` — Feature 19 Task 19.5's 6-panel workbook module, unwired from `09-monitoring.bicep` and its live Azure resource `az resource delete`d on 2026-08-24, superseded by `infra/monitoring/cost_health_workbook.json` + `workbook-cost-health-only.bicep`. Confirmed dead by grep before deleting: no `module … dashboard.bicep` declaration exists anywhere in `infra/`, only two prose comments (in `alert-rules.bicep` and `workbook-cost-health-only.bicep`), both rewritten so they no longer point a reader at a deleted file. **Preserved, not deleted:** (1) **`emit_online_signals()`'s scheduled caller** — Gap 305's fix lived inside `scripts/ops_digest_job.py`, so it was extracted *before* the delete into a standalone `scripts/emit_online_signals_job.py` (`--window-hours`, default 6 / `--dry-run` / `--json`), same shape as `scripts/sweep_azure_cost.py`. Both load-bearing properties carry over: emission happens **after** `configure_telemetry()` (`track_online_signal()` only reaches `customEvents` once the exporter is attached to `invoice_be_telemetry`), and `window_days` stays a **float** (six hours = 0.25; the old `int()` cast bug is still pinned). One deliberate behaviour change: a failed computation is now **exit 1**, not exit 0, because here the emission *is* the run rather than a rider on a job with its own reason to exist — and a run with no DB session still emits **nothing** rather than five zero-denominator events. Coverage moved with it: 7 of the 8 tests in the deleted file's "Gap 305" section were about the emitter having a caller at all, not about the digest, so they are now `tests/test_emit_online_signals_job.py` (8 tests, **8 passed**), plus one new `--dry-run` test. `tests/test_online_eval_signals.py` still covers `compute_online_signals()`/`emit_online_signals()` themselves and was not touched. (2) **`Monitoring Reader`** in `infra/modules/security/rbac-assignments.bicep` — kept deliberately; it was added for the digest agent but grants exactly the two ARM reads (`alertsmanagementresources` via Resource Graph, `actionGroups/read`) the future recommendation pass will need. Its comment block was rewritten to say so instead of pointing at the deleted modules; the resource itself is byte-identical. (3) **The Azure Resource Graph quirks list** — the four real-response facts that contradict the column names (`alertRule` is a full resource ID, `severity` is the string `"Sev2"`, `monitorConditionResolvedDateTime` is `""` while firing, and a row can be `monitorCondition: Resolved` + `alertState: New` at once) already live in `docs/feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`, not in the deleted code. **Left in place, flagged rather than removed as out of scope**: `telemetry.py::track_ops_digest_run()` and `OPS_DIGEST_EVENT_NAME` now have zero callers and zero tests — a small follow-up, not silently swept into this change. **Verified**: repo-wide grep for `ops_digest_routing` / `ops_digest_collect` / `services.ops_digest` / `ops_digest_delivery` / `ops_digest_job` returns no import anywhere; `az bicep build` clean on `08-apps.bicep` (only the two pre-existing warnings, `front-door.bicep` BCP081 and `invoice-be.bicep` BCP037); full `pytest tests/test_*.py` before **1449 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped**, after **1381 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped** — exactly 1449 − 76 (the deleted ops-digest tests) + 8 (the moved/extended Gap 305 ones), with the **same 3 pre-existing failures** (2x `test_connectors.py` needing a local Redis, 1x `test_rag.py` calling `post_chat_message()` without `background_tasks`), i.e. nothing new broke. Full history of the original build, including the live 72-hour dry run and the real gpt-5-mini synthesis: commit `bce9e38`, and `git log -- Prod_Invoice_LLM/apps/invoice-be/services/ops_digest*.py`. Feature 24's own `[~]` tracker entry above is left as the historical record of what was built and why.

- `[x]` **Gap 312 (BE/Infra): `benchmark-gate` removed from `.github/workflows/deploy-dev.yml` — CI/CD must never execute tests or benchmarks, second and final removal** — 2026-08-25. Not a bug fix; a deliberate deletion per an explicit standing instruction that the CI/CD deploy pipeline must never execute tests or benchmarks as part of a deploy, not even indirectly via an Azure-side job execution running under its own managed identity. **History**: `benchmark-gate` was first built inline 2026-08-23 (`fe021a3`), broke immediately because the runner's service principal (`Azure_Dev_Credentials`) couldn't read `AZURE-OPENAI-API-KEY` from Key Vault (`ForbiddenByRbac`), and was reverted same window (`b1b9ff3`). It was rebuilt 2026-08-24 to instead start a scoped-down on-demand execution of the existing `caj-benchmark-eval-dev` Container App Job (reading the key inside Azure under `id-invoicellm-dev`'s managed identity rather than on the runner) and block `deploy-backend`/`deploy-worker` on that execution's terminal status — which fixed the RBAC problem but reintroduced exactly the thing the standing rule prohibits: the deploy pipeline still executed a benchmark and gated deploys on its result, just one hop removed. It was also independently broken again for an unrelated CLI-syntax reason (`az containerapp job start --command /bin/sh -c` → `ERROR: unrecognized arguments: -c`, confirmed live in run `32805759161`, 2026-08-25) — moot now; the job is removed, not fixed. **What was removed** from `.github/workflows/deploy-dev.yml`: the entire `benchmark-gate` job (previously lines ~143-329, including its ~55-line design-history comment block and all four of its steps — start execution, poll for completion, fetch execution logs, gate on result). `deploy-backend` and `deploy-worker` had `needs: [changes, benchmark-gate]` and an `if:` requiring `benchmark-gate.result` be `success` or `skipped`; both restored to `needs: changes` / `if: needs.changes.outputs.be == 'true'` (resp. `worker`) only, matching what they depended on before the gate existed. No other job in the file referenced `benchmark-gate` (`resolve-fqdns`, `verify-deployment`, `deploy-frontend`, `deploy-website` were never wired to it). **Explicitly not touched**: `infra/benchmark-eval-job-only.bicep`, the `benchmarkEvalJob` module in `infra/08-apps.bicep`, and the standalone nightly 03:00 UTC scheduled execution of `caj-benchmark-eval-dev` — that job is not CI-triggered and remains the only place quality/regression checking happens for the benchmark suite; the CI pipeline now performs zero test/benchmark execution of any kind. **Verified**: `python -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(...)"` against the edited file parses clean; `deploy-backend`/`deploy-worker` `needs`/`if` inspected via the parsed YAML and confirmed correct; repo-wide grep for `benchmark-gate` in the workflow file returns zero matches after the edit.

- `[x]` **Gap 313 (BE/AI): Feature 6's four chat prompts each carried their own persona and their own copy of the currency rule, and none of them carried any tax-domain knowledge at all — one shared persona block now opens all four, derived from SAGE's** — 2026-08-25. **The asymmetry, not a defect in any one prompt.** The default chat route (`agents/query_agent.py`, the only route any real tenant is on, since `ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE` is off) had **four separately hand-written system prompts** — SQL generation (`build_sql_system_prompt()`), the SQL summary prompt built inline in `run_query_agent()`, the RAG prompt and the CHAT prompt. Each opened with its own one-line persona (*"You are a database SQL query expert"* / *"You are an assistant answering questions about invoice documents"* / *"You are a helpful assistant for an AI Invoice Processing platform"*) and each restated the same currency rule in its own words — three of them verbatim-identical, which is duplication with nothing enforcing that the copies stay equal. What **none** of them had was domain knowledge: nothing on this route told the model that CGST always equals SGST on an intra-state invoice, that an IGST-only invoice is inter-state rather than missing a component, that `tax_amount = 0` under RCM is CORRECT rather than an extraction gap, that GSTIN/VAT number/EIN are the same concept in three jurisdictions, that IRN/e-Way Bill/Peppol ID are compliance identifiers rather than tax amounts, that US sales tax and Indian GST mean different things by *"how much tax did we pay"*, that a vendor's own name is as good a category signal as a tag, or that a zero total is never a confident answer. Feature 21 had written every one of those as `agents/sage_prompts.py::PERSONA_BLOCK` and shared it between SAGE's planner and synthesis prompts — but SAGE is off by default, **so the route that actually answers users was the one without it**. **Fix**: `agents/query_agent.py::CHAT_PERSONA_BLOCK`, built once at import by `_build_chat_persona_block()` and prepended to all four prompts. **Derived from `PERSONA_BLOCK`, not copied** — the constant is imported and the tax/category/honesty text is byte-identical to SAGE's, so a rule added there reaches all four of these prompts with no edit here; a second copy of a persona is a persona that disagrees with itself as soon as one copy is edited, which is the same argument `sage_prompts.py` already makes for sharing one block between its own two prompts. The import is safe at module scope: `sage_prompts.py` is pure text plus a `models.Invoice` reflection, and `test_flag_off_never_imports_the_orchestrator_module` forbids `sage_orchestrator`/`query_tools` there, not the prompt text. **The opener needed no change** — `feature_6_rag.md`'s own title is "Conversational RAG & Thread Management — **SAGE Agent**", so *"You are SAGE, a financial-documents assistant…"* is correct on this path too. **Exactly two adaptations**, each because the sentence is *false* here rather than merely awkward: (1) the closing *"You answer only from what your tools actually returned"* — this route has no tools — restated by `_CHAT_GROUNDING_BLOCK` in terms of what it really puts in front of the model ("the query results, document context and invoice records given to you below"), which also agrees with rule 8a's existing "the conversation history is not a data source"; (2) the category section's promise that an ambiguous name match *"has already been routed to a clarifying question before you see it"*, true of the orchestrator (`ask_clarifying_question` is one of its tools) and false here, where it would have told the model that the ambiguity in front of it could not exist — replaced with "report every candidate the name matched and say it matched more than one". `_CHAT_GROUNDING_BLOCK` also carries the one Feature 6 rule `PERSONA_BLOCK` has no equivalent of: **currency presentation** (SAGE's persona forbids summing across currencies but never says which symbol to print), now stated once instead of three times. **Nothing route-specific was lost.** The SQL prompt keeps its schema block and all eleven rules verbatim — including rule 7 (*also* `SELECT` the `currency` column), which merely shared the "CRITICAL CURRENCY RULE" label with the presentation rule and is different SQL mechanics; the summary prompt keeps the line-item template, the reconciliation-mismatch exception and "YOU compute this total, not the database"; the RAG prompt keeps its chunk context and 1–3 sentence directive; the CHAT prompt keeps its SCOPE boundary against writing arbitrary code. `_tax_term_block_for()`/`_payment_status_block_for()` are untouched. Each prompt lost exactly one line: its own persona opener, or its own copy of the currency sentence. **Verified**: 5 new tests in `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` (`-k persona`) — one asserting the derivation against `sage_prompts.PERSONA_BLOCK` itself (sections and four distinctive sentences present verbatim; `"your tools"`/`"clarifying question"` present in SAGE's block and **absent** from this one, so a future upstream rewording fails in CI rather than silently reinstating agent-only framing), and four running the real route with the LLM mocked to assert the block reached each of the four prompts at its actual call site, fingerprinted on *"Peppol ID is an e-invoicing network address"* plus a second sentence from a different section, present **once** not twice, with the old per-prompt currency sentence gone and each prompt's own mechanics intact. **`tests/fixtures/query_agent_flag_off_parity.json` regenerated deliberately** (it compares eight scripted turns' prompts byte-for-byte): diffed field by field first — `result` **byte-identical on all eight cases** (same answers, same generated SQL, same citations), the only diffs being the intended one-line-out / one-block-in substitution in every prompt. Reasoning recorded in `tests/agentic_sage_parity_cases.py`'s regeneration log, per that file's own rule. `pytest tests/test_agentic_sage.py` **41 passed**; `pytest tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` 79 → **84 passed / 5 skipped**. Full `pytest tests/test_*.py -p no:randomly` before **1383 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped**, after ****1388 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped** (+5, exactly the new persona tests)** — the same 3 pre-existing failures (2x `test_connectors.py` needing a local Redis, 1x `test_rag.py::test_process_crash_during_agent_leaves_no_orphan_user_message` calling `post_chat_message()` without `background_tasks`, reproduced unchanged here). `ruff check` clean on all three touched files. **Deliberately out of scope**: `agents/sage_prompts.py` and `agents/sage_orchestrator.py` are unchanged and SAGE stays fully functional — this only *reads* from them. Design in `docs/feature_6_rag.md`, "Recent Fix (Aug 25, 2026) — Gap 313".

- `[x]` **Gap 314 (BE): orphaned telemetry — `telemetry.py::track_ops_digest_run()` and `OPS_DIGEST_EVENT_NAME` have zero callers and zero tests** — found during Gap 311's Feature 24 removal (2026-08-25), deliberately left in place rather than swept in silently, flagged again during the doc/code-vs-code review the same day. Both were Feature 24 Ops Digest's own run-telemetry emitter; the digest agent that called it is deleted, so the function and its event name are now dead code with no consumer. **Not fixed here** — tracked for a later small cleanup pass: confirm zero callers still holds, delete `track_ops_digest_run()` and `OPS_DIGEST_EVENT_NAME` from `telemetry.py`, confirm no test references either.

  **Fixed 2026-08-26 — both deleted, exactly the three steps this entry scoped and nothing else.** **Zero callers re-confirmed first**, independently of the two prior greps this entry rests on: repo-wide search across all of `Prod_Invoice_LLM/` for `track_ops_digest_run` / `OPS_DIGEST_EVENT_NAME` / `ops_digest_run` over `.py`/`.json`/`.kql`/`.bicep`/`.ps1`/`.yml`/`.yaml`/`.ts`/`.tsx` returned, outside `telemetry.py` itself, **exactly one hit** — a docstring in `tests/test_telemetry.py` naming `ops_digest_run` as a *peer* of `agent_eval_summary`/`extraction_benchmark_run` in an argument about which events carry no `tenant_id`, not a reference to the symbol. No test existed for either symbol (so no test was deleted); `telemetry.py` has **no `__all__`** or any other export list, so nothing else needed updating. The `.json` sweep matters more than the `.py` one here: it confirms **no workbook panel queries the event name**, so deleting it removes no panel's data source — consistent with Gap 311's record that `caj-ops-digest-dev` was never deployed and therefore that **no `ops_digest_run` row was ever emitted from a running replica**, in dev or anywhere else. **Deleted**: `OPS_DIGEST_EVENT_NAME` with its 10-line Feature 24 rationale comment (was line 154), and `track_ops_digest_run()` in full — signature, docstring and body, 52 lines (was 1255–1306). Both sites keep a short tombstone comment naming Gap 311/Gap 314 and the date, in the same style as `config.py`'s and `.env.example`'s `OPS_DIGEST_*` notes, so a reader who greps history lands on an explanation rather than an unexplained absence; full text is in git. `_STATUS_ERROR`/`_STATUS_SUCCESS`, the only module-level names the deleted function consumed, are still used by 5 other emitters and were not touched. **Three stale prose references corrected** rather than left pointing at a deleted symbol — the same "don't leave the doc claiming something the code no longer does" rule this repo has been bitten by: `telemetry.py::track_ops_recommendation`'s docstring and `tests/test_telemetry.py`'s peer-list docstring now say "(and the deleted `ops_digest_run`)" instead of listing it as live, and `agents/sage_prompts.py`'s module docstring — which cited Gap 314 as precedent for *flagging* an orphan rather than deleting it — now records that Gap 314 went on to delete, and states the distinction that keeps its own five orphans alive (zero callers **and** no plausible future use → delete; zero callers but an open founder question → keep). **Verified**: `pytest tests/test_telemetry.py` **60 passed** (the affected file; full suite is a track-boundary checkpoint per the standing rule, not this task). `ruff check` clean on all three touched files (`telemetry.py`, `tests/test_telemetry.py`, `agents/sage_prompts.py`), and all three re-parsed with `ast.parse` to prove the two excisions left no broken syntax. **Not touched**: `services/online_eval_signals.py` and `scripts/emit_online_signals_job.py` — the parts of Feature 24's telemetry Gap 311 deliberately *preserved* because they have live callers; this gap covers only the two orphans it names. Working notes: `.claude/tasklists/senior-dev-gap314-orphaned-telemetry-cleanup.md`.

- `[x]` **Gap 315 (BE/AI): Feature 6's SQL-summary step asked the LLM to do the arithmetic — Gap 269's root cause, left open when Gap 269 was closed at the formatting level — the totals are now computed in Python before the model is invoked** — 2026-08-25. **Two shipped fixes had between them moved the arithmetic from the database to the LLM and stopped there.** Gap 273 correctly removed SQL-side aggregation for rule 6d (letting one query both *find* and *sum/group* the matching lines was the repeated source of wrong answers — the wrong column summed, the wrong thing grouped), but replaced it with an *instruction*: *"YOU compute this total, not the database … Add the `line_amount` values yourself, per currency, from the rows actually listed above — carefully; this is real arithmetic on real numbers, not decoration."* Gap 269 had already measured what that produces: live 2026-08-19 (US tenant test, Q4/Q10) the model printed *"5000.00 units × USD 0.08 = USD 420.00"* — a false equation, since 5000 × 0.08 is 400.00 and 420.00 was the *stored* amount, i.e. a row that genuinely reconciles two ways with one figure taken from each side. **Gap 269 was closed at the formatting level** (a prose rule about when not to print an "="), so the root cause — LLM-performed arithmetic — survived its own fix. CONVENTIONS.md hard rule 3 is explicit that a check deciding correctness must be deterministic code, and a total is exactly that. **Fix**: `agents/query_agent.py::_computed_figures_block_for(db_result)` parses the turn's own results table and runs `query_tools.compute()` over it — the same LLM-free function SAGE uses, **imported, not reimplemented** — interpolating the figures into the summary prompt as `{db_result}{computed_figures_block}{full_record_block}`. **Two table shapes, both taken from `sage_orchestrator._grounded_arithmetic()`**: (a) rule 6d's line-item table (`line_qty`/`line_unit_price`/`line_amount`) → `reconcile_line_items` per row, so the stored amount, the amount `quantity × unit_price` actually computes to, and the difference are all present and labelled before the model runs; plus a per-currency total of the line amounts; plus **one subtotal per `vendor_name`** when the rows span more than one vendor (`MAX_COMPUTED_VENDOR_GROUPS = 10`) — not a bonus, since the prompt asks for that breakdown by name and without it the "don't do arithmetic" instruction would push the model straight back into arithmetic for that one case; (b) any other table with **more than one** row → a per-currency `sum_by_currency` of each money column, classified by `is_summable_money_column()` (`avg_grand_total` and `line_qty` are never summed — a header is the only thing that says what a number *means*). Single-row tables are skipped: the query already aggregated, and "summing" one row would label its own figure as a total this block computed. **The instruction swaps per turn**: `_DETERMINISTIC_TOTALS_INSTRUCTION` when figures were computed, `_LLM_TOTALS_INSTRUCTION` when not — and the fallback is the pre-Gap-315 wording **verbatim**, which is why a turn with nothing to compute renders a byte-identical prompt and **`tests/fixtures/query_agent_flag_off_parity.json` needed no regeneration** (all eight scripted cases return `NO_RECORDS_FOUND`, so all eight take the fallback; the golden passed untouched, which is the parity evidence for this change). **Disclosed in the prompt, deliberately not a runtime correction** — same shape as Gap 310's full-record block: a validate-then-correct pass would leave the model's own arithmetic on the critical path and then have to decide what to do with an answer whose number is wrong but whose sentences are built around it. The mismatch rule ("state both figures and the difference, never an 'x = y' equation") lives in the block's **header**, once, never beside the figure it applies to — a live SAGE run had gpt-5-mini copy exactly such a parenthetical straight into a user's answer. **Fail-soft on every axis**: an unreadable table, a money column with one unparseable cell, a `compute()` returning `error`, or any exception yields `""` and the old instruction — degraded to pre-Gap-315 behaviour, never a failed turn; and a `compute()` error drops **that figure only**, never a partial one (a total built from most of the rows is a wrong number that looks like a right one). The block is appended to `judge_context_parts` (Gap 304 half 2) — a total the model was told to quote is part of the evidence its answer is grounded in. **One boundary narrowed deliberately**: `compute` joins `get_full_record` as a named exception in the two tests that police the SAGE boundary (`test_agentic_sage.py::test_the_orchestrator_is_reachable_only_through_the_settings_flag`, `test_query_tools.py::test_no_orchestration_tool_is_wired_into_the_live_chat_pipeline`), on the identical criterion Gap 310 used — no LLM call, no SQL generation, no orchestration decision; the other four tools stay reachable only behind `ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE`, and the module-scope import ban on `query_tools` is unchanged (the import is inside the calling function, as Gap 310's is). **Verified**: 7 new tests in `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` (`-k "computed or line_item_totals or aggregated or compute_error"`) — the load-bearing one replays Gap 269's own row (5000.00 × 0.08 stored as 420.00) beside a line that does reconcile and asserts the prompt carries `"…USD 420.00 stated, 5000 x USD 0.08 computes to USD 400.00 -- USD 20.00 mismatch"`, the deterministic total `USD 720.00`, the plain `=` form for the reconciling line only, **no** `"5000 x USD 0.08 = USD 420.00"` anywhere, the swapped instruction (old wording gone), the mismatch rule positioned above the figures, and the figures present in the judge evidence; the rest cover the per-vendor/per-currency split (no blended `750.00`), the non-line-item money-column shape, the single-already-aggregated-row skip, five nothing-to-compute cases, the fail-soft path (a raising `compute` leaves answer, results table and original instruction intact), and a `compute()` error dropping only its own figure. `pytest tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py tests/test_query_tools.py tests/test_agentic_sage.py` **221 passed / 5 skipped**. Full `pytest tests/test_*.py -q` before **1388 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped** (measured on this same tree with the 7 new tests `--deselect`ed, and equal to Gap 313's recorded after-count), after **1395 passed / 3 failed / 7 skipped** (+7, exactly the new tests) — the **same 3 pre-existing failures** (2x `test_connectors.py` needing a local Redis, 1x `test_rag.py::test_process_crash_during_agent_leaves_no_orphan_user_message` calling `post_chat_message()` without `background_tasks`). `ruff check` clean on `agents/query_agent.py` and `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py`; the two F401s ruff reports in `tests/test_agentic_sage.py` / `tests/test_query_tools.py` are **pre-existing**, confirmed by running ruff against each file's `HEAD` version, and were left alone rather than swept in. **Deliberately out of scope**: `agents/query_tools.py` and `agents/sage_orchestrator.py` are unchanged — this only *reads* from them; `ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE` is untouched. **Known follow-on, stated not hidden**: Feature 6 now depends on two functions inside `query_tools.py` (`get_full_record` from Gap 310, `compute` from here), so whatever removes or relocates SAGE's code has to rehome both rather than delete them. Mocked unit tests assert mechanics — but here the mechanics *are* the fix, since the arithmetic no longer depends on model behaviour at all; what a live run would still show is whether the model quotes the given figures rather than restating them. Design in `docs/feature_6_rag.md`, "Recent Fix (Aug 25, 2026) — Gap 315".

- `[x]` **Gap 316 (BE/AI): Feature 21's SAGE orchestrator deleted — a real live-tenant head-to-head showed it cost +87% per turn and +90% median latency to answer nothing the default path got wrong, and leaked internal tool caveats to users on 5 of 25 turns** — 2026-08-25. Not a defect; a deliberate deletion of working, fully-tested code, filed as a Gap because every code change in this repo needs one. Same shape as Gap 311's Feature 24 removal, and for the same kind of reason: the requirement it answered had been met more cheaply elsewhere by the time it was measured.

  **The evidence, which is the whole justification.** First-ever run of `ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE` against real Postgres + real Chroma + a real question bank (25 questions × 2 paths = 50 real turns, 0 errors, 0 cache-served turns, judged by `services/agent_eval.score_answer` at the standard 0.80/0.70/0.70 floors — full method in the Feature 21 § "Phase 3" entry above): **default 23/25, SAGE 19/25. SAGE answered ZERO questions correctly that Feature 6's current default path got wrong; the default path answered four that SAGE got wrong (Q11, Q15, Q19, Q24).** Cost: **+137% LLM calls, +91% tokens-in, +85% tokens-out, +87% cost/turn, +90% median latency** (18.9s → 35.9s median, 54.4s → 101.7s max); `sage.planner` alone burned 285,970 input tokens across 70 calls, more than the entire default path's 25 turns (235,230), and `tool_call_budget_exhausted` fired on **8 of 25 turns** against `MAX_TOOL_CALLS = 4` — a budget that binds routinely is not a safety valve. Two defects, one of them a class the cheap path does not have at all: (1) SAGE lost conversational scope on a narrowing follow-up (Q11 pulled a Dec-2025 invoice into a "Q1 2026 hardware" follow-up set) — on the path whose *stated* advantage is multi-hop reasoning; (2) SAGE **leaked internal tool caveats into user-facing answers on 5 of 25 turns** (*"soft-deleted rows were not excluded"*, *"which invoice statuses count toward spend is not a settled decision"*), i.e. spoke its own unresolved product decisions aloud to the user. The default path leaked none. Honest reading of the margin, recorded rather than rounded away: three of SAGE's four losses were faithfulness-floor failures on answers the accuracy judge scored 1.0/1.0/0.92, so on an accuracy-only reading it is 23 vs 22 — which still leaves SAGE ahead on nothing while costing nearly twice as much. One run per path, not a repeated-trial mean.

  **Why the result came out this way, stated plainly**: Gaps 310 and 315 had already retrofitted SAGE's two genuinely load-bearing ideas — the whole-ORM-row fetch and LLM-free arithmetic — onto the default route, deterministically and without an extra model round-trip. What was left of SAGE was the loop itself: a planner deciding which tool to call. That is what the +87% bought.

- `[x]` **Gap 317 (BE/Infra): nightly `caj-benchmark-eval-dev` job crashes with `FileNotFoundError` after completing all real work — prerequisite for Gap 318's trigger** — closed 2026-08-25. **The premise this gap was filed on was half stale, and the correction matters more than the fix.** The code defect it describes was already fixed the day before, as **Gap 308** (`default_output_dir()`, `scripts/run_agent_eval.py`, commit `ed6f8c1`, 2026-08-24 13:18 UTC, 7 tests) — filing it again as "never separately fixed" was wrong. So: fixed in code, never shipped. Closing this gap does not by itself make the 03:00 UTC schedule green; **a backend image rebuild + push is what does**, and that is deliberately not part of this change (see the last paragraph).

  **Real docker repro, both directions**, against a fresh `docker build -f docker/Dockerfile.be -t invoice-be-gap317:local .` from `Prod_Invoice_LLM/` (exit 0) — not a mock, because this is a defect that only exists inside the built image. In that image `/app/tests` is **absent** and `/app/benchmarks` present, as `.dockerignore` line 47 (`**/tests/`) dictates. **Before** (the pre-Gap-308 body restored in-image, i.e. the code the deployed image carries — `default_output_dir()` returning `_CHECKOUT_OUTPUT_DIR` unconditionally), running the job's own persisted argv `python scripts/run_agent_eval.py --paths default --run-label nightly` with no `--out` against real Azure OpenAI (`gpt-5-mini`) and real Postgres: the turn ran and was graded (`llm_calls=3 latency=28443ms sql=yes err=None`, faithfulness 0.714 / relevance 1.0 / accuracy 0.5), `Persisted 1 agent_eval_run rows`, and then `FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: '/app/tests/agent_eval_output.json'`, **EXIT=1** — the live failure exactly, all real work done first. **After** (same image, unmodified): same argv, **EXIT=0**, `Wrote 1 turns to /tmp/agent_eval_output.json`, file present (7,845 bytes). Then the **whole nightly job, literally** — image rebuilt with this change, `--paths default --run-label nightly` and nothing else, all 35 cases, real `gpt-5-mini`, real Postgres, ~47 minutes wall clock: **`NIGHTLY_EXIT=0`**, 35 turns / **0 harness errors**, `Persisted 35 agent_eval_run rows`, `Mirror [nightly] -> stdout only: 1 telemetry event(s), no artifact` (no App Insights or storage connection string given locally, which is the swallowed path, not a failure), `Wrote 35 turns to /tmp/agent_eval_output.json` (187,046 bytes). Its quality figures — pass rate 0.171, faithfulness 0.779, relevance 0.983, accuracy 0.486, 84 chat calls + 171 judge calls — are recorded here only as proof the run was real work and not a stub; they are a local seeded-SQLite run and belong to Feature 23's quality trend, not to this gap.

Bicep is untouched and stays `--out`-free (`infra/08-apps.bicep`'s `benchmarkEvalJob` and `infra/benchmark-eval-job-only.bicep`); the two premise tests that fail if either file ever gains an `--out` are still the guard on that decision. **2 new tests** in `tests/test_run_agent_eval_cli.py` (9 total, was 7): one drives the real `main()` on the nightly argv with `--out` under a two-level missing directory, one asserts the container path is safe *because the temp fallback already exists* rather than because `mkdir` papers over it (it makes `Path.mkdir` raise for the duration and the nightly run must still complete). Mutation-checked: deleting the one-line fix fails the first of them and only it.

- `[x]` **Gap 318 (BE): the Feature 20/23/24 recommendation pass — check-and-flag across 3 categories, triggered off the nightly job** — closed 2026-08-25. Built as **`services/ops_recommendation.py`** (new, ~700 lines incl. docstrings) plus a nightly-only step in `scripts/run_agent_eval.py`. Categories: **container health, cost** (Feature 20) and **AI improvement** (Feature 23 quality). For each: `worked` / `recommend` / `no_data` / `insufficient_data`, never an exhaustive per-field dump.

  **Every threshold is a live workbook panel's, not a new one — and a test proves it stays that way.** The sample field-recommendation table in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md` supplied the *judgement* per field; the numeric bands were read out of the two deployed workbook JSONs' `formatter: 8` `thresholdsGrid` blocks so a category that flags red here is looking at a tile that is red there: CPU/memory ≥90 red / ≥70 yellow (`container-status`), `runningStatus != "Running"` red (`container-scale-config`), restarts-24h `> 0` (`container-restarts`' own filter), MTD-spend% and month-end-forecast% ≥100 red / ≥80 yellow (`cost-trend-budget`), pass rate <0.20/<0.30, faithfulness <0.70/<0.85, relevance <0.85/<0.95, accuracy <0.40/<0.55, context <0.50/<0.70, orchestration <0.60/<0.80 (`d1`–`d3`), cost/turn ≥$0.02/≥$0.01 (`d4`), alert recall <80/<100 (`e1-alert-recall` — its own title says 80% never reads green), clean FP rate ≥20/>0 (`e1-fp-rate`), and the eval run's own turn-error rate on `b1-error-rate`'s ≥5/≥2 band. `test_each_band_is_still_the_live_panels_band` parses the two workbook JSONs at test time and fails if any constant drifts from the tile it was derived from. The workbooks' n=20 minimum-sample guard is carried over as `STATUS_INSUFFICIENT_DATA` — values still reported, just not graded, the prose form of the `-1 → "n/a — see Detail"` sentinel. **Three fields are deliberately not judged**, stated in the module docstring rather than silently dropped: replica count (`container-replicas` is blue by design and its title says it is "not compared against configured max here"), persona score / median latency (uncoloured on purpose), and the sample table's "CPU persistently <20% → lower min replicas" row, because "persistently" is a multi-day trend judgement and a 1h average cannot support it.

  **Data sources reused, not rebuilt.** Cost is `azure_cost.collect_cost_snapshot()` verbatim, and the two ratios are computed the way `cost-trend-budget`'s KQL computes them (spend% from the snapshot's `month_to_date_total`, *not* the budget's lagging `currentSpend`; forecast% from the budget's own `forecastSpend`) so the tile and the recommendation cannot disagree — pinned by its own test. Container health is one Azure Resource Graph query (the same `resources | where type =~ "microsoft.app/containerapps"` projection the panel runs) plus one Azure Monitor metrics read per app, both through the existing `azure_cost.arm_request()` — one managed-identity/CLI token chain in this repo, not two. The metrics parse mirrors the panels exactly: newest **non-null** hourly average for CPU/memory/replicas (Azure returns the in-flight bucket all-null, and reading that as "no data" would blank the panel once an hour), `sum(total)` across 24h for restarts.

  **One deviation from the brief, and it needed a small addition.** The task assumed Track 1's extraction results were in `run_agent_eval.py`'s memory. They are not: the nightly job is `python scripts/run_extraction_benchmark.py … && python scripts/run_agent_eval.py …` (`infra/benchmark-eval-job-only.bicep`) — **two processes**, sharing only the replica filesystem. Rather than re-download Track 1's blob artifact, `services/benchmark_artifacts.py` gained a 4-function handoff (`track1_handoff_path` / `write_track1_handoff` / `read_track1_handoff` + `TRACK1_HANDOFF_ENV`): Track 1 drops its `summarise()` output in the temp directory, Track 2's pass reads it. Written **independently of `--no-write`** on purpose (that flag means "leave no artifact in the repo's docs/ tree", and the nightly job is the one caller that passes it and the one caller that needs this) and rejected when stale (>6h) or labelled with a different cadence, so a leftover file can never be graded as tonight's recall. Absent handoff ⇒ the AI-improvement category says so on `errors` and grades Track 2 only — never silently treats a missing Track 1 as a pass.

  **Wiring**: `recommendation_pass_step()` runs last in `main()`, after the graded turns, the Postgres persist, the `--out` write and the telemetry/blob mirror. Nightly-only (`--run-label nightly`): `predeploy` runs a 5-case subset that is below the n=20 guard *and* runs on every push, and putting two live ARM reads in the deploy path is exactly what the nightly-completion trigger design exists to avoid; `adhoc` is a developer's own run. The step swallows everything it can raise — a step bolted onto the end of an otherwise-successful job must not be able to turn that execution red, which is the Gap 308/317 failure class this pass was designed to *watch for*.

  **Nothing is persisted** — `run_recommendation_pass()` returns a `RecommendationPass` and prints. Gap 319 consumes exactly this shape: `{run_label, generated_at, categories: [{category, title, status, explanation, recommendation, worst_severity, findings: [{field, value, severity, detail, recommendation}], metrics, errors}]}`, one dict per category, JSON-round-trippable, pinned by `test_the_serialised_shape_is_what_gap_319_will_persist`. *(Still true of the pass itself — but Gap 319 closed the same day and `mirror_recommendation_pass()` now turns that return value into three `ops_recommendation` events; see the Gap 319 entry below.)*

  **Verified**: `pytest tests/test_ops_recommendation.py tests/test_run_agent_eval_cli.py tests/test_benchmark_artifacts.py tests/test_extraction_benchmark.py tests/test_azure_cost.py tests/test_model_substitution.py tests/test_telemetry.py -p no:randomly` → **360 passed, 1 skipped** (pre-existing skip), of which **64 are new** in `tests/test_ops_recommendation.py` and **4 new** in `tests/test_run_agent_eval_cli.py` (9 → 13). Boundary values, not round ones: 69.9/70.0/89.9/90.0 CPU%, 15,000/16,000/19,900/20,000 against a ₹20,000 budget, each score band at exactly its threshold and one hundredth under, 1.0/0.999/0.80/0.799 recall. Fail-soft covered twice — a thrown category becomes `no_data` while the other two still report, and an environment with no Azure credential at all still produces a full AI-improvement verdict. **Mutation-checked**: flipping the nightly gate to always-on fails both `test_no_other_cadence_fires_the_recommendation_pass` cases and nothing else; moving `CPU_MEMORY_YELLOW_PCT` 70 → 71 fails the boundary test and the workbook-parity test and nothing else. `ruff check` clean on all six touched files. Full suite not re-run: this is additive new code plus one call site, and the A-track checkpoint (Gap 316) already covered the surrounding files. **No Azure call was made and nothing was deployed** — the ARG/metrics paths are exercised against captured response shapes, so the *live* behaviour of the health category is unproven until the undeployed `Monitoring Reader` grant lands (see the blockers table in `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`); until then it degrades to `no_data` by design, which is the honest answer and not a crash.

- `[x]` **Gap 319 (BE): persistence for the recommendation pass — nothing survived past one run** — closed 2026-08-25. A Workbook can only read Log Analytics / App Insights / Resource Graph / ARM / ADX, **not Postgres**, so this is a custom-event mirror, the same established pattern as `agent_eval_run` / `online_eval_signal`.

  **The event Gap 320 must query: `ops_recommendation`** (`telemetry.OPS_RECOMMENDATION_EVENT_NAME`, emitted by the new `telemetry.track_ops_recommendation()`). **One row per category per run — three rows a night, not one row carrying a three-element array.** That is not a stylistic preference: a workbook grid is a flat row set and Gap 320's columns are `Category | Status | Explanation | Recommendation`, i.e. one row per category already, so the nested form would make every panel `mv-expand` before it could filter or colour on `status`. It is the same call `online_eval_signal` made in choosing one row per signal over one per window.

  **Full field schema** (flat, one `customDimensions` entry each): `run_label`, `category` (`container_health`/`cost`/`ai_improvement`), `title`, `status` (`worked`/`recommend`/`no_data`/`insufficient_data`), `explanation`, `recommendation`, `worst_severity` (`red`/`yellow`/`""`), `finding_count`, `red_count`, `yellow_count`, `findings` (a JSON array serialised as a string, entries `{field, value, severity, detail, recommendation}`), `findings_omitted`, `error_count`, `errors` (the category's error list joined with ` | `), `generated_at`.

  **Four decisions inside that schema.** (1) **`generated_at` is the run key** — set once by the pass and stamped byte-identically on all three rows, so "the latest run" is one `arg_max(generated_at, …)` and can never return two categories from one run and one from another; `TimeGenerated` is ingestion time and is deliberately not that key. (2) **The counts travel next to the blob** so a panel can count reds without `parse_json` + `mv-expand`, and so a trend across a *window* of runs is buildable at all — the same reason `extraction_benchmark_run` carries raw confusion-matrix cells alongside its derived ratios. (3) **`findings` is bounded so that it stays parseable.** Application Insights caps a single property value at 8,192 characters and cuts a longer one *mid-object* at ingestion, which silently turns the array into non-JSON; so the cut is made here instead — ≤25 entries (`MAX_RECOMMENDATION_FINDINGS`), each string field truncated at 400 chars, then whole entries dropped from the end until the serialised text fits 8,000 (`MAX_RECOMMENDATION_FINDINGS_CHARS`), with one same-shaped `{"field": "(omitted)", …}` marker entry appended stating how many went. `explanation`/`recommendation`/`errors` use this module's existing `_truncate()` marker at 2,000 chars, the `MAX_TURN_SQL_CHARS`/`MAX_TURN_TOOL_OUTPUT_CHARS` precedent. (4) **`metrics` is the one field of `to_dict()` not mirrored** — unbounded by design (a dict per container app) and every number in it already has its own event and panel (`azure_cost_snapshot`, `agent_eval_summary`, live Azure Monitor metrics); this event carries the verdict and the fields that produced it, which is what nothing else can say. **No `tenant_id`/`request_id`**, matching `agent_eval_summary` / `extraction_benchmark_run` / `azure_cost_snapshot` / `ops_digest_run` rather than the per-turn events: a scheduled ops pass has no request and no tenant in scope, and an always-empty column invites a join that can never match. The two severity strings are restated as literals in `telemetry.py` rather than imported (nothing there imports from `services/`) and pinned equal to `ops_recommendation.SEVERITY_RED/YELLOW` by test, because a drift would silently zero `red_count` on every event.

  **Wiring**: `services/ops_recommendation.py` gained `mirror_recommendation_pass()` — returns a `MirrorResult`, never raises, exactly the contract `mirror_extraction_run`/`mirror_agent_eval_run` hold — called from `recommendation_pass_step()` in `scripts/run_agent_eval.py` right after the pass prints. `run_recommendation_pass()` itself still emits nothing, so computing a verdict and persisting one stay separable. Nightly-only (the existing gate now covers the emission too) and inside the existing swallow-everything wrapper, so this is fail-soft twice over. Skipped under `--no-mirror`, which already means "emit no event, upload no artifact" for the `agent_eval_summary` mirror — one flag, not a second. **The non-obvious part is the second flush**: `main()`'s mirror block has already called `flush_run_telemetry()` by the time this step runs, and the OTel exporter batches on a timer, so without re-flushing here the three events would be dropped on process exit — the Gap 292/309 "the job ran and the workbook shows nothing" class.

  **Verified**: `pytest tests/test_ops_recommendation.py tests/test_telemetry.py tests/test_run_agent_eval_cli.py tests/test_benchmark_artifacts.py tests/test_extraction_benchmark.py tests/test_azure_cost.py tests/test_model_substitution.py -p no:randomly` → **381 passed, 1 skipped** (pre-existing skip), of which **21 are new**: 8 in `tests/test_telemetry.py` (52 → 60), 7 in `tests/test_ops_recommendation.py` (64 → 71), 6 in `tests/test_run_agent_eval_cli.py` (13 → 19). Re-run **without** `-p no:randomly` across the five telemetry-touching files → 197 passed, so nothing here depends on ordering. Covered: three events from a *real* `run_recommendation_pass()` result in `CATEGORY_ORDER`, one shared `generated_at`; a `no_data` category still emitting with its errors (two rows where three were expected reads as "health is fine"); a 120-app pass producing 240 findings, where the event stays under the char cap, still parses as JSON and still reports `finding_count == 240`; a 400-finding × 5,000-char pathological input; a non-JSON-serialisable `Finding.value`; `--no-mirror` and both non-nightly labels emitting nothing; a raising `track_ops_recommendation` reported on the `MirrorResult` and never propagated (the run still writes its output and exits normally). **Mutation-checked**: replacing the drop-whole-entries loop with a naive `text[:8000]` fails exactly the "stays valid JSON" test; deleting the second `flush_run_telemetry()` fails exactly the flush test. `ruff check` clean on all six touched files. Full suite not re-run — additive new code plus one call site, and the same surrounding files were covered at the end of the A-track (Gap 316).

  **Not done here, on purpose**: no panel (Gap 320), no bicep, no workbook JSON, and **no `az` command of any kind** — this is code only. Live proof is impossible until a backend image carrying Gaps 308/317/318/319 is pushed and one nightly execution runs; that is the same pending image refresh `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s blockers table already carries, and `ops_recommendation` is 0 rows until it lands.

- `[x]` **Gap 320 (Infra): new Workbook panel rendering the persisted recommendations** — closed 2026-08-26. One plain-grid panel added to **both** workbooks: `ops-recommendations-header` + `ops-recommendations` in `cost_health_workbook.json` (25 → 27 items, inserted after `alerts-table`, before `footer`) and `section-h-header` + `h1-ops-recommendations` in `ai_control_tower_workbook.json` (49 → 51 items, inserted after `g2-production-scores`, before `footer`, becoming Section H). Both grid items carry the **byte-identical** `query` string, only the `title` differs — proven by `sha256` on the string extracted programmatically from each written file: `1dbc6ccc69e65474f7c01d143165cd2fae22419fcaf0b1b951821800cbaa96aa` on both.

  **A real KQL trap surfaced mid-task and is now folded into the traps list**: `title` is a reserved word in KQL — `tostring(d.title)` fails to parse (`SYN0002` at token `title`); confirmed live against `law-invoicellm-dev` with a minimal repro (`print x = todynamic('{"title":"a"}').title` → same `SyntaxError`). Fixed with bracket notation, `tostring(d["title"])`, which parses and executes cleanly. Add this to `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s existing traps list alongside `latest`.

  **Query shape**, matching the founder-approved design exactly: `let rows = AppEvents | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d) | where Name == "ops_recommendation" | extend d = parse_json(Properties) | extend run_ts = todatetime(tostring(d.generated_at)); let latest_run = toscalar(rows | summarize max(run_ts)); rows | where run_ts == latest_run | project Category = tostring(d["title"]), Status = tostring(d.status), Explanation = tostring(d.explanation), Recommendation = iff(isempty(tostring(d.recommendation)), "No recommendation yet", tostring(d.recommendation)), Findings = toint(d.finding_count), Red = toint(d.red_count), Yellow = toint(d.yellow_count), Errors = tostring(d.errors), Run = strcat(tostring(d.run_label), " @ ", tostring(d.generated_at)) | order by Category asc`. Global max via `toscalar(... | summarize max(run_ts))`, not `arg_max(...) by category` — one run or zero rows, never a mix. `run_ts` computed via `extend` before `summarize`, never inlined. Run key is `generated_at`, never `TimeGenerated`. No `run_label` filter (emission already nightly-gated upstream). `Errors` projected, not dropped. "No recommendation yet" is rendered literally whenever `d.recommendation` is empty — never inferred from data volume, no extra minimum-sample guard layered on top (the emitter already guarantees the contract per Gaps 318/319).

  **Not done here, on purpose** (founder decision, stated in scope): **no colouring** — plain grid only, no `visualization`, no `tileSettings`, no column-formatter/threshold coloring, no `customWidth`, no `conditionalVisibility` — because a row can carry a "bad" `worst_severity` internally while `recommendation` is empty, and colouring on that would visually contradict "No recommendation yet"; no counts tile; no new bicep file (existing `workbook-*-only.bicep` `loadTextContent()` the JSON directly); no backend/telemetry/test code changes; no image build/push; Monitoring Reader RBAC grant and `alert-rules.bicep`'s pending change untouched.
