- `[x]` **Gap 322 (Infra): ops workbook redesign — priority-ranked information architecture** — design approved by the founder 2026-08-26, **implemented and deployed live to `rg-invoice-llm-dev` 2026-08-26**. Full spec: `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md` → "Workbook redesign — priority-ranked information architecture (2026-08-26)". Gap number assigned by the founder at implementation time. **Correction, merge with a concurrent thread:** Gap 307's closure note had recommended 321 for the future online production judge, but 321 was independently spent the same day on real SendGrid work (see the Gap 321 entry above) — that reference is stale and needs repointing to 324, not resolved here.

  **What it is.** Both workbooks (`infra/monitoring/cost_health_workbook.json`, `infra/monitoring/ai_control_tower_workbook.json`) are reorganised onto one shared 3-tier structure, ordered by the founder's stated priority: **Tier 1 Cost & Reliability** (cost awareness / reliability / speed) → **Tier 2 Extraction & Chat Quality** (extraction quality / chat quality nightly / chat quality real usage) → **Tier 3 Cost Reduction** (where cost concentrates). Cost *reduction* is deliberately last despite being the most built-out area today — today's page is ordered by build effort, not by what has to be read first. The `ops_recommendation` cards move **inline into each tier next to the data they explain** (filtered on the event's `category`), replacing Gap 320's single bottom-of-page grid — the founder was explicit that burying recommendations at the end is a real usability problem.

  **Scope: workbook JSON + bicep only.** No production code, no new telemetry, no emitter change — `ops_recommendation` already carries `category`, one row per category per run (Gap 319). Implementation is infra-devops's; this tracker entry and the spec section are senior-dev's docs-only output.

  **Carries an 11-item panel fix list**, each verified against the committed workbook JSON during the 2026-08-26 audit and written out in full in the spec section: (1) `cost-trend-budget`/`cost-by-service` read a stale ₹150 snapshot vs. the real deployed ₹20,000 budget; (2) `db-status-redis-liveness` renders a permanent false "Unhealthy" red because dev Redis has no geo-replication; (3) `db-status-postgres-liveness` averages a liveness signal over 6h and stays "Down" for 6h after recovery; (4) `alerts-trend` is titled "per day" but has no daily binning at all; (5) `a5-genai-dependency-duration` never filters `run_source`, blending eval and production traffic into a panel whose job is a production cross-check; (6) `a6-sage-per-tool-cost` — **delete**, `agent_name startswith "sage."` can never match since Gap 316; (7) `b6-stop-reasons`' label is actively wrong (`ENABLE_AGENTIC_SAGE` no longer exists and the field is populated on the live default route); (8) `e2-confusion-cells` still uses the `top 1 by TimeGenerated desc` trap that returns zero rows instead of an honest "n/a"; (9) the two recommendation panels — **already resolved**, record as fixed, they only need relocating; (10) `f1-breached-signals` colours off `breached` alone and never reads `value`, so a permanently-zero signal renders false green forever; (11) `d1-latest-pass-rate` / `d2-accuracy` band recalibration.

  **Fix 11 needs its own separate gap number, and this is the one real cross-boundary risk.** Tonight's real 25.7% pass rate and 60% accuracy both render green/blank under the current bands — the bands were calibrated to what the system happened to be doing, so the tile can no longer report a bad number as bad. But `d1`'s grid is pinned by `tests/test_ops_recommendation.py::test_each_band_is_still_the_live_panels_band` against `services/ops_recommendation.py::SCORE_BANDS["pass_rate"]` — **changing the JSON alone turns the backend suite red**, and moving the constant is a production-code change. Worse, `d2-accuracy` is **not** in that test's parametrize list even though `SCORE_BANDS["accuracy"]` mirrors it, so retuning it would diverge *silently*. Whoever takes Fix 11 must move both constants and add `d2-accuracy` to the parametrize list.

  **Also decided here: the n=3-run guard on the golden bank is wrong on its own terms** and comes off `d1`/`d2`. The bank is a fixed, deterministic, exhaustive 35-case script run identically every night — there is no sampling error for a "wait for more runs" guard to protect against. This is the same argument `services/ops_recommendation.py` already makes in code when it exempts `context_drift` from its own n=20 guard, applied one level up. Narrower than it sounds: **the n=20 guard on live-traffic rates stays**, because those genuinely are rates over a variable sample.

  **New observability, no fix: Dashboard Insights latency.** P95 23.1s over 7 days, root-caused in the code's own docstring (`routers/dashboard.py::get_dashboard_insights`, Gap 30 / Gap 279) — a synchronous Azure OpenAI call, 13–19.5s on a cache miss and sub-second on a hit, confirmed bimodal in live data (4 of 6 real calls 13–23s, 2 under 1s). The severe half of this was already fixed under Gap 279 (it used to be `async def` with a fully blocking body, freezing the worker and stalling unrelated concurrent requests). A panel showing the cache-miss/cache-hit split is in scope; **actually fixing the latency — pre-warming the cache or moving generation to a background job — is explicitly out of scope and is a separate future gap.**

  **Explicitly out of scope** (listed so it cannot be silently expanded): scheduling `scripts/sweep_azure_cost.py` as a real Azure job (separate infra task — Fix 1 makes staleness *visible*, not the data fresh); scheduling Gap 305's `scripts/emit_online_signals_job.py` (blocked on the founder's still-open dead-signal decision — note Fix 10's colouring bug is **not** blocked on it and is correct either way); fixing Dashboard Insights' latency; any production code change beyond Fix 11's own gap.

  **Update 2026-08-26: Fix 11's own gap is `Gap 323`, and its code half is closed** — see the entry below. The remaining 10 fixes, the 3-tier reorganisation, and Fix 11's JSON half are all still open and still infra-devops's.

  **Update 2026-08-26 (infra-devops, implementation and live deploy) — DONE, all 11 fixes + the 3-tier restructure + the new Dashboard Insights panel, deployed to `rg-invoice-llm-dev`.**

  **Restructure.** `cost_health_workbook.json` reorganised into **Tier 1 — Cost & Reliability** (Cost awareness → Reliability → Speed sub-sections) and **Tier 3 — Cost Reduction** (no Tier 2 content on this workbook — those fields live only on AI Control Tower). `ai_control_tower_workbook.json` reorganised into **Tier 2 — Extraction & Chat Quality** (nightly golden-bank → real-usage production judge → extraction quality) and **Tier 3 — Cost Reduction** (`a1`/`a2`), with all panels not named in the founder's tier field list (Section A's `a3`-`a5`, Section B chat-turn behaviour, Section C sessions, Section F online signals) kept intact under an appended "Supplementary operational detail" area rather than deleted or force-fit into a tier. Item counts: `cost_health_workbook.json` 27 → **30** items; `ai_control_tower_workbook.json` 51 → **55** items (net of deleting `a6` and the old bottom-of-page recommendation section, and adding tier/sub-tier headers, 3 inline recommendation cards, and the new Dashboard Insights panel).

  **Recommendation cards moved inline**, replacing Gap 320's two bottom-of-page grids entirely (`ops-recommendations-header`/`ops-recommendations` and `section-h-header`/`h1-ops-recommendations` deleted). `cost_health_workbook.json` carries `tier1-cost-recommendation` (Tier 1 Cost awareness) and `tier1-container-health-recommendation` (Tier 1 Reliability). `ai_control_tower_workbook.json` carries `tier2-ai-improvement-recommendation` (Tier 2) only — `container_health`/`cost` cards are **not** duplicated onto AI Control Tower, since that workbook has no underlying reliability/cost panels for them to sit next to; this is a design decision made during implementation, not stated explicitly in the spec, and is flagged here for founder review. All three inline queries keep Gap 320's `toscalar` global-max-before-filter pattern and bracket-notation `d["title"]`/`d["category"]`, verified live against real Log Analytics: `container_health`/`cost` returned real `no_data` rows, `ai_improvement` returned a real `recommend` row with live explanatory text.

  **The 11 fixes, each verified against real Log Analytics data post-deploy:**
  1. `cost-trend-budget` — converted from `top 1 by TimeGenerated desc` to the `toscalar`/equality pattern; stale-snapshot age now surfaced in `Detail` rather than presented as live. Live query confirms the snapshot is real and stale: `budget_amount=150` (INR, the known-stale figure vs. the actual ₹20,000 deployed budget), `snapshot_age_hours=74.1`.
  2. `db-status-redis-liveness` — absent metric now renders `"N/A — not configured in this tier"` (blue) instead of a false red `"Unhealthy"`. Live query today actually returns `Healthy` (the metric is currently present, not absent) — the null-guard path is structurally correct and verified by code review/logic, not exercised by today's live data, since the specific "metric never arrives" condition described in the audit is not the state right now.
  3. `db-status-postgres-liveness` — converted from a 6h average to a real-time read (latest sample in the last 15 min) with a 6h blip count carried in `Detail`. Live query returns `Alive`, `0 sub-1 samples in last 6h`.
  4. `alerts-trend` — converted to real `bin(fired, 1d)` daily binning (Resource Graph `bin()`, plain grid, not tiles). Live query returns 11 distinct day/severity rows across the last 14 days, not one 14-day cumulative count.
  5. `a5-genai-dependency-duration` — added the `run_source == "production"` filter (the same pattern `b6-stop-reasons` already used correctly). Live query now correctly scopes to production and returns 6 agents including `dashboard.insights` (p50/p95 ≈ 17.7s, matching finding 5's Dashboard Insights root-cause).

- `[x]` **Gap 323 (Code): `SCORE_BANDS` recalibrated for `pass_rate` and `accuracy` — the two bands that were tuned to what the system scores, not to what "good" means** — closed 2026-08-26. This is Fix 11 of the workbook-redesign fix list (entry above), split out because it is production code. **Scope was the Python constant and its tests only**; the two workbook `thresholdsGrid`s are deliberately untouched and are infra-devops's mirror task.

  **The defect.** `services/ops_recommendation.py::SCORE_BANDS` copied `d1-latest-pass-rate` (red `<0.20`, yellow `<0.30`) and `d2-accuracy` (red `<0.40`, yellow `<0.55`) from the tiles, and both tiles had been calibrated against whatever the system happened to be scoring when they were written. The consequence, measured on the 2026-08-26 nightly run: a real **25.7% pass rate** rendered yellow-at-best and a real **0.600 accuracy** rendered **green**. A coloured tile that cannot report a bad number as bad has no function.

  **New values:**

  | Band | Was | **Now** |
  |---|---|---|
  | `SCORE_BANDS["pass_rate"]` | `(0.20, 0.30)` | **`(0.60, 0.75)`** |
  | `SCORE_BANDS["accuracy"]` | `(0.40, 0.55)` | **`(0.75, 0.90)`** |

  No other `SCORE_BANDS` entry was touched — faithfulness, relevance, context and orchestration still mirror their tiles exactly, as do the CPU/memory, budget, cost-per-turn, alert-recall, FP-rate and error-rate constants.

  **Why these numbers, since this is a judgement call and not a derivation.** *accuracy* is the only dimension graded against a **known-correct reference answer** — faithfulness and relevance are reference-free judge scores where some spread belongs to the judge, not the product — so its bar is held above faithfulness's `(0.70, 0.85)`. Its red bound is anchored rather than picked: `services/agent_eval.py::decide_pass()` already requires `accuracy >= ACCURACY_FLOOR = 0.70` on **every** turn, so a run whose *mean* accuracy sits under that floor means the average turn is failing the gate outright; red therefore starts just above the floor at 0.75, so the mean has to clear it with a margin instead of sitting on it. Yellow at 0.90 is the working target — roughly nine right answers in ten with room left for judge noise — deliberately short of relevance's near-free 0.95. *pass_rate* is the strictest and the only **conjunctive** number the system produces: `decide_pass()` passes a turn only if faithfulness ≥ 0.80 **and** relevance ≥ 0.70 **and** accuracy ≥ 0.70, all on the same turn. That compounding is exactly why its bar is not simply the highest one in the table — three dimensions each clearing their floor on ~90% of turns still lands the joint rate well below 0.90 with no regression at all, so an 0.85-style bar would flag a genuinely healthy system every night and be tuned out inside a week. Green ≥ 0.75 = three turns in four clean on all three checks; yellow 0.60–0.75 = a majority pass but about one in three fails something; red < 0.60 = more than two in five fail a required check, which is not a shippable product. Net shape: `pass_rate` now carries the **lowest yellow** of any band in the module (0.75) because it is the only conjunction, but **not** the lowest red, because a red pass rate is the loudest single statement this module can make.

  **No minimum-run guard is needed for either band to be honest**, and the module already makes that argument one level down — the `context_drift` exemption comment in `evaluate_ai_improvement()` ("a fixed, exhaustive, deterministic script set — the same handful of pinned checks every night, with no sampling error to guard against"). The same reasoning applies to the workbook's n=3-**run** guard on the golden bank, but removing that guard is JSON and was **not** done here. The n=20 **graded-turns** guard (`MIN_GRADED_TURNS`) is a different thing and stays — that one is about how few turns a given run graded, which genuinely can be small.

  **The test-pinning gap this also closed.** `test_ops_recommendation.py::test_each_band_is_still_the_live_panels_band` pinned `d1-latest-pass-rate` to the constant but had **no `d2-accuracy` entry at all**, even though `SCORE_BANDS["accuracy"]` mirrored that tile exactly — so retuning either side would have diverged in silence. `d2-accuracy` is now in the parametrize list.

  **The transitional state, which is deliberate and self-clearing.** The constant moved and the JSON did not (out of scope here), so the two are genuinely inconsistent until infra-devops mirrors them. Rather than delete the pin (next drift goes unnoticed) or leave the suite red (the signal stops meaning anything), both params carry `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` plus a new `test_the_two_recalibrated_bands_still_await_their_json_mirror` that asserts the live grid is still *exactly* the old `(0.20, 0.30)` / `(0.40, 0.55)` — so a third wrong value written into the grid is caught too, which a bare `xfail` would not catch. When infra mirrors the JSON, the two params XPASS, which `strict=True` reports as a failure, forcing removal of both markers and that transitional test.

  **Also flagged, not fixed — a second unpinned copy of these numbers.** `infra/alert-ai-eval-critical-only.bicep` (Gap 299) hardcodes the red bands as parameter defaults (`passRateRedBelow = '0.20'`, `accuracyRedBelow = '0.40'`), plus the same values inside the alert's `description` string and its header comment. **No test pins that file**, so it fails silently: the critical alert would keep firing on the old permissive bands while the tile and the recommendation pass use the new ones. It needs `'0.60'` / `'0.75'` in the same infra pass. Not touched here — bicep is infra scope and needs a real deploy.

  **Update 2026-08-26 (infra-devops, Gap 322) — done.** `passRateRedBelow`/`accuracyRedBelow` mirrored to `'0.60'`/`'0.75'` (plus the `description` string and header comment), redeployed live, `az monitor scheduled-query show` confirms the new values are in effect. Both workbook `thresholdsGrid`s mirrored the same pass; `test_each_band_is_still_the_live_panels_band`'s two `d1`/`d2-accuracy` params XPASSed as predicted, and the transitional `_AWAITING_JSON_MIRROR` markers + `test_the_two_recalibrated_bands_still_await_their_json_mirror` were removed per this entry's own handover note. See the Gap 322 entry above for full evidence.

  **Verification (real, this run).** `pytest tests/test_ops_recommendation.py` — baseline before the change **83 passed**; after the change **84 passed, 2 xfailed in 33.81s** (the 2 xfails are the pending-mirror params above; the +1 pass is the new transitional test, whose second param is the `d2-accuracy` entry). `ruff check services/ops_recommendation.py tests/test_ops_recommendation.py` → All checks passed. Behavioural proof on the numbers that motivated the gap, by running the module rather than reading it: a 35-turn payload with `pass_rate=0.257` / `accuracy_mean=0.600` now returns `recommend` with findings `pass_rate 0.257 red — below 0.60` and `accuracy 0.600 red — below 0.75`, where both previously produced no finding at all. **No Azure call, no deploy, no workbook JSON, no bicep.** Full reasoning also written into the module docstring ("The two recalibrated bands (Gap 323)") and into `feature_20_23_24_ops_workbook.md`'s Fix 11, which carries the exact mirror table for infra-devops.

  **Part 1 — new "Ops Summary" workbook.** `infra/monitoring/ops_summary_workbook.json` (new) + `infra/workbook-ops-summary-only.bicep` (new), following the exact `loadTextContent()` + pinned-GUID pattern of `workbook-cost-health-only.bicep`/`workbook-ai-control-tower-only.bicep`/`rbac-monitoring-cost-only.bicep`. New pinned GUID `7107048d-2102-4882-ae14-f1e51c8bc21d`. One table, one combined KQL query (`union` of 4 per-category sub-queries sharing a 7-column schema — the cleanest shape given all 4 rows share the same output columns), 4 rows, no scrolling: **Category | Status | Key metric | What's flagged | Recommendation | Recent Activity | Go to detail**.

  - **Cost Control** — Status/Flagged/Recommendation from the latest `ops_recommendation` event, `category=="cost"` (same `toscalar(max(generated_at))` pattern as `h1-ops-recommendations`); Key metric from `azure_cost_snapshot`'s own `budget_percent_used` (independent source, per the founder's field table — real data even on a night the `ops_recommendation` cost category itself reads `no_data`, since the two are different collection paths); Recent Activity from the same event's `day_over_day_change_pct`, only rendered when `abs(pct) > 10`.
  - **Infrastructure** — same `ops_recommendation` pattern, `category=="container_health"`; Key metric is `red_count + yellow_count` from the event, rendered as `"not measured"` (not a false-safe "0 app(s) with an issue") when status is `no_data`/`insufficient_data`; Recent Activity from a live `ContainerAppSystemLogs_CL` query, filtered to exactly 5 event types (`SuccessfulRescale`/`TriggeredScaleUp`/`ReplicaUnhealthy`/`ContainerCrashing`/`RevisionUpdate`, routine noise like `SyncingSecretFromAzureKeyVaultForContainerAppSucceeded`/`KEDAScalersStarted`/`ContainerAppReady` excluded), translated to plain English via a `datatable` lookup, formatted `"<app> <plain-English event>, <relative time>"`.
  - **API Health** — new, live-computed, **not** from `ops_recommendation` (that category does not exist in `services/ops_recommendation.py` and none was added — explicitly out of scope). Queries `AppRequests` directly (same source `api-perf-error-rate` uses), last 24h, overall error rate. Status `healthy` (<5%), `degraded` (>=5%), `no data` (0 requests). Recent Activity is the worst hourly-binned per-area spike (`Requests >= 5` floor to avoid single-request 100%-error-rate noise), formatted `"<area> error rate spiked to N% at HH:mm"`, matching the founder's literal example format.
  - **AI Health** — `ops_recommendation`, `category=="ai_improvement"`; Key metric (pass rate) extracted from the event's own `findings` JSON array (`field=="pass_rate"`), since `mirror_recommendation_pass()` deliberately does not mirror raw `metrics` onto the event (see `telemetry.py::track_ops_recommendation` docstring) — falls back to `"n/a — see AI Control Tower Section D"` on a run where pass_rate was in-band and therefore never became a `Finding`; Recent Activity is the single highest-severity finding's `detail` text (red ranked above yellow), per the founder's "most severe finding" instruction.
  - **Status coloring**: normalized to a 5-value vocabulary (`healthy`/`warning`/`critical`/`degraded`/`no data`) so one `gridSettings` `thresholdsGrid` colors all 4 rows correctly — `worked`→healthy(green), `recommend`+red→critical(red), `recommend`+yellow→warning(yellow), `no_data`/`insufficient_data`→"no data"(blue); API Health emits `healthy`/`degraded`/`no data` directly per its own literal 3-state spec.
  - **"What's flagged"**: broadened from "only when `status==recommend`" to "whenever `status != worked`" — a `no_data` row's `explanation` (e.g. "Monitoring Reader grant... never deployed") is genuinely useful and would otherwise render as a blank cell indistinguishable from a rendering bug.

The report's other structural suggestion — merging the dozens of single-row Metric\|Value\|Detail grids into fewer multi-row grids per section — was not done: it restructures each section's underlying query (each single-row grid today is its own independent KQL query; merging them means combining N queries into one union per section), which is more than a caption/formatting change and doesn't fit an "only visual mess" scope even though it touches no computed metric's own logic.

- `[x]` **Gap 328 (Infra): merge Postgres's 4 single-row panels into 1, Redis's 4 into 1** — founder-requested follow-up to Gap 327's Issue 9 discussion ("all postgre together and all redis together"): each service's own panels combined into one table (not Postgres+Redis merged together — that was explicitly ruled out as forcing N/A cells from a shared column schema across two structurally different services). `cost_health_workbook.json` 34→28 items (`db-status-postgres-connections`/`-connection-util`/`-liveness` and `db-status-redis-counts`/`-hit-ratio`/`-liveness` deleted, their logic folded into `db-status-postgres`/`db-status-redis`).

  **Shape.** Each service's 3-4 independent KQL queries combined into one query per service (`let` per sub-metric, unioned, explicit `SortOrder` for display order since `union` doesn't guarantee row order) — every computed value's own business logic is unchanged, only co-located. Postgres now renders 7 rows (CPU %/Memory %/Disk %/Connection utilization %/Active connections/Max connections/Liveness); Redis renders 7 rows (Server load %/Memory used %/Connected clients/Cache hits/Cache misses/Cache hit ratio %/Geo-replication health). Introduced a shared `Status` vocabulary (`healthy`/`warning`/`critical`/`info` — `info` for a metric that's real but has no threshold judgment, e.g. a raw connection count) with one colored `Status`-column formatter per table, replacing each old panel's own bespoke color rule — matches the pattern already established on `ops_summary_workbook.json`'s per-category rows.

  **Live-tested before deploy, not just after — 2 real bugs the merge would otherwise have shipped.** (1) `db-status-redis`'s cache-hit-ratio row: dev Redis genuinely has 0 cache hits and 0 misses right now (0 requests in 6h, confirmed via a direct raw-metric query), so the ratio is null by construction — the first draft's `case()` fell through its own null comparison to a false `"healthy"` with a blank `Detail` (`"%"`, no number) — this is the **exact bug the founder's original visual-QA report described** (Issue 2: `cache_hit_ratio_pct` renders a red/green bar with no number). Fixed: `isnull(Pct)` now maps to `Status="info"`, `Detail="no cache traffic in 6h"`. (2) `db-status-redis`'s geo-replication row: the pre-merge panel's own caption asserted "dev Redis has no geo-replication configured, so the metric never arrives" — live data shows `geoReplicationHealthy=1` continuously over the last 6h, i.e. that claim is stale (geo-replication is now configured and reporting healthy). The `case()` logic itself was already correct (handles `isnull` → info, `>=1` → healthy, else → critical); only the caption asserted a now-false permanent fact. Caption reworded to describe the mechanism (`info` = not reporting) rather than assert current state as fixed.

  **Verification.** Both merged queries run live against `law-invoicellm-dev` before touching the deployed resource (this is what caught the 2 bugs above) and again after the fix. `az bicep build` clean. `az deployment group what-if` → exactly 1 `Modify`, no unrelated changes. `az deployment group create` → `provisioningState: Succeeded`. Pull-back via direct `curl` (`?canFetchContent=true`) confirmed byte-identical: 28/28 items.

- `[~]` **Gap 329 (BE): `GET /dashboard/metrics` has no `flow_direction` filter — outbound spend silently contaminates every inbound dashboard number** — founder-flagged 2026-08-27 ("top clients and vendor section is showing unknown vendor whereas no such vendor exists, i deleted them still its showing").
  - **Root cause, confirmed in code and with real live data.** `routers/dashboard.py`'s shared `conditions` list (`get_dashboard_metrics`, ~line 93-103) filters on `tenant_id` and `invoice_not_deleted()` only — grepped the whole file, `flow_direction` appears **zero** times. Compare `routers/outbound_dashboard.py`, which correctly adds `Invoice.flow_direction == "OUTBOUND"` to its own conditions in both query blocks (line 68-70, 152-154) — the inbound side never got the mirrored `== "INBOUND"` filter, despite a docstring elsewhere (`outbound_dashboard.py`'s own comment) asserting `dashboard.py` is already inbound-only ("the zero-touch rule... duplicated here against flow_direction == OUTBOUND instead"). That assumption is false in the actual query.
  - **This is not a "deleted vendor lingering" bug — the founder's premise was wrong in a useful way.** `vendor_expr = func.coalesce(Invoice.vendor_name, "Unknown Vendor")` (line 152) coalesces any row with a null `vendor_name` into the "Unknown Vendor" bucket — and every OUTBOUND invoice structurally has `vendor_name = NULL` (it has `customer_name` instead). Live proof, this exact tenant (`4aa68e27-38dd-4b3b-bd21-56256e0d37f2`): 3 real, non-deleted OUTBOUND rows — `Deccan Machinery Ltd` (SENT, ₹484,390), `Kaveri Auto Components Pvt Ltd` (SENT, ₹483,210), `Deccan Machinery Ltd` (PAID, ₹484,390) — all bleeding into the inbound dashboard's "Unknown Vendor" row. No number of deleted named inbound vendors will ever make it disappear, because it was never a vendor.
  - **Blast radius is wider than Top Vendors — same `conditions` list feeds the entire endpoint**: `totals_by_currency` (all 4 KPI cards — total invoiced, paid, outstanding, at-risk), `status_counts`, `top_vendors`, and `spend_over_time` (the Invoice Spend Trend chart) are all built from the same unfiltered query. For any tenant with both flows enabled, every number on the inbound dashboard is inflated by outbound spend, not just the vendor list.
  - **Outbound side independently verified clean** — `outbound_dashboard.py`'s Top Customers and Invoice Receivables Trend correctly scope to `flow_direction == "OUTBOUND"` in code; not contaminated by inbound data.
  - **Fix is a one-line addition** (`Invoice.flow_direction == "INBOUND"` added to the shared `conditions` list) **but the blast radius demands real before/after verification**, not a type-check: every aggregate this endpoint returns changes once outbound rows are excluded, on a tenant that actually has both flows populated (this exact tenant, live, has real data for both).
  - **Built 2026-08-27.** `routers/dashboard.py`'s shared `conditions` list now reads `[Invoice.tenant_id == context.tenant_id, Invoice.flow_direction == "INBOUND", invoice_not_deleted()]` — every aggregate in `get_dashboard_metrics()` (totals, status breakdown, top vendors, spend-over-time) inherits the fix from the one shared list, no per-query changes needed. `routers/outbound_dashboard.py` untouched (already correct). `queue_worker/handlers.py::get_trainer_impact()`'s own separate `select(Invoice)...` call at line ~350 is a different endpoint (`/trainer-impact`), out of this Gap's scope, left as-is — flagged, not silently fixed alongside this.
  - **Verification**: `pytest tests/test_dashboard.py` — **11 passed** (real Postgres, not SQLite — Hard rule 2). Live before/after proof on the exact tenant this Gap was root-caused against, to be appended here once deployed.

- `[x]` **Gap 330 (BE): PDF field-highlight overlay renders as tiny dots pinned in the top-left corner instead of boxes over the real fields — both inbound and outbound** — founder-flagged 2026-08-27 via a screenshot of the Auditor Review Console (VPI-OUT-2012): a cluster of near-invisible green dots stacked in the corner, not boxes over the vendor name/line items/totals.
  - **Root cause, confirmed in code.** `queue_worker/handlers.py::_run_ocr()` reads each field's position from Document Intelligence's `bounding_regions[].polygon` and stores the raw min/max values directly (`x_min`, `y_max - y_min`, etc.) with no unit conversion. Document Intelligence returns polygon points in the page's own physical unit — inches for `prebuilt-invoice` — never a 0-100 percentage. `components/audit/PdfViewerCanvas.tsx` has always positioned overlay boxes with `left: ${coord.x}%` / `width: ${coord.width}%`, i.e. it has always assumed the stored value already *is* a percentage. A real value like `x=1.0in` on an 8.5in-wide page rendered as `"1% from the left, 1% wide"` — every box collapsed to a sliver pinned near the origin, regardless of the field's true position. Never caught before because this code path (raw polygon → `coordinates` list) had **zero existing test coverage** — confirmed by grepping the whole test suite for `coordinates_list`/`bounding_regions`/`polygon` before writing this fix.
  - **Confirmed single shared code path, so one fix covers both directions.** `queue_worker/outbound_handlers.py` imports and calls the exact same `_run_ocr()` (`from queue_worker.handlers import _run_ocr`) — not a duplicated copy. Inbound and outbound extraction have been equally affected since coordinates existed; this was never an outbound-only bug, the screenshot just happened to be an outbound invoice.
  - **Fix.** Build a `{page_number: (width, height)}` lookup from Document Intelligence's own `result.pages[]` (reported in the same unit as the polygon points, by construction of the API) before the field loop, then normalize every coordinate against its own page's dimensions: `x = (x_min / page_w) * 100`, and the equivalent for `y`/`width`/`height`, rounded to 3 decimals. A field whose `page_number` has no matching `pages[]` entry (dimensions genuinely unknown) is **dropped, not stored with a guessed unit** — silently misrendering one field's box is worse than omitting it.
  - **Not a data-loss bug, but not self-healing either.** Only the overlay position was ever wrong — the extracted field *values* (vendor name, totals, etc.) were always correct, since those come from the LLM extraction step, not the coordinate math. This fix only corrects coordinates for invoices processed *after* it deploys; every invoice already in the database has the bad percentages already baked into its `coordinates` column and will keep showing the broken overlay until re-extracted. A backfill (re-running Doc Intelligence + re-normalizing for existing rows) is a real, separate cost/scope decision, deliberately not bundled into this fix — flagging it rather than silently leaving it undiscussed.
  - **Tests added — this code path had none before.** `tests/test_extraction.py`: `test_run_ocr_normalizes_coordinates_to_percentage` exercises the real `_run_ocr()` Azure branch (not a mocked black box, unlike every other test in this file) against a fake `AnalyzeResult` shaped like the real SDK response — an 8.5in×11in page, a field polygon at 1.0-3.0in × 1.0-1.5in — and asserts the stored percentages are mathematically exact (`x≈11.765`, `y≈9.091`, `width≈23.529`, `height≈4.545`) and every value lands in `[0, 100]`. `test_run_ocr_skips_coordinate_when_page_dimensions_missing` asserts a field on a page with no matching `pages[]` entry is dropped, not stored wrong.
  - **Verification**: `pytest tests/test_extraction.py` — **9 passed** (7 pre-existing + the 2 new ones above), real Postgres, not SQLite. Live before/after screenshot proof of a re-extracted invoice's overlay boxes landing on the real fields, to be appended here once deployed.

## Nice-to-Have / Future Enhancements


Not gaps against any spec'd design — the current pipeline behaves correctly end-to-end. These are UX/observability improvements worth doing later, kept separate from the Gap list above so they don't get mistaken for defects.
